# Ogabassey Ultra-Low-Cost Storefront Delivery Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` (recommended) or `superpowers:executing-plans` to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. Each task is a separate reviewed PR unless it explicitly defines smaller review slices; Task 2 defines three, while Tasks 1 and 12 each require a reviewed executable/tooling PR followed by a separate non-executable evidence PR after owner-approved operations.

**Goal:** Make anonymous browsing for the Ogabassey merchant (`merchantSlug: "ogabassey"`, canonical host `ogabassey.com`) independent of Vercel, Supabase, and the VPS, while checkout, accounts, payments, repairs, IMEI, and inventory validation remain authoritative and dynamic. Keep the separate no-Gemini/free-plan savings work in this plan, but do not enroll any other storefront in the Worker/R2 pilot.

**Architecture:** Keep Baci's shared multi-tenant dashboard and transaction APIs as the control plane. The phase-one release plane is explicitly single-merchant: a bounded publisher materializes immutable Ogabassey releases into one dedicated production R2 bucket exposed through separate preview and production read-only custom domains. Static acceptance runs first on an exact Worker Custom Domain, while production alone uses the exact Ogabassey Worker Route. A credentialless Worker reads an uncached, strongly consistent `ogabassey.com` pointer through the R2 custom domain, fetches only immutable release-ID objects through the normal Cloudflare cache, and returns canonical HTML with `no-store`; code-owned dynamic routes use same-URL origin pass-through so the original hostname, cookies, method, body, `Origin`, and CSRF behavior reach the unchanged application. PostgreSQL and R2 activation are reconciled with an explicit state machine rather than described as a cross-system atomic transaction. Multi-merchant or wildcard delivery requires a later plan and fresh approval.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16, TypeScript, Supabase PostgreSQL/RLS, React 19, Cloudflare Workers, R2, Vitest, pnpm/Turborepo, existing VPS worker runner.

## Global Constraints

- Do not move checkout, payment webhooks, customer accounts, orders, receipts, wallets, or server-authoritative price/inventory checks to static delivery.
- Public HTML and release data must contain only already-public, published merchant data. No service-role key, merchant credential, customer data, draft data, provider response body, or signed URL may enter R2.
- The public Cloudflare Worker must have no R2 binding and no Supabase, payment, AI, email, service-role, or storage-write credential. It may fetch only the public serving origin over HTTPS.
- Release object keys are immutable. Activating a release changes one hostname pointer only after every referenced object exists and passes validation.
- The R2 hostname pointer is the source of truth because R2 is strongly consistent. Workers KV must not be the activation source of truth because it is eventually consistent.
- The pointer path `/hosts/{normalizedHostname}.json` must bypass Cloudflare caching. Canonical Worker HTML must use `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Only immutable `/releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/...` objects may receive long edge caching; their direct release-origin browser TTL is capped at six hours so a deleted account cannot keep serving a year-old browser-cache copy inside the 24-hour erasure contract.
- Production R2 access uses custom domains only; the `r2.dev` development URL is disabled. Explicit Cache Rules bypass `/hosts/*`, cache only immutable `/releases/*` objects, and enable Smart Tiered Cache for the release origin after the live account gate confirms it is included and approved.
- The Worker validates a hostname pointer before any request can reach origin. Only an explicit `origin` pointer, or an explicit `edge` pointer plus a code-owned route-and-method rule, may use same-URL `fetch(request)` pass-through to the configured Cloudflare origin. There is no blanket non-`GET`/`HEAD` fallback: an unknown method/path combination terminates at the edge with `405` and never invokes Vercel. Once a hostname pointer is in `edge` mode, every other `GET`/`HEAD` stays at the edge regardless of attacker-supplied cookies, queries, or random paths: known routes serve the release, unknown routes serve the release 404, `/search?q=` uses the static search index, and every other query is redirected to the canonical query-free URL. Do not reconstruct the request against a Vercel hostname.
- Phase one attaches a Worker Route only to `ogabassey.com/*`, after static-only acceptance on the Worker Custom Domain `edge-preview.ogabassey.com`; preview is not a Route and does not claim same-host origin parity. The known Ogabassey aliases `www.ogabassey.com` and `ogabassey.usebaci.com` receive exact-host Cloudflare Redirect Rules only for `GET`/`HEAD` requests that the shared policy classifies as static browsing candidates, targeting `https://ogabassey.com/$path` with query preservation. API/machine/dynamic paths and only non-`GET`/`HEAD` combinations explicitly allowed by the shared route-and-method policy retain the current same-origin alias-aware origin path because cross-origin canonical redirects can lose cookies, fail CORS, or alter request semantics; they are measured as dynamic origin traffic, not counted as eligible alias redirects. A generated pre-Worker WAF rule blocks every other alias mutation combination before Vercel. Task 1 inventories DNS, certificate, request volume, method/path class, and every additional live hostname that resolves/redirects to this merchant before implementation. No wildcard or other merchant hostname is enrolled. The publisher accepts only canonical pointer hostname `ogabassey.com`, slug `ogabassey`, and the owner-approved merchant ID. The Worker separately validates the routed host against its environment (`edge-preview.ogabassey.com` or `ogabassey.com`) and still requires the pointer hostname to remain canonical `ogabassey.com`; request-host and pointer-host identities are never substituted.
- The Ogabassey renderer is a merchant-parity renderer, not the generic 25-product free theme. Its bounds are derived from a live, read-only preflight inventory and checked into an approved capacity file with at least 2x item/route and 1.5x byte headroom. The separate free-plan contract remains one Baci subdomain with one static theme, 25 active products, 20 categories, 10 posts, 10 content pages, 250 MiB media, no custom domain, and no included AI/image generation. Free merchants remain on the current origin delivery path in this plan; Task 11 is not an enrollment condition for the Ogabassey pilot.
- Every AI/vision/image provider call, including background work, must cross one server-only spend-authorization gateway. Anonymous/free/missing/malformed/expired-plan requests fail before provider/model construction; no route-local UI check is accepted as the cost boundary.
- Paid AI is outside this release-plane project. Do not add or widen paid-plan AI entitlements here. Any later paid AI plan must consume prepaid/metered credits before provider construction and must fail closed when the credit or provider budget is exhausted.
- Every store except the one approved Ogabassey merchant stays on the current Next.js delivery path. A later merchant may be considered only after this pilot and through a separate reviewed rollout plan.
- Checkout recalculates price, discount, shipping, availability, and inventory from authoritative server state. Snapshot values are display-only.
- Existing migrations are append-only. Any new table has RLS, explicit grants, indexes on foreign keys, bounded claims, generation fencing, retry/backoff, and dead-letter behavior.
- Do not modify `apps/web/src/proxy.ts` during Tasks 1-9. If later origin compatibility requires a proxy change, obtain explicit owner approval and isolate it in its own reviewed PR.
- Do not add a server or database for Ogabassey. One dedicated Ogabassey R2 bucket, its two exact read-only custom domains, one static-only preview Worker Custom Domain, and one production Worker Route are permitted for isolation in this pilot; the code and schemas must remain reusable, but runtime allowlists stay single-merchant.
- Production has both an authenticated-owner one-action pointer CAS rollback to `origin` through the dedicated highest-eligible `edge_pointer_rollback` VPS publication path and an emergency exact-route disable that restores the pre-Worker Cloudflare-to-origin path. Neither requires a code deployment. The route-safe action is always first when Worker health is uncertain; pointer rollback never delays exact-route deletion.
- PostgreSQL is the durable authority for the accepted Worker generation, current deployment head, completed 24-hour gate receipts, and every provider-mutation attempt. Cloudflare deployment history is reconciliation evidence only: no guardian, recovery path, promotion, or later Worker rotation may require an old provider deployment to remain listable. Every deployment POST—including the origin-100/candidate-0 staging deployment required before the first 1% canary—is preceded by an atomic database `provider_attempted` transition and may execute at most once for that operation ID; a timeout or lost response never becomes permission to issue the POST again. `ambiguous` is terminal for that script identity and generation: no abandonment or same-script replacement operation exists. Recovery confirms one exact match or removes the route and requires a fresh script identity plus re-enrollment. Production ingress never permits a version-override header; version overrides are confined to Task 1's isolated disposable evidence Worker.
- R2 and PostgreSQL cannot commit atomically. Serving follows the R2 pointer; PostgreSQL records `building`, `assets_verified`, `pointer_committed`, `active`, `failed`, and `retired`, and a reconciler repairs every crash window.
- The VPS publisher must not receive `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` or a PostgreSQL login. It uses the public Supabase anon key plus a rotating HMAC capability accepted only by the release RPCs. Separate IP-restricted, bucket-scoped R2 parent credentials locally sign short-lived, exact-action/exact-path temporary sessions for publication and GC; the parent secrets never enter child script environments, publisher/GC sessions are not interchangeable, and GC cannot start until the last recorded publisher session has expired by database time. Every R2 mutator shares one absolute VPS lock and a database publication epoch/lease/session fence. The wrappers start with clean, allowlisted environments.
- The lifecycle-bound account-deletion finalizer is a new privileged credential boundary, not an inherited service-role exception. Its migration/PR requires explicit owner and security approval of the exact Ogabassey-only HMAC action, no caller-selected identity, pointer-proof precondition, transactional identifier erasure, grants, and tests before merge; no sibling RPC or merchant inherits it.
- Static HTML contains no executable inline script or inline style, no Next.js RSC reference, and no `/_next/static` URL. The only inline scripts are deterministic `application/ld+json` blocks whose SHA-256 values appear in the route manifest and response CSP. Speculation rules are an immutable allowlisted release-origin `application/speculationrules+json` object referenced by the `Speculation-Rules` response header with credentialless CORS, never an inline `<script type="speculationrules">`. Versioned executable JavaScript, CSS, fonts, and integration loaders are immutable release assets.
- Static pages reproduce the reviewed storefront security-header contract. Mutation CSRF remains enforced by the unchanged origin `proxy.ts` after same-host pass-through.
- Cross-origin immutable CSS, JavaScript, fonts, and speculation-rules objects use one closed credentialless R2 CORS policy: allow only `GET, HEAD`; allow only the exact origins `https://ogabassey.com` and (while preview exists) `https://edge-preview.ogabassey.com`; allow no credentials, wildcard origin, wildcard method, request-header reflection, write method, or CORS preflight cache beyond the reviewed policy. The deployment read-back serializes this exact origin/method/credential set, and the browser contract probes every asset class with each admitted origin. Only the fenced, HMAC-ledgered topology operation may perform teardown: it first detaches/reads back the preview Worker Custom Domain, then changes/reads back canonical-only CORS and proves preview-origin rejection, then detaches/reads back the preview R2 domain. Every provider attempt/read-back is bound to one ordered step and its exact before/after tuple. A lost response enters read-only reconciliation: an exact after-tuple confirms that step and permits only the next ledgered step; an exact unchanged before-tuple after the qualified provider visibility bound proves no effect and closes the operation without another write; if deletion is waiting, first-step no-effect binds the still-attached pair, while no-effect after any prior confirmed step classifies the partial tuple as deletion `terminal_drift -> erasure_recovery` and seals conservative `[0,1]`; any mixed/unknown tuple is terminal drift. `confirmed_no_effect` is reserved for a step whose provider write was durably marked attempted; a prepared step preempted before any provider call closes as `cancelled_preempted`. If deletion is already accepted, terminal topology drift automatically enters deletion-safe conservative-host erasure recovery rather than waiting for an owner; without a deletion intent it remains fenced for manual topology repair. A missing canonical origin, extra origin/method, unbounded visibility, or unresolved drift is `STOP` for rollout topology, but cannot suspend the accepted deletion clock.
- Release-domain topology is database-authoritative: the Ogabassey delivery profile has monotonic `release_domain_topology_generation`, closed `preview_attached | preview_detached` state, the canonical active-host-set hash, and at most one fenced topology operation/lease. A `preview_attached` topology is exactly the ordered two-host set; `preview_detached` is exactly the canonical singleton. An accepted deletion intent is an immutable fact row plus a hash-chained append-only event stream; mutable current state exists only in a transactionally derived projection and is never receipt authority. When deletion seals the attached pair, the lifecycle owns a non-expiring `lifecycle_deletion` topology fence from the atomic snapshot through every host proof and finalization; it is not a renewable worker lease and cannot expire during VPS downtime. Teardown takes the exclusive expiring `teardown` operation only when no accepted deletion intent, lifecycle `gone` job, pair-host claim, or lifecycle-deletion fence exists. The profile lock linearizes deletion either after a confirmed detach (singleton) or before it (pair and detach blocked); a request arriving during teardown remains durably queued and is bound immediately after the current step/operation reaches one confirmed topology. Any fence transfer for that request is atomic, gapless, and bound to the same immutable intent. Only successful erasure finalization may clear the last `lifecycle_deletion` fence; recovery can replace it only in the same transaction and never exposes a null-fence interval. Route-safety route deletion retains precedence over every topology operation.
- Before production routing, Cloudflare must reject invalid method/path combinations and rate-limit allowed origin prefixes before they reach Vercel using account-included WAF/rate-limit controls generated from the same shared policy used by the Worker and alias redirects. Apply/read back/probe the rules on the apex and every alias. If the live account cannot express both the block rule and the approved pre-origin rate limits, do not cut over; client-controlled `/api`, `/checkout`, arbitrary mutation paths, or aliases must not become an origin-cost bypass.
- The exact Ogabassey Worker route is security- and cost-critical and must use Cloudflare request-limit fail-closed behavior (`request_limit_fail_open: false`). The default production gate is an owner-approved Workers Paid capacity envelope with at least 4x measured p99 daily headroom. A Free-plan exception is allowed only when Task 12 proves an account-wide usage counter, its maximum observation lag, the UTC reset boundary, GitHub scheduling/action delay, and `remainingAllowanceAtRemoval > measuredWorstCaseRequestsPerMinute * maximumDetectionAndRemovalMinutes`; it freezes promotion at 35% and automatically removes only `ogabassey.com/*` no later than 45%. If any input is estimated, sampled, delayed beyond the proven bound, or cannot include every Worker in the account, the exception is unavailable and production requires Paid. Route removal is read back, probed against the recorded origin, and followed by notification; re-enable is manual and owner-approved. Cloudflare 1027 is never the rollback mechanism.
- The Worker is a Module Worker with `wrangler.jsonc` as configuration source of truth, initial `compatibility_date: "2026-07-31"`, and explicit `compatibility_flags: ["global_fetch_private_origin", "nodejs_compat"]`. `nodejs_compat` follows current Workers production guidance and prevents transitive workspace dependencies from silently failing on Node built-ins; the governed bundle graph and `workerd` tests still reject unreviewed polyfill/dependency growth. The deployment rejects `global_fetch_strictly_public`, reads the deployed settings back, and proves same-host `fetch(request)` reaches the existing origin without Worker recursion. Any later Worker-code, dependency, compatibility-date, compatibility-flag, binding, or observability change requires a fresh reviewed merge/tag and the database-ledgered steady-state rotation protocol in Task 12; direct replacement is forbidden.
- Live delivery evidence is mandatory. Every Worker decision emits exactly one bounded classification (`edge-release`, `edge-reject`, `origin-fallback`, `terminal`, or `edge-error`) without hostname, URL, query, cookie, merchant ID, release ID, or customer data. Synthetic probes validate routing behavior but cannot by themselves prove the production origin-avoidance rate.
- Workers observability may not inherit provider defaults. Operational Gate B selects and prices exactly one checked-in production log sampling contract (`1` only when complete Workers Logs are the census, otherwise `0.01` diagnostic sampling), disables traces in phase one, and binds live settings read-back into every deployment/evidence fingerprint. Costing treats the platform invocation log and the one code-emitted decision log as two distinct billable events for every sampled invocation, qualifies any additional provider error-event multiplier with live probes, and subtracts only the independently measured remainder of the account-wide included quota after other Workers' consumption.
- Workers Logs capacity is plan- and period-aware. Gate A records whether the account is Free or Paid, the provider-returned entitlement/reset contract, every other Worker's consumption, the Free daily allowance or Paid monthly allowance/overage price, and Cloudflare's account-wide daily hard event ceiling that triggers forced sampling. `worker-log` census mode is unavailable unless the owner-approved worst-case account event projection, including the qualified event multiplier and 4x headroom, remains strictly below every no-sampling ceiling for every UTC day in the seven-day proof window; Free has no assumed paid overage path. A quota-period mismatch, undocumented excess behavior, projected forced sampling, or inability to measure all-account usage is `STOP`, not a later `NOT_PROVEN` surprise.
- Retain the active release plus the two most recent retired releases. Delete other retired/failed/orphaned release objects only after seven days, with pointer, publication-epoch, and publisher-lease revalidation before every page. The sole exception is an `erasure_admitted` deletion intent behind a proven `gone` pointer: its release prefixes and cached custom-domain representations are removed within 24 hours while the tiny hostname tombstone remains.
- Do not buy or enable a paid Cloudflare product until the live account limits and price are captured and approved.
- No Cloudflare, R2, DNS, ruleset, Worker-route, migration, secret, crontab, or garbage-collection mutation may run from an uncommitted worktree. The implementing PR must first pass green required checks and fresh exact-head review, merge, and record separate reviewed-head, required-checks, and merged SHAs plus the governed artifact hash; owner-approved operational workflows then run from a protected annotated tag peeled to the recorded merge SHA only after governed artifacts are byte-identical across those identities. Redacted evidence produced by those operations lands in a separate evidence-only PR and cannot alter the reviewed executable artifact. The sole break-glass exception is owner-approved deletion of exactly the `ogabassey.com/*` Worker route when both immutable emergency-workflow dispatch and automatic guardian removal are unavailable; it may not create, update, or delete any other resource and must use the separately scoped break-glass token, inventory/read-back guards, restored-origin probes, and an incident record.
- The Task 1 pre-build gate must prove whether the live `ogabassey.com` zone can express the exact single-cookie `http.request.cookies` Transform Rule used for version affinity. Because Cloudflare currently documents that field as requiring Pro, Business, or Enterprise, any incremental zone-plan cost is part of the business case; unsupported or uneconomic affinity stops the edge pilot before renderer/publisher implementation rather than being discovered during Task 12.
- Account-deletion release cleanup has a capacity-derived 24-hour SLA measured from the database acceptance time of the immutable owner deletion intent, not from later topology binding, `gone`, finalization, or GC claim. The profile-locked lifecycle receipt snapshots the database-authoritative closed active raw-serving-host set and topology generation: exactly `['releases.ogabassey.com']` only after a confirmed `preview_detached` operation, or exactly `['releases.ogabassey.com', 'releases-preview.ogabassey.com']` while `preview_attached`; no caller, fresh provider inventory, or later teardown may select, omit, reorder, or add a host. Cleanup persists that set and an opaque continuation cursor, deletes at most 100 objects per page, never marks a prefix complete until a fresh listing proves it empty, then performs separately fenced exact-host purge-and-absence phases for every sealed active serving host. The capacity proof includes bounded in-flight topology-step reconciliation before binding a queued intent, every host's temporary-credential issuance/expiry, host-purge rate limits/retries, multi-location absence probes, lifecycle-fence crash recovery, and enough bounded drain invocations—with at least 2x timing headroom—to finish every required host inside 24 hours. If the qualified provider visibility/recovery bound cannot preserve that budget, teardown is unavailable while deletion remains accepted and incident-escalated; the SLA is never silently restarted.
- Production deployment remains prebuilt-only. No `vercel deploy --prod` without `--prebuilt`.
- Execution may not begin from a worktree until this plan is either committed on the execution branch or deliberately copied there and its SHA-256 is recorded in the first PR description. The currently untracked root copy is review input, not an executable branch artifact.





---

## Ecommerce-Builder Boundary

The WordPress analogy in this plan is limited to publishing mechanics: a merchant edits structured state in one control plane, a reconciler produces an immutable release, and an edge runtime serves a read-optimized projection. It is not a product-model, page-model, plugin, checkout, or authority analogy. Baci remains an ecommerce builder whose control plane owns catalog identity, product and variant identity, condition offers, price and compare-at price, inventory, availability, promotions, cart identity, checkout reconciliation, shipping, tax, payment, orders, customer accounts, reviews, merchant entitlements, and publish/unpublish workflows.

The Ogabassey R2 release is a bounded browsing read model, never commerce authority. Static documents may present catalog, merchandising, condition-offer, variant, rating, and availability summaries, but they cannot calculate a payable total, reserve stock, authorize a discount, choose a shipment price, accept payment, create an order, mutate a customer account, or become the source of truth for any of those decisions. Cart handoff carries stable identifiers and bounded selections only; the existing dynamic commerce path reloads and validates current product, offer, variant, quantity, price, promotion, inventory, shipping, tax, payment, merchant, and customer state before accepting the transaction. Stale, invalid, unsupported, or semantically incomplete projections fall back to the existing origin path rather than approximating commerce at the edge.

Builder pages and blocks remain commerce-aware contracts, not generic content blobs. Product grids preserve product IDs and pagination semantics; product detail blocks preserve product/offer/variant identity; price, condition, inventory, reviews, and promotion displays declare their source projection and freshness behavior; cart controls emit only the governed identifier handoff; checkout, order, and account components remain dynamic. A generic content-page abstraction must not erase these identities, flatten condition offers into variants, copy transactional state into HTML, or make an edge document authoritative.

The pilot passes only when the following Ogabassey journeys retain their current commerce semantics:

| Journey | Edge responsibility | Authoritative dynamic responsibility | Failure behavior |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog/search/category | Render the complete bounded public projection and stable product links | Search/index source and unpublished/private filtering remain control-plane governed | Unsupported or stale projection uses origin |
| Product detail | Render canonical product, media, brand, base/compare-at price, condition offers, variant options, rating, and review summary | Current offer/variant availability and every transactional decision | Missing identity or invalid offer/variant relation uses origin |
| Condition and variant selection | Preserve separate canonical condition-offer and variant identifiers; reject `attributes.condition` | Revalidate the selected combination and current sellability | Invalid/unknown selection cannot enter an authoritative cart |
| Cart handoff | Send product/offer/variant IDs, quantity, and bounded non-reserved selections only | Reprice, recheck promotions and inventory, and build the authoritative cart | Drift is surfaced and reconciled before checkout |
| Checkout | Link to the existing same-host dynamic checkout | Price, discount, inventory, shipping, tax, payment, fraud, and order creation | Never statically served or silently downgraded |
| Inventory/promotion freshness | Display only the release snapshot within the approved freshness contract | Current inventory and promotion eligibility | Deadline breach switches the serving pointer to origin |
| Customer/order flows | Preserve same-host route and method behavior | Authentication, customer data, orders, returns, and mutations | Always origin-owned |
| Reviews | Render only approved public review projection and aggregates | Submission, moderation, customer binding, and recalculation | Mutations remain origin-owned |
| Merchant builder/publish | Preview structured commerce blocks and expose queued/building/current/stale-origin status | Draft ownership, validation, generation commit, publish/unpublish, and rollback | Unsupported block/config prevents edge eligibility, not merchant editing |

## Current-State Findings

Baci already has several publishing-control-plane primitives that resemble WordPress operationally while retaining Baci's ecommerce domain model:

- Anonymous storefront HTML has layered browser, Vercel, and Cloudflare cache policy in `apps/web/src/config/storefront-cdn-cache-control.ts` and `apps/web/src/proxy.ts`.
- Next Cache Components and a resilient shared remote cache already cover merchants, products, categories, content pages, and blogs in `apps/web/next.config.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/cached-data.ts`.
- Merchant images bypass Vercel's image optimizer through `apps/web/src/lib/image-loader.ts`.
- Plan flags already make the free tier empty and custom domains start at `starter` in `apps/web/src/lib/feature-flags.ts`.
- The existing VPS runner can execute `apps/web` TypeScript directly through `vps-workers/bin/run-web-script.sh`.
- The production workflow and release-coherence checks use `https://ogabassey.com`, and `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` identifies `ogabassey.com` as the Cloudflare zone; that exact host—not the `*.usebaci.com` fleet—is the phase-one cost target.
- Ogabassey has dedicated routes/components under `apps/web/src/app/(storefront)/ogabassey` and `apps/web/src/components/storefront/ogabassey`; a generic default-theme export is not parity.
- The current public application is not origin-independent: a Cloudflare miss still runs Next/Vercel, authenticated hints and arbitrary queries bypass shared HTML caching, and the monolith contains hundreds of API routes.
- `apps/web/src/proxy.ts` derives tenant rewrites from the request hostname and owns mutation Origin/CSRF checks, so a fallback that reconstructs a Vercel-host URL is not equivalent to same-host pass-through.
- A Cloudflare R2 Worker binding exposes object write/delete operations as well as reads; it cannot honestly be treated as a read-only capability.
- Pre-signup onboarding still exposes an anonymous paid AI action through `apps/web/src/ai/flows/guide-business-onboarding.ts` and `apps/web/src/components/onboarding/steps/step2-branding.tsx`.

The architectural objective is therefore not "move Vercel to AWS." It is "stop doing dynamic origin work for anonymous browsing."

## Success Metrics and Stop Conditions

The rollout is successful only when a seven-day production window shows all of the following:

- At least 99.9% of all anonymous Ogabassey ingress requests that are eligible for static document delivery or `GET`/`HEAD` canonical-host redirection avoid Vercel across `ogabassey.com`, `www.ogabassey.com`, `ogabassey.usebaci.com`, and every additional live alias discovered by Task 1. The exact rate is `(canonicalEligibleOriginAttempts + aliasEligibleOriginAttempts) / (canonicalEligibleRequests + aliasEligibleRequests)`; the canonical apex appears only in Worker evidence, aliases appear only in alias HTTP/Redirect evidence, and overlap, omission, or a zero denominator is `NOT_PROVEN`. Report raw totals by host, method, path class, and edge rule ID before eligibility filtering. Policy-allowed API/mutation and other dynamic requests remain separate in the raw report; invalid method/path blocks are reported explicitly. The result comes from an approved exact seven-day census bound to one hostname inventory, eligibility-policy hash, redirect ruleset version, WAF ruleset version, and Worker deployment; adaptive/sampled or configuration-mixed evidence is `NOT_PROVEN`.
- Separately, static-page browser journeys stay within the owner-approved interaction-origin cost envelope established from Task 1's baseline: report exact same-host dynamic calls, Vercel invocations/duration, calls per 1,000 static document sessions, and projected monthly cost by interaction-contract row. The threshold is sealed in the rollout manifest before preview and cannot be inferred from the 99.9% equation. An unclassified call, incomplete browser-network census, or any seven-day production breach freezes edge promotion and restores origin pending a reviewed projection/removal/exception decision.
- 100% of eligible static-candidate `GET`/`HEAD` requests to a noncanonical Ogabassey alias receive the reviewed edge redirect with zero Worker invocation and zero Vercel request; API, machine-readable, other dynamic, and policy-allowed mutation traffic preserve the existing same-origin alias behavior and are reported separately.
- 100% of method/path combinations outside the approved origin policy terminate before Vercel on the apex and every alias; allowed origin traffic is reported separately by rule ID, method, host, and status class and remains inside its approved WAF/rate-limit envelope.
- 100% of requests presented to the Worker with a non-Ogabassey host, missing/mismatched pointer, or cross-tenant release are rejected without a Vercel request; the production Cloudflare route itself matches only `ogabassey.com/*`.
- Zero Supabase queries occur while serving an R2-backed release request.
- Zero free-tier AI or image-generation provider calls occur.
- R2/Worker `5xx` rate is below 0.1%, excluding deliberate origin fallbacks.
- Release activation p95 is below 120 seconds for the approved live Ogabassey capacity fixture.
- A content mutation never exposes a partially written release.
- Checkout rejects a stale snapshot price or unavailable item through the existing authoritative validation.
- One-action apex pointer rollback to `origin` completes within 60 seconds; the separately tested exact-route disable also restores the recorded prior origin within 60 seconds.
- No active hostname pointer or one of its two rollback releases is deleted by retention cleanup.
- From immutable account-deletion intent `acceptedAt`, its public release objects and cached custom-domain representations are removed within 24 hours while the tiny `gone` hostname tombstone remains to prevent origin fallback until the hostname is explicitly reassigned; topology waiting, reconciliation, `gone`, and finalization do not restart that clock.
- Free-tier media, release-byte, route-count, and abuse budgets fail closed on new writes without deleting existing merchant content.
- A pre-implementation seven-day baseline attributes current Cloudflare hits/misses and Vercel invocations/Fluid CPU/memory by Ogabassey host and path class. Implementation stops if anonymous document misses cannot plausibly repay the Worker/R2 build and operating cost, or if dynamic APIs dominate the reducible bill. The same measures are repeated after cutover.
- Zero anonymous/free merchant provider-construction attempts appear at the centralized AI spend gateway.
- Unpublish does not report completion before an `unpublished` pointer is observed, and account deletion does not remove merchant/auth state before a `gone` pointer is observed.

Stop the rollout and leave affected hosts in `origin` mode if any of these occur:

- private/draft/customer data is found in a release object;
- a release references a missing object;
- a hostname resolves to another merchant's release;
- authenticated traffic receives a cached anonymous response where origin behavior is required;
- the Worker cannot distinguish an origin fallback from a release response;
- checkout accepts a snapshot value without authoritative recalculation.
- a dynamic request loses its original hostname, method, body, query, cookies, or `Origin` header during pass-through;
- the public Worker can write to or delete from R2;
- a pointer response reports a `CF-Cache-Status` outside the sealed Gate-B `acceptedCfCacheStatuses`, omits the status, reports any `Age` header, fails the exact Trace/rule-fingerprint read-back, or otherwise shows that `/hosts/*` entered or was served through a Cloudflare cache path;
- the R2 `r2.dev` development URL remains enabled, `/hosts/*` is cacheable, or an existing immutable `/releases/*` key is overwritten;
- either raw release domain lacks the fixed root disallow-all `robots.txt`, lacks the exact noindex response header, or differs from the sealed ordered response-header ruleset fingerprint; a later rule that can overwrite `X-Robots-Tag` is drift even when the intended rule remains enabled;
- the exact Ogabassey route is configured fail-open, its compatibility flag/fail mode cannot be read back, projected next-24-hour requests reach the 35% promotion-freeze threshold without approved paid headroom, or the automatic exact-route disable is untested;
- delivery telemetry is missing, its daily aggregate is incomplete, `MAX(_sample_interval) !== 1`, its bounded decision total does not exactly equal Worker invocations, it is outside its retention/export window, or it contradicts Vercel origin evidence;
- an arbitrary path, unapproved method/path combination, fake auth cookie, or noncanonical query can force an edge-enabled request to Vercel;
- a non-Ogabassey hostname, mismatched live Ogabassey merchant ID, missing-pointer request handled by the Worker, or an eligible alias `GET`/`HEAD` request reaches Vercel; policy-allowed alias API/mutation origin traffic is not misclassified as a failure, while any rejected-method origin request is a failure;
- the generated Cloudflare invalid-method/path block or an approved dynamic-route rate-limit rule is absent, disabled, differs from the shared policy hash, or cannot be proven with `403`/`405` and `429` probes on the apex plus every alias;
- a free, missing-plan, malformed-plan, or expired-plan caller reaches AI provider/model construction;
- a mobile or web public-media upload bypasses an exact live reservation;
- an enrolled unpublish/delete operation removes its database state before the terminal R2 pointer is proven;
- an authenticated deletion response lacks its immutable intent row/receipt/`acceptedAt`, a topology wait resets the 24-hour clock, a provider-attempted topology step is written twice, read-only reconciliation cannot distinguish its exact before/after tuple inside the qualified bound, or a lifecycle-deletion fence expires before finalization;
- the serving Worker buffers or hashes full release bodies on the public request path, or a cross-origin WOFF2/CSS/JavaScript probe fails CORS.

### Task 1: Establish the Origin-Avoidance Cost Contract

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes Zod-validated, privacy-bounded daily aggregates plus a `StorefrontDeliveryEvidenceManifest` covering exactly seven contiguous UTC days. It never accepts raw request rows as production proof.
- Produces `summarizeStorefrontDelivery(manifest): StorefrontDeliverySummary`. A production result is `PASS` only for a complete unsampled census; any provider sampling, count mismatch, missing day, cross-host/tenant origin attempt, or incomplete export yields `NOT_PROVEN` or `FAIL` and blocks production routing.
- Produces `evaluateOgabasseyOriginBusinessCase(input): { verdict: 'PROCEED' | 'STOP' | 'NOT_PROVEN'; reasonCodes: readonly string[] }` from a current seven-day, all-ingress baseline. It rejects percentage-only or apex-only evidence.
- Produces a Zod-validated `CloudflareWorkersLogsPlanContract = { plan: 'free' | 'paid'; allowanceEvents: bigint; allowancePeriod: 'utc_day' | 'billing_month'; allowancePeriodStartsAt: string; allowancePeriodEndsAt: string; currentAllowancePeriodAllAccountEvents: bigint; allowanceUsageSourceFingerprint: string; allowanceMaximumObservationLagSeconds: number; allowanceObservedAt: string; utcDayStartsAt: string; utcDayEndsAt: string; currentUtcDayAllAccountEvents: bigint; utcDayUsageSourceFingerprint: string; utcDayMaximumObservationLagSeconds: number; utcDayObservedAt: string; overageAllowed: boolean; overageUsdPerMillion: string | null; forcedSamplingDailyThreshold: bigint; forcedSamplingRate: string; officialDocsSha256: string; authenticatedEntitlementSha256: string }`. The allowance-period counter prices Free daily or Paid monthly consumption, while the independent UTC-day counter gates Cloudflare's daily forced-sampling threshold; neither may stand in for the other. Each counter carries its own closed boundaries, source fingerprint, observation timestamp, and maximum observation lag. The as-of-implementation expected public contract is Free `200_000` events per UTC day, Paid `20_000_000` included per billing month plus `$0.60` per additional million, and an account maximum of `5_000_000_000` logs per UTC day after which Cloudflare applies `0.01` sampling; implementation must retrieve current official docs and authenticated entitlement, reject any drift instead of silently retaining these values, and never coerce decimal prices/rates through binary floating point.
- Produces `verifyCloudflareEvidenceTokenPolicy(liveToken, ownerExport, reviewedPolicy): Promise<VerifiedEvidenceTokenCapability>`. `/user/tokens/verify` proves only live token ID/status; the separately owner-exported token policy supplies permission/resource arrays and expiration. The function requires the endpoint ID to equal both artifacts, requires the owner export to equal the reviewed account/zone scopes, current permission-group IDs, expiration, and policy hash, and records that provider self-verification cannot independently prove negative permission scope before returning the branded capability consumed by the mutation tool.
- Produces `verifyCloudflareEvidenceReadTokenPolicy(liveToken, ownerExport, reviewedPolicy): Promise<VerifiedEvidenceReadCapability>` for a separate analytics/HTTP/WAF-event read-only token. It rejects every write permission and cannot satisfy the mutation capability type.
- Produces atomic mode-`0600` `CloudflareEvidenceRunJournal` records under the required absolute `EVIDENCE_RUN_STATE_DIR` on durable encrypted operator storage. A journal stores no token or request nonce; before mutation it stores `runId`, approval/policy/write-token/read-token IDs, account/zone IDs, every deterministic run-ID-bearing planned resource name, and pre-inventory hashes, then records returned resource IDs as soon as each mutation succeeds plus expected probe counts, phase, cleanup attempts, read-back evidence, `writeTokenRevokedAt`, and `readTokenRevokedAt`. `openEvidenceRun`, `recordEvidenceMutation`, `recordEvidencePhase`, and `loadEvidenceRunForCleanup` use write-temp/fsync/file-fsync/rename/directory-fsync semantics and reject symlinks, permissive modes, path traversal, or a second active run. Cleanup discovers a resource created during the API-success/journal-write crash gap only by the pre-journaled exact name plus run-ID description and then records its provider ID before deletion. Neither `proof_complete` nor `closed_stop` is a valid terminal phase until every issued token has a verified revocation timestamp.
- Produces three journal-fenced, separately invoked commands for the owner-approved isolated fallback; there is no function, CLI mode, parent process, shell environment, or CI job that accepts both credentials. Credentialless `qualify-cloudflare-evidence-sources.ts --prepare` validates options, creates the initial journal, and prints only the run ID/next phase. `mutate-cloudflare-evidence-sources.ts --run <runId> --apply` accepts only `VerifiedEvidenceTokenCapability`, hard-codes the evidence hostname and synthetic paths/headers, rejects pre-existing collisions, creates/probes/cleans up the bounded resources, reads back mutation-resource absence using only permissions already present on that write token, records the results, exits, and is followed by write-token revocation. Only after the journal proves that process exited, cleanup passed, and revocation was verified may a fresh `measure-cloudflare-evidence-sources.ts --run <runId>` process receive `VerifiedEvidenceReadCapability` to poll historical evidence and close the journal. `--cleanup-run <runId>` is a write-only invocation that performs no creation, probing, or measurement and deletes only journaled exact IDs after matching account/zone/name/inventory guards. Tests inspect `/proc`-equivalent injected environment snapshots and child-spawn arguments to prove each process receives exactly one token variable and that every inherited environment starts from a closed allowlist.
- Produces `CloudflareWorkerArtifactReadbackQualification`, which pins one non-interchangeable Cloudflare API family and endpoint/schema fingerprint. Phase one uses the production Scripts Versions family: `POST /accounts/{account_id}/workers/scripts/{script_name}/versions`, `GET /accounts/{account_id}/workers/scripts/{script_name}/versions/{version_id}`, and the matching Scripts Deployments endpoints. The version-detail `resources.script` ETag/modules plus version-scoped settings—not the script-scoped latest-download endpoint—must canonically reconstruct and distinguish two locally hashed Wrangler bundles. Failure to map either version's bytes and settings deterministically is `STOP` before Tasks 4-12.
- Owns the independent `@baci/cloudflare-evidence-qualification-worker` fixture and pins its workspace-local `wrangler` to exactly `4.115.0`. Its two minimal deterministic versions return distinct fixed `204` markers and contain no storefront import, R2 binding, origin fetch, secret, or dependency on the not-yet-created `apps/storefront-edge`. Gate B bundles only these fixture versions, records their source/config/tool hashes, and proves the production Scripts Versions/Deployments API can distinguish both artifacts. Task 9 later pins the same verified Wrangler version but does not retroactively satisfy Gate B.
- Produces `CloudflarePointerNonCacheQualification = { cacheRuleId; cacheRulesetVersion; traceExpressionSha256; acceptedCfCacheStatuses: readonly ['DYNAMIC'][]; requestCacheMode: 'no-store'; repeatedProbeCount: number; ageObserved: false; hitObserved: false; missObserved: false; qualifiedAt; expiresAt; canonicalSha256 }`. The accepted status set is nonempty and contains only the live-qualified `DYNAMIC` value. Authority requires Cloudflare Trace to prove the exact `/hosts/*` bypass rule matched, `cache: 'no-store'`, no `Age`, and repeated independent `GET`/`HEAD` probes with no `HIT`, `MISS`, `EXPIRED`, `REVALIDATED`, `STALE`, or `UPDATING`; a status header alone is never authority.
- Produces a two-stage `CloudflareReleaseCacheHostPurgeContractQualification`. Gate B binds the exact Cache Purge endpoint/schema, current rate limits, reviewed temporary purge-policy fingerprint, custom-domain read endpoint/schema, and independent provider-time behavior; it records `productionResourceState: 'present_verified' | 'absent_requires_bootstrap'` and may use only the cleaned run-scoped evidence bucket/domain when production is absent. Task 12 Step 4A then seals the production stage against the exact `ogabassey.com` zone, `baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases` bucket, reviewed production purge/read policies, DNS record fingerprints, both custom-domain attachments, and the active protected-deployment fingerprint. Its initial provisioning read-back seeds only the database-authoritative confirmed `preview_attached` ordered pair; after that, a deletion consumer derives `activeServingHosts` exclusively from its profile-locked lifecycle receipt, and the only transition to the singleton is the confirmed topology operation. For each member it seals an independent ownership proof and exact body `{ "hosts": [host] }`; no multi-host body, caller-selected hostname, missing host, extra host, or stale host set is valid. Only this production-stage receipt may satisfy Task 8. Separately defines `CloudflareReleaseCacheHostOwnershipProof = { claimToken; publicationEpoch; zoneId; dnsRecordId; dnsRecordType: string; normalizedHostname: 'releases.ogabassey.com' | 'releases-preview.ogabassey.com'; activeServingHosts: readonly string[]; dnsContentSha256; proxied: true; bucketName: 'baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases'; domainEnabled: true; ownershipStatus: 'active'; sslStatus: 'active'; deploymentFingerprint; readPolicySha256; dnsObservedAt; r2DomainObservedAt; expiresAt; canonicalSha256 }`. Each production proof uses independent qualified provider `Date` values and exactly `expiresAt = min(dnsObservedAt, r2DomainObservedAt) + 2 minutes`; a temporary qualification receipt, caller-selected hostname, missing/extra/ambiguous serving host, missing permission, stale proof, deployment mismatch, or inventory drift is `STOP`.

**Mandatory execution order:** The operational gates below specify the evidence that Task 1 must obtain, but they must not run from the draft worktree. First implement Steps 1-5, commit the tooling/policies/tests, open the Task 1 tooling PR, obtain green required checks plus fresh exact-head review, merge it, and record the merge SHA. Only then run Operational Gates A and B from that SHA. Commit their redacted baseline/qualification outputs in a separate evidence-only PR; that PR contains no executable or policy change. A `PROCEED` verdict is valid only when both the tooling SHA and evidence-only SHA are recorded in the owner approval.

- [ ] **Operational Gate A, after the Task 1 tooling PR merges: Prove the current bill is reducible before designing the release plane**

Inventory every hostname that currently serves or redirects the Ogabassey merchant. The minimum set is `ogabassey.com`, `www.ogabassey.com`, and `ogabassey.usebaci.com`; follow live DNS/HTTP redirects and trusted `domains`/slug-alias state to discover more, but never infer another merchant into scope. Record DNS proxy state, certificate state, redirect chain, Cloudflare zone, seven closed UTC days of requests by host/path class, Cloudflare cache status, Vercel invocations, Fluid CPU, provisioned memory, and attributable cost in `docs/ops/ogabassey-current-origin-baseline.md`. Redact request identity and URLs beyond bounded path classes.

`ogabassey-current-origin-baseline.ts` requires complete evidence for every discovered hostname and computes: current anonymous-document origin-miss rate, alias-origin rate, dynamic-origin rate, reducible Vercel cost, projected Worker/R2/Analytics Engine/Workers Logs/traces cost under each allowed evidence mode, any incremental Cloudflare zone-plan cost needed for the exact cookie-map Transform Rule, and conservative monthly payback derived from independently verified one-time implementation cost divided by the projected monthly savings. A caller-reported payback value is never used as the gate. Workers Logs projection uses `sampledInvocations * max(2, qualifiedLogEventsPerSampledInvocation)`: the lower bound is one platform invocation log plus the Worker's exactly one custom decision log, while Gate B failure/exception probes establish a conservative higher multiplier if Cloudflare produces additional events. It prices the account, not an isolated Worker, through a closed `CloudflareWorkersLogsPlanContract` containing plan (`free | paid`), allowance amount and period (`utc_day | billing_month`), overage availability/rate, provider daily hard-event ceiling, and two independent all-account usage observations: one bounded to the current allowance period for billing and one bounded to the current UTC day for forced-sampling safety. Each observation has its own closed boundaries, source fingerprint, maximum lag, and timestamp; a Paid monthly counter cannot satisfy the daily hard-limit gate. Free calculations use the current daily allowance and never invent overage; Paid calculations use the current monthly included amount plus approved overage. For every projected UTC day, add all other Workers' measured worst-case events to Ogabassey's projected sampled invocations times the qualified multiplier, apply owner-approved traffic/error headroom, then require an additional 4x capacity margin to remain strictly below Cloudflare's account-wide forced-sampling ceiling. Query current events from every Worker sharing the allowance and subtract only the proven unused remainder in the matching provider period. Tests cover independently stale/missing/mismatched counters, Free daily reset, Paid billing-month reset, zero remaining allowance, unavailable Free overage, another Worker consuming quota mid-window, a period-boundary crossing, exactly-at/above/below the forced-sampling ceiling, the two-event lower bound, qualified extra error events, and the currently observed shape where the apex is a Cloudflare `HIT` while `www` and the Baci subdomain redirect through Vercel. Return `STOP` before renderer/publisher work when any of these hold: incomplete host inventory, missing current Vercel attribution, existing origin avoidance already meets the target, dynamic requests are the dominant reducible cost, unpriced observability events/defaults, unknown shared-account quota consumption, plan/period/reset ambiguity, undocumented excess behavior, projected forced sampling, insufficient 4x log-cap headroom, projected savings are non-positive, required Transform capability is unsupported/unapproved, or derived payback exceeds the owner-approved horizon recorded in the document. The owner must explicitly approve the baseline, entitlement/observability cost, verified implementation cost, and horizon before Operational Gate B.

- [ ] **Operational Gate B, after Gate A approval: Qualify exact evidence and cohort-affinity sources before downstream implementation**

First run a read-only Cloudflare account/product spike before downstream renderer, publisher, serving Worker, DNS, or R2 work. This pre-build gate qualifies the provider contracts and export completeness—not events from production rules that do not exist yet—for: (a) an exact Worker invocation/decision census, (b) unsampled hostname/method/path-class HTTP and Redirect Rule counts, (c) unsampled WAF/rate-limit action counts grouped by rule ID, (d) version-scoped script/settings read-back needed to bind reviewed bundle bytes to the deployed provider artifact, and (e) the dedicated-host Cache Purge endpoint, permission, request schema, rate limits, and retry contract. Record the account, both zones, exact API family and endpoint/schema fingerprints, API/GraphQL queries, retention, export-completeness signals, `_sample_interval` behavior, invocation-total source, alias-request-total source, WAF/action source, permissions, exact Workers Logs/traces configuration and sampling, observed log events per successful and failed sampled invocation, other-Worker account-quota consumption, host-purge qualification fingerprint, expected monthly telemetry/logging cost, and redacted samples in `docs/ops/ogabassey-storefront-evidence-source.md`.

Qualify pointer non-caching on the exact dedicated custom-domain mechanics used by the present or run-scoped evidence bucket. Create/read back an exact `/hosts/*` cache-bypass rule, use Cloudflare Trace to seal its rule ID, ruleset version, complete expression hash, and match result, and send repeated independent `GET` and `HEAD` requests with the Worker-fetch equivalent of `cache: 'no-store'`. Record `acceptedCfCacheStatuses` only as the nonempty live-qualified `DYNAMIC` value with no `Age` and no `HIT | MISS | EXPIRED | REVALIDATED | STALE | UPDATING`; do not infer authority from the response header alone. A missing status, cache-path status, inconsistent GET/HEAD result, Trace drift, rule mismatch, or inability to reproduce the result is `STOP`. Task 12 must read back the same production rule fingerprint and repeat the probes; Gate-B evidence cannot bless a later configuration by status name alone.

If current account history cannot demonstrate a source without live events, the owner may approve temporary qualification resources on the isolated `edge-evidence.ogabassey.com` hostname: one no-origin probe Worker, one exact-host redirect from `/__redirect-probe/{runNonce}` to `/__redirect-result/{runNonce}`, one harmless exact-host WAF block matching `X-Baci-Evidence-Probe: 1` plus `X-Baci-Evidence-Run: {runNonce}`, one harmless exact-host rate-limit rule matching `X-Baci-Evidence-Rate-Probe: 1` plus the same run header, one run-scoped R2 bucket/custom domain when the production bucket is absent, and one exact-host request-header Transform Rule that sets a nonce-scoped probe header only when exactly one `baci-edge-cohort` cookie matches `^[a-f0-9]{32}$`. `runNonce` is a freshly generated 128-bit-or-stronger lowercase-hex value used only for that run and redacted from logs/artifacts. A temporary proxied DNS record exists only for the bounded probe window and is never linked or advertised; the hostname has no Vercel/custom-origin route, merchant pointer, R2 binding, cookies, or user traffic. The Worker returns fixed `204` responses only for the nonce-bound synthetic result/probe paths and fails closed otherwise. Probe missing, duplicate, malformed, uppercase, undersized, oversized, and exact valid cookies; only the exact valid singleton may arrive with the transformed probe header. During the same reviewed temporary-Worker window, generate two deterministic, deliberately distinct bundles with the pinned Task 1 qualification package commands `pnpm --filter @baci/cloudflare-evidence-qualification-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --config wrangler.version-a.jsonc --outdir .qualification-dist/version-a` and `pnpm --filter @baci/cloudflare-evidence-qualification-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --config wrangler.version-b.jsonc --outdir .qualification-dist/version-b`. Upload version A through the Scripts Versions endpoint, immediately fetch that exact version ID through Scripts Get Version Detail, and canonically hash its `resources.script` modules/ETag plus version-scoped settings; repeat for version B. The script-scoped raw-download and settings endpoints may be recorded only as latest-state corroboration and can never establish per-version provenance. List both version details, create through the exact raw Scripts Deployments endpoint a two-version deployment with A at canonical numeric `100` and B at canonical numeric `0`, read it back with both version IDs and exact weights, then send a protected `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Overrides` request through the isolated evidence hostname and require Version Metadata to prove B served while ordinary requests prove zero B invocations. The qualification artifact records `zeroWeightDeploymentSupported`, `zeroWeightOpenApiContradiction`, product-document and OpenAPI fingerprints, exact live request/response hashes, read-back tuple, visibility bound, override result, and owner acceptance. Authority is deliberately hierarchical: the current version-override product contract plus a successful exact live endpoint request/read-back authorize zero weight; the current Create Deployment OpenAPI minimum of `0.01` is sealed as a known schema contradiction and does not alone force `STOP`. The implementation must use a bounded raw serializer/fetch path and must not use a generated client that rejects zero. A product contract that no longer authorizes zero, live rejection/coercion/rounding, ambiguous read-back, nonzero B traffic, override fallback to A, or inability to attribute the served version is `STOP` before Tasks 4-12. This qualification is the sole authority for the production `stage_candidate` operation; documentation alone or OpenAPI alone is insufficient. Prove the recorded A/B resources cannot be swapped or satisfied by only the latest script. Do not mix the Scripts API with the separate Workers Beta `/workers/workers/.../versions` family: although the Beta family currently exposes per-version deletion, its identifiers, schemas, permissions, and lifecycle are not accepted by this plan. The temporary Scripts Deployment is the script's latest deployment even with zero public routes, and Cloudflare's Scripts Deployments delete endpoint cannot delete the latest actively serving deployment; the cleanup contract therefore never calls that endpoint for this resource and never treats an expected rejection as success. Instead, delete the entire temporary script through `DELETE /accounts/{account_id}/workers/scripts/{script_name}`; detach/read back the run-scoped R2 custom domain, prove its bucket empty, delete/read back the bucket; then require the script download/settings, deployment list/detail, version list, and both version-detail reads to return the qualified absent response (`404` or the exact empty-list shape established by Gate B). Tests reject an attempted delete-latest call, accepting a `400`/`409` as cleanup, or closing the journal before every endpoint's absence is proven. Any endpoint/schema/family drift other than the sealed zero-minimum contradiction, latest-only ambiguity, version detail that cannot bind the recorded artifact, mixed-family response, or incomplete cleanup is `STOP` before Tasks 4-12. Record the live zone plan, expression-validation result, rule phase, field entitlement, artifact-readback/API-family result, and incremental monthly plan cost. If the field/expression is unavailable, or requires an unapproved/uneconomic upgrade, return `STOP` before Tasks 4-12. Use Cloudflare's [version-override contract](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/version-overrides/), [Create Deployment contract](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/scripts/subresources/deployments/methods/create/), [Delete Deployment contract](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/scripts/subresources/deployments/methods/delete/), and [Delete Worker contract](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/scripts/methods/delete/) as the lifecycle references.

The same qualification sends a fixed count of successful, caught-error, and terminal requests with Workers Logs sampling at `1`, then waits through the proven export lag and counts platform invocation events separately from the exactly one structured decision event. `qualifiedLogEventsPerSampledInvocation` is the maximum complete observed event count across those classes and may never be below `2`; mismatch, truncation that hides event class, adaptive sampling, inability to attribute shared-account allowance consumption, or inability to retrieve the current plan/period/reset/daily-hard-limit contract is `STOP` for `worker-log` and excludes those numbers from the business case. The qualification records Cloudflare's current account-wide maximum logs per UTC day and forced-sampling behavior; the production capacity calculation must remain below it with 4x headroom rather than waiting for sampling to invalidate a seven-day window. Tests inject one versus two decision logs, another Worker exhausting the included quota, additional provider error events, Free/Paid period mismatch, hard-limit equality/overflow, and quota drift between baseline and approval.

Before downstream cache design, perform a read-only inventory for the exact production bucket `baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases` and its intended `releases.ogabassey.com` and `releases-preview.ogabassey.com` custom domains, then record the sealed state as exactly `present_verified | absent_requires_bootstrap`. When `present_verified`, prove `releases.ogabassey.com` is proxied, production-only, and dedicated to that bucket, and qualify the exact DNS-record read plus `GET /accounts/{account_id}/r2/buckets/{bucket_name}/domains/custom/{domain}` response schemas, status/clock semantics, permission/resource policy, canonical ownership-proof serializer, and two-minute freshness bound. When `absent_requires_bootstrap`, the owner-approved Gate-B mutation may instead create one run-scoped bucket `baci-ogabassey-storefront-evidence-{runId}` plus `r2-edge-evidence-{runId}.ogabassey.com`, qualify the same custom-domain endpoint/schema/independent HTTP `Date` semantics and isolated host-purge contract, then delete the custom domain and bucket and read every endpoint back absent before the journal can close. That temporary result qualifies provider mechanics only: Task 12 Step 4A must create and independently prove the real production bucket/domains before any R2 parent credential, publisher timer, preview, or apex route is enabled. Mere production-resource absence is not `STOP` when the temporary qualification passes and cleans up; missing permissions, endpoint/schema/clock drift, ambiguous cleanup, or inability to establish the later production ownership proof is `STOP`. The runtime production proof stores independent `dnsObservedAt` and `r2DomainObservedAt` values and derives `expiresAt = min(dnsObservedAt, r2DomainObservedAt) + 2 minutes`; neither response may borrow the other's timestamp. The candidate production read policy contains only exact-zone DNS Read plus the narrowest provider-supported R2 read scope; unavoidable account-wide R2-read or zone-wide purge authority is recorded and constrained by fixed wrappers and negative mutation tests. Tests reject `purge_everything`, `files`, `prefixes`, `tags`, a second host, a production host during temporary qualification, caller-selected selectors, incomplete ownership reads, either stale/future observation, delayed-second-read freshness extension, residual temporary resources, and treating temporary evidence as production dedication.

The owner creates a dedicated, expiring evidence-qualification write token from `.github/cloudflare/ogabassey-evidence-qualification-policy.json`; never reuse the Task 12 production bootstrap token. The reviewed policy names the exact Cloudflare account and `ogabassey.com` zone, permits only the current permission-group IDs required for the temporary Worker script/route, one DNS record, at most one run-scoped evidence R2 bucket/custom domain, Redirect/WAF/rate-limit/Transform rules, resource read-back, and Cache Purge, expires within two hours, and records the unavoidable residual scope where Cloudflare grants Worker-script, zone-ruleset, or cache-purge authority only at account/zone level. The qualification wrapper may use Cache Purge only for the fixed `edge-evidence.ogabassey.com` body. A second token from `.github/cloudflare/ogabassey-evidence-measure-policy.json` has only the exact account/zone analytics, HTTP, Redirect, WAF, and rate-limit-event permissions required after cleanup; it has no Worker/DNS/ruleset/cache-purge permission and expires after the qualified maximum evidence export lag plus a one-hour margin, capped at 24 hours. A longer required lag or inability to express that read-only policy makes this evidence mode `STOP` pending a separately reviewed duration.

Before mutation, `verify-cloudflare-evidence-token-policy.ts` requires the live write token to be active, its `/user/tokens/verify` ID to match the owner-exported and reviewed `tokenId`, the owner-exported permission/resource arrays and expiration to equal the reviewed artifact, and both artifact SHA-256 values to match the approval record. `verify-cloudflare-evidence-read-token-policy.ts` applies the same ID/export/hash checks to the read token and rejects any write permission. The mutation tool snapshots and hashes the complete Worker script/route, account R2 bucket/custom-domain, and zone DNS/ruleset inventories before mutation, refuses any name/ID collision or inventory drift, hard-codes the temporary names and zone/account IDs, and proves after cleanup that every unrelated inventory entry and hash is unchanged. Because token self-introspection proves only ID/status and cannot prove omitted permissions beyond the reviewed owner export, the plan records that residual provider limitation explicitly rather than claiming negative authority.

Implement and test the three bounded qualification commands during the reviewed tooling slice. The credentialless prepare invocation requires a writable validated `EVIDENCE_RUN_STATE_DIR`, the exact recorded tooling merge SHA, and the matching recorded owner-approval identifier, then persists the initial journal before printing the write-phase handoff. A new clean-environment mutation process receives only the write capability and `--apply`, atomically appends each returned resource ID before the next mutation, emits the known bounded probes, records expected counts/completion time, immediately deletes in dependency-safe reverse order, and reads back absence of the Worker route/script, redirect/WAF/rate-limit/Transform rules, DNS record, run-scoped R2 custom domain, and run-scoped R2 bucket—never wait for export while a write resource remains. After it exits, the owner revokes that token from a separate operator surface, verifies `/user/tokens/verify` fails, and advances the journal to `mutation_cleanup_verified` with `writeProcessExitedAt` and `writeTokenRevokedAt`. Only then may a new clean-environment measurement process receive the read-only capability and poll historical events by saved deleted IDs. It cannot import the mutation client or accept the write-token variable. Operational Gate B can calculate a proof only when historical events remain queryable after deletion and raw emitted counts equal complete unsampled exported counts; otherwise that source is ineligible. After a proof result or terminal `STOP`, the read process exits, the owner revokes its token, verifies failure, records `readProcessExitedAt`/`readTokenRevokedAt`, and only then transitions to `proof_complete` or `closed_stop`. CI/static boundary tests fail if either executable imports the other's client/capability module, if an environment contains both token variable names, or if journal ordering is bypassed.

On startup, any journal not in `proof_complete` or `closed_stop` blocks a new run. `--cleanup-run <runId>` loads its exact IDs, verifies the current account/zone and resource name/description contain the same run ID, obtains a newly reviewed replacement write capability when the original expired, deletes remaining resources in reverse order, proves absence plus unrelated-inventory equality, revokes that token, and records its revocation. It also requires revocation/read-back for the journaled read token whenever one was issued; if its secret is unavailable after a crash, the owner revokes it by token ID and supplies the provider revocation receipt/audit event named in the runbook before the journal can close. It never recreates a missing resource, emits probes, or accepts raw CLI resource IDs. The runbook includes the same exact-name/run-ID inventory queries for operator recovery if the original host disk is unavailable; any matching residual resource or unrevoked token keeps Operational Gate B at `STOP`. Tests inject fake filesystem, process interruption after every successful mutation/version upload/read-back/deployment and before each token revocation, expired write/read tokens, replacement cleanup token, fake Cloudflare/read-token clients, and cover wrong token ID, expired/inactive token, write permission on the read token, extra or missing scope, policy-hash mismatch, default dry run, invalid/reused nonce, unrelated traffic exclusion, pre/post inventory drift, every partial-create failure, SIGTERM-resume cleanup, probe mismatch, missing/duplicate/malformed/uppercase/undersized/oversized/valid affinity cookies, unavailable cookie-map field, paid-plan entitlement without cost approval, deterministic two-bundle success, ambiguous latest-only content, swapped version IDs, changed settings, ETag/hash mismatch, unpriced/default observability, leftover version/deployment content, deleted-rule history unavailable, export timeout, cleanup retry, missing revocation receipt, and residual-resource `STOP`. A request escaping the isolated response path, any unrelated resource mutation, incomplete deletion, any unrevoked token, or missing journal is `STOP`. These resources qualify source completeness, cohort affinity, observability cost, and Worker artifact read-back only and never count as storefront or production traffic evidence.

Select exactly one production Worker evidence mode during Operational Gate B:

1. Complete Workers Logs/Logpush (or an equivalent Cloudflare account export) whose provider contract and live probe prove one record per Worker invocation with no adaptive sampling; or
2. Analytics Engine only when every production day reports `MAX(_sample_interval) = 1` **and** a separately verified exact Cloudflare invocation counter matches the bounded decision count exactly.

The selected evidence mode also fixes the checked-in Workers observability contract, guard-proof contract, and cost model before downstream implementation. For `worker-log`, production uses persisted Workers Logs at `head_sampling_rate: 1` because anything lower cannot be an exact census; the owner must approve its current plan, allowance period/reset, quota/overage, hard daily event limit, forced-sampling behavior, and privacy/retention contract. Its minimum event multiplier is exactly two—one platform invocation event and exactly one structured decision event emitted by application code—and the priced multiplier is `max(2, qualifiedLogEventsPerSampledInvocation)`. `worker-log` is ineligible unless every projected UTC day plus every other Worker's consumption and 4x headroom remains strictly below the account hard limit and inside an approved allowance/overage contract for the correct period. Tests and static checks reject a second `console.log`/`console.error`/uncaught error path; caught errors place their bounded code in the single decision event. In this mode only, a hostile-override proof may require exact per-request absence from the complete invocation export after the sealed export lag. For `worker-analytics`, Workers Logs remain diagnostic only at exactly `head_sampling_rate: 0.01`, traces are disabled, and Analytics Engine plus the independent invocation counter remain the storefront decision proof sources; cost projection still applies the qualified event multiplier to sampled invocations and the same hard-limit check. Sampled diagnostic-log absence is never evidence of zero invocations. In this mode, a hostile-override proof is eligible only when Gate B has qualified a complete unsampled terminal WAF-action dataset whose exact rule ID, hostname, method, bounded path, action, timestamps, emitted-count equality, retention, export-lag, and completeness signal prove that every bounded hostile request terminated at the unconditional block before Worker dispatch; it makes no per-request Worker-log absence claim. A sampled/adaptive WAF source, Security Events view without a completeness contract, Ray ID uniqueness assumption, aggregate-only invocation delta, or unexplained count is `STOP`. Preview uses logs at `1` only during the bounded acceptance window, then is disabled or returned to `0.01`. Any other/missing value, provider default, unpriced trace/log event, unknown other-Worker quota use, plan/period mismatch, insufficient headroom, projected forced sampling, or runtime dashboard drift is `STOP`. Task 12 reads back the deployed observability settings, selected guard-proof mode, qualified source fingerprint, and plan contract; re-reads account-wide current-period and current-day consumption before each promotion; and includes them in the deployment fingerprint and seven-day cost comparison.

`workersInvocationsAdaptive`, sampled GraphQL aggregates, dashboard charts, and weighted `SUM(_sample_interval)` alone are not an exact independent denominator. Alias/WAF qualification independently requires unsampled counts grouped by exact hostname, method class (`GET`/`HEAD` versus other), bounded path class, and resource/rule ID. If any source contract cannot be proven exact, stop this edge implementation with `NOT_PROVEN` and present the required telemetry product/cost for owner approval. Passing Operational Gate B authorizes downstream implementation and isolated preview only. It does not authorize apex routing: Task 12 must separately apply the final generated alias/WAF/rate-limit rules, bind their read-back IDs and hashes into the evidence manifest, emit exact per-host live probes, wait through the qualified export lag, and prove those production rule-ID counts before the apex Worker route is attached.

Gate B also qualifies the three production-topology endpoint families without touching production: Worker Custom Domain detach/read, R2 CORS write/read, and R2 custom-domain detach/read. The isolated evidence resources exercise successful mutation, deliberately lost client response, repeated read-only observation until convergence, and a control no-effect request. The receipt records per-step synchronous/asynchronous semantics, operation/request ID if exposed, maximum provider visibility lag, exact before/after/intermediate response schemas, and a conservative `maximumTopologyVisibilityAndRecoverySeconds`. A no-effect conclusion is valid only when the exact before-tuple remains unchanged through that bound and no provider operation remains pending; otherwise the step is still reconciling. If any endpoint can apply after the bound, exposes no complete read-back, or cannot distinguish exact before/after/intermediate tuples, protected preview teardown is `STOP` and no 24-hour deletion capacity claim may include it.

Define and test the exact all-ingress equations: `canonicalEligibleRequests = sum(day.canonicalEligibleRequestCount)`, `aliasEligibleRequests = sum(day.aliasEligibleRequestCount)`, `allEligibleIngress = canonicalEligibleRequests + aliasEligibleRequests`, `canonicalEligibleOriginAttempts = sum(day.canonicalEligibleOriginAttemptCount)`, `aliasEligibleOriginAttempts = sum(day.aliasEligibleOriginRequestCount)`, `allEligibleOriginAttempts = canonicalEligibleOriginAttempts + aliasEligibleOriginAttempts`, and `originRate = allEligibleOriginAttempts / allEligibleIngress` (`NOT_PROVEN` when the denominator is zero). `canonicalHostname` must be exactly `ogabassey.com`; `aliasHostnames` must be sorted, unique, nonempty, exclude the canonical hostname, and exactly equal the noncanonical host set in the canonical hostname-inventory artifact whose SHA-256 appears in the manifest and every day. The exporter validates and hashes unsampled per-host raw aggregates before reducing them to daily fields. Overlap, omission, an unknown host, or a daily inventory-hash mismatch is `NOT_PROVEN`, so an alias cannot be double-counted or used to conceal an apex origin attempt.

Any daily hostname-inventory, eligibility-policy, alias/WAF ruleset, deployment, or version-ID mismatch against the manifest returns `NOT_PROVEN` and requires a fresh seven-day window. Add the regression case that an origin fetch which ends as `edge-error` still increments `canonicalEligibleOriginAttemptCount`; final response classification must never erase an attempted Vercel request. A small but complete unsampled census may pass on its observed rate—do not manufacture an unsupported confidence interval.
