**Review slices:** This logical task lands as three sequential PRs and is not production-deployed until 2C is merged and green.

- **2A — authorization and transport foundation:** `apps/web/src/ai/ai-spend-*`, provider gateway/transports, limits, the append-only anonymous-allowance migration/RPC/owner tool, typed and cryptographically separate `AI_SPEND_IP_HASH_SECRET` plus `AI_SPEND_ALLOWANCE_HMAC_SECRET` environment contracts, provider descriptor/chain modules, and their tests. It adds only lazy guarded primitives; no caller is considered migrated or activatable until the database and Vercel secret receipts match.
- **2B — foreground and signup callers:** AI flows; hero services; web/mobile onboarding; `/api/generate`, FAQ/edit/grade/builder/admin/CopilotKit/merchant generation routes; dashboard product/SEO actions; initial-template generation; the extracted under-300-line helpers; and their tests. A scoped boundary fixture lists every migrated foreground caller.
- **2C — raw, chat, analytics, quiz, repair, CAC, and background callers:** raw-client domain wrappers, chat/Santa, analytics routes, quiz/CAC/repair routes and libraries, both AI job workers, AI-storefront processing, removal/inert-compatibility of the legacy Gemini builder endpoint, and the repository-wide boundary test. This slice deletes the temporary scoped fixture and proves there is no remaining provider-capable production path outside the global allowlist or Gemini-capable production path anywhere.

Each slice must pass its focused tests plus `pnpm turbo lint` and `pnpm turbo typecheck`; 2C additionally runs the full monorepo test suite and CodeRabbit. Do not deploy any of the slices to production before 2C is merged, because only 2C establishes the global fail-closed invariant.

**Interfaces:**

- Produces opaque `AiSpendAuthorization` values only through `authorizeMerchantAiSpend`, `authorizeStorefrontMerchantAiSpend`, `authorizeBackgroundMerchantAiSpend`, and `authorizePlatformAdminAiSpend` after server-side identity, tenant/job-claim, and entitlement checks.
- The `provider-spend-gateway.ts` facade produces `generateAuthorizedText`, `generateAuthorizedObject`, `generateAuthorizedVision`, `generateAuthorizedImage`, `createAuthorizedTextStream`, `createAuthorizedAgenticResponse`, and `createAuthorizedCopilotAdapter`. Provider SDK generation primitives, provider adapter construction, and raw provider HTTP endpoints exist only behind this facade or in its exact audited transport allowlist; every transport requires and validates the same opaque authorization before constructing a model, URL, or authorization header.
- Requires every merchant provider call to pass an authorization whose merchant has a recognized, unexpired non-free plan plus any stricter existing feature check. `free`, missing, malformed, and expired plans fail with `PAID_AI_REQUIRED` before provider/model construction. This rule does not add an entitlement to any paid tier.
- Requires a fail-closed database-backed merchant/capability allowance before issuing authorization; the current process-local AI limiter and the generic database helper's fail-open behavior are not accepted as spend controls.
- Background jobs re-check current authorization immediately before provider construction; enqueue-time authorization is not sufficient and no reusable authorization token is persisted.
- `apps/web/src/env.ts`, deployment-environment validation, and provider descriptors require only the reviewed non-Gemini provider keys. They declare no Gemini key, model, SDK, endpoint, image-provider key, or fallback; CI inventories the production environment names/configuration and rejects a legacy Gemini variable instead of treating it as optional. Existing encrypted value material is removed only through the separately authorized provider-secret rotation process, but until then is intentionally inert because no production code may read it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write provider-boundary and entitlement regression tests**

Test anonymous requests, free/missing/malformed/expired plans, active paid plans that pass their existing feature gates, platform-admin-only jobs, background jobs downgraded after enqueue, and forged plain objects passed as authorization. Every denied case must assert that provider factory construction, provider URL/header construction, outbound `fetch`, and SDK generation functions were not called. Add an AST/import boundary test over production TypeScript that fails when `generateText`, `generateObject`, `generateImage`, `activeImageModel`, `new OpenAI`, `OpenAIAdapter`, `/v1/chat/completions`, a provider-base concatenation ending in `/api/chat`, or a known external provider endpoint appears outside `provider-spend-gateway.ts`, inert provider declarations, and the exact guarded-transport allowlist. The same test is a production no-Gemini gate: it fails on every `@google/generative-ai` or Google AI SDK import, `GoogleGenerativeAI`/Gemini adapter constructor, `generativelanguage.googleapis.com` or Gemini endpoint, `gemini-` model ID, and `GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY`/Gemini provider environment lookup in a production-reachable source, configuration, deployment manifest, or generated runtime artifact. A configured legacy Gemini key is neither read nor forwarded and fails CI until removed from production deployment configuration; the only permitted compatibility response from the legacy `/api/builder/gemini` path is a bounded non-provider `410 GEMINI_PROVIDER_REMOVED` response. Same-origin client calls such as `fetch('/api/chat')` are explicitly not provider endpoints. The allowlist is code-owned and contains no route, action, job, flow, general-purpose utility, Gemini SDK, Gemini endpoint, or Gemini model.

The SQL/route matrix for the anonymous exception additionally covers direct `anon` invocation without a valid HMAC, malformed/expired/future/replayed proofs, missing current/previous secret, cross-host/merchant substitution, forged capability/limit/window/time, raw-IP leakage, concurrent requests at both exact limits, UTC-day and UTC-hour boundaries from database time, IP-secret rotation versus allowance-secret rotation, current/previous overlap and removal, upload bytes at/above the ceiling, and database lock/schema/RPC failure. Every denied case proves the route reads zero upload bytes and constructs no provider/model/header/URL. The owner tool tests TTY/no-echo, prepared binds, project/role/TLS verification, crash recovery through `--status`, non-secret receipts, and rejection of argv/environment/pipe/file secret input. The migration is append-only, updates history-replay manifests, and its catalog/grant test proves generic `check_rate_limit` remains unavailable to `anon`, only the narrow proof RPC is granted, and `service_role` receives no new application edge.

`AiSpendAuthorization` is module-opaque and carries only server-side fields needed for auditing:

```ts
export type AiSpendCapability =
  | 'text_assist'
  | 'product_description'
  | 'price_list_import'
  | 'faq_generation'
  | 'blog_edit'
  | 'seo_generation'
  | 'builder_generation'
  | 'storefront_layout'
  | 'analytics_insights'
  | 'customer_chat'
  | 'quiz_generation'
  | 'cac_verification'
  | 'repair_import'
  | 'vision_analysis'
  | 'device_grading'
  | 'image_generation'
  | 'platform_admin_generation';

export async function authorizeMerchantAiSpend(input: {
  supabase: SupabaseClient<Database>;
  userId: string;
  merchantId: string;
  capability: Exclude<AiSpendCapability, 'platform_admin_generation'>;
}): Promise<AiSpendAuthorization>;
```

Start with conservative code-owned ceilings; changing one is a reviewed cost-policy change, not a route-local override:

```ts
export const AI_SPEND_LIMITS = Object.freeze({
  text_assist: { operations: 20, windowMinutes: 60, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  product_description: { operations: 50, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  price_list_import: { operations: 5, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  faq_generation: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  blog_edit: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  seo_generation: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  builder_generation: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  storefront_layout: { operations: 2, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  analytics_insights: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  customer_chat: { operations: 200, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 512 },
  quiz_generation: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  cac_verification: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  repair_import: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
  vision_analysis: { operations: 10, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024 },
  device_grading: { operations: 5, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxOutputTokens: 1_024, maxInputBytes: 25_000_000 },
  image_generation: { operations: 3, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxImages: 3 },
  platform_admin_generation: { operations: 5, windowMinutes: 1_440, maxImages: 5, maxOutputTokens: 2_048 },
} satisfies Record<AiSpendCapability, AiSpendLimit>);

`image_generation` and `platform_admin_generation` remain named capabilities solely for stable authorization/error typing, but their gateway availability is hard-disabled in this phase: no authorization factory may issue either to a provider transport, their positive historical ceilings never authorize a call, and tests assert zero provider construction even for paid/admin callers.
```

The authenticated factory must validate user-to-merchant access, a recognized and unexpired paid plan, and the call site's existing feature/permission requirement. Every entitlement expiry, allowance window, proof age, and receipt timestamp comes from PostgreSQL `transaction_timestamp()`; no exported production factory accepts `now`, a clock, or request-derived time. Deterministic tests inject a fake clock only into a non-exported constructor in the test module.

`authorizeStorefrontMerchantAiSpend` is only for existing anonymous customer features paid for by a merchant: it independently resolves the merchant from the normalized request hostname/custom domain, never trusts a body/query/header-selected merchant ID, and then consumes the dedicated anonymous allowance RPC below. `authorizeBackgroundMerchantAiSpend` accepts only an already-claimed job ID/type/merchant/fence tuple, re-reads that exact live claim, verifies its tenant and capability, and re-checks current entitlement; a caller cannot mint background authorization from an arbitrary merchant ID. Authenticated/background factories may call the existing `check_rate_limit` RPC through the user-scoped client only where its current grants permit it. They fail closed on false, RPC error, malformed response, or exception.

The append-only Task 2 migration creates private current/previous anonymous-AI HMAC secret state, a nonce ledger, and `consume_anonymous_storefront_ai_allowance(p_proof jsonb)`. The RPC is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, executable by `anon` only through its proof, and accepts no generic identifier, merchant ID, user ID, endpoint, limit, window, timestamp override, or caller-selected bucket. Its strict maximum-4-KiB proof uses a fixed action/subject, five-minute database-time window, unique nonce, normalized request hostname, closed capability `device_grading`, HMAC-SHA256 IP digest, declared upload byte count, and a request receipt hash. After constant-time HMAC validation it independently resolves the verified storefront domain/merchant, requires the current recognized non-free plan, derives the code-owned merchant/day and merchant-IP/hour limits, and atomically consumes both buckets under one transaction. A false/expired/replayed/forged/malformed proof, unresolved/free/expired merchant, cross-host substitution, exhausted bucket, missing secret, or database error returns one bounded denial and writes no attacker-amplified per-attempt row. Raw IP, HMAC, hostname, customer identifier, upload bytes, and provider details never enter logs or responses. Direct calls to generic `check_rate_limit` remain revoked from `anon`; the new RPC does not inherit its caller-controlled argument surface.

The route-side proof uses a separate server-only `AI_SPEND_ALLOWANCE_HMAC_SECRET`; `AI_SPEND_IP_HASH_SECRET` is used only to pseudonymize the client address, and neither secret substitutes for the other. The private database copy is initialized/rotated only by `postgres` through fixed `SECURITY INVOKER` functions and the no-echo direct-database owner tool `manage-ai-spend-allowance-secret.mjs`. That tool accepts the TLS-verified production database URL and secret only through separate bounded interactive TTY prompts, sends secret values only as prepared bind parameters after verifying `session_user = current_user = 'postgres'` and the sealed project identity, prints only non-secret database-time receipts, and supports exact `--initialize | --rotate | --remove-previous | --status` modes. It never accepts a secret in argv, environment, pipe, or regular file. The matching Vercel production secret is installed through the existing protected secret workflow; activation remains blocked until a credential-separated proof-only request matches the database current slot, previous-slot overlap/expiry is verified during rotation, and both secret surfaces are read back by hash/receipt without revealing the value. No service-role client or new service-role exception is introduced.

`AI_SPEND_LIMITS` defines bounded operations per window and maximum tokens/images/bytes per capability; callers cannot raise them. A provider-chain fallback is one authorized operation and stays inside the gateway. Issued values are frozen, branded with a module-private symbol, and registered in a module-private `WeakMap`; the facade/guarded transport atomically changes the first provider operation from `issued` to `consumed`, so a caller cannot reuse one allowance for a second operation, while internal provider fallbacks share that already-consumed operation context. Assertions check registry membership, lifecycle state, capability, and merchant scope rather than trusting TypeScript shape alone. The registry/assertion is internal to the authorization facade and guarded transports, and the boundary test forbids other imports. Authorization data, plan details, and provider errors never enter client responses or persisted jobs.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the focused tests and prove current provider access is still open**

```bash
pnpm --filter @baci/web exec vitest run src/ai/ai-spend-authorization.test.ts src/ai/provider-spend-gateway.test.ts src/ai/provider-spend-boundary.test.ts src/ai/provider-spend-transports src/services/hero-image-generator.test.ts src/lib/onboarding/assign-zero-spend-hero-images.test.ts src/app/api/generate/route.test.ts src/app/api/ai/generate-faq src/app/api/ai/edit-blog src/app/api/ai/grade-device src/app/api/builder/gemini src/app/api/copilotkit src/app/api/ai-jobs src/app/api/admin/generate-hero-images src/app/api/merchant/regenerate-hero-images src/app/api/merchant/quiz/generate src/app/api/merchant/verify-cac src/app/api/repairs/catalog/import/parse
```

Expected: FAIL because the authorization/gateway modules do not exist and current routes can reach provider code without a paid-plan authorization.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement review slices 2A, 2B, and 2C in order**

`provider-spend-gateway.ts` accepts the opaque authorization as its first argument and constructs/selects a provider only after validating it. Replace module-scope `activeTextModel`, `fallbackTextModel`, `activeImageModel`, and other instantiated exports with lazy model factories called only inside the gateway. `text-provider-chain.ts` returns inert provider descriptors until the gateway materializes them after authorization. The sole authorized paid text/vision chain is the reviewed Cerebras → Groq → OpenRouter order; it contains no Gemini SDK, endpoint, model, key, or fallback. Image generation is disabled at the gateway and every caller returns the bounded `IMAGE_GENERATION_UNAVAILABLE` result unless a separately owner-approved non-Gemini paid-provider plan changes this contract; no image-provider environment variable is operational in this phase. Refactor `generateTextWithChain`, `generateObjectWithChain`, builder provider execution, image generation, vision analysis, quiz generation, and the CopilotKit adapter to require the authorization explicitly. Remove direct SDK generation calls and instantiated model imports from route, flow, service, and job modules. The boundary test owns an exact allowlist, so a new provider call cannot bypass authorization silently.

Extract the existing raw network primitives for Ollama/OpenAI-compatible chat into the four files under `provider-spend-transports/`. Their first parameter is `AiSpendAuthorization`; `assertAuthorizedTransport` runs synchronously before base URL/model/credential access, and their unit tests prove a forged or missing authorization causes zero `fetch` calls. Existing analytics, Gemma repair/quiz, CAC, chat, and AI-storefront modules retain only prompt/domain logic and delegate through the facade; none may retain a provider URL or `/api/chat`/`/v1/chat/completions` fetch. Routes and background scripts obtain authorization before calling them. `process-ai-storefront-jobs.ts` re-checks the job merchant's current `storefront_layout` authorization after claim and immediately before transport use; a downgrade completes the claim with a bounded non-retryable entitlement code and makes no provider/VPS request.

The price-list AI worker must select explicit job columns including `merchant_id`, replace its current unfenced `.select()`/status-only claim with the table's `locked_by` plus lease fence, and pass that exact claim tuple to `authorizeBackgroundMerchantAiSpend`. Both job workers re-check immediately before each provider attempt, including fallbacks; they never serialize `AiSpendAuthorization`. Tests cover cross-merchant job tampering, stale/lost leases, a downgrade after claim, and entitlement removal between provider fallbacks.

Split `hero-image-generator.ts` into a zero-spend pool assignment path and an explicitly unavailable generation path. `assignPooledHeroImagesToMerchant` may select only existing active rows and returns placeholders when the pool is empty; it has no `autoGenerate` boolean and cannot call an image provider. `generateAuthorizedHeroImageBatch` and `regenerateAuthorizedHeroImagesForMerchant` return `IMAGE_GENERATION_UNAVAILABLE` without provider construction until a separately owner-approved non-Gemini paid-provider plan is implemented. The oversized web and mobile onboarding handlers call the small tested `assignZeroSpendHeroImages` helper, keeping their edit to orchestration while moving touched hero-assignment logic below 300 lines. Remove the merchant route's existing three-free-regenerations branch: regeneration returns the same unavailable result rather than spending. Platform-admin batch generation is also unavailable in this phase. CopilotKit uses `createAuthorizedCopilotAdapter` after merchant `builder_generation` authorization and has no direct provider fallback.

Make `generateInitialTemplate` deterministic unless an explicit valid authorization is supplied. Its existing profile copy and placeholders remain the zero-spend result; only the authorized enhancement branch may call `generateAuthorizedObject`. Web signup, mobile signup, and default builder config pass no authorization, so account creation cannot spend on AI implicitly.

Apply the repository's over-300-line rule while touching existing large modules: move price-list AI orchestration to `process-price-list-with-ai.ts`, SEO generation to `generate-authorized-seo-suggestions.ts`, chat provider selection to `resolve-authorized-chat-response.ts`, Santa completion to `generate-authorized-santa-response.ts`, initial-template provider work to `initial-template-ai-content.ts`, and hero pool/generation logic to the two dedicated service files. The original routes/actions keep auth, validation, and orchestration only; each extracted unit has its own focused test and no new file may exceed 300 lines.

`POST /api/generate` must add authentication as its first protected operation, validate its body with a dedicated Zod schema, resolve the authenticated merchant, and authorize `text_assist` before rate limiting or provider construction. FAQ, blog edit, builder, CopilotKit, AI job creation/worker execution, product-description, chat, analytics, quiz, CAC, dashboard-product, hero-image, and admin-generation paths follow their existing permissions plus the new spend authorization. Platform-admin authorization requires the existing platform-admin check and must never be constructible from a merchant role.

`POST /api/ai/grade-device` is the only anonymous-customer exception: before reading or base64-encoding the upload, validate only request framing/content type and declared bounded byte count, independently resolve the paying merchant from the trusted normalized storefront host/custom-domain context, compute the IP pseudonym, and call `authorizeStorefrontMerchantAiSpend('device_grading')`. That factory creates the narrow HMAC proof and requires the database RPC's exact `{ allowed: true, retryAt, databaseNow, merchantReceiptSha256, allowanceReceiptSha256 }` before the first upload byte is read; false/malformed/missing evidence fails closed. Only afterward may the route read and validate the allowlisted video bytes and call `generateAuthorizedVision`, which materializes only the reviewed non-Gemini chain. Absent compatible provider capability it returns a bounded unavailable result without constructing a model. Scope product-price lookup to the database-resolved merchant. Root-domain, unresolved-host, free, malformed-plan, expired-plan, over-budget, oversized, wrong-MIME, Gemini-configured, unsupported-provider, or allowance-secret-drift requests fail before upload bytes or provider construction. Tests cover the Ogabassey custom-domain path and prove a body/header-forged merchant, caller clock, direct generic-rate-limit RPC, or forged allowance result cannot authorize spend.

After authorization, `grade-device` uses one bounded streaming multipart reader suitable for chunked bodies; it never calls an unbounded form-data helper. A present `Content-Length` is canonical-decimal validated and rejected before reading when it exceeds the raw-request ceiling. The reader independently enforces running raw-request, multipart-overhead, part-count, field-count, field-name/value, header-line/header-total, and video-file byte ceilings; requires exactly one allowlisted video part; rejects duplicate video/field parts, filename/MIME/signature mismatch, nested multipart, transfer encodings, and trailing bytes; and binds the observed raw/file byte counts into the allowance receipt before provider construction or base64 encoding. The file is streamed to an owner-only temporary spool and unlinked on success, denial, parser error, provider error, abort, or process cancellation. Tests cover absent/dishonest `Content-Length`, chunk boundaries inside delimiters/headers, over-limit raw bytes with an under-limit file, excessive overhead/parts/fields, duplicates, wrong signatures, truncated terminators, cleanup on every exit, and zero provider/model construction before the complete bounded receipt exists.

- [ ] **Step 4: Validate and commit each review slice**

```bash
# 2A, after its focused authorization/gateway/transport tests
pnpm turbo lint
pnpm turbo typecheck
pnpm turbo test
coderabbit review --agent -t uncommitted
git commit -m "refactor(ai): add guarded provider spend gateway"

# 2B, after its focused foreground and signup tests
pnpm turbo lint
pnpm turbo typecheck
pnpm turbo test
coderabbit review --agent -t uncommitted
git commit -m "fix(ai): gate foreground provider spend"

# 2C final global gate
```

**Interfaces:**

- Produces separately Zod-validated release-input, renderer manifest-draft, final release-manifest, and hostname-pointer contracts shared by the ledger, publisher, renderer, edge Worker, and tests. The draft has every final field except provider-assigned `originEtag`; only the publisher may convert a draft into the final manifest after authenticated upload/read-back.
- Produces `normalizeReleaseDocumentPath(pathname): string | null` that rejects traversal, encoded separators, control characters, queries, and fragments.
- Produces `validatePublicReleaseUrl(value): URL | null`, accepting only public HTTPS URLs without credentials, signatures, localhost, link-local, or private-network hosts.
- Produces `compileStaticNetworkPolicy(integrations): StaticNetworkPolicy` from a closed integration enum and code-owned origin map; release input can select reviewed integrations and public IDs but cannot inject a URL or CSP token.
- Produces one shared machine-readable route vocabulary. `apps/web` re-exports/imports it so the existing proxy and the edge classifier cannot drift.
- Produces the sole runtime definition `storefrontReleaseResponsePolicySchema = z.enum(['document-no-store','ads-txt-public-host-vary','indexnow-public-immutable','release-object-immutable'])`, derives `StorefrontReleaseResponsePolicy = z.infer<typeof storefrontReleaseResponsePolicySchema>`, and exports `buildStorefrontReleaseResponseHeaders(policy): Readonly<Record<string, string>>` plus `validateStorefrontReleaseResponsePolicyForRoute(path, contentType, policy): boolean`. The manifest imports the schema and compatibility function rather than repeating literals. The builder emits exact code-owned `Cache-Control`/`Vary` values and rejects unknown policies; no release input, R2 response, or merchant value can inject a header.
- Produces the single primary export `STOREFRONT_STATIC_ROOT_ASSETS` from `public-root-assets.ts`, shaped as `{ adsTxt: { path, body, contentType, responsePolicy: 'ads-txt-public-host-vary' }, indexNow: { path, key, body, contentType, responsePolicy: 'indexnow-public-immutable' } } as const`. The existing web handlers consume this object and the shared header builder; `apps/web/src/lib/indexnow.ts` preserves its existing `DEFAULT_INDEXNOW_KEY` export by deriving it from `STOREFRONT_STATIC_ROOT_ASSETS.indexNow.key`. The renderer consumes the same object, so there is no duplicated seller line, key, path, content type, or response-header literal.
- Produces branded `CspScriptHash` from `cspScriptHashSchema = z.string().regex(/^sha256-[A-Za-z0-9+/]{43}=$/).brand('CspScriptHash')`; the manifest stores this bare canonical form and never stores quotes or a complete CSP token.
- Produces branded `StorefrontGenerationDecimal` from one strict canonical positive-decimal schema matching `^[1-9][0-9]{0,18}$` plus a guarded upper bound of PostgreSQL signed `bigint` maximum `9223372036854775807`. Every JavaScript/JSON/R2/proof boundary names it `generationDecimal`; SQL alone stores/compares `bigint`. Shared comparators use length-then-lexicographic decimal comparison and never `Number()`, `parseInt()`, or JavaScript `bigint` in JSON.
- Produces schema-versioned `PublishedBuilderProjection`, a bounded, sanitized, `.strict()` ordered-block discriminated union generated from the reviewed Ogabassey component allowlist and its per-component public prop schemas. Its commerce members are explicit: `product_collection` carries one closed collection/category/product-ID selector plus bounded sort/limit/layout options; `featured_product` carries one product ID plus presentation options; and `category_collection | category_grid` carry bounded category IDs plus presentation options. Commerce block props contain identifiers and presentation only—never copied price, inventory, availability, discount, image/product copy, merchant ID, arbitrary query/filter, API URL, JSX, HTML, nested Puck zones, or arbitrary JSON. Validation resolves every reference against the same Ogabassey merchant and current published catalog; a missing, unpublished, cross-merchant, or semantically invalid reference fails that publication. The renderer resolves identifiers only from the normalized release input and performs no browse-time database/API fetch. Non-commerce members contain only bounded public copy/media references and reviewed theme/display choices. The union rejects `CodeEmbed`, unknown Puck component types/props/zones, executable markup, draft SEO/settings, private setup data, and request-time components. This is the sole builder representation allowed in `StorefrontReleaseInput`; raw `page_configs.published_config` never enters R2.
- Produces one versioned `published-builder-projection-vectors.json` authority containing canonical valid projections plus closed invalid cases for every block discriminant, required/optional prop, unknown key, nested object/array, count/UTF-8 byte boundary, duplicate/order rule, unsupported component, and commerce identifier shape. Every vector has a stable case ID, expected validity, and bounded expected error code rather than runtime-specific error text. Task 4 TypeScript tests and Task 6 PostgreSQL tests consume these exact bytes; either runtime accepting or rejecting a different case is schema drift and blocks publication.
- Produces one declarative `STOREFRONT_EDGE_ROUTE_RULES: readonly StorefrontEdgeRouteRule[]`, where a rule is `{ id: string; disposition: 'origin' | 'edge-observation'; kind: 'exact' | 'segment-prefix' | 'suffix'; value: string; methods: readonly StorefrontEdgeMethod[] }`. `StorefrontEdgeMethod` is the closed union `GET | HEAD | POST | PUT | PATCH | DELETE | OPTIONS`; `STOREFRONT_ORIGIN_ROUTE_RULES` is a compatibility projection filtered from this authority, never a second list. The sole `edge-observation` rule is segment-prefix `/__baci/edge-observation`, permits only `GET`, and its handler additionally requires exactly one lowercase-hex64 suffix segment. Both `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest` and Task 12's Cloudflare alias/WAF expressions consume the same array; no second hand-maintained path or method list is allowed.
- Produces `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest(request): 'static-candidate' | 'origin' | 'edge-observation' | 'reject-method'` and `normalizeStaticStorefrontQuery(url): { kind: 'serve'; searchQuery?: string } | { kind: 'redirect'; location: string }` from that code-owned route-and-method policy, which a release object cannot override. A path matching any shared rule with a disallowed method returns `reject-method`, including `HEAD` or mutation on the observation prefix; unmatched non-`GET`/`HEAD` also returns `reject-method`. `edge-observation` bypasses static-query canonicalization and accepts one bounded cache-separation query nonce only after the handler validates the exact challenge grammar; it never reaches origin or R2.
- Produces the closed union `StorefrontEdgeObservationBindingState = { mode: 'disabled'; rolloutIdentitySha256: ZERO_SHA256; challengeSha256: ZERO_SHA256; notBefore: UNIX_EPOCH_MS; notAfter: UNIX_EPOCH_MS; capabilityArtifactSha256: ZERO_SHA256 } | { mode: 'active'; admission: StorefrontEdgeObservationAdmission }`, where `ZERO_SHA256` is exactly 64 ASCII zeroes and `UNIX_EPOCH_MS` is exactly `1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z`. `StorefrontEdgeObservationAdmission = { rolloutIdentitySha256: string; challengeSha256: string; notBefore: string; notAfter: string; capabilityArtifactSha256: string }` has its own byte-exact schema/serializer. Production versions receive those five values as non-secret, version-scoped text bindings named `OBSERVATION_ROLLOUT_IDENTITY_SHA256`, `OBSERVATION_CHALLENGE_SHA256`, `OBSERVATION_NOT_BEFORE`, `OBSERVATION_NOT_AFTER`, and `OBSERVATION_CAPABILITY_ARTIFACT_SHA256`; generated Wrangler types are the only `Env` authority. The handler first recognizes only the complete five-member disabled tuple and returns the bounded edge `404`; a partial/mixed sentinel is malformed and also fails closed. For active state it hashes the supplied challenge with Web Crypto, validates both digests as exact 32-byte values, compares them with `node:crypto.timingSafeEqual` under the pinned `nodejs_compat` contract, requires the rollout/version settings to equal the sealed preview acceptance and provider version-detail receipt, and serves only while Cloudflare runtime time satisfies `notBefore <= now < notAfter`. Missing, malformed, length-mismatched, future-skewed, expired, or over-two-hour admission settings fail closed with the same bounded edge `404`. These bindings grant no origin, R2, database, mutation, or version-selection authority.

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement the manifest schema**

```ts
export const cspScriptHashSchema = z
  .string()
  .regex(/^sha256-[A-Za-z0-9+/]{43}=$/)
  .brand('CspScriptHash');

export type CspScriptHash = z.infer<typeof cspScriptHashSchema>;

export const storefrontReleaseManifestSchema = z.object({
  schemaVersion: z.literal(1),
  releaseId: z.string().uuid(),
  merchantId: z.string().uuid(),
  generationDecimal: storefrontGenerationDecimalSchema,
  createdAt: z.string().datetime(),
  hostname: z.string().min(1).max(253),
  routes: z.record(
    z.string(),
    z.object({
      status: z.literal(200),
      objectKey: z.string().min(1).max(1024),
      originEtag: z.string().min(1).max(160),
      contentType: z.enum([
        'text/html; charset=utf-8',
        'application/json',
        'application/xml',
        'application/speculationrules+json',
        'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
        'text/css; charset=utf-8',
        'application/javascript; charset=utf-8',
        'font/woff2',
      ]),
      responsePolicy: storefrontReleaseResponsePolicySchema,
      sha256: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/),
      byteLength: z.number().int().nonnegative().max(2_000_000),
      cspScriptHashes: z.array(cspScriptHashSchema).max(4),
    }).strict()
  ),
  notFound: z.object({
    status: z.literal(404),
    objectKey: z.string().min(1).max(1024),
    originEtag: z.string().min(1).max(160),
    contentType: z.literal('text/html; charset=utf-8'),
    responsePolicy: z.literal('document-no-store'),
    sha256: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/),
    byteLength: z.number().int().nonnegative().max(2_000_000),
    cspScriptHashes: z.array(cspScriptHashSchema).max(4),
  }).strict(),
  staticNetworkPolicy: z.object({
    scriptOrigins: z.array(z.enum([
      'https://www.googletagmanager.com',
      'https://www.gstatic.com',
      'https://securepubads.g.doubleclick.net',
      'https://connect.facebook.net',
      'https://analytics.tiktok.com',
      'https://sc-static.net',
    ])).max(6),
    connectOrigins: z.array(z.enum([
      'https://www.google-analytics.com',
      'https://region1.google-analytics.com',
      'https://pagead2.googlesyndication.com',
      'https://securepubads.g.doubleclick.net',
      'https://www.facebook.com',
      'https://analytics.tiktok.com',
      'https://tr.snapchat.com',
    ])).max(7),
    frameOrigins: z.array(z.enum([
      'https://www.google.com',
      'https://www.gstatic.com',
      'https://securepubads.g.doubleclick.net',
      'https://googleads.g.doubleclick.net',
    ])).max(4),
    imageOrigins: z.array(z.enum([
      'https://www.google-analytics.com',
      'https://www.facebook.com',
      'https://analytics.tiktok.com',
      'https://tr.snapchat.com',
      'https://pagead2.googlesyndication.com',
    ])).max(5),
  }).strict(),
  rendererVersion: z.literal('ogabassey-static-v1'),
}).strict().superRefine((manifest, context) => {
  for (const [path, route] of Object.entries(manifest.routes)) {
    if (
      !validateStorefrontReleaseResponsePolicyForRoute(
        path,
        route.contentType,
        route.responsePolicy
      )
    ) {
      context.addIssue({
        code: 'custom',
        path: ['routes', path, 'responsePolicy'],
        message: 'response policy is incompatible with route path/content type',
      });
    }
  }
});

const storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema = z.object({
  schemaVersion: z.literal(1),
  hostname: z.string().min(1).max(253),
  merchantId: z.string().uuid(),
  merchantSlug: z.string().min(1).max(80),
  generationDecimal: storefrontGenerationDecimalSchema,
  activatedAt: z.string().datetime(),
}).strict();

const releaseTargetSchema = z.object({
  releaseId: z.string().uuid(),
  manifestSha256: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/),
}).strict();

const storefrontReleasePointerDiscriminatedSchema = z.discriminatedUnion('mode', [
  storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema.extend({ mode: z.literal('edge') }).merge(releaseTargetSchema),
  storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema.extend({
    mode: z.literal('origin'),
    releaseId: z.string().uuid().nullable(),
    manifestSha256: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/).nullable(),
  }),
  storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema.extend({
    mode: z.literal('suspended'),
    retryAfterSeconds: z.number().int().min(60).max(86_400),
  }),
  storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema.extend({
    mode: z.literal('unpublished'),
    publicDisplayName: z.string().min(1).max(160),
  }),
  storefrontReleasePointerCommonSchema.extend({ mode: z.literal('gone') }),
]);

export const storefrontReleasePointerSchema = storefrontReleasePointerDiscriminatedSchema.superRefine(
  (pointer, context) => {
    if (
      pointer.mode === 'origin' &&
      (pointer.releaseId === null) !== (pointer.manifestSha256 === null)
    ) {
      context.addIssue({
        code: 'custom',
        message: 'origin releaseId and manifestSha256 must both be null or both be set',
      });
    }
  }
);
```

Factor the object schemas as `releaseObjectDraftMetadataSchema` (status, object key, content type, response policy, SHA-256, byte length, CSP hashes) and `releaseObjectMetadataSchema = releaseObjectDraftMetadataSchema.extend({ originEtag: ... })`. `storefrontReleaseManifestDraftSchema` uses only draft metadata; `storefrontReleaseManifestSchema` uses final metadata and is the only schema accepted by the pointer/Worker. Both import `storefrontReleaseResponsePolicySchema`, iterate every `[path, route]` entry through `validateStorefrontReleaseResponsePolicyForRoute(path, route.contentType, route.responsePolicy)`, require every normal route to have status `200`, require `notFound.objectKey` to be distinct from every normal route key and remain under the exact release prefix, and validate dedicated not-found metadata as HTML plus `document-no-store` with status `404`. The compatibility function permits only the exact shared `/ads.txt` path with `text/plain; charset=utf-8` to use `ads-txt-public-host-vary`; only the exact shared IndexNow path with that content type to use `indexnow-public-immutable`; canonical HTML/XML/JSON documents to use `document-no-store`; and immutable release-origin CSS, JavaScript, font, speculation-rules, search-index, or other noncanonical asset objects to use `release-object-immutable`. A route cannot select another route's policy. Unit tests prove the renderer draft rejects an injected ETag, the final schema requires every ETag, and only `finalizeStorefrontReleaseManifest(draft, verifiedUploads): StorefrontReleaseManifest` can add ETags after matching every exact object key/hash/length/content type with no missing or extra upload. Tests also import the same schema/function from renderer and Worker boundaries and include an exhaustiveness assertion over `storefrontReleaseResponsePolicySchema.options`; no second enum/literal array is permitted. `responsePolicy`, route status, and dedicated not-found metadata are inside the pointer-hashed final manifest and are checked before the Worker reads a response body. `manifest.json` is deliberately not an entry in `routes` or `notFound`: its integrity is the pointer's `manifestSha256` plus its separately verified upload ETag/length/content type, avoiding a self-referential manifest hash. Separately, every object under the immutable `/releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/...` storage prefix receives browser-facing `Cache-Control: public, max-age=21600, immutable` at R2 upload; the release-origin Cache Rule supplies the independently read-back long edge TTL. That object-origin metadata is not the canonical-host response policy and is never copied blindly to a Worker response.

`storefrontReleaseInputSchema` uses these exact bounded public projections; every nested schema is `.strict()` and every URL receives `validatePublicReleaseUrl` validation in this task:

```ts
const publicImageSchema = z.object({
  url: z.string().url().max(2048),
  alt: z.string().max(160),
}).strict();

const publicProductConditionSchema = z.enum([
  'new',
  'used',
  'open_box',
]);

const commerceSelectionAttributesSchema = z
  .record(z.string().min(1).max(80), z.string().max(160))
  .superRefine(validateCommerceSelectionAttributes)
  .transform(sortCommerceSelectionAttributes);

const publicVariantSchema = z.object({
  id: z.string().uuid(),
  name: z.string().min(1).max(160),
  attributes: commerceSelectionAttributesSchema,
  condition: publicProductConditionSchema.nullable(),
  displayPriceMinor: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
  available: z.boolean(),
  displayQuantityLimit: z.number().int().min(0).max(100).nullable(),
}).strict();

const publicConditionOfferSchema = z.object({
  id: z.string().uuid(),
  condition: publicProductConditionSchema,
  displayPriceMinor: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
  compareAtPriceMinor: z.number().int().nonnegative().nullable(),
  available: z.boolean(),
  displayQuantityLimit: z.number().int().min(0).max(100).nullable(),
  grade: z.enum(['A', 'B', 'C', 'D']).nullable(),
  notes: z.string().max(500).nullable(),
  images: z.array(publicImageSchema).max(12),
}).strict();

export const storefrontReleaseInputSchema = z.object({
  releaseId: z.string().uuid(),
  merchantId: z.string().uuid(),
  generationDecimal: storefrontGenerationDecimalSchema,
  hostname: z.string().min(1).max(253),
  merchantSlug: z.string().min(1).max(80),
  merchant: z.object({
    businessName: z.string().min(1).max(160),
    businessType: z.string().min(1).max(80),
    logoUrl: z.string().url().max(2048).nullable(),
    brandColors: z.object({ primary: z.string(), secondary: z.string(), accent: z.string() }).strict(),
    publicEmail: z.string().email().max(320).nullable(),
    publicPhone: z.string().max(32).nullable(),
    locale: z.string().min(2).max(35),
    currency: z.string().length(3),
    currencyMinorUnit: z.number().int().min(0).max(3),
    seoTitle: z.string().max(70).nullable(),
    seoDescription: z.string().max(170).nullable(),
  }).strict(),
  publishedStorefrontConfig: publishedBuilderProjectionSchema,
  publicIntegrations: z.object({
    googleAnalyticsMeasurementId: z.string().regex(/^G-[A-Z0-9]{4,20}$/).nullable(),
    facebookPixelId: z.string().regex(/^\d{5,32}$/).nullable(),
    tiktokPixelId: z.string().regex(/^[A-Z0-9]{5,32}$/).nullable(),
    snapchatPixelId: z.string().regex(/^[A-Fa-f0-9-]{16,64}$/).nullable(),
    googleStoreWidget: z.object({
      enabled: z.boolean(),
      merchantCenterId: z.string().regex(/^\d{1,32}$/).nullable(),
    }).strict(),
    googleAdManagerEnabled: z.boolean(),
  }).strict(),
  categories: z.array(z.object({
    id: z.string().uuid(), slug: z.string().min(1).max(120),
    name: z.string().min(1).max(160), description: z.string().max(2_000).nullable(),
  }).strict()).max(500),
  products: z.array(z.object({
    id: z.string().uuid(), slug: z.string().min(1).max(160),
    categoryIds: z.array(z.string().uuid()).max(500), name: z.string().min(1).max(240),
    description: z.string().max(20_000).nullable(), brand: z.string().max(160).nullable(),
    displayPriceMinor: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
    compareAtPriceMinor: z.number().int().nonnegative().nullable(),
    condition: publicProductConditionSchema.nullable(),
    availableConditions: z.array(publicProductConditionSchema).max(3),
    conditionOffers: z.array(publicConditionOfferSchema).max(4),
    ratingAverage: z.number().min(0).max(5).nullable(),
    reviewCount: z.number().int().nonnegative().max(1_000_000),
    available: z.boolean(), displayQuantityLimit: z.number().int().min(0).max(100).nullable(),
    images: z.array(publicImageSchema).max(12),
    variants: z.array(publicVariantSchema).max(100),
  }).strict()).max(5_000),
  posts: z.array(z.object({
    id: z.string().uuid(), slug: z.string().min(1).max(160), title: z.string().min(1).max(240),
    excerpt: z.string().max(500).nullable(), body: z.string().max(100_000), publishedAt: z.string().datetime(),
  }).strict()).max(1_000),
  pages: z.array(z.object({
    id: z.string().uuid(), slug: z.string().min(1).max(120), title: z.string().min(1).max(240),
    body: z.string().max(100_000),
  }).strict()).max(100),
}).strict();
```

Monetary values are integer minor units, not JavaScript floats. `displayQuantityLimit` is a bounded public UI cap derived as `null` for untracked stock or `min(current public stock, 100)`; it is display-only, never used as checkout authority, and a stock change that alters it enqueues a release. Product, variant, available-condition, and legacy condition-offer conditions are first-class typed commerce projections using the existing shared canonical union `new | used | open_box`; `uk_used` and `refurbished` normalize through `normalizeCanonicalProductCondition`, so the release does not invent a competing condition vocabulary. A non-null variant condition overrides the product condition for that SKU selection. Legacy `product_offers` remain a separate bounded `conditionOffers` axis and are mutually exclusive with the SKU-matrix variant model under the current database invariant; they are not flattened into arbitrary variant attributes. `attributes.condition` is never accepted as authority or used as a fallback. A typed-versus-attribute conflict, inconsistent `availableConditions`, duplicate canonical offer condition, invalid compare-at price, or simultaneous incompatible offer/variant models fails the release fixture rather than silently changing the offer. Review aggregates are display projections only; submitting or mutating a review remains origin-owned. `publicIntegrations` contains public browser identifiers/enablement only; it explicitly excludes GA API secrets, CAPI/access tokens, enhanced-conversion customer data, ad-platform credentials, and arbitrary URLs. `compileStaticNetworkPolicy` maps enabled integrations to the closed origin sets above, sorts/deduplicates them, rejects any unknown origin, and stores the result in the pointer-hashed manifest. The schema explicitly excludes cost prices, raw/unbounded stock counts, supplier fields, customer/order data, credentials, draft configuration, provider responses, signed URLs, and arbitrary JSON. `StorefrontReleaseInput` is `z.infer<typeof storefrontReleaseInputSchema>` and is the exact return contract of `load_storefront_release_input`.

Variant attributes and cart-selection attributes share one bounded schema with explicit maximum entry count, canonical bounded key/value byte limits, stable ordering, and duplicate-key rejection. The key `condition` is reserved and rejected case-insensitively after canonicalization so no attribute can shadow the typed condition/condition-offer axis. The release, client runtime, cart handoff, hydration, and checkout schemas consume that same contract; tests cover maximum and maximum-plus-one entries, duplicate/confusable/reserved keys, oversized values, order normalization, and a handoff that attempts to smuggle a conflicting condition.

Do not include merchant credentials, customer objects, unpublished records, inventory reservation data, provider tokens, dynamic-route configuration, arbitrary headers, or an origin URL in any release schema.

- [ ] **Step 3: Define the origin-owned routing policy**

`routing-policy.ts` is the only edge request classifier. `origin-route-policy.ts` defines these reviewed rule groups with boundary-aware matching:

- `api`: `/api` segment-prefix, methods `GET|HEAD|POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE|OPTIONS`; this namespace remains rate-limited before the Worker/origin and does not authorize a sibling prefix.
- `platform`: segment-prefix rules for `/admin`, `/auth`, `/builder`, `/dashboard`, `/forgot-password`, `/login`, `/logout`, `/onboarding`, `/reset-password`, `/signup`, `/staff`, `/update-password`, and `/verify`, using `GET|HEAD` only. Any platform mutation remains under `/api`; a contract test fails if a future handler adds another method without a reviewed shared-policy update.
- `storefront-dynamic`: segment-prefix rules for `/account`, `/my-account`, `/checkout`, `/cart`, `/orders`, `/order-success`, `/track`, `/track-order`, `/receipts`, `/wallet`, `/wishlist`, `/delete-account`, `/imei-check`, `/member-status`, `/quiz`, `/repair`, `/repairs`, `/reviews`, `/swap`, and `/unlock-orders`, with `GET|HEAD`; any mutation is authorized only by its `/api` rule, never by the page path.
- `runtime`: `/_next` with `GET|HEAD`; `/baci-relay` with `GET|HEAD|POST|OPTIONS`; exact `/analytics/conversion` with `POST`; and exact `/wc-api/klp_wc_payment_webhook` with `POST`.
- `machine`: exact `GET|HEAD` rules for every non-API value in the shared `STOREFRONT_AGENT_ROUTES` and every `STOREFRONT_FEED_ROUTES` value, `/llms.txt`, `/llms-full.txt`, `/.well-known/llms.txt`, `/.well-known/llms-full.txt`, `/favicon.ico`, `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association`, `/.well-known/assetlinks.json`, `/.well-known/http-message-signatures-directory`, `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`, and `/.well-known/openid-configuration`; `GET|HEAD|POST` for exact `/.well-known/agent-auth` and the `/.well-known/agent-auth` segment-prefix actions; `GET|HEAD` for the `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` segment-prefix; and `GET|HEAD` for the `.md` suffix. Values under `/api` inherit the `api` rule. The existing `/.well-known/[...rest]` catch-all remains an edge `404` for unknown names rather than an origin lookup; every concrete `apps/web/src/app/.well-known` handler must appear in this policy or the contract test fails.

`STOREFRONT_STATIC_ROOT_ASSETS.adsTxt.path` and `STOREFRONT_STATIC_ROOT_ASSETS.indexNow.path` are deliberate static-release root assets, not origin rules. The object maps each exact path to its shared byte-for-byte body, content type, and closed response policy; both accept `GET|HEAD` only and must classify as `static-candidate`. The Web route handlers use that same object plus `buildStorefrontReleaseResponseHeaders`, so current-origin and release parity cannot drift. An unknown root `.txt` file remains a normal static candidate and therefore a bounded release `404`; never authorize all `.txt` paths to origin.

Segment-aware means `/repair` matches `/repair` and `/repair/...`, not `/repairman`. `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest` returns `origin` only when both path and method match, `edge-observation` only for the shared observation prefix with `GET`, `reject-method` whenever a known rule path has a disallowed method and for every other unmatched non-`GET`/`HEAD`, and `static-candidate` for remaining `GET`/`HEAD`. The observation handler then requires exactly `/__baci/edge-observation/<lowercase-hex64>` with no extra segment, accepts exactly one bounded code-generated query key `n=<lowercase-hex32>` for cache separation, and rejects every missing, duplicate, unknown, malformed, or oversized query; URL fragments are rejected by the observer before `fetch` because fragments are not transmitted in HTTP requests and the Worker does not claim to inspect them without redirecting or consulting a release. `apps/web/src/config/storefront-agent-routes.ts` and `storefront-feed-routes.ts` become compatibility re-exports of the shared constants, so the existing `proxy.ts` keeps its current imports and behavior unchanged. Add a behavior-level matrix to `apps/web/src/proxy.test.ts` that sends every shared origin rule through the current proxy and proves the expected pass-through/redirect/rewrite; this is the drift gate without modifying protected `proxy.ts`. A filesystem contract test enumerates concrete `apps/web/src/app/.well-known` handlers plus `llms-full.txt` and fails when a handler lacks a shared rule. Cookie names and values never influence classification. The Worker imports `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest` directly and switches exhaustively over all four outcomes. Static query normalization runs only for `static-candidate`, never for `edge-observation`. The production Worker accepts the observation result only on the exact apex and only when the version-scoped `StorefrontEdgeObservationAdmission` bindings match the sealed rollout and current half-open admission window; aliases, raw-release hosts, expired/inactive admission, and every other host terminate at the edge without redirect or origin fetch. The isolated preview Worker uses its separately sealed preview environment and the same candidate version/admission tuple; it cannot make the production apex eligible. After an `edge` pointer is found, `/search` accepts one bounded `q` and uses the release search index; other static-candidate queries redirect to canonical query-free URLs. Only an explicit `mode: origin` pointer may otherwise pass to origin. `releases.ogabassey.com`, `releases-preview.ogabassey.com`, `edge-preview.ogabassey.com`, and every hostname other than the environment's exact route are never production merchant release requests.

The release-input contract is a normalized renderer projection, not a mirror of one table. `load_storefront_release_input` maps the current public sources deliberately: merchant identity/branding/SEO and `pages`, `about_page`, `faq_items`, `trust_profile`, and legacy public pixel IDs from `merchants`; page enablement, blog enablement, normalized public pixel IDs, Google Store widget enablement/Merchant Center ID, and public ad enablement from `merchant_feature_settings`; direct `products.category_id` plus every `product_categories` membership into sorted `categoryIds`; published products/variants/legacy condition offers/categories/review aggregates/blog posts only; and `page_configs.published_config` only after transforming it through `PublishedBuilderProjection`. Feature settings override legacy public IDs using the existing `buildMerchantAnalyticsSettings`/Google widget precedence, while secrets are discarded before the release object is constructed. Fixed trust/content pages are emitted into the bounded `pages` array as sanitized public content. The shared schema ceilings are abuse/safety maxima, not the approved pilot capacity. Task 5's checked-in Ogabassey capacity fixture is derived from the live read-only inventory and applies the tighter publication limit. `ogabassey-static-v1` must reproduce the approved Ogabassey builder/component and storefront invariants and may not silently substitute the generic free theme. Unsupported published components block static publication and retain/restore origin; they are never omitted to make the release pass. Add contract fixtures proving each source field/component either maps to a named public output or is explicitly excluded.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes one sanitized `StorefrontReleaseInput` only when slug, hostname, and the owner-approved live merchant ID all identify Ogabassey; contains only public merchant, product, category, content, and blog projections.
- Produces `RenderedStorefrontRelease` with immutable assets and a validated `StorefrontReleaseManifestDraft`; it cannot know or supply R2 ETags.
- Produces checked-in `maximumObjectsPerRelease` and `maximumReleasesPendingDeletion` bounds from the approved capacity fixture. Publication may coalesce newer generations but must not activate another release when doing so would exceed the deletion-SLA bound; it first retires/cleans an eligible old release or returns a bounded capacity failure while the current release remains serving.
- Produces `createStaticGuestCartItem(product, selection, quantity): StoredStaticGuestCartItem`, where `selection` is the closed union `{ kind: 'base' } | { kind: 'variant'; variantId; selectionAttributes } | { kind: 'condition_offer'; conditionOfferId; condition; selectionAttributes }`, and the exact existing storage keys `baci-cart-{merchantSlug}-guest` / `baci-cart-merchant-slug`, shared by `cart.js` and the dynamic storefront cart hydrator. Variant and condition-offer identity are mutually exclusive by construction.

```ts
export type StoredStaticGuestCartItem = {
  id: string;
  merchant_id: string;
  name: string;
  mpn: string;
  description: string;
  status: 'active';
  price: number; // current cart contract uses major units; derived from integer minor units once
  manage_stock: false; // snapshot never claims an authoritative stock count
  stock: 0;
  image: string;
  imageLarge: string;
  imageHint: '';
  brand: string;
  gtin: '';
  slug: string;
  quantity: number;
  cartItemId: string;
  variantId?: string;
  conditionOfferId?: string;
  variantAttributes?: CommerceSelectionAttributes;
  condition?: 'new' | 'used' | 'open_box' | 'refurbished';
};
```

`validateCommerceSelectionAttributes` enforces at most 24 entries, maximum 80 UTF-8 bytes per canonical key and 160 UTF-8 bytes per value, case-folded/canonical duplicate rejection, and case-insensitive rejection of the reserved key `condition`; `sortCommerceSelectionAttributes` emits stable canonical-key order and the branded `CommerceSelectionAttributes` type. The same schema is used by `publicVariantSchema`, `StoredStaticGuestCartItem.variantAttributes`, the cart-handoff Zod schema, hydration, and checkout. Renderer-input tests include 24/25 entries, reserved and Unicode-confusable condition keys, duplicate canonical keys, byte limits, and stable order. The stored snapshot includes `name` and `mpn` because the existing cart decoder and `CartItem extends Product` contract require them; compatibility tests pass the generated item through the current decoder/type boundary while proving no snapshot field becomes checkout authority.

`createStaticGuestCartItem` clamps quantity to a positive integer, uses the existing `generateCartItemId` algorithm extended with the mutually exclusive `variantId | conditionOfferId` identity, and rejects unavailable products/variants/offers. The browser may retain the bounded display snapshot above for an offline/cart-drawer experience, but the static-to-dynamic handoff transmits only `{ productId, variantId?, conditionOfferId?, selectionAttributes?, quantity }`. `conditionOfferId` is the stable identifier of the selected separate condition offer and is mutually exclusive with the SKU-matrix variant path; `selectionAttributes` uses the shared bounded non-`condition` schema and carries only selections that are not already represented by the variant ID. The legacy `variantAttributes` cart field stores that branded selection map for compatibility, and every schema/provider/checkout consumer converts it through the same validator rather than accepting an unbounded `Record<string, string>`. Release IDs, stored price, condition labels, stock, promotion, tax, shipping, or entitlement values never cross into order/payment creation as authority. The first same-host cart hydration, `/api/cart/validate`, order-input construction, order-idempotency hashing, and every checkout submission carry the identifiers without loss and re-resolve them through merchant-scoped current product/variant/offer records. The server replaces the snapshot with authoritative current price/condition/availability/promotion/quantity bounds and removes or explicitly blocks stale/deleted/cross-merchant lines before totals; `/api/orders` repeats the authority check in the final transaction boundary and never trusts successful earlier hydration. Tests change price, condition offer, selected variant, selection attributes, stock, promotion, shipping eligibility, and product status between release/cart creation, hydration, validation, and checkout and prove the current commerce data wins. Cross-merchant product/variant/offer IDs, an offer-plus-variant pair, stale offers, malformed attributes, and tampering that changes only `conditionOfferId` must fail; the idempotency digest must distinguish otherwise identical variant/offer/attribute selections.

The condition-offer lineage is explicit end to end: product normalization preserves each separate offer's immutable database ID; `resolveCurrentOffer` returns `conditionOfferId` only for that selected separate offer and returns `variantId` only for a resolved SKU-matrix variant; product-details state and the cart handler pass the exact identifier into `AddToCartOptions`. Condition text, product ID, price, or array position can never substitute for offer identity. A regression fixture follows one real normalized condition-offer ID through resolution, product state, add-to-cart, storage, hydration, validation, order-input construction, idempotency hashing, and final order authority, and proves an otherwise identical condition label with a different or missing offer ID is rejected.

Because `storefront-cart-provider.tsx`, `place-order.ts`, and `apps/web/src/app/api/orders/route.ts` already exceed 300 lines, they remain wiring only. `storefront-cart-item-identity.ts` owns construction, normalization, mutual-exclusion, hashing, and hydration of product/variant/condition-offer/attribute identity for the provider. `build-authoritative-order-request.ts` owns the checkout-side identifier-only request construction and rejects snapshot authority before network submission. `storefront-order-line-authority.ts` owns the final merchant-scoped product/variant/condition-offer/reference lookup and authoritative line reconstruction used by the order route. Their colocated tests cover success, malformed/cross-tenant/stale identity, snapshot-field stripping, catalog races, and exact error projection; the oversized files gain no new inline branching or validation logic.

- [ ] **Step 2: Define the renderer interface**

```ts
export type RenderedReleaseAsset = {
  path: string;
  contentType:
    | 'text/html; charset=utf-8'
    | 'application/json'
    | 'application/xml'
    | 'application/speculationrules+json'
    | 'text/plain; charset=utf-8'
    | 'text/css; charset=utf-8'
    | 'application/javascript; charset=utf-8'
    | 'font/woff2';
  responsePolicy: StorefrontReleaseResponsePolicy;
  body: Uint8Array;
  sha256: string;
};

export type RenderedStorefrontRelease = {
  manifestDraft: StorefrontReleaseManifestDraft;
  assets: readonly RenderedReleaseAsset[];
};

export type StorefrontRendererOptions = {
  assetOrigin: 'https://releases.ogabassey.com';
};

export async function renderStorefrontRelease(
  input: StorefrontReleaseInput,
  options: StorefrontRendererOptions
): Promise<RenderedStorefrontRelease>;
```
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the Ogabassey parity theme without request-time data**

User-controlled content uses the repo sanitization contract and HTML escaping appropriate to its reviewed public representation; never use `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. The renderer must reproduce the approved visible, interactive, conversion, revenue, and SEO invariants without importing request-time Next/Supabase code. `build-client-assets.ts` bundles the five focused client runtimes plus `runtime-bootstrap.ts` into content-hashed external JavaScript; no inline executable script is added. `theme-runtime.ts` implements the reviewed `storefront-theme-v2` date/cookie contract. `product-commerce-runtime.ts` implements variant/condition resolution, quantity bounds, cart item creation, drawer/badge updates, and same-host handoff. `search-runtime.ts` consumes the bounded release search index. `chrome-runtime.ts` owns menu/chrome behavior and only the approved same-host hydration calls from the interaction contract. `integration-runtime.ts` implements the existing consent-first behavior for the normalized public pixel IDs, Google Store widget, and Google Ad Manager using external code only; it dynamically loads only origins present in the pointer-hashed `StaticNetworkPolicy`, sends no enhanced-conversion/customer data from the static plane, and gates every side effect on activation/consent exactly as the parity contract requires. Internal navigation uses document URLs rather than Next RSC requests. `/checkout` hydrates the existing cart and immediately runs authoritative validation. JSON-LD is deterministically escaped and hashed into `cspScriptHashes`. Do not include any telemetry/third-party behavior unless the interaction contract preserves it and the CSP/network test names it explicitly.

`render-speculation-rules.ts` writes one immutable allowlisted release-origin `application/speculationrules+json` object with `"relative_to":"document"`. The route manifest names its object path, and the Worker emits `Speculation-Rules: "https://releases.ogabassey.com/releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/assets/speculation-rules.json"` (preview uses the same canonical production asset origin, because it serves the exact immutable candidate rather than a rewritten copy). Tests prove the quoted structured-header syntax, content type, credentialless CORS, exclusion of origin-owned/cart/account/API paths, and absence of inline `type="speculationrules"` markup. Unsupported browsers simply ignore the header.

The renderer emits immutable `theme.css`, the content-hashed client bundles, and required WOFF2 files under the release prefix. HTML references those files through the exact `assetOrigin` plus `/releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/assets/...`; both production and preview use the canonical immutable asset origin `releases.ogabassey.com`, and no release asset is fetched from Vercel. CSS and JavaScript tags include `crossorigin="anonymous"` plus SRI generated from their manifest SHA-256; the R2 CORS contract permits those credentialless reads.

`render-static-assets.ts` also emits immutable document entries for `STOREFRONT_STATIC_ROOT_ASSETS.adsTxt` and `.indexNow`; it never reconstructs either body, content type, or response policy. It emits exactly one dedicated `documents/404.html` asset and records it only in `manifest.notFound` with status `404`; it is not a public route key and a direct reserved-path request is rejected. The release route manifest maps the two root assets' exact `.path` values to their objects, preserves each `.contentType`/`.responsePolicy` and status `200`, returns an empty body for `HEAD`, and never derives the IndexNow path independently from the key. Tests run each policy through `buildStorefrontReleaseResponseHeaders`, compare release bytes and canonical headers directly with both existing Next route handlers—including `Vary: Host` for `/ads.txt`—and prove unknown root `.txt` and random paths fetch `manifest.notFound`, return its bytes with HTTP `404`, and never fall back to origin.

- [ ] **Step 4: Enforce bounded output**

Before implementation, run the read-only live Ogabassey projection and record product/category/post/page/variant/route counts, rendered byte estimates, current release-prefix count, and the maximum observed seven-day activation count. `ogabassey-capacity.ts` freezes owner-reviewed maxima with at least 2x item/route and 1.5x byte headroom, while retaining hard per-object limits. It exports `maximumObjectsPerRelease` from the complete route/asset formula and `maximumReleasesPendingDeletion` from the larger of the current retained-prefix inventory or twice the observed seven-day activation count; the publisher/ledger enforce both bounds before activating another release. Publication fails closed above those checked-in bounds and reports which dimension needs a reviewed increase; it never truncates the catalog or evicts a protected release. Product media remains referenced by reviewed public HTTPS URLs and is not copied into the release. Reject signed, localhost, private-address, credential-bearing, and non-HTTPS media URLs. Sort every collection deterministically before hashing.

**Interfaces:**

- Produces `storefront_delivery_profiles`, `storefront_release_jobs`, `storefront_releases`, `storefront_hostname_registry`, and merchant-independent `storefront_hostname_lifecycle_jobs`. The Ogabassey delivery profile persists the latest committed public generation/time, requested generation, active generation/time, freshness deadline, and closed freshness state `current | queued | building | stale_origin | failed_origin`. The freshness deadline is derived only from the owner-approved maximum publication freshness in the checked-in capacity contract; it is never caller-selected or unbounded. SQL constraints require monotonic generations, `activeGeneration <= latestCommittedPublicGeneration`, deadline/time coherence, and origin mode for both stale/failed-origin states.
- Produces Ogabassey-only append-only `storefront_edge_deployment_generations`, `storefront_edge_deployment_gate_receipts`, `storefront_edge_deployment_operations`, `storefront_edge_candidate_observation_receipts`, `storefront_edge_control_rule_operations`, and `storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_jobs`. A generation records immutable rollout identity, stable/candidate/origin version IDs, state `canary | active | retired | aborted`, and predecessor generation. A gate receipt records one confirmed deployment head, nonzero percentage, exact UTC-millisecond observation window of at least 24 hours, measurement artifact hash, configuration/evidence fingerprint, and canonical receipt. A deployment operation records database-derived action `bootstrap_origin | stage_candidate | temporary_proof | initial_confirmation | promote | rotate | rollback`, generation, prior deployment head, derived target percentage, expected version/weight tuple, exact annotation, request hash, nullable candidate artifact-readback receipt, nullable candidate-observation receipt, and state `prepared | provider_attempted | confirmed | ambiguous`; only `prepared -> provider_attempted -> confirmed` is the success path. `stage_candidate` requires a non-null candidate artifact receipt; `temporary_proof | rotate` inherit that exact staged receipt and require a non-null bounded Version Metadata observation receipt before confirmation; operations without a candidate require both null. Each observation row stores the exact parsed compact receipt, its canonical hash, and the bounded raw-evidence artifact hash under a unique operation/deployment constraint; it is inserted atomically with confirmation and cannot be replaced by a caller-supplied hash. `ambiguous` has no abandonment transition. A control-rule operation records kind `activate_affinity | remove_affinity | lock_override_guard | remove_override_guard`, state `prepared | provider_attempted | confirmed | ambiguous`, rollout identity, complete prior/expected ruleset versions and hashes, target rule ID/fingerprint, unrelated-inventory hash, exact request hash, attempt/read-back evidence, and canonical receipt. A pointer-rollback job records the closed `initiator: 'owner' | 'freshness_fallback'`, exact pre-CAS pointer tuple, epoch, claim/session fences, committed origin-pointer ETag, completion state, and canonical receipt. Owner jobs additionally record the authenticated owner request, one of the four owner reason codes, and approval ID; freshness jobs require all owner fields null and instead record the database-derived overdue `generationDecimal` and freshness deadline. Deployment, observation, control-rule, rollback, generation, and receipt rows are never deleted while the pilot is enrolled.
- Produces immutable `storefront_account_deletion_intents`, a scrubbed `storefront_account_deletion_intent_authority`, hash-chained append-only `storefront_account_deletion_intent_events`, a transactionally maintained `storefront_account_deletion_intent_projection`, append-only `storefront_release_domain_topology_operations`, and a one-row profile projection `{ releaseDomainTopologyGeneration; releaseDomainTopologyState: 'preview_attached' | 'preview_detached'; activeServingHostsSha256; topologyFenceToken; topologyFenceIntentId: string | null; topologyFenceKind: 'lifecycle_deletion' | 'teardown' | null; topologyFenceDeadline: string | null; activeTopologyOperationId }`. The authority row is uniquely keyed by the fixed Ogabassey delivery-profile ID and has unique nullable `activeIntentId`; under the profile `FOR UPDATE` lock it is the sole selector for byte-identical replay and for the lifecycle target stored in the separately scrubbed authority. An authenticated deletion request first inserts or byte-identically reuses that authority row's one immutable intent fact `{ intentId; ownerAuthorizationReceiptSha256; acceptedAt; requestReceiptSha256 }`. State exists only as ordered events `{ intentId; sequenceDecimal; priorEventSha256; event: 'accepted_waiting_topology' | 'topology_bound' | 'gone_committed' | 'erasure_recovery' | 'erasure_admitted' | 'terminal_drift' | 'finalized'; databaseAt; topologyBound: boolean; topologyGenerationDecimal: string | null; activeServingHostsSha256: string | null; receiptSha256 }`; `(intentId, sequenceDecimal)` is unique, every noninitial event seals the preceding event hash, and byte-identical replay is the only idempotent replay. Facts/events deny `UPDATE` and `DELETE` to every runtime role, append is permitted only inside the fixed `SECURITY DEFINER` lifecycle functions, and constraint triggers reject a missing predecessor, wrong prior hash, non-monotonic sequence, illegal event transition, or second initial event. Only `accepted_waiting_topology` has `topologyBound = false` and both topology fields null; every later event has `topologyBound = true` and non-null generation/set hash. The closed graph is `accepted_waiting_topology -> topology_bound | terminal_drift`, `terminal_drift -> erasure_recovery`, `topology_bound | erasure_recovery -> gone_committed`, `gone_committed -> erasure_admitted`, and `erasure_admitted -> finalized`; topology terminal drift appends `terminal_drift` then `erasure_recovery` as two ordered events in the same transaction, and every replay must be byte-identical. Every appender locks the authority/projection row, verifies its current sequence/event hash, inserts exactly one successor, and refreshes the projection in the same transaction. The mutable projection is rebuildable from the latest valid chain and is never used as receipt authority; it stores the current sequence/event hash for serialization, while the authority row survives only until successful finalization scrubs its lifecycle target and nulls `activeIntentId` without deleting the immutable fact/event evidence; the fixed Ogabassey owner/merchant identifiers required for destructive work live only in the separately scrubbed lifecycle authority, while the surviving immutable intent stores no raw user ID, email, merchant ID, hostname, or caller-selected identifier. Only then may the same transaction inspect topology. `lifecycle_deletion` requires a null deadline and a non-null `topologyFenceIntentId` equal to the authority row's active intent and is released only by successful erasure finalization, while `teardown` requires the bounded qualified recovery deadline. The closed topology-operation kind is `attach_preview | teardown_preview`, records full pre/final tuples, exact rollout identity, and parent state `prepared | executing | reconciling | confirmed | confirmed_no_effect | cancelled_preempted | terminal_drift`. Each operation owns append-only, ordered, at-most-once step rows with fixed `preview_worker_detach | canonical_cors_transition | preview_r2_domain_detach` names (the inverse closed attachment sequence is used only by `attach_preview`); each step records exact before/after Worker-domain/CORS/R2 tuples, request/read-back hashes, provider time, visibility deadline, and state `prepared | provider_attempted | reconciling | confirmed | confirmed_no_effect | terminal_drift`. The next provider write is forbidden until the prior step is confirmed. A timeout/lost response changes only that step and parent to `reconciling`; a credential-separated read-only recovery may change it to `confirmed` on the exact after-tuple and return the parent to `executing` for the next step, or to `confirmed_no_effect` only when the exact before-tuple remains unchanged after the Gate-B-qualified maximum visibility bound, closing the parent without another write. If deletion is waiting, first-step no-effect truthfully binds the unchanged attached pair; no-effect at step 2 or 3 after a confirmed predecessor is a partial tuple and the same transaction must append intent `terminal_drift` then `erasure_recovery`, transfer the same-intent fence, and seal conservative `[0,1]` rather than append `topology_bound` or release topology authority. A mixed tuple, visibility ambiguity, or unrecognized state records `terminal_drift`, preserves the deletion intent, and removes the apex route through the independent safety path; it never authorizes a duplicate provider write. Without an accepted deletion intent the topology fence remains for bounded owner/security repair. With an accepted intent, the same profile-locked transaction appends `terminal_drift` then `erasure_recovery`, atomically replaces any teardown fence with a non-expiring same-intent `lifecycle_deletion` fence, and seals the conservative ordered host pair `[0,1]`; no owner action is required to start erasure recovery and there is no intermediate null fence. `claim_storefront_release_gc_batch` locks this profile row and atomically snapshots generation/state/hash. A pair topology is protected by the lifecycle-owned non-expiring `lifecycle_deletion` fence created while binding the intent, not by a GC-worker lease; it survives every claim/phase/retry, VPS outage, and claim replacement until finalization. The HMAC-ledgered teardown RPC may reserve `teardown` only after the same lock proves no accepted deletion intent, lifecycle `gone` job, pair claim, or lifecycle-deletion fence. `attach_preview` has the same exclusive preconditions and requires fresh owner acceptance; it never revives a detached resource.
