Neither family may perform a provider call before its matching delivery record exists. GC claims additionally require no live rollback/release/lifecycle publication lease and database `now()` strictly after every recorded publisher session expiry, then persist the exact immutable prefix, nullable opaque continuation cursor, explicit `deleting | verify_from_start | purging_cache | verifying_public_absence` phase, `verification_required_since`, deleted-object count, last empty-list proof time, bounded current-host-ownership evidence, host-purge operation evidence, topology generation/lease fence, and claim fence. A non-empty terminal checkpoint or any empty deleting scan at the exact persisted cursor enters verification; completion requires a later, separately proved empty scan from cursor start followed, for `erasure_admitted` `gone`, by each sealed host's closed `active_owned` ownership/purge/public-absence path or `detached_absent` authoritative non-serving/public-absence path.
- Cache-host GC checkpoints and `record_storefront_release_gc_cache_host_ownership`/`record_storefront_release_gc_detached_absent`/`record_storefront_release_gc_cache_purge` take no host selector. The database reconstructs the sealed `activeServingHosts` set from the lifecycle's immutable ownership receipt, records one outcome-specific ownership-or-detachment plus absence receipt for each member, and rejects a missing, duplicate, extra, changed-order, or post-teardown preview host. `finalize_storefront_account_deletion` cannot commit before the intent is `erasure_admitted`, every release prefix is independently empty, and every sealed host has either an `active_owned` exact-host purge acknowledgement plus post-purge public absence or a `detached_absent` authoritative non-serving receipt plus public absence. Confirmed preview detachment seals `[0]`; it never bypasses the production host outcome.

- The Task 6 migration adds the `ActiveReleaseServingHost` SQL domain/closed ordinal mapping, `gc_active_serving_hosts_sha256`, and a child `storefront_release_gc_host_phases` table keyed by `(release_id, gc_claim_token, host_ordinal)` with the closed transition union `pending_classification | active_owned | ownership_recorded | purge_recorded | detached_absent | r2_rechecked | public_absence_verified`. Claim creation derives the ordered set/hash only from the sealed lifecycle receipt. Cache classification/ownership/detachment/purge/public-absence RPCs accept only `(claimToken, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256, proof)` and recompute the canonical hostname internally. The HMAC proof binds that exact ordered tuple, and the database rejects it before provider evidence is accepted unless every field equals the current claim/authority/event/profile/fence row. They also reject raw hostnames, a non-current ordinal, changed set hash, phase skip, duplicate receipt, purge after `detached_absent`, or mismatched ownership/detachment/purge/R2/probe hashes. Canonical request, result, journal, provider-operation, terminal-GC, and finalization serializers include the same intent/event/generation/fence tuple, ordinal, host-set hash, phase, and derived-host hash but never a caller hostname. The finalizer requires a fresh R2-empty proof plus outcome-specific public-absence receipt after every `active_owned` purge, or the authoritative non-serving plus public-absence receipt for `detached_absent`, before it may append `finalized`, scrub the authority target, clear the same-intent fence, or delete auth/merchant rows.

- The same migration makes topology generation and deletion acceptance time part of every deletion proof. `request_storefront_account_deletion` authenticates, locks the fixed profile plus its one intent-authority row, and inserts or byte-identically reuses only `activeIntentId`; concurrent distinct idempotency keys cannot create two active intents. It then inserts the immutable fact and initial `accepted_waiting_topology` event before returning. That initial event alone has `topologyBound = false` with null generation/set hash; every successor has `topologyBound = true` and both fields non-null. With confirmed detached topology it binds the singleton; with confirmed attached topology and no started teardown it binds the pair and installs a non-expiring `lifecycle_deletion` fence whose `topologyFenceIntentId` equals the authority-selected intent; with a prepared unattempted teardown it closes `cancelled_preempted` and binds the pair; with an executing/reconciling operation it returns the durable waiting receipt. The protected reconciler binds that same intent or atomically transfers the fence to same-intent conservative-pair recovery on terminal drift. The lifecycle and GC claim never discover topology later from Cloudflare. Every GC/host/socket/provider/finalizer receipt requires the bound generation, event head, fence token, and `topologyFenceIntentId`; a drift preserves the intent/fence and enters same-receipt recovery. A post-finalization retry can return only the bounded finalized tombstone for the original request receipt and cannot create a fresh fact. The lifecycle finalizer and ordinary rollback cannot detach preview.

- `topologyFenceDeadline` is an alert/capacity/recovery deadline for `teardown` only; reaching it never clears a token or grants another operation. `reconciling | terminal_drift` without an accepted deletion intent retains the teardown fence until an exact ledgered repair closes it. Once deletion is accepted, the profile-locked recovery transaction atomically replaces that fence with a `lifecycle_deletion` fence whose non-null `topologyFenceIntentId` equals the authority row's `activeIntentId`, and appends the transfer receipt with no intermediate null token. `lifecycle_deletion` always stores a null deadline; terminal recovery may rotate its token only while preserving exact intent-ID equality, and successful erasure finalization is the sole authority that may clear it.
- Conservative-host erasure recovery always seals the ordered pair `[0,1]` from the last confirmed topology plus the drift receipt and resolves each host independently to one closed outcome. `active_owned` requires fresh exact bucket/custom-domain ownership, then permits only that singleton host's purge after R2 is empty and requires authoritative purge acknowledgement plus multi-location public absence. `detached_absent` requires authoritative absence from the deleted bucket, with expected DNS absent or independently proven not to serve that bucket, preview Worker-domain non-serving/absence when applicable, and the same public-absence probes; it performs no purge. An active but misbound or foreign-owned host is never purged: it can close only as `detached_absent` after proving that it does not serve the deleted bucket and that deleted release hashes are absent publicly. Recovery retains the tombstone and same-intent fence until successful finalization, then clears only the fence and leaves the incident open for topology repair. The recovery worker has read-only topology credentials and may use only the existing narrowly scoped R2 GC and singleton cache-purge capabilities after ownership is proven. Owner/security repair can restore rollout topology later, but cannot delay, reset, or authorize erasure and cannot clear the deletion fence.
- Produces the separate lifecycle RPC protocol `claim_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_job`, `record_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_pointer_commit`, `finish_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_job`, and `finalize_storefront_account_deletion`. A publisher never reads the lifecycle table directly and release-job RPCs never claim lifecycle work implicitly.
- Produces authenticated control-plane RPCs `request_storefront_unpublish`, `request_storefront_account_deletion`, `request_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback`, `begin_storefront_edge_cutover`, `record_storefront_preview_acceptance`, `complete_storefront_edge_cutover`, and `cancel_storefront_edge_cutover`, plus authenticated-owner `get_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_status`, capability-bound read-only `verify_storefront_edge_cutover_hold`, capability-bound one-time `issue_storefront_edge_observation_capability_window` and `register_storefront_edge_observation_capability`, and single-merchant read-only `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_guard_state`. The seven owner mutations are eligible only for the verified Ogabassey profile; the two capability-registration mutations derive that same immutable profile exclusively from the unguessable hold capability pair and cannot select another tuple. The lifecycle pair records the hostname/generation/public display name needed for `unpublished`/`gone` before destructive merchant/auth deletion. The pointer rollback and cutover mutation functions are executable only by `authenticated`, derive the merchant owner from `auth.uid()`, are `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, and accept no merchant or hostname selector. Their exact signatures are `request_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback(p_expected_pointer_etag text, p_expected_release_id uuid, p_reason_code text, p_owner_approval_id text)`, `begin_storefront_edge_cutover(p_release_id uuid, p_generation_decimal text, p_pointer_etag text, p_manifest_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text)`, `record_storefront_preview_acceptance(p_hold_id uuid, p_begin_receipt_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text, p_worker_version_id uuid, p_preview_route_fingerprint text, p_acceptance_challenge_sha256 text, p_capability_registration_sha256 text)`, `complete_storefront_edge_cutover(p_hold_id uuid, p_initial_production_proof_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text)`, and `cancel_storefront_edge_cutover(p_hold_id uuid, p_reason_code text, p_owner_approval_id text)`. Before any epoch advance or job insert, rollback locks the profile and calls the deletion-phase classifier; any accepted/waiting/recovery/gone/erasure-admitted intent, lifecycle fence/admission, or terminal-GC receipt returns bounded `LIFECYCLE_DELETION_ACTIVE` with no mutation. Otherwise rollback accepts only `operator_abort | evidence_drift | worker_health | cost_guard`, locks the exact active pointer/release, atomically advances `publication_epoch`, revokes only application/database authority for lower-priority admissions, claims, and session use, terminally invalidates any live cutover hold, inserts one immutable rollback job, and returns `{ status: 'accepted'; rollbackId; expectedPointerEtag; expectedReleaseId; reasonCode; ownerApprovalId; publicationEpochDecimal; requestedAt; receiptSha256 }`; same-approval replay is idempotent only for the identical tuple, and a mismatched/stale ETag or release performs no mutation. An already issued R2 temporary credential remains cryptographically valid at Cloudflare until its exact `notAfter` unless the parent credential is revoked; Cloudflare exposes no selective single-session revocation operation. Parent revocation immediately invalidates every derived session in that family and is the emergency family-wide kill switch. Normal correctness therefore depends on the installed worker/process honoring the epoch watcher, an explicit trusted-runtime boundary. Watcher abort is operational fail-fast rather than selective provider revocation; any in-flight mutation is reconciled by exact `If-Match`, response/read-back, and epoch evidence, and an unexpected result enters `terminal_drift`, removes the route, and marks both control rules `cleanup_pending`. Compromise of the installed Worker/root boundary requires parent-token rotation plus route removal. Tests cover a pointer CAS completing immediately before and after epoch advancement and parent revocation invalidating every live family session. A higher-priority lifecycle transition instead wins the same lock order and makes the rollback stale before pointer mutation. Begin returns the closed union `{ status: 'opened'; holdId; releaseId; generationDecimal; pointerEtag; manifestSha256; ownerApprovalId; databaseOpenedAt; databaseExpiresAt; receiptSha256 } | { status: 'busy'; blockingClass: 'lifecycle' | 'pointer_rollback' | 'admission' | 'claim' | 'undelivered_session' | 'unexpired_session'; databaseNow; retryNotBefore: string | null }`; `retryNotBefore` is null for lifecycle or pointer rollback work and otherwise database-derived no earlier than the blocking lease/session expiry; either non-retryable class aborts cutover rather than polling. Complete returns the same immutable tuple plus `initialProductionProofSha256`, `databaseCompletedAt`, and `receiptSha256`; cancel permits only `expired | operator_abort | evidence_drift | lifecycle_preempted` and returns the tuple plus `reasonCode`, `databaseCancelledAt`, and `receiptSha256`. The begin RPC first requires `p_generation_decimal ~ '^[1-9][0-9]{0,18}$'`, then performs guarded PostgreSQL `bigint` conversion with explicit overflow rejection before comparing it to the stored value; tests include zero, leading zero, sign, whitespace, 20 digits, the largest 19-digit overflow, and the maximum accepted stored generation. Every UUID/hash/ETag/approval ID is schema-bounded, `generationDecimal` is the canonical positive base-10 string form of the PostgreSQL bigint, and PostgreSQL hashes a fixed scalar serialization rather than generic JSON.

- `get_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_status(p_rollback_id uuid, p_acceptance_receipt_sha256 text)` is authenticated-owner-only, derives the Ogabassey owner from `auth.uid()`, accepts no merchant or hostname selector, and returns one bounded indistinguishable denial for malformed, wrong-receipt, unknown, cross-job-substituted, or cross-owner capability pairs. Repeated polling of the exact owner/job/acceptance-receipt tuple is intentionally idempotent. Its exact success projection is `{ rollbackId; state; requestedAt; nextAttemptAt; completedAt; terminalAt; committedOriginPointerEtag; reasonCode; terminalReason; routeSafetyReceiptSha256; receiptSha256 }`; nullability is closed by state, `completed` requires completion time and committed ETag, `terminal_drift | superseded` require terminal time/reason and route-safety receipt, and nonterminal states cannot carry terminal fields.

- Control-rule activation/removal reads the complete ruleset before and after mutation and proves unrelated-inventory equality. Profile and guard projections contain `affinityRuleState: 'absent' | 'active' | 'cleanup_pending'`, active rule ID/SHA-256/ruleset version plus last confirmation receipt, and the analogous `overrideGuardState`, ID, SHA-256, version, and receipt. `absent` nulls only the live ID/hash/version; it retains the last confirmed removal operation and removed fingerprint as immutable evidence. Database rollback/abort/drift transitions mark any non-absent rule `cleanup_pending` transactionally. After a guardian or provider job removes the route, a separate HMAC-only reporter consumes the redacted provider receipt and calls `record_storefront_edge_route_safety_event`; it cannot receive the Cloudflare token. Entry into a fresh preview/enrollment and any route recreation require both states `absent`; after bootstrap, the protected workflow must transition the override guard to `active` before staging and later the affinity rule to `active` before the first mixed deployment. Any `cleanup_pending` state blocks all of those actions. The normal protected workflow alone reconciles and confirms cleanup.
- The authoritative and exhaustive control-rule operation kinds are `activate_affinity | remove_affinity | lock_override_guard | remove_override_guard`; no alias, probe-open, or generic activation kind is accepted. Override state is the closed projection `{ state: 'absent' | 'active' | 'cleanup_pending'; ruleId: string | null; ruleSha256: string | null; rulesetVersion: string | null; confirmationReceiptSha256: string | null }`. `absent` requires every live field null; `active` unconditionally blocks every request containing `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Overrides` on every production ingress host; `cleanup_pending` retains the last fully observed fingerprint and always requires route absence. There is no production nonce, temporary allow state, or header-removal Transform Rule. The guard-state v3 receipt includes every projection scalar in fixed order.
- The exact HMAC RPC signatures are `prepare_storefront_edge_control_rule_operation(p_kind text, p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_expected_live_ruleset_version text, p_expected_live_ruleset_sha256 text, p_target_rule_sha256 text, p_unrelated_inventory_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, `mark_storefront_edge_control_rule_attempted(p_operation_id uuid, p_request_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, `confirm_storefront_edge_control_rule_operation(p_operation_id uuid, p_provider_ruleset_version text, p_provider_ruleset_sha256 text, p_rule_id text, p_rule_sha256 text, p_unrelated_inventory_sha256 text, p_provider_response_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, `mark_storefront_edge_control_rule_ambiguous(p_operation_id uuid, p_observation_started_at timestamptz, p_observation_ended_at timestamptz, p_live_ruleset_version text, p_live_ruleset_sha256 text, p_unrelated_inventory_sha256 text, p_provider_read_receipt_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, and read-only `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_control_rule_operation(p_operation_id uuid, p_challenge_sha256 text)`. Preparation derives the complete provider request from the exact current full ruleset and fixed kind. No caller supplies a rule body, phase, zone, host, rule ID, position, action, nonce, version target, or arbitrary deadline.
- Control-rule transitions are exactly `prepared -> provider_attempted -> confirmed` and `provider_attempted -> ambiguous`. `confirm` also accepts `ambiguous -> confirmed` only when a later complete provider read is byte-identical to that operation's expected full ruleset/rule/unrelated-inventory tuple; it never issues another mutation. After the qualified visibility interval, a prior or conflicting live ruleset closes `ambiguous`, requires route absence, and marks the projection `cleanup_pending`; activation operations cannot be retried or replaced. Recovery may prepare only the corresponding removal operation after two complete provider reads, separated by the qualified consistency interval, agree on the same current ruleset/version and no unrelated drift. Removal uses that exact version as its precondition and targets the sealed pre-enrollment inventory; if an earlier mutation later appears, the guardian detects the fingerprint immediately, keeps the route absent, and repeats read-only reconciliation before another removal. There is no abandonment, force-confirm, caller-selected target, or transition back to `prepared`. Tests interrupt before/after every database/provider boundary and cover exact expected, exact prior, delayed exact, conflicting, duplicate-rule, changed-unrelated-inventory, and late-appearance cases for all four kinds.
- `record_storefront_edge_route_safety_event(p_route_pattern text, p_expected_worker_name text, p_route_state text, p_route_inventory_before_sha256 text, p_route_inventory_after_sha256 text, p_prior_origin_probe_sha256 text, p_provider_operation_id text, p_provider_observed_at timestamptz, p_provider_receipt_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` accepts only fixed action/subject `record_edge_route_safety` / `ogabassey.com:route-safety:v1`, exact route state `absent`, the sealed apex tuple, unchanged sibling inventory, and a qualified prior-origin proof. It is idempotent for identical evidence and transactionally marks any non-absent control rule `cleanup_pending`; a conflicting replay fails. The route-mutating provider job never receives this HMAC. Instead it emits one maximum-16-KiB canonical redacted receipt artifact, and the protected `report-ogabassey-edge-route-safety.yml` HMAC-only job verifies repository/run/tag/identity/artifact hashes before calling the RPC. If reporting fails, every later guardian tick that re-proves the route absent reproduces the same canonical receipt and retries the reporter; terminal rollback/cutover closure remains pending until the database receipt exists. Thus provider route safety is immediate, while audit-state convergence is durable and retryable without co-locating credentials.
- Database epoch invalidation cannot selectively revoke an issued R2 temporary credential. Such a credential remains usable until its `notAfter` unless its parent token is revoked; Cloudflare documents that revoking the parent immediately invalidates every credential derived from it. Parent revocation is therefore an emergency family-wide kill switch with explicit publisher/GC availability blast radius, not the normal per-session fence. The normal correctness boundary remains trusted-process epoch enforcement plus exact CAS/read-back; tests cover expiry, database invalidation without provider revocation, and emergency parent revocation invalidating all live derived sessions.
- `verify_storefront_edge_cutover_hold(p_hold_id uuid, p_begin_receipt_sha256 text, p_challenge_sha256 text)` is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, side-effect-free, executable only by `anon` and `authenticated`, and accepts no merchant, hostname, release, or generation selector. Possession of both the random hold UUID and its 256-bit begin-receipt hash is the capability; `p_challenge_sha256` is a fresh caller-generated 256-bit lowercase-hex challenge, not a credential. It performs a constant-shape lookup and returns `HoldVerificationReceipt = { status: 'live' | 'expired' | 'invalidated' | 'completed' | 'cancelled'; holdId; releaseId; generationDecimal; pointerEtag; manifestSha256; ownerApprovalId; databaseOpenedAt; databaseExpiresAt; databaseNow; beginReceiptSha256; challengeSha256; terminalReceiptSha256: string | null; verificationReceiptSha256 }` only for the exact capability pair and exact challenge; every unknown, mismatched, malformed, or cross-hold pair returns one bounded indistinguishable denial without tuple data. Revoke it from `PUBLIC` and `service_role`; the workflow uses only the explicitly governed non-secret Supabase public configuration described in Task 12, never a user session or privileged secret. Tests prove it cannot mutate or enumerate, expiry equality is non-live, lifecycle invalidation is visible immediately, terminal states cannot be mistaken for live, a receipt from one hold cannot verify another, a response cannot satisfy a different challenge or rollout boundary, duplicate challenges are rejected by workflow state before RPC, and replay of an earlier live response fails the current challenge.
- `verificationReceiptSha256` is SHA-256, lowercase hexadecimal, over one byte-exact preimage shared by PostgreSQL, Node, and the browser. The preimage begins with the ASCII domain separator `baci-storefront-edge-cutover-hold-verification-v1\n`, then encodes exactly these fields in this order: `status`, `holdId`, `releaseId`, `generationDecimal`, `pointerEtag`, `manifestSha256`, `ownerApprovalId`, `databaseOpenedAt`, `databaseExpiresAt`, `databaseNow`, `beginReceiptSha256`, `challengeSha256`, `terminalReceiptSha256`. Every non-null value is framed as `<UTF8-byte-length>:<exact-UTF8-bytes>\n`; the sole nullable field is encoded as the three ASCII bytes `-1:\n`. UUIDs and hashes are lowercase, generation is canonical positive decimal, and all other scalar values are schema-bounded ASCII. PostgreSQL renders each `timestamptz` exactly once as UTC RFC 3339 milliseconds `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.SSSZ`, returns that exact string, and hashes those returned bytes; consumers never reformat timestamps. No JSON serialization, locale formatting, Unicode normalization, platform newline, or implicit null coercion participates. `packages/shared/src/storefront/edge-cutover-receipt.ts` is the TypeScript implementation; the migration implements the identical SQL function. The checked-in JSON fixture contains live and each terminal status, ASCII boundary values, multibyte rejection, null/non-null terminal receipt, and timestamp boundaries with literal preimage bytes represented as Base64 plus expected hash. SQL tests and the shared TypeScript/GitHub/browser tests must all consume that one fixture and fail on a one-byte field-order, delimiter, timestamp, length, or null-encoding difference.
- `record_storefront_preview_acceptance(p_hold_id uuid, p_begin_receipt_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text, p_worker_version_id uuid, p_preview_route_fingerprint text, p_acceptance_challenge_sha256 text, p_capability_registration_sha256 text)` is authenticated-owner-only and is the sole constructor of `StorefrontPreviewAcceptance`; no caller supplies `acceptedAt`, `expiresAt`, a `HoldVerificationReceipt`, a preview-acceptance hash, release/generation/pointer/manifest fields, observation-window fields, or capability-registration contents. Under one transaction and hold-row lock it derives the Ogabassey owner from `auth.uid()`, requires the exact live nonterminal hold/begin-receipt/approval tuple, loads the immutable capability-registration row by its hash, requires its nested capability receipt to match that hold/rollout/tuple, requires the fresh acceptance challenge to differ from the capability challenge, and validates the bounded worker-version UUID plus exact sealed preview-route fingerprint. PostgreSQL sets one `databaseNow = transaction_timestamp()`, requires `observationNotBefore <= databaseNow < observationNotAfter = hold.databaseExpiresAt`, constructs the complete live `HoldVerificationReceipt` with `databaseNow` as both its database time and the acceptance's `acceptedAt`, embeds the complete stored capability registration, constructs the complete `StorefrontPreviewAcceptance`, serializes/hashes both with the shared SQL authorities, and atomically stores and returns the exact canonical object plus its hash. Byte-identical replay of the same closed scalar inputs returns the stored object; a second challenge/acceptance, stale capability registration, tuple/version/route/approval drift, deadline equality, lifecycle invalidation, or concurrent terminal transition fails before storage. The RPC cannot complete/cancel the hold or mutate publication/provider state, and every later stage/proof operation binds this stored database-constructed acceptance rather than workflow memory or caller-authored receipt fields.
- The migration also produces a single-use, rollout-bound proof-registration capability that is separate from the publisher HMAC: private owner-only `initialize_storefront_cutover_proof_secret(p_hmac_base64 text, p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text)`, private owner-only `revoke_storefront_cutover_proof_secret(p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text)`, HMAC-authenticated `register_storefront_edge_cutover_proof(p_hold_id uuid, p_begin_receipt_sha256 text, p_proof_file_sha256 text, p_initial_production_proof_sha256 text, p_initial_production_proof jsonb, p_preview_acceptance_sha256 text, p_capability_registration_sha256 text, p_pre_attach_receipt_sha256 text, p_pre_mixed_receipt_sha256 text, p_post_probe_receipt_sha256 text, p_staged_deployment_id text, p_staged_deployment_created_at timestamptz, p_staged_deployment_receipt_sha256 text, p_candidate_artifact_readback_sha256 text, p_candidate_observation_sha256 text, p_temporary_deployment_id text, p_temporary_deployment_created_at timestamptz, p_route_fingerprint text, p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, and authenticated-owner read-only `get_storefront_edge_cutover_proof_registration(p_hold_id uuid)`. `p_initial_production_proof` is the already bounded/strictly parsed redacted proof object; the final `p_proof` is only the HMAC envelope and can never substitute for it. The initializer/revoker are `SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''`, executable only by direct database-owner `postgres`, and use a dedicated private singleton secret plus operation ledger; the registration RPC is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, executable only by `anon`, and verifies fixed action `register_cutover_proof`, subject `holdId`, fixed ordered payload of the raw proof-file hash, canonical proof hash, nested preview-acceptance hash, nested capability-registration hash, three production hold-receipt hashes, staged deployment ID/provider time/receipt, candidate artifact read-back hash, candidate-observation hash, temporary deployment ID/provider creation time, route fingerprint, and rollout identity, a fresh single-use nonce, database time within a five-minute validity window, the exact live hold capability/tuple, and the active secret's bound rollout identity before storing one immutable registration. Before ledger access, SQL requires an exact object/string/null-only proof shape, `octet_length(p_initial_production_proof::text) <= 131072`, closed root/nested key sets, and the same scalar bounds as the shared schema; it independently serializes the capability-registration receipt and its nested hold receipt, preview acceptance and its nested acceptance hold receipt, the three production hold-receipt cores, candidate-observation core, and outer proof core and recomputes every nested/canonical hash. It requires the embedded capability registration to equal the immutable database registration row and the embedded preview acceptance to equal the immutable owner-acceptance evidence for the same hold/tuple/rollout. The staged and temporary deployment fields must equal both the parsed strict proof and contiguous database operation chain; their creation times cannot precede the hold, must be ordered, and cannot exceed database time by the bounded provider-clock skew; the route fingerprint must equal the sealed apex route. The proof's complete `CandidateObservationReceipt` must recompute to `p_candidate_observation_sha256`, equal the immutable observation row attached to the confirmed temporary deployment operation, and bind the same rollout, operation, deployment, candidate version, embedded preview acceptance, database-registered capability-artifact hash, exact admission window, locked override guard, and raw evidence artifact. It accepts idempotent replay only for byte-identical raw-file/proof/preview/capability/receipt/stage/artifact-readback/observation/admission/temporary evidence, consumes the nonce, and atomically disables further registration for that hold. `service_role`, `authenticated`, and `PUBLIC` receive no registration execute grant. Revocation is mandatory after successful registration or any abort/expiry and prevents new registration without deleting the immutable non-secret registration row. A matching immutable registration remains valid after revocation; completion requires that registration plus the matching `revoked` operation receipt and never revalidates an active HMAC. The secret is never the publisher HMAC, never authorizes a release/pointer/provider operation, and is present only in the protected GitHub production environment and the private database row for the bounded pilot window.
- `private.get_storefront_cutover_proof_secret_operation(p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_owner_approval_id text)` is the exact database-owner-only status function used by `--cutover-proof-status`; initialize, status, and revoke return the same bounded non-secret shape `{ version: 1; rolloutIdentitySha256; approvalId; state: 'active' | 'revoked'; initializedAt; revokedAt: string | null; receiptSha256 }`. Its receipt preimage begins with `baci-storefront-cutover-proof-secret-operation-v1\n`, then length-frames `rolloutIdentitySha256`, `approvalId`, `state`, `initializedAt`, and nullable `revokedAt` with the plan's standard `<UTF8-byte-length>:<exact-UTF8-bytes>\n` and `-1:\n` encodings. PostgreSQL returns and hashes the exact UTC RFC 3339 millisecond strings. Same-operation recovery is idempotent, conflicting identity/approval fails closed, and no status/receipt includes the HMAC or its hash.
- Proof registration additionally parses the strict proof and requires its staged deployment ID/time/receipt, candidate artifact read-back hash, complete candidate-observation receipt/hash, temporary deployment ID/time, version IDs, weights, route fingerprint, and rollout identity to form one contiguous confirmed `stage_candidate -> temporary_proof` chain in the deployment ledger. The stage must be the exact origin-100/edge-0 predecessor; the candidate version-detail receipt must prove that exact version's module/settings/bundle hash equals the owner-accepted preview bundle; and the temporary proof must be the exact 99/1 successor whose stored candidate-observation receipt proves the exact candidate Version Metadata ID through the bounded observation route. `probeSetSha256` must equal that receipt's `rawEvidenceArtifactSha256`; no second independently supplied probe hash exists. A provider deployment that merely matches the proof JSON or annotation but lacks both confirmed at-most-once operations and the independently sealed artifact and observation identities cannot register or complete the hold.
- `get_storefront_edge_cutover_proof_registration(p_hold_id uuid)` returns only to the authenticated Ogabassey owner the immutable registration fields plus `cutoverSecretOperation` in the exact non-secret shape above. It derives the owner and rollout/approval join from the locked hold and accepts no caller-selected rollout identity or approval ID. Before revocation it returns `state: 'active'` for display but completion remains disabled; after revocation it returns the matching immutable `revoked` receipt consumed by the route and completion RPC. A missing/mismatched operation returns one bounded failure rather than a partial registration.
- `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_guard_state(p_challenge_sha256 text)` is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, executable only by `anon` and `authenticated`, revoked from `PUBLIC` and `service_role`, accepts no merchant/host/hold/deployment selector, and reads only the single verified Ogabassey profile. It returns the closed union `StorefrontEdgeGuardState = InactiveGuardState | LiveUnregisteredGuardState | LiveWorkflowGuardState | RegisteredGuardState | CompletedGuardState`. Every member includes `affinityRuleState: 'absent' | 'active' | 'cleanup_pending'` and `affinityRuleSha256: string | null`; `absent` requires null, while `active | cleanup_pending` require the exact last read-back rule fingerprint. `InactiveGuardState = { status: 'inactive'; affinityRuleState; affinityRuleSha256; databaseNow; challengeSha256; receiptSha256 }`. `LiveUnregisteredGuardState = { status: 'live_unregistered'; holdId; databaseExpiresAt; affinityRuleState; affinityRuleSha256; databaseNow; challengeSha256; receiptSha256 }`. `LiveWorkflowGuardState = { status: 'live_workflow'; holdId; databaseExpiresAt; routeFingerprint; currentDeployment: EdgeDeploymentHead | null; pendingOperation: EdgeDeploymentOperationSummary; pointerRollback: EdgePointerRollbackSummary | null; affinityRuleState; affinityRuleSha256; databaseNow; challengeSha256; receiptSha256 }` exists only while the exact live hold and rollout-bound `bootstrap_origin | stage_candidate | temporary_proof | rollback` operation ledger authorize the pre-proof route/deployment window. `RegisteredGuardState = { status: 'live_registered' | 'expired_registered' | 'cancelled_registered' | 'invalidated_registered'; holdId; temporaryDeploymentId; temporaryDeploymentCreatedAt; originOnlyWorkerVersionId; edgeWorkerVersionId; databaseExpiresAt; routeFingerprint; initialProductionProofSha256; terminalAt: string | null; terminalReceiptSha256: string | null; pointerRollback: EdgePointerRollbackSummary | null; affinityRuleState; affinityRuleSha256; databaseNow; challengeSha256; receiptSha256 }`. `CompletedGuardState = { status: 'completed_registered'; holdId; temporaryDeploymentId; temporaryDeploymentCreatedAt; originOnlyWorkerVersionId; edgeWorkerVersionId; databaseExpiresAt; routeFingerprint; initialProductionProofSha256; terminalAt: string; terminalReceiptSha256: string; activeGeneration: EdgeDeploymentGeneration | null; currentDeployment: EdgeDeploymentHead | null; pendingOperation: EdgeDeploymentOperationSummary | null; pointerRollback: EdgePointerRollbackSummary | null; deploymentLedgerReceiptSha256: string | null; affinityRuleState; affinityRuleSha256; databaseNow; challengeSha256; receiptSha256 }`. Before initial confirmation, completed state has null generation/head only while the exact registered temporary deployment remains deadline-bounded; afterward its ledger fields satisfy locked foreign-key/state invariants. `live_registered` requires terminal fields null; every terminal status requires both non-null and bound to the matching database transition. The receipt hashes a byte-exact `baci-storefront-edge-guard-state-v2\n` length-framed preimage over the discriminant and every present scalar/projection field in defined order, including affinity state/fingerprint; v1 vectors are rejection fixtures only. Unknown profile state, malformed challenge, impossible hold/registration/terminal/ledger/rollback/affinity combination, or multiple current holds returns one bounded denial. The immutable registration, terminal hold, generations, heads, operations, rollback jobs, and receipts remain for the lifetime of enrollment and cannot become `inactive` while the apex route or an enrolled deployment exists. Archival first removes and reads back the route, Transform Rule, versions, and enrollment. The guardian treats `live_unregistered`, every failed terminal state, wrong deployment evidence, cleanup-pending with an active route, or an unreadable/invalid receipt as exact-route-removal authority only; a pending pointer rollback never delays route removal.
- The preceding inline v2 guard shape is retained only to identify the rejected predecessor and must not be implemented. Every final guard-state member additionally carries `affinityRuleId`, `affinityRulesetVersion`, `affinityRuleConfirmationReceiptSha256`, `overrideGuardState: 'absent' | 'active' | 'cleanup_pending'`, `overrideGuardRuleId`, `overrideGuardRuleSha256`, `overrideGuardRulesetVersion`, and `overrideGuardConfirmationReceiptSha256`, with live fields null only when absent and immutable last-removal evidence retained separately. These fields participate in the authoritative `baci-storefront-edge-guard-state-v3` receipt; v1/v2 vectors are rejection fixtures. An active override guard is valid only for the exact live-hold bootstrap interval immediately preceding stage or an active staged/canary/rotation lifecycle. An active guard outside those states, either cleanup-pending rule with a route, or any projection/ledger mismatch is invalid guard state and route-removal authority.
- The exact redacted projections are `EdgeDeploymentGeneration = { generationDecimal: string; state: 'canary' | 'active' | 'retired' | 'aborted'; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; stableWorkerVersionId: string; candidateWorkerVersionId: string | null; originOnlyWorkerVersionId: string | null; predecessorGenerationDecimal: string | null; receiptSha256: string }`, `EdgeDeploymentHead = { generationDecimal: string; deploymentId: string; providerCreatedAt: string; percentage: 0 | 1 | 5 | 25 | 50 | 100; versions: Array<{ versionId: string; percentageDecimal: string }>; workersMessage: string; operationId: string; receiptSha256: string }`, `EdgeDeploymentOperationSummary = { operationId: string; kind: 'bootstrap_origin' | 'stage_candidate' | 'temporary_proof' | 'initial_confirmation' | 'promote' | 'rotate' | 'rollback'; state: 'prepared' | 'provider_attempted' | 'confirmed' | 'ambiguous'; priorDeploymentId: string | null; targetPercentage: 0 | 1 | 5 | 25 | 50 | 100; expectedVersions: Array<{ versionId: string; percentageDecimal: string }>; workersMessage: string; requestSha256: string; attemptedAt: string | null; recoveryDeadline: string | null; candidateArtifactReadbackSha256: string | null; candidateObservationSha256: string | null; receiptSha256: string }`, `EdgeControlRuleSummary = { operationId: string; kind: 'activate_affinity' | 'remove_affinity' | 'lock_override_guard' | 'remove_override_guard'; state: 'prepared' | 'provider_attempted' | 'confirmed' | 'ambiguous'; priorRulesetVersion: string; priorRulesetSha256: string; expectedRulesetSha256: string; ruleId: string | null; ruleSha256: string; unrelatedInventorySha256: string; requestSha256: string; attemptedAt: string | null; recoveryDeadline: string | null; receiptSha256: string }`, and `EdgePointerRollbackSummary = { rollbackId: string; state: 'requested' | 'claimed' | 'pointer_committed' | 'completed' | 'retryable_failure' | 'terminal_drift' | 'superseded'; expectedPointerEtag: string; expectedReleaseId: string; reasonCode: 'operator_abort' | 'evidence_drift' | 'worker_health' | 'cost_guard'; approvalId: string; publicationEpochDecimal: string; committedOriginPointerEtag: string | null; requestedAt: string; nextAttemptAt: string | null; completedAt: string | null; terminalAt: string | null; terminalReason: string | null; routeSafetyReceiptSha256: string | null; receiptSha256: string }`. Arrays use the exact provider request order stored at preparation; consumers never sort them independently. Percentage decimals use the canonical no-exponent grammar `^(0|100|[1-9][0-9]?)(\\.[0-9]{1,4})?$`, sum exactly to `100.0000` after guarded fixed-scale conversion, and reject binary-float serialization. Zero is permitted only as the candidate weight/head percentage for a qualified `stage_candidate`; gate registration rejects zero.
- The `EdgePointerRollbackSummary` owner-only shape in the preceding compact list is superseded by this authoritative discriminated union over its shared state/pointer/epoch/time/receipt fields: `{ initiator: 'owner'; reasonCode: 'operator_abort' | 'evidence_drift' | 'worker_health' | 'cost_guard'; ownerApprovalId: string; overdueGenerationDecimal: null; freshnessDeadline: null; freshnessReceiptSha256: null } | { initiator: 'freshness_fallback'; reasonCode: 'freshness_deadline'; ownerApprovalId: null; overdueGenerationDecimal: string; freshnessDeadline: string; freshnessReceiptSha256: string }`. The fallback branch's generation uses the shared branded schema, cannot be submitted through the owner RPC, and the status UI renders only the safe public state `stale_origin` rather than fabricating owner attribution. Shared serializers, SQL text aliases, guard receipts, status fixtures, and max-bigint tests cover both branches and reject cross-branch/nullability drift.
- Rollback transitions are exactly `requested -> claimed -> pointer_committed -> completed` and `requested | claimed -> retryable_failure -> claimed`, with database-derived bounded retry count/backoff. A nonmatching ETag/body after the one permitted precondition-failure reconciliation closes `claimed -> terminal_drift`; this releases admission/claim, preserves immutable provider evidence, requires route absence, marks both control rules `cleanup_pending`, and blocks re-enrollment. `requested | claimed | pointer_committed -> superseded` is allowed only when a higher-priority lifecycle transition supplies its matching generation/receipt and proves no later origin-pointer write; it also releases the claim while retaining evidence. `finish_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_job` accepts only these closed terminal outcomes and their state-required receipts. Tests cover every permitted/forbidden transition, bounded retry exhaustion, late worker completion, epoch races, lifecycle supersession before/after CAS, terminal release, and duplicate finish.
- The migration also creates a deployment-operation HMAC boundary distinct from publisher and cutover-proof secrets: owner-only `private.initialize_storefront_edge_deployment_secret`, `private.rotate_storefront_edge_deployment_secret`, `private.remove_storefront_edge_deployment_previous_secret`, and `private.get_storefront_edge_deployment_secret_operation`; proof-only `verify_storefront_edge_deployment_secret_rotation_proof`; HMAC-gated `register_storefront_edge_deployment_gate`, `prepare_storefront_edge_deployment_operation`, `mark_storefront_edge_deployment_attempted`, `mark_storefront_edge_deployment_ambiguous`, and `confirm_storefront_edge_deployment_operation`; and read-only `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_deployment_operation`. There is no ambiguous-operation abandonment function or grant. Each private function is `SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''` and direct-`postgres` only. Each HMAC function is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, executable only by `anon`, revoked from `PUBLIC`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`, verifies a five-minute nonce proof for one fixed action and operation/generation subject, and accepts no merchant or hostname selector. The proof-only function is hard-bound to action `verify_edge_deployment_secret_rotation`, subject `ogabassey.com:edge-deployment-secret:v1`, and empty payload, consumes one nonce, reads no deployment/release row, and returns only `{ matchedSlot: 'current' | 'previous'; databaseNow; previousPresent; previousExpiresAt; currentOperationReceiptSha256; previousRemovalReceiptSha256 }`. The secret is stored only in the private singleton and protected GitHub deployment environment, never on the VPS runtime, in application code, or in a provider/route-mutating guardian job. It may be exposed only to the distinct protected HMAC ledger jobs, including the route-safety reporter, which receive no Cloudflare credential.
- `register_storefront_edge_deployment_gate(p_current_deployment_id uuid, p_window_started_at timestamptz, p_window_ended_at timestamptz, p_measurement_artifact_sha256 text, p_evidence_fingerprint_sha256 text, p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` locks the current confirmed database head, requires exact deployment/rollout/configuration identity, provider-derived UTC-millisecond boundaries with `window_ended_at - window_started_at >= interval '24 hours'`, database time at or after the end, an allowed current percentage, no existing conflicting receipt, and a fresh HMAC proof before inserting one immutable canonical gate receipt. It cannot choose a percentage, version, next deployment, or provider target. Same-evidence replay returns the existing receipt; every conflicting replay fails.
- `prepare_storefront_edge_deployment_operation(p_kind text, p_candidate_worker_version_id uuid, p_candidate_bundle_sha256 text, p_candidate_artifact_readback_sha256 text, p_rollout_identity_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` accepts only `bootstrap_origin | stage_candidate | temporary_proof | initial_confirmation | promote | rotate | rollback`. It locks the enrolled profile/current generation/head and derives every provider mutation field. `bootstrap_origin` requires the exact live hold, pre-attach receipt, reviewed origin version, no apex route, and derives origin-only at 100%. `stage_candidate` has two closed predecessors: during initial cutover it requires the confirmed bootstrap head, attached exact route, origin-equivalence proof, reviewed edge candidate, the owner-accepted preview bundle, and the still-current Gate-B `zeroWeightDeploymentSupported` contract, deriving origin-at-100/edge-at-0; during steady-state rotation it requires the active old-stable-at-100 head, new protected rollout identity, recreated preview acceptance, reviewed candidate version/bundle proof, and the same live zero-weight contract, deriving old-stable-at-100/new-candidate-at-0 in the new generation. For `stage_candidate`, all three candidate arguments are non-null and the version-detail receipt must bind the exact candidate ID's module/settings hash to both `p_candidate_bundle_sha256` and the accepted preview bundle. Candidate-free operations require all three null; later operations inherit the staged candidate/artifact tuple from the locked head and reject caller replacement. `temporary_proof` requires the initial confirmed stage-candidate head, that immutable candidate artifact read-back receipt, an active locked override guard, attached exact route, and the pre-mixed receipt, then derives origin-at-99/edge-at-1; confirmation later requires the exact canonical observation receipt, not a boolean or unbound probe hash. All three initial operations remain bounded by the hold and make `live_workflow` guardian state authoritative before proof registration. `initial_confirmation` requires the exact `completed_registered` temporary proof and derives a fresh 1% deployment; `promote` requires the immutable gate receipt for the current nonzero head and derives the sole next member of `1 -> 5 -> 25 -> 50 -> 100`; it rejects a supplied candidate. `rotate` requires the confirmed rotation `stage_candidate` head, the exact inherited candidate artifact read-back receipt, and the active locked guard, then derives old-stable-at-99/new-candidate-at-1 and likewise cannot confirm without the exact canonical observation receipt. `rollback` derives only the immediately retained safe head allowed by the current generation/operation state and never accepts a caller-selected version. It returns `{ operationId; kind; generationDecimal; priorDeploymentId; targetPercentage; versions; workersMessage; requestSha256; candidateArtifactReadbackSha256; preparedAt; receiptSha256 }`. No caller supplies a target percentage, weight tuple, annotation, predecessor, rollback target, or operation ID.
- `claim_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_job(p_proof jsonb)` validates fixed action/subject `claim_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_job` / `ogabassey.com:edge-pointer-rollback-queue:v1` and empty payload before reading the ledger, requires a live `edge_pointer_rollback` admission, and returns at most one fenced `{ rollbackId; claimToken; publicationEpochDecimal; expectedPointerEtag; expectedReleaseId; reasonCode; ownerApprovalId; requestedAt }`. It accepts no selector. The worker constructs the canonical `mode: 'origin'` pointer from the locked database release tuple, preserving the exact release ID and manifest hash as rollback metadata, obtains an exact-key `PutObject` temporary session through the existing parent-only signer, revalidates rollback/epoch/admission/session state immediately before the provider call, and sends one conditional R2 write with `If-Match: expectedPointerEtag`. `record_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_commit(p_rollback_id uuid, p_claim_token uuid, p_publication_epoch bigint, p_previous_pointer_etag text, p_origin_pointer_etag text, p_origin_pointer_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` verifies the exact canonical bytes/hash and provider ETag before recording `pointer_committed`. `finish_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback_job(p_rollback_id uuid, p_claim_token uuid, p_publication_epoch bigint, p_outcome text, p_retry_after_seconds integer, p_error_code text, p_terminal_reason text, p_lifecycle_receipt_sha256 text, p_route_safety_receipt_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` accepts only the closed outcomes `completed | retryable_failure | terminal_drift | superseded` and never changes a deployment or route. `completed` requires `pointer_committed`, the exact committed ETag/read-back, and null retry/terminal fields; `retryable_failure` requires `requested | claimed`, bounded database backoff, and null terminal fields; `terminal_drift` requires an exact authenticated nonmatching provider read, a route-safety receipt, and affinity plus override cleanup pending; `superseded` requires a higher lifecycle generation/receipt, route-safety evidence, and proof that no later origin write occurred. Replays are idempotent only when every outcome and evidence field is byte-identical. A precondition failure reads the pointer: exact already-committed origin bytes reconcile success, while any other ETag/body is terminal drift requiring route removal and owner review. The dedicated VPS processor polls every five seconds, claims no other work, and its measured database/session/R2/read-back path must complete within 30 seconds p99 so the 60-second public rollback SLO retains 2x headroom. Lifecycle `gone | unpublished` and every accepted deletion phase exclude new rollback admission entirely and cannot be overwritten by an origin rollback. A rollback superseded by newly accepted deletion loses CAS authority through the epoch change and may perform read-back only under the classifier contract.
- The exact deployment annotation is `baci-ogabassey-edgeop-v1:<operationId>:<generationDecimal>:<targetPercentage>:<priorDeploymentIdOrNone>:<rolloutIdentitySha256>:<terminalReceiptSha256OrNone>:<initialProductionProofSha256OrNone>` using lowercase UUID/hash values, literal ASCII `none` only for an unavailable bootstrap predecessor/terminal/proof field, and canonical positive decimal generation/percentage, with no whitespace or escaping; preparation rejects it if the UTF-8 length would exceed Cloudflare's qualified annotation ceiling. `requestSha256` hashes the literal UTF-8 provider request body generated by the shared serializer with exact key order `strategy`, `versions`, `annotations`; `strategy` is `percentage`; each version object orders `version_id` then numeric `percentage`; annotations order `workers/message` then `workers/triggered_by`; and `workers/triggered_by` is exactly `deployment`. The serializer emits no insignificant whitespace, trailing newline, exponent notation, or unknown key. The provider job sends those exact bytes with `Content-Type: application/json` and verifies the response against the prepared scalar fields rather than reserializing it as authority.
- `mark_storefront_edge_deployment_attempted(p_operation_id uuid, p_request_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` atomically changes only the matching `prepared` row to `provider_attempted` and records database attempt time before the provider POST. The workflow may send exactly one POST only after reading back that receipt. A crash before the transition permits retrying the transition; a crash after the transition, including one occurring before the POST, never permits another POST. Recovery performs read/list only: one exact provider deployment is confirmed and more than one or any conflicting current deployment removes the route. After the provider-qualified visibility interval, zero exact matches plus proof that the prior head remains current is recorded through `mark_storefront_edge_deployment_ambiguous(p_operation_id uuid, p_observation_started_at timestamptz, p_observation_ended_at timestamptz, p_current_deployment_id uuid, p_provider_read_receipt_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)`, which accepts only `provider_attempted`, validates the complete interval/current-head/read receipt, and changes it atomically to terminal `ambiguous`. Neither provider audit-log absence nor direct database-owner approval can prove that an accepted request will never surface; therefore no RPC, manager mode, workflow, or runbook may abandon, replace, or create another deployment operation for the same script identity/generation after ambiguity. Recovery may only confirm an exact late match. Otherwise it removes the apex route, retires that script identity from enrollment, and requires a newly named script plus fresh Gate-B binding, preview acceptance, hold, deployment-secret approval, and generation. There is no transition from `provider_attempted | ambiguous | confirmed` back to `prepared`.
- `confirm_storefront_edge_deployment_operation(p_operation_id uuid, p_provider_deployment_id uuid, p_provider_created_at timestamptz, p_versions jsonb, p_workers_message text, p_provider_response_sha256 text, p_candidate_observation jsonb, p_candidate_observation_sha256 text, p_proof jsonb)` accepts only the exact attempted operation, validates every provider field against its database-derived expectation and qualified clock/schema bounds, inserts the new confirmed chain head, and atomically marks the operation `confirmed`. `temporary_proof | rotate` require both non-null candidate-observation arguments; every other operation requires both null. The governed caller rejects receipt bytes above 16 KiB before the RPC; SQL also requires `octet_length(p_candidate_observation::text) <= 16384`, an exact top-level object with the closed scalar key set, no array/nested/number/boolean/unknown value, and bounded ASCII strings before field validation. The function independently serializes the fixed scalar core, recomputes its hash, requires the HMAC proof payload to bind that hash, and atomically inserts the receipt row under the same operation/deployment transaction. It never treats the caller-supplied hash or provider annotation as evidence by itself. It is idempotent only for identical provider and observation evidence. A completed 100% initial generation becomes `active`; a completed 100% rotation retires the predecessor generation and makes its candidate the new stable version while retaining the previous stable version and receipts for the approved rollback window. `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_guard_state` therefore extends every `completed_registered` response with canonical `activeGeneration`, `currentDeployment`, `pendingOperation`, and `deploymentLedgerReceiptSha256`; the guardian validates only the provider's current deployment against this database head/pending state and never reconstructs authority from provider history. Shared SQL/TypeScript/Node golden vectors cover every operation transition, exact annotation/request bytes, null/required/forbidden candidate observation, 0/16,384/16,385-byte boundaries, unknown/nested/non-string values, rotation generation, replay, and timestamp boundary.
- The exact compact type is `CandidateObservationReceipt = { schemaVersion: '1'; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; operationId: string; generationDecimal: string; deploymentId: string; deploymentCreatedAt: string; stableWorkerVersionId: string; candidateWorkerVersionId: string; observationPath: '/__baci/edge-observation'; observationChallengeSha256: string; observationCapabilityArtifactSha256: string; observationNotBefore: string; observationNotAfter: string; attemptCountDecimal: string; nonCandidateResponseCountDecimal: string; candidateObservationCountDecimal: '1'; candidateAttemptOrdinalDecimal: string; requestSetSha256: string; responseSetSha256: string; candidateResponseSha256: string; rawEvidenceArtifactSha256: string; hostileProbeSetSha256: string; guardProofMode: 'complete-worker-log' | 'unsampled-terminal-waf'; guardEvidenceSourceSha256: string; overrideGuardOperationReceiptSha256: string; observationStartedAt: string; observationEndedAt: string; canonicalSha256: string }`. String schema version preserves the proof parser's object/string/null-only contract. Attempt and ordinal fields use `^[1-9][0-9]{0,3}$`; non-candidate response count uses `^(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,3})$`. Guarded conversion requires attempts from 1 through 2,000, candidate count exactly one, non-candidate response count plus one equal attempts, and candidate ordinal equal attempts, proving the sequential observer stopped at the first candidate without claiming that every prior response exposed or identified the stable version. Prior responses must be the bounded inactive-admission `404` emitted by the origin/current-stable version; no marker from such a response can satisfy candidate success. `guardProofMode` and `guardEvidenceSourceSha256` must equal the selected Gate-B contract and the exact read-back source fingerprint; `complete-worker-log` requires the complete invocation-absence evidence, while `unsampled-terminal-waf` requires complete terminal-action count equality and forbids a Worker-log-absence assertion. Times are credentialless-observer UTC milliseconds, ordered no earlier than deployment creation and no later than database confirmation time plus the qualified clock-skew bound. The observation challenge, capability-artifact hash, `notBefore`, and `notAfter` must equal the complete embedded preview acceptance, active candidate version settings, and immutable database capability-registration receipt; the observation interval must remain inside that exact half-open window. Every ID/hash/path/version/mode/source must equal the locked operation, confirmed provider read-back, active override-guard receipt, and staged candidate tuple. The receipt core uses ASCII separator `baci-storefront-edge-candidate-observation-v1\n` followed by the listed fields in order through `observationEndedAt`, with the existing length framing; `canonicalSha256` is excluded. SQL, shared TypeScript, and governed Node ESM implementations consume one golden-vector file containing both guard modes, success at attempts 1 and 2,000, candidate at the final ordinal, multiple/early candidate rejection, sampled-source rejection, capability-registration/artifact/admission/challenge/window/half-open-boundary drift, mode/source/count/timestamp/path/deployment/version/guard drift, one-byte response drift, replay, and every null/forbidden case.
- `CandidateObservationEvidence` is the bounded combined artifact behind `rawEvidenceArtifactSha256`, never an RPC JSON argument or browser input. It is at most 4 MiB and contains the exact validated capability-artifact hash, admission window, and candidate version-setting receipt; the selected Gate-B `guardProofMode: 'complete-worker-log' | 'unsampled-terminal-waf'` plus its qualified source fingerprint; one ordered entry per attempt with ordinal, lowercase-hex hash of the unique valid affinity cookie rather than its value, exact request URL/method/header hash, response status and bounded allowlisted header map, `versionMetadataId: string | null`, response hash, and observation time; and separately ordered hostile override-probe entries, full-ruleset/guard operation receipt hashes, provider deployment read-back hash, mode-specific read-only query hashes, and artifact hash. `versionMetadataId` is null only for the exact inactive-admission `404` shape from the origin/current-stable version; it is the exact candidate UUID only for the single accepted `204`, and any other status/header/nullability combination is rejected. Unknown headers, bodies, cookies, authorization values, IP addresses, Ray IDs as unique identifiers, or unbounded provider payloads are rejected rather than preserved. The deployment-token job only creates/reads the deployment. A separate credentialless observer sends the public observation/hostile requests and uploads one bounded response artifact. A third job, isolated in the `ogabassey-edge-observation-read` environment with only the exact sources qualified for the selected mode, waits through the sealed export-lag bound and uploads one bounded redacted event artifact; it has no script/deployment/ruleset mutation permission. In `complete-worker-log`, that job requires exact WAF terminal actions and zero matching invocation records from the complete unsampled Worker export for every hostile request. In `unsampled-terminal-waf`, it requires exact equality between emitted hostile requests and complete unsampled terminal-block records grouped by the sealed rule ID, apex hostname, method, bounded observation-path instances, action, and window; it neither queries nor asserts per-request Worker-log absence, and any sampled/adaptive/incomplete source is `STOP`. The HMAC-only confirmation job downloads those two exact same-run artifacts, verifies repository/tag/rollout/job identity, size, unique names, raw hashes, all entry hashes/counts/times, the exact capability/admission/window and candidate response, locked guard, selected mode/source fingerprint, and its mode-specific terminal proof, then emits the single combined artifact, constructs the compact receipt, and submits both receipt and hash. No job receives the union of deployment, observation-read, and deployment-HMAC credentials, and no secret enters an artifact. A missing, expired, duplicate, truncated, reordered, mixed-deployment, cache-hit, mode/source mismatch, sampled terminal source, export-lag-incomplete, or unverifiable artifact cannot confirm the operation.
- `complete_storefront_edge_cutover` must lock the hold and require one immutable registration whose hold, rollout identity, outer proof hash, complete preview-acceptance/hash, complete capability-registration/hash, all five nested hold-receipt hashes, candidate-observation receipt/hash, deployment/version/guard/raw-evidence bindings, tuple, owner approval, and live/nonterminal state match the submitted completion, plus the matching cutover-secret operation in `revoked` state. It cannot complete from a caller-supplied hash alone. Registration tests prove a missing or already-revoked secret, wrong rollout, wrong hold capability, reused nonce, second registration, omitted/substituted nested receipt, or registration after expiry/invalidation fails closed. Completion tests prove arbitrary hashes, locally forged or semantically invalid receipt hashes, an absent registration, an active/not-yet-revoked secret, a mismatched revocation receipt, and a direct authenticated-owner completion without the exact registered proof fail closed, while the exact immutable registration remains completion-eligible after its secret is revoked. Registration cannot begin/complete/cancel a hold or mutate publication/provider state.
- Produces `createStorefrontPublisherRouteProof(action, subjectId, payload): StorefrontPublisherRouteProof` using the existing quiz route-proof design as the repository analogue, plus proof-only `verify_storefront_publisher_secret_rotation_proof(proof jsonb)` for the fixed action `verify_secret_rotation`, fixed subject `ogabassey.com:publisher-secret-rotation:v1`, and fixed empty payload hash. A valid proof consumes its nonce and returns exactly `{ matchedSlot: 'current' | 'previous', databaseNow: rfc3339UtcMillis, previousPresent: boolean, previousExpiresAt: rfc3339UtcMillis | null, currentOperation: { version: 1, action: 'initialize' | 'rotate', approvalId: string, completedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, previousExpiresAt: rfc3339UtcMillis | null, receiptSha256: lowercaseHex64 }, previousRemoval: { version: 1, action: 'remove', approvalId: string, expectedPreviousExpiresAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, removedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, receiptSha256: lowercaseHex64 } | null }`. Initialization has null `currentOperation.previousExpiresAt`. Rotation retains its original non-null operation expiry even after removal: while `previousPresent` is true the top-level expiry equals it; after removal the top-level expiry is null and `previousRemoval.expectedPreviousExpiresAt` equals it. `previousRemoval` is non-null only after a completed removal for the current operation; initialization and every new rotation clear prior removal metadata. Invalid input returns one byte-stable bounded denial shape before any release-ledger read, regardless of whether its signature is an expired former secret, a random unknown secret, or malformed cryptographic material that otherwise passes the outer schema.
- Produces owner-only `private.initialize_storefront_publisher_secret(p_new_hmac_base64 text, p_approval_id text)`, `private.rotate_storefront_publisher_secret(p_new_hmac_base64 text, p_approval_id text)`, `private.remove_storefront_publisher_previous_secret(p_expected_previous_expires_at timestamptz, p_approval_id text)`, and `private.get_storefront_publisher_secret_operation(p_approval_id text)`. All are `SECURITY INVOKER`, have `SET search_path = ''`, validate canonical Base64 decoding to at least 32 bytes plus the approval-ID grammar, and are executable only by the direct database-owner `postgres` role; revoke schema usage and function execute from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`. Initialize requires no current/previous value. Rotate locks the singleton row, rejects a same-current secret, moves current to previous, installs the new current, fixes `previousExpiresAt = transaction_timestamp() + interval '15 minutes'`, and clears prior removal metadata in one transaction. Remove locks the row, requires database time at or after the exact expected expiry, nulls previous/expiry, and records removal in one transaction. The private operation ledger has unique `approval_id`, action `initialize | rotate | remove`, database timestamps, nullable previous expiry/removal time, and no secret, secret hash, connection value, or auth identifier. Initialize/rotate return canonical `{ version: 1, action, approvalId, completedAt, previousExpiresAt, receiptSha256 }`; remove returns canonical `{ version: 1, action: 'remove', approvalId, expectedPreviousExpiresAt, removedAt, receiptSha256 }`. For initialize/rotate, `receiptSha256` is SHA-256 of `v1 + "\n" + action + "\n" + approvalId + "\n" + completedAt + "\n" + (previousExpiresAt ?? "")`; for remove it is SHA-256 of `v1 + "\nremove\n" + approvalId + "\n" + removedAt + "\n" + expectedPreviousExpiresAt`. PostgreSQL calculates and stores those fixed scalar cores; generic JSON serialization is never a hash authority. The singleton row binds the live current operation receipt hash and, after removal, the removal receipt hash so the proof-only RPC can return them. Same-approval replay returns the original receipt only when live state still matches that completed operation; conflicting reuse or a later superseding rotation fails closed. The status function returns only that exact non-secret receipt for connection-loss recovery.
- Produces trigger-only enqueue function `private.enqueue_storefront_release_job(uuid, text)`.
