Core schema:

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.storefront_release_jobs (
  merchant_id uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  generation bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (generation > 0),
  status text NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('pending','claimed','failed','completed','dead_letter')),
  claim_token uuid,
  claimed_generation bigint,
  claimed_at timestamptz,
  attempts integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (attempts >= 0),
  next_attempt_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  last_error text,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

`storefront_delivery_profiles` records explicit `origin | ogabassey_static_v1` delivery mode, renderer version, `publication_epoch bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (publication_epoch > 0)`, bounded last R2 mutation-session signer-run-ID/family/claim/path-scope-hash/session-hash/nullable-transfer-nonce-hash/issued-at/nullable-delivered-at/`not_after` evidence, one expiring R2 mutation-admission lease with server-generated token, code-derived class, claimed-at, and expires-at, and one owner-approved edge-cutover hold bound to exact `release_id`, generation, pointer ETag, manifest SHA-256, approval ID, opened-at, and database-derived expiry no later than two hours. SQL checks require a null transfer-nonce hash for publisher and a non-null transfer-nonce hash for GC; the signer run ID is a canonical UUID unique to that issuance; either family has null `delivered_at` immediately after issuance and may set it exactly once through the matching delivery RPC before `not_after`. They also require publisher `not_after = issued_at + interval '180 seconds'`, publisher issuance within 45 seconds of admission start, and publisher `not_after <= admission_expires_at - interval '15 seconds'`, while GC `not_after <= issued_at + interval '10 minutes'`. Admission class is exactly `lifecycle_pointer | edge_pointer_rollback | erasure_admitted_gc | release_publish | retention_gc`; callers never submit it. The database derives the highest currently eligible class under one row lock in that strict order. A lifecycle transition can first publish `gone`; an authenticated owner rollback can then restore an `origin` pointer without waiting for ordinary GC/publication; and any erasure-admitted `gone` prefix prevents new release publication or ordinary retention until erasure is drained. A live lower-priority lease may finish only inside its bounded deadline; after expiry it loses authority at every RPC. It defaults to `origin`; only the trusted Ogabassey eligibility function can select `ogabassey_static_v1`, and it does so only after owner approval plus Task 5 parity/capacity proof. No generic onboarding or plan string can select it. `begin_storefront_edge_cutover` locks the verified Ogabassey profile and current release, verifies the authenticated owner and exact active release/generation/pointer-ETag/manifest tuple, atomically applies the existing stale-lease recovery to expired release/lifecycle/rollback claims, and refuses while a lifecycle or pointer-rollback request, live `lifecycle_pointer | edge_pointer_rollback | release_publish` admission, live lifecycle/rollback/release claim, undelivered publisher session with `not_after >= transaction_timestamp()`, or any publisher session with `not_after >= transaction_timestamp()` exists. An expired claim is fenced/requeued before evaluation; an expired undelivered session remains immutable audit evidence but has no authority and does not block begin. Every issuance, delivery, worker, and pointer-CAS RPC already rejects it by database time. The authenticated owner control retries only from the returned database time; the RPC never sleeps, and equality with expiry remains busy until a later transaction observes database time beyond the boundary. Once opened, the hold blocks every `release_publish` admission and ordinary release claim/session issuance/delivery/revalidation, so queued content generations remain pending. It permits only an exact `edge_pointer_rollback` job created by `request_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback` for that same hold/pointer tuple; requesting it terminally invalidates the hold, advances the epoch, and fences every older session before the rollback admission can issue. Lifecycle requests retain higher priority and can invalidate either state. Every signer, delivery RPC, worker pre-provider check, and immediate pre-pointer-CAS database revalidation rejects stale ordinary work even if it was claimed earlier; the race tests interleave begin and rollback at each boundary. `complete_storefront_edge_cutover` consumes only the matching live tuple plus the owner-reviewed initial-production proof hash; `cancel_storefront_edge_cutover` is idempotent only for the same bounded reason/approval. Expiry merely re-enables normal admission after database read-back and never authorizes cutover. `request_storefront_unpublish` and `request_storefront_account_deletion` atomically increment the epoch, invalidate outstanding release/rollback claims, and prevent new release claims while lifecycle desired mode is not normal `edge`; re-enrollment advances it again rather than reusing an old epoch. A published Ogabassey page-config change enqueues a new generation; a change that breaks the parity contract transitions desired delivery to `origin`. `storefront_releases` records `release_id`, immutable merchant ID, generation, claimed publication epoch, manifest hash, object prefix, state (`building`, `assets_verified`, `pointer_committed`, `active`, `retired`, `failed`), pointer ETag/version, and timestamps. Its bounded GC columns are `gc_claim_token uuid`, `gc_claimed_at timestamptz`, `gc_publication_epoch bigint`, `gc_scan_phase text CHECK (gc_scan_phase IN ('deleting','verify_from_start','purging_cache','verifying_public_absence'))`, `gc_continuation_cursor text CHECK (octet_length(gc_continuation_cursor) BETWEEN 1 AND 4096)`, `gc_verification_required_since timestamptz`, `gc_deleted_object_count integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (gc_deleted_object_count >= 0)`, `gc_last_empty_listing_observed_at timestamptz`, bounded `gc_cache_host_ownership_sha256`/DNS-observed-at/R2-domain-observed-at/expires-at/deployment-fingerprint/read-policy evidence, and bounded host-purge/public-probe evidence. SQL checks enforce phase/cursor/fence combinations, require either family session delivery to match the issued signer run and session and occur before `not_after`, require GC delivery additionally to match the transfer nonce, require ownership evidence only in `purging_cache | verifying_public_absence`, require `expires_at = least(dns_observed_at, r2_domain_observed_at) + interval '2 minutes'`, validate each observation independently against database time/future skew, clear an expired/unconsumed proof before replacing it, prohibit replacement after a purge acknowledgement, and keep the unique non-null claim token. Only one database-active release is permitted for the approved profile, while the R2 pointer remains serving truth.

`storefront_hostname_registry` remains keyed by normalized hostname, but this migration seeds only the serving host `ogabassey.com` after resolving the live merchant row by trusted slug/custom-domain join and requiring owner confirmation of the UUID. Its `immutable_merchant_id uuid NOT NULL` is tombstone authority, deliberately has no foreign key to `merchants` or `auth.users`, and cannot be updated by normal application roles. Add a unique constraint on `(normalized_hostname, immutable_merchant_id)`; the lifecycle table references that exact pair with `ON UPDATE RESTRICT ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so its copied merchant authority cannot differ from the registry. Neither merchant/auth deletion nor an application cascade can remove or rewrite either tombstone row. Only a future separately approved hostname-reassignment operation may retire/reassign that tombstone, and no such RPC exists in phase one. Migration tests inspect `pg_constraint`, reject a mismatched lifecycle merchant ID, delete the merchant/auth rows through the real finalizer, and prove the registry plus lifecycle row survive with the original immutable merchant ID. The database eligibility function rejects every other merchant/host. Alias Redirect Rules are Cloudflare control-plane resources and never receive release-registry rows or pointers; there is no all-merchant backfill.

The release profile/job/release rows needed for erasure likewise must not cascade from `merchants` or `auth.users`. They bind the same immutable registry tuple through restrictive tombstone authority, expose no post-request user-facing mutation path, and retain only epoch/session fences, release IDs/prefixes/hashes/states, and bounded cleanup evidence while the intent is `erasure_admitted`; auth/merchant deletion has not yet occurred. When the last erasure-admitted `gone` prefix and sealed host complete their outcome-specific public-absence proofs, the GC finish transaction seals one immutable terminal-GC receipt in the lifecycle tombstone but does not delete any authority or input required by the finalizer. On the next highest-priority lifecycle tick, the finalizer locks the intent authority, intent projection, lifecycle row, profile/fence, releases, host phases, and terminal-GC receipt; after verifying the same intent and complete proof set it deletes auth/merchant state, appends `finalized`, clears only that intent's fence, scrubs the mutable lifecycle target, and then removes now-unneeded release/job/profile rows while preserving the hostname registry, lifecycle tombstone, immutable intent/events, and terminal receipt. Tests inspect the foreign keys, prove GC cannot delete finalizer inputs or auth/merchant state, prove publication can never resume while erasure is admitted, and prove a successful finalizer leaves only the bounded `gone` tombstone/evidence.

`storefront_hostname_lifecycle_jobs` is keyed by normalized hostname and references only registry state, never a live merchant row. It stores `request_id uuid NOT NULL` for every lifecycle transition, `deletion_request_id uuid NULL`, the copied immutable merchant ID, requesting auth-user ID only until finalization and without a foreign key, monotonic generation, desired mode `origin | unpublished | gone`, bounded public display name for `unpublished`, claim/fence state, committed pointer ETag, and finalization state `not_required | pending | erasure_admitted | finalized`. SQL checks require `(desired_mode = 'gone') = (deletion_request_id IS NOT NULL)`, `gone` to use `pending | erasure_admitted | finalized`, non-`gone` to use only `not_required`, `unpublished` to have a 1-160-character display name, and other modes to have no display name. The claim RPC uses the fixed code-owned HMAC subject `ogabassey.com:lifecycle-queue:v1`, because the publisher cannot know an unclaimed row's request ID. After claim returns, `request_id` is the HMAC subject for pointer-commit/finish; `deletion_request_id` is separately the finalizer subject and is never synthesized for `origin`/`unpublished`. `request_storefront_unpublish` atomically flips `merchants.is_published` and creates the lifecycle job. Account deletion is durable and closed-phase: the authenticated request records the intent and `pending`; the publisher commits and proves `gone`, appends `gone_committed` then `erasure_admitted`, and leaves the lifecycle job claimable for priority GC/finalization without the original browser/session. A direct delete of an edge-enrolled merchant before the exact `gone` pointer, admitted erasure, terminal-GC receipt, and complete sealed-host evidence must fail closed. Only the finalizer deletes auth/merchant state, appends `finalized`, sets the lifecycle state to `finalized`, and nulls the stored auth-user ID in one PostgreSQL transaction; the surviving lifecycle row retains only hostname/merchant tombstone authority, opaque request/deletion IDs, event/terminal-receipt hashes, and bounded completion time/result. The existing claim/attempt/next-attempt fields represent pointer publication, erasure admission, GC wait, and retryable finalization rather than a second status machine.

Create `private.storefront_publisher_secrets` with `current` and time-bounded `previous` values, the live current-operation and nullable previous-removal receipt metadata, its owner-operation ledger, plus a nonce table with a unique nonce and automatic 10-minute expiry cleanup. Enable RLS on every new table; define no client policy for either private table and only the exact proof-function nonce policy/ownership path required by the migration tests. Revoke the private schema/tables from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`; grant the four owner-operation functions only to `postgres`. Each public release RPC is `SECURITY DEFINER`, uses `SET search_path = ''`, sets a bounded local `statement_timeout`, rejects oversized/nonconforming proof JSON before secret or ledger reads, validates a five-minute HMAC proof before reading or writing release state, consumes the nonce once, exposes no generic SQL/table access, and is executable by `anon` only through that proof. Invalid proofs return a bounded failure without inserting one row per attempt; aggregate/sample observability must not create anonymous write amplification. The proof-only `verify_storefront_publisher_secret_rotation_proof` follows the same parser, nonce, constant-time comparison, search-path, timeout, and fixed Ogabassey binding, but reads no release ledger and returns exactly `{ matchedSlot, databaseNow, previousPresent, previousExpiresAt, currentOperation, previousRemoval }` with the nullability and receipt-hash invariants above; it accepts no caller-selected action, subject, payload, merchant, or hostname. `finalize_storefront_account_deletion` is additionally hard-bound to the single approved Ogabassey merchant/hostname and locks the one authority-selected active intent plus lifecycle/projection/profile/GC rows. It requires `desired_mode = 'gone'`, matching committed pointer generation/ETag, `finalization_state = 'erasure_admitted'`, an event head at `erasure_admitted`, matching non-null `topologyFenceIntentId`, the same live lifecycle fence token, and one terminal-GC receipt covering every immutable release prefix and sealed-host outcome. `finalized` is an idempotent success only when the surviving tombstone hashes match the same intent and terminal receipt. The function accepts no user, merchant, hostname, intent selector, or terminal-receipt selector; it derives them under lock, deletes only the stored auth user through the same database cascade contract asserted by `delete_current_user`, appends `finalized`, updates the projection/lifecycle tombstone, clears only the same-intent fence, nulls `activeIntentId`, scrubs the lifecycle target and auth identifier, and removes dispensable release rows in one transaction. No other HMAC RPC may delete auth or merchant state or clear a lifecycle-deletion fence.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the complete transactional public-output dependency matrix**








| `merchant_slug_aliases` | no phase-one release effect; aliases are outside the exact `ogabassey.com` route | none |
| `storefront_delivery_profiles` | only the verified Ogabassey desired-delivery/generation changes | verified Ogabassey `merchant_id` |
| `products` | published/public name, slug, description, brand, base/compare-at price, canonical condition, available-condition summary, status/availability, stock-display cap, media, SEO, variant-model, merchandising, or direct `category_id` changes | row `merchant_id` |
| `product_variants` | public price/compare-at price, typed condition, attributes, media, availability/stock-display cap, default selection, or merchant/product ownership changes | resolve product merchant; enqueue old and new |
| `product_offers` | active legacy condition-offer canonical condition, price/compare-at price, availability/stock-display cap, grade, notes, media, status, or merchant/product ownership changes | resolve product merchant; enqueue old and new |
| `product_reviews` | only approved/published aggregate rating or review-count inputs change | resolve product merchant; enqueue old and new |
| `categories` | public category name/slug/description or ownership changes | row `merchant_id`; enqueue old and new |
| `product_categories` | membership insert/update/delete | resolve product merchant; enqueue old and new products |
| `blog_posts` | published projection or publish-state changes | row `merchant_id` |
| `page_configs` | only a changed, fully validated Ogabassey published projection/revision affecting a parity-contract input | enqueue that exact fenced `ogabassey-static-v1` generation in the same transaction; draft saves/previews do nothing |

The migration test must mutate every listed source inside both committed and rolled-back transactions and prove the generation changes exactly when its normalized release input can change. Product, category, variant, condition-offer, review aggregate, and feature changes enqueue because published builder commerce references are resolved against those current public projections. If a deletion/unpublish makes a published commerce block invalid, the newest generation fails closed and the current pointer remains only until the existing freshness deadline forces origin. Enqueue both source and destination merchants for ownership reassignment. Do not enqueue drafts or tracked-unlimited-stock quantity-only changes.

- [ ] **Step 4: Add HMAC-proof-gated fenced RPCs**

Use `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a server-generated UUID claim token, claimed generation, bounded attempts, exponential backoff, and generation-aware completion. `load_storefront_release_input` returns explicit public columns only and fails when the HMAC proof, claimed token, or generation do not match. The assets-verified and pointer-committed release RPCs accept only bounded hashes/ETags and enforce forward-only state transitions. `finish_storefront_release_job` may reconcile `pointer_committed` to `active` after independently supplied pointer proof, or record a retryable failure without moving the serving pointer backwards.

`private.classify_storefront_deletion_admission(...)` is the single profile-locked phase classifier used by deletion requests, rollback requests, and `claim_storefront_r2_mutation_admission`. It returns exactly `lifecycle_pointer_to_gone | erasure_admitted_gc | lifecycle_finalize | no_deletion_work` from the live intent event head, lifecycle state, pointer receipt, terminal-GC receipt, claim/session fences, and topology fence; callers never select a phase. Before `gone`, only `lifecycle_pointer_to_gone` is eligible. After `erasure_admitted` and before a terminal-GC receipt, only `erasure_admitted_gc` is eligible. Once the exact terminal-GC receipt exists, GC is ineligible and a fresh `lifecycle_finalize` claim maps to the existing `lifecycle_pointer` work class; the expired/released pointer-publication claim and admission can never be reused. After `finalized`, no deletion work is eligible. SQL checks make the four phases mutually exclusive and total for every accepted-intent state.

`claim_storefront_r2_mutation_admission(proof jsonb)` accepts no family, class, phase, merchant, hostname, release, or prefix argument. Its HMAC subject is fixed per executable (`ogabassey.com:r2-admission:publisher:v1` or `ogabassey.com:r2-admission:gc:v1`), and under `FOR UPDATE` it derives the one highest eligible work class from the classifier and durable queue state. The effective order is deletion `lifecycle_pointer_to_gone`, deletion `erasure_admitted_gc`, deletion `lifecycle_finalize`, non-deletion `edge_pointer_rollback`, `release_publish`, then `retention_gc`; all accepted deletion phases outrank and exclude rollback/publication/retention. It returns only `{ admissionToken, workClass, deletionPhase, publicationEpoch, expiresAt }` and no work identity. `deletionPhase` is non-null only for the three deletion states and must match the claimed lifecycle/GC RPC. `lifecycle_pointer | edge_pointer_rollback | release_publish` leases expire after exactly four minutes; `erasure_admitted_gc | retention_gc` leases expire after exactly five minutes. Each daemon obtains admission before the VPS `flock`, revalidates it after locking, and every claim, session, checkpoint, pointer commit, finish, and finalizer requires the same live token/phase/epoch. A lease expiring behind the lock performs no provider or database work. `release_storefront_r2_mutation_admission(...)` remains idempotent only for its matching token and cannot clear a newer lease.

An accepted deletion intent atomically advances the epoch, revokes lower-priority release/retention authority, and supersedes any prepared/claimed rollback. A rollback already past provider attempt may perform read-back only: exact unchanged predecessor closes `superseded`; an origin pointer that committed before deletion acceptance is incorporated as the deletion publisher's observed predecessor; any origin write after the deletion epoch is terminal drift and route safety removes the apex route before deletion continues. No rollback request, claim, temporary session, or CAS can be created or used once the classifier reports any deletion phase. Tests race deletion against rollback before claim, after claim/before CAS, after provider response/before database commit, with an issued temporary session, and on repeated calls; they prove at most one authoritative pointer transition, no rollback can replace or undo `gone`, the original deletion `acceptedAt` never changes, GC receives admission within the bounded cadence, and a fresh finalizer admission becomes reachable only after the terminal-GC receipt.

`verify_storefront_r2_mutation_admission(p_admission_token uuid, p_publication_epoch bigint, p_proof jsonb)` verifies fixed action `verify_storefront_r2_mutation_admission`, subject `admissionToken`, and ordered payload `publicationEpoch`; it returns only `{ state: 'live' | 'revoked' | 'expired'; workClass; publicationEpochDecimal; databaseNow; expiresAt; receiptSha256 }` for that exact previously claimed token and cannot enumerate, extend, release, or mutate it. The fixed holder mints one fresh single-use proof immediately before each two-second poll from its already authorized in-memory HMAC, zeroes the proof bytes after the response, and stops at its runtime deadline; no shared reusable proof or newly caller-selected identity is accepted. Rollback/lifecycle advancement atomically changes a lower-priority token to `revoked`, and equality with expiry is `expired`. PostgreSQL/Node fixtures cover nonce replay, wrong token/epoch, transition during a provider call, runtime-bound poll count, and receipt-byte drift.

`claim_storefront_release_gc_batch(proof jsonb)` validates a fixed `ogabassey.com:release-gc:v1` subject and `max_prefixes=1`, refuses a claim while any release/lifecycle publisher lease is unexpired, and independently requires database `transaction_timestamp()` to be strictly later than the persisted publisher-family `r2_mutation_session_not_after`. Missing session evidence is allowed only for a database-proven never-uploaded staging/failed prefix; any object-upload/pointer state requires a matching publisher session. Malformed, future beyond the ten-minute issuance bound, claim-family/path-scope mismatch, or overwritten/ambiguous session evidence fails closed. It then returns at most one fenced `{ claimToken, releaseId, immutablePrefix, observedPointerEtag, publicationEpoch, lastPublisherSessionNotAfter, scanPhase, continuationCursor, verificationRequiredSince, deletedObjectCount, reason }`. `immutablePrefix` is selected from stored release state, never a caller argument. A new claim begins as `{ scanPhase: 'deleting', continuationCursor: null, verificationRequiredSince: null }`; a resumed claim returns the exact persisted epoch/session/phase/cursor/verification fence. Every checkpoint and finish requires that same epoch, proves the claim began after `lastPublisherSessionNotAfter`, and rechecks absence of a live publisher lease. SQL tests use database-controlled time to prove equality is rejected and only a strictly later timestamp can claim.

`checkpoint_storefront_release_gc_prefix(proof jsonb, claimToken uuid, observedPointerEtag text, listedFromCursor text, nextContinuationCursor text, listedObjectSha256 text, pageDeletedCount integer)` requires the claim-token subject and binds every scalar in that exact order. It requires `listedFromCursor IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` the persisted cursor, permits `nextContinuationCursor` to be null or a 1-4096-byte opaque provider cursor, requires a 64-character lowercase-hex SHA-256 over the length-prefixed UTF-8 sequence of ordered exact listed keys, `pageDeletedCount BETWEEN 1 AND 100`, matching pointer ETag, and a live claim before mutation. It atomically increments the bounded count and stores `{ scanPhase: 'deleting', continuationCursor: nextContinuationCursor, verificationRequiredSince: null }` when a continuation exists, or `{ scanPhase: 'verify_from_start', continuationCursor: null, verificationRequiredSince: transaction_timestamp() }` after a non-empty terminal page. The nullable terminal checkpoint is mandatory; it is not treated as cursor omission.

`checkpoint_storefront_release_gc_empty_scan(proof jsonb, claimToken uuid, observedPointerEtag text, listedFromCursor text, emptyListingSha256 text, emptyListingObservedAt timestamptz)` is the only zero-object checkpoint. It binds every scalar in that order, requires `scanPhase = 'deleting'`, `listedFromCursor IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` the persisted nullable cursor, the canonical empty length-prefixed-sequence digest, a live claim, unchanged pointer ETag, and an observation no older than five minutes and not in the future by database time. It atomically stores `{ scanPhase: 'verify_from_start', continuationCursor: null, verificationRequiredSince: transaction_timestamp() }` without incrementing `deletedObjectCount`. It rejects use from `verify_from_start`, a cursor mismatch, malformed/oversized cursor, nonempty digest, or replayed nonce. This handles both an initially empty prefix and an empty terminal page reached from a non-null provider continuation; either case requires a second independent full scan rather than immediate completion.

`checkpoint_storefront_release_gc_verified_empty(...)` accepts the second from-start empty proof only in `verify_from_start`, binds the claim/pointer/epoch/session/lease fences, requires its observation after `verificationRequiredSince`, and either atomically completes an ordinary retention claim or moves an erasure-admitted `gone` claim to `purging_cache`. For each current database-owned ordinal, `record_storefront_release_gc_cache_host_ownership(...)` records only the `active_owned` classification and its fresh exact DNS/R2 ownership proof; `record_storefront_release_gc_detached_absent(...)` records only the alternative `detached_absent` classification and a fresh authoritative R2-custom-domain absence/non-serving proof plus the bounded DNS/Worker non-serving evidence required for that derived host. Both accept no host selector, validate the claim/epoch/ordinal/host-set/deployment/read-policy bindings and independent provider observations against database time/future skew, and store only bounded hashes/times/IDs. Ambiguous, foreign-serving, incomplete, 401/403/429/5xx, or timed-out reads record neither outcome. A retry may replace only an expired, unconsumed proof for the same claim. `record_storefront_release_gc_cache_purge(...)` is valid only for an `active_owned` ordinal in `purging_cache`; it consumes the live ownership proof and enters `verifying_public_absence`. It rejects `detached_absent` before any provider mutation. A detached ordinal enters `verifying_public_absence` without a purge only after the detachment RPC commits. `finish_storefront_release_gc_batch(...)` accepts only `completed | retryable_failure`; `completed` is valid only for `gone` after every sealed ordinal has one closed proof path: active-owned exact-host purge acknowledgement, fresh post-purge R2-empty proof, and public absence, or detached-absent fresh authoritative non-serving recheck and public absence with no purge. Completion seals a terminal-GC receipt but does not append `finalized`, delete auth/merchant state, clear the fence, scrub authority, or remove finalizer inputs. `retryable_failure` preserves phase, cursor, epoch, session/verification fences, classification/purge evidence, and progress. Tests prove caller-selected prefixes/cursors/URLs/hosts, a scheme/path/query-bearing host, `purge_everything`, a purge for `detached_absent`, `files`, `prefixes`, `tags`, a second or wrong host, stale/future/swapped provider observations, a delayed response that extends another, wrong DNS/R2 bucket/domain/Worker/deployment/read-policy/zone evidence, ambiguous or foreign-serving detachment, proof replacement after acknowledgement, purge outside the proof window, cursor/digest/fence errors, completion from the initial state, fake/old/future proof, a missing outcome-specific host proof, and premature finalization all fail before mutation. They also prove ordinary retention completes without host classification, erasure-admitted `gone` cannot; a non-empty verification rescan returns to deletion without skipping keys; last-host GC seals but cannot consume the finalizer's inputs; and only the subsequent finalizer commits deletion.

`claim_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_job(proof jsonb)` validates action `claim_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_job`, fixed subject `ogabassey.com:lifecycle-queue:v1`, and fixed ordered payload `max_jobs=1` before any ledger read. It then returns at most one `ClaimedStorefrontHostnameLifecycleJob` and only for the hard-bound Ogabassey registry entry. The return type is a discriminated union over this common shape: `{ requestId, normalizedHostname, immutableMerchantId, generationDecimal, claimToken, claimedGenerationDecimal, previousPointerEtag }` plus exactly one of `{ desiredMode: 'origin'; deletionRequestId: null; publicDisplayName: null; finalizationState: 'not_required' }`, `{ desiredMode: 'unpublished'; deletionRequestId: null; publicDisplayName: string; finalizationState: 'not_required' }`, or `{ desiredMode: 'gone'; deletionRequestId: string; publicDisplayName: null; finalizationState: 'pending' | 'erasure_admitted' | 'finalized' }`. Both decimal fields use the shared branded schema and canonical SQL text aliases. A unique non-null claim-token constraint makes the server-generated token the opaque row selector for subsequent calls. PostgreSQL tests prove a request-ID-subject claim is rejected, the fixed proof cannot select a caller-chosen row/tenant or claim more than one, and every variant decodes through the generated Supabase TypeScript type while impossible/nullability combinations fail.

`record_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_pointer_commit(proof jsonb, requestId uuid, claimToken uuid, claimedGeneration bigint, pointerMode text, pointerEtag text)` validates the `requestId`-subject proof and its complete ordered scalar payload before any ledger read, then locates by the opaque claim token and requires the stored request ID, exact claim, and generation to match. It also enforces closed `pointerMode` equality with the row's desired mode and a 1-160-character opaque pointer ETag. For `gone`, the same transaction locks the sole intent authority/projection/profile, proves `topologyFenceIntentId` and fence token belong to that authority-selected intent, records the terminal pointer, appends `gone_committed` and then `erasure_admitted` as two ordered events with distinct sequence/hash receipts, binds the immutable release-prefix and sealed-host set into the GC admission receipt, and changes finalization from `pending` to `erasure_admitted`. The GC queue may now run even though the lifecycle job is not yet `completed`; no pointer commit can append either event twice or admit a different intent.

`finish_storefront_hostname_lifecycle_job(proof jsonb, requestId uuid, claimToken uuid, claimedGeneration bigint, outcome text, retryAfterSeconds integer, errorCode text)` likewise validates the request-ID-subject proof and bounded arguments before ledger reads, then compares the row selected by claim token with `requestId`/generation. It permits only `outcome IN ('completed','retryable_failure')`; `completed` requires `retryAfterSeconds/errorCode` null, while `retryable_failure` requires `retryAfterSeconds BETWEEN 60 AND 86400` plus `errorCode ~ '^[A-Z0-9_]{1,64}$'`. It computes `next_attempt_at` with database time, never accepts an arbitrary timestamp or error detail, completes `origin`/`unpublished`, records a retryable fenced failure, or completes `gone` only after finalization is `finalized`; it cannot move a pointer or select a deletion target. Tests prove a valid proof paired with another request ID or claim token fails before mutation.

`finalize_storefront_account_deletion(proof jsonb, deletionRequestId uuid, claimToken uuid, claimedGeneration bigint, committedPointerEtag text)` accepts only opaque workflow identifiers and fence evidence—never a user, merchant, hostname, intent, or receipt selector. Its HMAC `subjectId` is the immutable `deletionRequestId`, and its ordered payload binds the other four scalar arguments. The function locates and locks the unique claimed lifecycle row by claim token and independently derives the authority-selected active intent, projection head, same-intent topology fence, release/host phase set, and terminal-GC receipt. It requires the stored deletion request, hard-bound registry tuple, `desired_mode = 'gone'`, generation/pointer ETag, `finalization_state = 'erasure_admitted'`, event head `erasure_admitted`, non-null matching `topologyFenceIntentId`, every immutable prefix empty, and every sealed ordinal's outcome-specific proof to match that terminal receipt. It then deletes auth/merchant state, appends `finalized`, updates the projection/lifecycle tombstone, clears only that intent's fence, nulls `activeIntentId`, scrubs the mutable target/auth identifier, and removes dispensable cleanup rows in one transaction. A crash rolls the whole transaction back; a committed retry returns success only when the tombstone's intent/event/terminal-receipt hashes are byte-identical. A stale/replaced/released claim, mismatched fence intent, absent terminal receipt, or GC-only completion cannot finalize.

Canonical proof input is `version + "\n" + action + "\n" + subjectId + "\n" + issuedAt + "\n" + nonce + "\n" + payloadHash`. Each RPC defines a fixed ordered scalar payload string and PostgreSQL independently calculates the same `payloadHash` from its bounded parameters; do not rely on JavaScript/PostgreSQL generic JSON serialization parity. Compare signatures in constant time. The owner-only initialization/rotation routines return the canonical, database-hashed operation receipt defined above; Task 7 binds that receipt to the fresh-current proof before activating the ciphertext, and rotation then decrypts and proves the new active ciphertext and saved previous ciphertext strictly sequentially through `verify_storefront_publisher_secret_rotation_proof`. After database time reaches the stored expiry, the owner-only removal routine clears previous and returns its canonical removal receipt; the importer requires a current-valid response with `previousPresent: false`/null expiry and matching `previousRemoval`, then requires the former-previous denial bytes to equal a random-unknown denial. Neither secret is printed or stored as plaintext. Tests use fixed clocks/nonces and prove Unicode, null, number, parameter-order, matched-slot, operation/removal receipt binding, `previousPresent`/expiry nullability, overlap-boundary, denial equivalence, and current-only behavior match in TypeScript and PostgreSQL.
