**Interfaces:**

- Obtains one database-derived `lifecycle_pointer | edge_pointer_rollback | release_publish` mutation admission before the VPS lock, revalidates it after locking, and claims at most one job of exactly that class. The proof-gated claim RPCs can return only the approved Ogabassey tuple; publisher code cannot request or substitute a class.
- Runs `process-storefront-edge-pointer-rollbacks.ts` from its own fixed systemd service/timer every five seconds. The processor accepts only `edge_pointer_rollback`, claims the oldest eligible immutable rollback job, constructs the closed `origin` pointer while preserving the job's rollback release/manifest evidence, obtains one publisher-family session whose sole object path is `hosts/ogabassey.com.json`, performs the exact recorded `If-Match`, records the returned bytes/hash/ETag, and finishes the job. It has no merchant, hostname, release, prefix, mode, object-key, or admission-class selector. A precondition failure is success only when a fresh authenticated read exactly equals the committed origin pointer bytes and job tuple; every other mismatch closes `terminal_drift`, releases admission, preserves the exact provider receipt, requires apex-route removal, and marks affinity plus override-guard cleanup pending. A higher-priority lifecycle may close `superseded` only with its immutable generation/receipt and proof that no later origin-pointer write occurred. Retryable failures use database-derived bounded backoff and never silently loop or create a second job. The R2 session remains provider-valid until `notAfter` unless its parent access key is revoked; parent revocation immediately invalidates every derived session in that credential family and is therefore a family-wide emergency kill switch, not selective job revocation. The epoch watcher stops a trusted installed process but is not credential revocation, so an in-flight CAS is always reconciled through exact read-back before closure. Its five-second cadence plus bounded signer/worker path must demonstrate p99 completion within 30 seconds so the 60-second owner-action SLO retains 2x headroom.
- Reconciles hostname lifecycle jobs through the exact admission, claim, pointer-commit, finish, and finalizer RPCs before release publication becomes eligible, so initial/transition `origin`, `unpublished`, and `gone` pointers cannot be starved by normal publication traffic; once `gone` is finalized, database admission blocks release publication until all gone GC completes.
- Finalizes an accepted Ogabassey account-deletion lifecycle job after the matching `gone` pointer proof, through the exact HMAC RPC, until `finalized`; it does not require the initiating web/mobile session to remain alive.
- On every publisher cadence, invokes a fixed proof-gated database freshness transition before claiming normal release work. Database time compares `latestCommittedPublicGeneration`, `activeGeneration`, and the persisted deadline; an overdue newest generation atomically becomes `stale_origin`, blocks any older activation, coalesces one newest-generation retry, and creates or byte-identically reuses the priority origin-pointer rollback job. The evaluator accepts no merchant, generation, deadline, mode, or retry selector. Tests prove missed timers, VPS restart, source mutation during a build, repeated failures, deadline equality, rollback races, and later recovery cannot leave stale edge commerce active or reactivate an older release.
- Loads only the fenced public projection from `load_storefront_release_input`.
- Uses the public Supabase anon key plus a rotating HMAC capability to call only the proof-gated RPCs; it never constructs an admin/service-role client.
- Writes all immutable objects, verifies them, writes the manifest, then conditionally replaces `hosts/{hostname}.json` with an exact ETag compare-and-swap. The claim's `publicationEpoch` is revalidated before every upload, manifest write, and pointer CAS.
- Produces a provider-credentialless non-root publisher coordinator plus `requestStorefrontReleasePublisherPhase(request, phase): Promise<StorefrontReleasePublisherPhaseResult>`, where `phase` is the closed enum `r2_sign | r2_publish` mapped internally to two fixed systemd activation sockets. The coordinator receives the Supabase anon configuration and `storefront-publisher-hmac` only; it never imports `r2-s3-client.ts`, reads an R2 credential, or launches a unit.
- Produces `createR2S3Client(credentials): R2S3Client` backed only by pinned `aws4fetch@1.0.20` plus `fast-xml-parser@5.10.1` for bounded S3 list/delete XML. `R2S3Client` exports bounded `headObject`, `getObject`, `listObjectsV2`, `putObjectIfAbsent`, `putPointerIfMatch`, and `deleteObjects` methods; each derives the fixed account endpoint/bucket from verified configuration, supplies the temporary `sessionToken`, signs `service: 's3'` with region `auto`, rejects redirects, and never exposes generic signed fetch or caller-selected method/host/bucket. `r2-temporary-credentials.ts` has one primary export, `createR2TemporaryCredentials(parent, verifiedGrant): R2TemporaryCredentials`. Its internal parent type is exactly `{ accessKeyId: lowercaseHex32, secretAccessKey: lowercaseHex64 }`. Its internal grant union is exactly `{ family: 'publisher', accountId: sealedLowercaseHex32CloudflareAccountId, bucket: 'baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases', issuedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, notAfter: rfc3339UtcMillis, prefixPath: stringMatching^releases/[0-9a-f-]{36}/[0-9a-f-]{36}/$, objectPath: stringMatching^hosts/[a-z0-9.-]{1,253}\.json$ } | { family: 'gc', accountId: sealedLowercaseHex32CloudflareAccountId, bucket: 'baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases', issuedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, notAfter: rfc3339UtcMillis, prefixPath: stringMatching^releases/[0-9a-f-]{36}/[0-9a-f-]{36}/$, objectPath: null }`; the UUID segments are additionally parsed as canonical lowercase UUIDs and the hostname as the exact registry hostname. The grant is created only by the signer-side database-claim validator and is never raw socket or caller-selected scope. The internal return type is exactly `{ version: 1, family: 'publisher' | 'gc', accessKeyId: lowercaseHex32, secretAccessKey: lowercaseHex64, sessionToken: stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9+/]+={0,2}$WithMaximum16KiB, endpoint: 'https://<sealedAccountId>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com', bucket: 'baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases', actions: readonly ['HeadObject','GetObject','ListObjectsV2','PutObject'] | readonly ['HeadObject','GetObject','ListObjectsV2','DeleteObjects'], prefixPaths: readonly [string], objectPaths: readonly [string] | readonly [], issuedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis, notAfter: rfc3339UtcMillis, pathScopeSha256: lowercaseHex64 }`; publisher family requires the publisher action tuple and one object path, while GC requires the GC tuple and an empty object-path tuple. `pathScopeSha256` is SHA-256 of `v1 + "\n" + family + "\n" + bucket + "\n" + actions.join(",") + "\n" + prefixPath + "\n" + (objectPath ?? "") + "\n" + issuedAt + "\n" + notAfter` and is the exact path-scope hash recorded by `record_storefront_r2_mutation_session`. The module uses only governed Node `crypto` primitives and exposes no general JWT signer or arbitrary claims/actions/paths. Separately, `r2-parent-credential-probe.ts` has one primary export, `createR2ParentCredentialProbe(credentials): R2ParentCredentialProbe`, for the installed importer. Its closed returned interface has only `proveAccepted(rotationId): Promise<{ result: 'accepted'; completedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis; endpointSha256: lowercaseHex64; bucket: 'baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases'; canarySha256: '7a579146f5b6ec3f296fb307bb7917fd578763d65fddf17ec6f38af9bfbaad15'; probeObjectKey: string; acceptedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64 }>` and `proveRevoked(): Promise<{ result: 'revoked'; observedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis; providerCode: qualifiedCloudflareAuthenticationInvalidCode; httpStatus: qualifiedCloudflareAuthenticationInvalidStatus; revokedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64 }>`; those result types are internal to the module rather than additional exports. The factory derives the fixed endpoint/bucket/control keys from immutable configuration, contains its own bounded SigV4/error parsing backed by the same pinned packages, and exposes no client, request primitive, URL, method, bucket, key, body, expected-status parameter, or generic signed fetch.

- [ ] **Step 0: Define and test the narrow publisher capability contracts**

This step is contract-only and performs no production provisioning. Define the installed database-secret-manager preflight/receipt contract, the no-echo importer contract for `storefront-publisher-hmac`, the two distinct IP-restricted bucket-scoped R2 parent policies (`storefront-r2-publisher-parent` and `storefront-r2-gc-parent`), and the coordinator/signer/worker separation. The publisher coordinator contract runs as `baci-storefront-publisher-coordinator` with only Supabase anon configuration plus `storefront-publisher-hmac`, acquires/revalidates admission and the shared lock, claims one job, generates one fresh `signerRunId` plus distinct single-use issuance and delivery proofs whose fixed subject/payload are `signerRunId`/`claimToken`, constructs one maximum-8-KiB canonical request containing both proofs and hashes, connects to the fixed publisher-signer socket, sends EOF, and waits for bounded readiness; it cannot import the signer or S3 client. Steps 1-4C implement, attest, and install the runtime with every publisher/GC timer and activation socket disabled. Live HMAC initialization, R2 parent creation/import, canary creation, capability probes, and unit enablement occur only in Task 12 Step 4A after the protected rollout has created and read back the production bucket/domains.

During the later Task 12 Step 4A provisioning gate, before either parent is accepted or any unit is enabled, the owner creates and reads back exactly `__baci_control__/credential-status-v1.txt` in its one approved bucket with body bytes `baci-r2-credential-status-v1\n`, content type `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, and SHA-256 `7a579146f5b6ec3f296fb307bb7917fd578763d65fddf17ec6f38af9bfbaad15`. The control object is outside every release/host prefix, is never public-linked or garbage-collected, and is used only for non-mutating `HeadObject` plus bounded `GetObject` credential-status probes. `createR2ParentCredentialProbe(credentials).proveAccepted(rotationId)` runs exactly this fixed matrix: `HeadObject` and bounded `GetObject` of that canary with exact body/hash/content type; `ListObjectsV2` with prefix `__baci_control__/` and `max-keys=100`; conditional `PutObject` of `__baci_control__/credential-probe/{rotationId}.txt` with `If-None-Match: *`, fixed body `baci-r2-parent-probe-v1\n`, and fixed content type; exact read-back; exact `DeleteObject`; and a final `HeadObject` requiring `NoSuchKey`. It then requires provider-qualified authorization denial for the non-mutating account-level `ListBuckets` request and bucket-configuration `GetBucketCors` request. The probe object is journal-bound, never public-linked, and recovery deletes only that exact key after proving its recorded body hash; an ambiguous mutation or response fails closed for owner recovery. `createR2ParentCredentialProbe(credentials).proveRevoked()` performs only the fixed canary `HeadObject` plus bounded `GetObject`, caps an error body at 16 KiB, rejects DTD/entity input, and accepts only Cloudflare's qualified invalid-authentication code/status—not `AccessDenied`, `NoSuchKey`, `NoSuchBucket`, timeout, transport failure, or an unparseable body. Initial import records the full accepted proof; rotation accepts old-invalid only when the same endpoint/bucket/canary and unchanged policy fingerprint previously passed, the new parent still produces the full accepted proof, and the old parent produces that strict revoked proof.

The publisher signer runs as `baci-storefront-publisher-r2-signer`, receives only `storefront-r2-publisher-parent`, verifies the socket request and database claim using the supplied issuance proof, while retaining the separately supplied delivery proof without HMAC access, and uses Cloudflare's documented local JWT-signing form to issue an exact-180-second session restricted to `HeadObject`, `GetObject`, `ListObjectsV2`, and `PutObject` on the exact claimed `releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/` prefix plus exact object `hosts/{normalizedHostname}.json`. `createR2TemporaryCredentials` serializes the protected header as exact UTF-8 `{"alg":"HS256","typ":"JWT"}` and the payload with exact property order `bucket`, `scope`, `actions`, `paths`, `sub`, `iss`, `aud`, `iat`, `exp`; `scope` is `"object-read-write"`, `sub` is the sealed Cloudflare account ID, `iss` is the parent access-key ID, `aud` is the fixed `<accountId>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` endpoint host without scheme/path, and `iat`/`exp` are integer seconds derived only from the database issuance/expiry timestamps. Publisher actions are exactly `["HeadObject","GetObject","ListObjectsV2","PutObject"]`; GC actions are exactly `["HeadObject","GetObject","ListObjectsV2","DeleteObjects"]`. `paths` always has exact inner property order `prefixPaths`, `objectPaths`: publisher contains its one release prefix plus its one pointer object, while GC contains its persisted release prefix plus an empty `objectPaths` array. The implementation base64url-encodes header/payload without padding, signs `header.payload` with HMAC-SHA256 under the parent secret, derives the temporary secret as lowercase SHA-256 hex of the complete signed JWT, derives the session token as standard padded Base64 of UTF-8 `jwt/` plus that signed JWT, and reuses the parent access-key ID. It rejects fractional/non-database time, any TTL/action/path/account/issuer/audience drift, duplicate or reordered claim fields, and any generic scope input. It refuses issuance after second 45 of the admission or unless database time proves the resulting `notAfter` is at least 15 seconds before admission expiry. It cannot import `r2-s3-client.ts` or any publish operation. After `record_storefront_r2_mutation_session` commits the exact supplied `signerRunId`, it exposes the exact canonical temporary-credential bytes through one signer-owned mode-`0600` one-shot socket `/run/baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session/session.sock`, returns canonical `signer_ready` plus EOF on its half-open fd-3 stream, and remains alive only for the root service manager's credential fetch. Starting the fixed publisher-worker socket causes the service manager to resolve `LoadCredential=baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session:/run/baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session/session.sock` into the distinct worker credential directory; after the credential bytes are written and the socket closes, the signer calls `record_storefront_r2_mutation_session_delivery` with that signer run ID, the live claim token, issued session hash, null transfer nonce, and the distinct pre-minted delivery proof; the database derives family `publisher`. The signer then exits. The publisher worker hashes the credential bytes, waits only within its fixed startup deadline for that exact delivery record, and performs no provider call if it is absent, late, replaced, or mismatched. It runs as `baci-storefront-publisher-r2-worker`, receives that one temporary provider credential plus the internal `storefront-publisher-hmac`, permits no new provider call after `notAfter - 15 seconds`, and performs all fenced projection loads, revalidations, S3 writes/read-backs, pointer CAS, and final RPCs. The worker may receive its internal HMAC capability plus one temporary provider session; the signer instead receives the two coordinator-minted one-shot proofs in its request plus its one parent credential. No process, unit, environment, credential directory, or import graph receives two provider credentials or both an R2 parent and temporary session.

`CopyObject`, multipart operations, publisher Delete, and GC Put are credential-denied, not merely code-denied. A record/delivery/scope/TTL/action mismatch destroys the unused session and performs no provider call. The coordinator has no signal, cgroup, D-Bus, or unit-control authority over signer/worker services: it closes its socket, records the retryable checkpoint, and exits at its own deadline, while systemd independently enforces each activated unit's `RuntimeMaxSec` and `TimeoutStopSec`. GC remains ineligible until database time exceeds every recorded publisher expiry. Do not place any parent/session value in Vercel, GitHub Actions, repository files, the shared VPS environment, coordinator memory, socket request/result, stdout, or shell history. Prove with live capability probes that each session is denied an out-of-path read/write and every opposite-family action. Use Cloudflare's [R2 temporary credentials](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/s3/temporary-credentials/) rather than a long-lived S3 credential in a publishing worker.

The protected `build-storefront-release-runtime.yml` workflow runs only for the exact protected merge SHA on `refs/heads/main` after required checks. `storefront-release-runtime-toolchain.json` pins Node `24.11.1`, pnpm `11.7.0`, esbuild `0.28.1`, `aws4fetch` `1.0.20` with registry integrity `sha512-/djoAN709iY65ETD6LKCtyyEI04XIBP5xVvfmNxsEP0uJB5tyaGBztSryRr4HqMStr9R06PisQE7m9zDTXKu6g==`, `fast-xml-parser` `5.10.1` with registry integrity `sha512-IEMIf7298kXuZSRFoGfMYrl7is8LpavODgbNz1cwIudv7KwVFnuU+UsMporfq6PD6aXSlawZlARiA3UywCTfMw==`, `postgres` `3.4.9` with registry integrity `sha512-GD3qdB0x1z9xgFI6cdRD6xu2Sp2WCOEoe3mtnyB5Ee0XrrL5Pe+e4CCnJrRMnL1zYtRDZmQQVbvOttLnKDLnaw==`, Linux `x64`, GNU tar `1.35`, zstd `1.5.5`, archive media type `application/zstd`, `nodeDistributionUrl: "https://nodejs.org/dist/v24.11.1/node-v24.11.1-linux-x64.tar.xz"`, `nodeDistributionSha256: "60e3b0a8500819514aca603487c254298cd776de0698d3cd08f11dba5b8289a8"`, and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` equal to the protected commit's integer author timestamp. The root `package.json` declares esbuild exactly `0.28.1` plus manager-only `postgres` exactly `3.4.9`; `apps/web/package.json` declares `aws4fetch` exactly `1.0.20` and `fast-xml-parser` exactly `5.10.1`; `pnpm-workspace.yaml` replaces the current security override and its explanatory comment with the separately reviewed exact `fast-xml-parser: "5.10.1"` policy while retaining the independent `fast-xml-builder` mitigation; and the governed root `pnpm-lock.yaml` resolves those exact packages/platform binaries plus every web/shared dependency imported by a runtime entrypoint. A dependency-policy test fails if the app manifest, workspace override, toolchain manifest, lockfile, or recorded registry integrity names a different parser version; the upgrade must pass the existing XML security fixtures before the runtime artifact can be accepted.

The workflow's first `fetch-dependencies` job has `permissions: { contents: read }`, no secrets or OIDC, a clean GitHub-hosted runner, the exact protected checkout, and network access only while it runs `pnpm fetch --frozen-lockfile --store-dir <jobTemp>/pnpm-store` from the repository root; omitting both `--prod` and `--dev` is deliberate so pnpm fetches the complete lockfile graph needed by the build. It verifies `pnpm --store-dir <jobTemp>/pnpm-store store status`, the lockfile integrities for `aws4fetch@1.0.20`, `fast-xml-parser@5.10.1`, and `postgres@3.4.9`, the exact esbuild platform package, and a canonical sorted inventory of every store file hash, then creates a normalized `storefront-release-pnpm-store-<rootLockSha256>.tar.zst` plus SHA-256 and uploads it as the sole dependency-store artifact. It writes no `node_modules`, executes no dependency lifecycle script, and has no provider/repository-write capability. Both reproducibility jobs download that exact artifact by ID and digest, rehash/extract it into separate fresh directories, disable network at the process namespace/firewall layer, and run `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline --ignore-scripts --store-dir <freshStore>` from separate fresh checkouts. A missing artifact, cold Actions cache, extra/missing store entry, lock hash mismatch, attempted network connection, or integrity drift fails before bundling; Actions cache is an optional transport optimization and never an authority. Building from the independent `vps-workers` workspace is forbidden because that workspace cannot resolve the `apps/web` and root-workspace package graph. A clean-container test begins with no `node_modules` or ambient pnpm cache, consumes only the protected checkout plus verified store artifact, and must bundle all entrypoints successfully. The builder rejects a different Node/pnpm/esbuild/aws4fetch/fast-xml-parser/postgres/OS/architecture/tar/zstd version or any lock/store mismatch.

The workflow downloads the exact official Node tarball above, verifies its SHA-256 before extraction, rejects every archive path/link/device ambiguity, and copies only its `bin/node` plus `LICENSE` into the runtime closure as `node/bin/node` and `node/LICENSE`; it records the source URL, distribution hash, installed binary hash, license hash, and `node --version` output in the canonical artifact manifest. All publisher/coordinator/phase `ExecStart` values invoke `/opt/baci/storefront-release-runtime/<protectedMergeSha>/node/bin/node` followed by an exact closure-local bundle path. Runtime units may not use `/usr/bin/node`, `/usr/bin/env node`, a shebang-resolved Node, `PATH` lookup, a checkout, or `node_modules`. The installer verifies the embedded ELF is Linux x86-64, executes it in a sanitized environment, requires exactly `v24.11.1`, records its dynamic loader and system-library resolution as part of the qualified Ubuntu host baseline, and aborts on a missing or changed loader/library before unit activation. A runtime test removes host Node from `PATH` and proves every installed entrypoint starts with only the closure-local interpreter; rollback therefore selects the prior receipted Node binary and bundles together.

The builder compiles the publisher coordinator, publisher R2 signer, publisher R2 worker, GC coordinator, and four GC phase entrypoints into standalone production JavaScript bundles with no runtime import from a checkout or mutable `node_modules`, emits esbuild metafiles, and closes the complete transitive input graph over tracked regular files, root `package.json`, `apps/web/package.json`, root `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, every participating workspace manifest, the imported web/shared source files and their TypeScript/build config, shell helpers, units, sysusers, tmpfiles, installer, credential importer, database-secret manager, verified dependency-store inventory, and pinned Node runtime files. Static graph tests require `aws4fetch` and `fast-xml-parser` to be reachable only through one of two closed roots: `r2-s3-client.ts`, reachable only from the two temporary-session worker bundles, or `r2-parent-credential-probe.ts`, reachable only from the credential-importer bundle. The importer graph may reach only the probe factory's two fixed returned methods and must not reach `r2-s3-client.ts`; neither coordinator nor either parent signer bundle may contain `AwsClient`, XML parsing, SigV4, S3 operations, or either package's bytes. `postgres` is reachable only from the database-secret-manager bundle; no application, coordinator, signer, worker, importer, installer, or deploy graph may contain it or a PostgreSQL connection primitive. Static tests reject any exported `AwsClient`, generic signed fetch, caller-selected request field, third S3-package-reachable bundle, or second postgres-reachable bundle. The builder rejects symlinks, native addons, unresolved dynamic local imports, runtime `require()`/`import()` outside the bundle, source maps, absolute build paths, ungoverned inputs, or an externalized package other than the explicit Node built-in allowlist. It copies only that reviewed closure, normalizes archive order/UID/GID/mtime/modes, and emits `storefront-release-runtime-<protectedMergeSha>.tar.zst` plus a canonical manifest containing the commit, toolchain, dependency-store digest/inventory, canonical module graph, complete file list, per-file SHA-256/mode, Node distribution/install evidence, each bundle SHA-256, installer/importer/database-secret-manager SHA-256, manifest SHA-256, and archive SHA-256. The workflow rebuilds twice from separately extracted verified store artifacts and fresh checkout directories and requires byte-identical module graphs/manifests/archives.

The workflow starts with `permissions: {}` and grants its build/attestation job only `contents: read`, `id-token: write`, and `attestations: write`; artifact upload receives no repository-write permission. Every external Action is pinned to a reviewed full commit SHA, including `actions/attest` commit `f7c74d28b9d84cb8768d0b8ca14a4bac6ef463e6`, and the attestation subject is the exact archive digest. The protected job runs on GitHub-hosted infrastructure and the installer-side verifier uses `gh attestation verify` with `--repo ogabasseyy/Baci`, `--signer-workflow ogabasseyy/Baci/.github/workflows/build-storefront-release-runtime.yml`, `--source-digest <protectedMergeSha>`, `--source-ref refs/heads/main`, `--deny-self-hosted-runners`, and JSON output; it requires exactly one matching SLSA-v1 subject digest and validates repository/workflow/source/runner claims from the signed certificate. Run ID and predicate metadata are supporting correlation only and never override a certificate mismatch. `vps-workers/deploy.sh` downloads/verifies that exact attested artifact in a provider-credentialless step, stages it and the standalone installer under `/home/bassey/baci-workers/releases/<protectedMergeSha>/`, and prints the hashes/owner procedure; it must never invoke sudo, install a system unit, copy a credential, or claim activation. GitHub's required attestation permissions and strict signer-workflow verification flags are implementation contracts, not documentation suggestions.

For artifact retrieval, “credentialless” means provider-credentialless. The live `ogabasseyy/Baci` repository is public, but GitHub workflow-artifact download still requires signed-in repository read access; the download subprocess may receive only a short-lived fine-grained token scoped to this repository with `Actions: read` and no contents/deployment/workflow write permission. A parent process opens no `gh` keychain, creates a clean `env -i` child containing only that token and bounded download inputs, receives only the verified output path/hashes, waits for child exit, and then invokes `rsync` from a fresh tokenless process. “Destroy” means the token existed only in the terminated child's environment and pipe buffers—never in the parent environment, GitHub CLI credential store, argv, filesystem, or shell history. The staged artifact contains no credential.

The live host preflight records the exact systemd version and privilege state before implementation; the current qualification baseline is systemd `255.4` and `bassey` has interactive sudo membership but no non-interactive sudo. Production installation is an explicit owner checkpoint. The owner first copies the staged installer to `/usr/local/sbin/baci-install-storefront-release-runtime.candidate` with `/usr/bin/install --owner=root --group=root --mode=0555`, verifies that root-owned candidate in place with `/usr/bin/sha256sum -c` against the installer hash recorded in the protected build receipt, and only on success atomically renames it to `/usr/local/sbin/baci-install-storefront-release-runtime`; the candidate is deleted on any mismatch. No process ever executes the staged user-owned installer as root. The owner then runs only the verified root-owned command with the fixed staged artifact path plus `<protectedMergeSha>`, `<attestedArchiveSha256>`, and `<reviewedManifestSha256>`. Before extraction, the installer verifies the archive/manifest hashes and rejects an absolute/traversal/duplicate/noncanonical path, symlink, hardlink, device, FIFO, socket, unexpected file, commit mismatch, mode mismatch, or path outside the closed allowlist. It extracts into a new root-only temporary directory without preserving archive ownership, rehashes every regular file, and atomically renames the verified closure to `/opt/baci/storefront-release-runtime/<protectedMergeSha>/`, owned `root:root`, directories mode `0755`, executable files `0555`, configuration `0444`, with no writable parent controlled by `bassey` or a service user. Credential-bearing system units use only exact absolute paths inside that immutable directory, never `/home/bassey/baci-workers`, a mutable symlink, `PATH` lookup, a staged archive, checkout, or external `node_modules`. The verified root installer is the only component allowed to create sysusers/tmpfiles, units, and timers and to install the separately hashed credential importer; only that installed importer may create or rotate encrypted credstore entries. The installer performs `systemd-analyze verify`, installs atomically, reloads PID 1, starts dry-run services, and writes a root-owned mode-`0444` secret-free receipt plus detached SHA-256 containing the protected merge SHA, installer/manifest/archive SHA-256, installed-file hashes/modes/owners, unit hashes, systemd version, and read-back state. Activation remains blocked until a second read-only verification command reproduces that receipt and digest. `--rollback <previousProtectedMergeSha>` atomically restores the previously retained receipted immutable closure and unit set, reloads PID 1, and proves the rejected closure is inactive; deletion of retained closures is a separate later owner operation.

The staged installer source is exactly `/home/bassey/baci-workers/releases/<protectedMergeSha>/install-storefront-release-runtime.sh` and the archive is exactly `/home/bassey/baci-workers/releases/<protectedMergeSha>/storefront-release-runtime-<protectedMergeSha>.tar.zst`; the owner procedure accepts no alternate source or destination path. Tests race replacement of either staged file before and during copy/open, alter the archive/manifest/attestation/expected hash, substitute every forbidden archive entry type/path, and prove that only a root-owned candidate whose post-copy SHA-256 equals the protected receipt can be renamed or executed and only a fully rehashed closure can become active.

The installer places the separately hashed importer at `/usr/local/sbin/baci-import-storefront-release-credential`, root-owned mode `0500`; neither the installer nor deploy script accepts a secret value. Its owner-facing grammar is exactly one of `--name <fixedName>` for initial import/rotation or `--resume <rotationId>` for an unfinished journal, never both. Both owner modes require an interactive root TTY, disable echo before any secret read, restore terminal state on every signal/exit, and reject every extra argument/environment input. Initial mode accepts exactly one fixed name from `storefront-publisher-hmac`, `storefront-r2-publisher-parent`, `storefront-r2-gc-parent`, `storefront-cache-host-read-token`, or `storefront-cache-host-purge-token` and reads one maximum-16-KiB canonical UTF-8 JSON value. Resume mode reads no secret and accepts only the exact bounded non-secret receipt required by the journal state. The HMAC schema is exactly `{ "version": 1, "hmacBase64": string, "databaseOperationReceipt": { "version": 1, "action": "initialize" | "rotate", "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$, "completedAt": rfc3339UtcMillis, "previousExpiresAt": rfc3339UtcMillis | null, "receiptSha256": lowercaseHex64 } }`; `initialize` requires null expiry and `rotate` requires non-null expiry. Each R2-parent schema is exactly `{ "version": 1, "accessKeyId": string, "secretAccessKey": string, "ownerExportedTokenId": string, "reviewedPolicySha256": lowercaseHex64 }`; each Cloudflare-token schema is exactly `{ "version": 1, "apiToken": string, "tokenId": string, "reviewedPolicySha256": lowercaseHex64 }`. Initial mode rejects stdin pipes, argv/environment values, unknown/reordered/duplicate properties, noncanonical JSON, weak HMAC material, publisher/GC parent reuse, and read/purge token reuse. It recalculates the database receipt's fixed scalar-core hash before encrypting and, after its fresh current proof, requires every returned `currentOperation` field to equal the receipt; an unbound, stale, forged, wrong-action, wrong-expiry, or superseded receipt fails before activation. For Cloudflare API tokens it requires live `/user/tokens/verify` ID/status plus owner-exported policy-hash equality; because R2 S3 credentials cannot self-introspect their policy, it records that limitation, requires the reviewed owner-export token ID/policy hash, and completes import only after exact allow/deny S3 capability probes. It passes plaintext only through an anonymous pipe to exact-path `/usr/bin/systemd-creds encrypt --name=<fixedMappedCredentialName> - /var/lib/baci/storefront-release-credential-rotation/<rotationId>/next/<fixedMappedCredentialName>`, whose output basename exactly matches the credential name embedded by `systemd-creds`. The sole active path is exactly `/etc/credstore.encrypted/<fixedMappedCredentialName>`, root-owned mode `0400`; exact name-to-unit mapping is checked in and each unit uses only `LoadCredentialEncrypted=<fixedMappedCredentialName>`. Staging and rollback ciphertext never live in `/etc/credstore.encrypted` or another credential search directory.

The installer also places the separately hashed database-secret manager at `/usr/local/sbin/baci-manage-storefront-publisher-database-secret`, root-owned mode `0500`; it is an owner-invoked operations tool, never a service, timer, application route, deploy hook, or dependency of the publisher/importer. Its grammar is exactly one of `--preflight`, `--initialize`, `--rotate`, `--remove`, or `--status`. Every mode requires interactive root TTY plus `env -i`, restores terminal state on every signal/exit, rejects stdin pipes and extra arguments/descriptors, and first reads one maximum-4-KiB canonical `{ "version": 1, "databaseUrl": string }` with echo disabled. The URL must use `postgresql:`, TLS certificate/hostname verification, database `postgres`, the sealed linked production Supabase project reference, and exactly either the qualified direct endpoint on port `5432` with username `postgres` or the qualified Supavisor session-mode endpoint on port `5432` with username `postgres.<sealedProjectRef>`; transaction pooling on port `6543`, a dedicated transaction pooler, every other host/port/mode, service-role key, Supabase HTTP URL, alternate project/database/user, disabled TLS, query option outside the fixed allowlist, or connection string in argv/environment/filesystem is rejected. Backed only by pinned `postgres@3.4.9`, it opens one TLS connection and requires `session_user = current_user = 'postgres'` plus the sealed database/project identity. Before any operation transaction, it runs every qualification `SET`/`SHOW` as an individual autocommit statement so an allowed failed `SET` cannot leave an aborted transaction behind. It executes fixed literal `SET log_parameter_max_length = 0` and requires exact zero read-back. It then attempts fixed literal `SET log_parameter_max_length_on_error = 0`; success requires zero read-back, while only SQLSTATE `42501` or `0A000` from that exact statement may fall back to read-only `SHOW log_parameter_max_length_on_error`, which must already equal zero. Every other error or nonzero/unavailable value closes the connection. The final autocommit qualification query is exactly `SELECT current_setting('server_version_num') AS server_version_num, transaction_timestamp() AS qualified_at`; it must return one row, a `server_version_num` matching `^[0-9]{5,6}$`, and one canonical UTC-millisecond timestamp. `--preflight` reads no second input, calls no private function, and prints only canonical `{ "version": 1, "result": "qualified", "projectRefSha256": lowercaseHex64, "connectionMode": "direct_5432" | "supavisor_session_5432", "normalBindLimit": 0, "errorBindLimit": 0, "errorBindLimitMode": "set_and_verified" | "show_only_verified", "serverVersionNum": stringMatching^[0-9]{5,6}$, "qualifiedAt": rfc3339UtcMillis, "receiptSha256": lowercaseHex64 }`; `qualifiedAt` is exactly the database-derived `qualified_at`, never VPS wall time. `receiptSha256` is SHA-256 of exactly `v1 + "\nqualified\n" + projectRefSha256 + "\n" + connectionMode + "\n0\n0\n" + errorBindLimitMode + "\n" + serverVersionNum + "\n" + qualifiedAt`; it excludes the hash field itself. Production provisioning requires this receipt from the installed bundle. Every other mode repeats the same preflight before reading its operation JSON, so no HMAC is read on an unqualified session. Initialize/rotate then read one separate maximum-1-KiB canonical `{ "version": 1, "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$, "hmacBase64": string }`; remove reads `{ "version": 1, "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$, "expectedPreviousExpiresAt": rfc3339UtcMillis }`; status reads `{ "version": 1, "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$ }`. Every secret-bearing function call uses only PostgreSQL's extended prepared/bind protocol on that same isolated session, so the verified zero settings cover `log_statement`, duration/sampling logging, and error paths. It then calls exactly the matching private function in one new transaction. It accepts no caller-supplied SQL, schema, table, function, overlap, URL, role, logging setting, timeout selector, or version representation. It prints only the canonical non-secret operation receipt defined in Task 6, never a connection value, secret, SQL parameters, or provider response; closes the connection and zeroes mutable buffers best-effort. A connection loss after dispatch must recover only through `--status` with the same approval ID before any retry. This direct database-owner tool is the sole privileged database rotation/removal path; no service-role application edge, Management API token, stored database credential, or generic SQL executor is added.

The same compiled importer has one non-owner child-only mode `--verify-hmac-slot`, reachable only from its own fixed internal spawn table. That mode requires root, an `env -i` environment containing only the closed non-secret Supabase configuration, exactly one inherited anonymous input fd and one bounded result fd, no TTY, and no path/name/rotation argument. Before reading the secret fd it snapshots `/proc/<ppid>/stat` start time, requires `/proc/<ppid>/exe` to resolve to the exact immutable closure-local Node path and match the Node binary SHA-256 in the installed receipt, hashes `/usr/local/sbin/baci-import-storefront-release-credential` against the importer SHA-256 in that receipt, and requires NUL-split `/proc/<ppid>/cmdline` to be exactly either `[nodePath, importerPath, '--name', 'storefront-publisher-hmac']` or `[nodePath, importerPath, '--resume', canonicalRotationUuid]`; the latter UUID must equal the sole unfinished HMAC journal selected by the parent. It then rereads the parent start time and requires it unchanged. This is an accidental/direct-invocation guard inside the explicit root TCB, not authentication against hostile root. It reads at most 16 KiB of HMAC JSON from the inherited fd, creates exactly one fresh fixed rotation proof, calls only `verify_storefront_publisher_secret_rotation_proof`, emits one maximum-512-byte canonical non-secret result, zeroes mutable buffers best-effort, and exits. Direct owner invocation, ordinary stdin, an extra descriptor, a different owner-mode/Node/importer/receipt/start-time tuple, or any other RPC/action/subject/payload fails before reading the fd.

Each initial import or rotation creates a root-owned mode-`0700` directory `/var/lib/baci/storefront-release-credential-rotation/<rotationId>/` outside every systemd credential search path. Its `next/<fixedMappedCredentialName>` and optional `previous/<fixedMappedCredentialName>` files retain the exact basename embedded in the ciphertext. The root-owned mode-`0600` `journal.json` is the strict union `CredentialRotationJournal = CloudflareProviderInitializationJournal | CloudflareProviderRotationJournal | R2ParentInitializationJournal | R2ParentRotationJournal | HmacCredentialRotationJournal`. Every member contains exactly `{ version: 1, kind, operation, rotationId: uuid, credentialName, state, oldCiphertextSha256, newCiphertextSha256: lowercaseHex64, updatedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis }`; the shared `oldCiphertextSha256` property is mandatory and null only for initialization, while rotation requires lowercase hex. No other old/revocation property is present in an initialization member.

`CloudflareProviderInitializationJournal` adds exactly `{ kind: 'provider', providerKind: 'cloudflare_api_token', operation: 'initialize', credentialName: 'storefront-cache-host-read-token' | 'storefront-cache-host-purge-token', state: 'prepared' | 'activated' | 'new_verified' | 'committed', newTokenId: lowercaseHex32, newPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64 }`. It contains no `oldTokenId`, `oldPolicySha256`, R2 probe field, owner-revocation field, `revocationProofKind`, or `providerRevocationReceipt`. `CloudflareProviderRotationJournal` adds exactly `{ kind: 'provider', providerKind: 'cloudflare_api_token', operation: 'rotate', credentialName: 'storefront-cache-host-read-token' | 'storefront-cache-host-purge-token', state: 'prepared' | 'activated' | 'new_verified' | 'awaiting_owner_revocation' | 'old_revoked' | 'committed', oldTokenId: lowercaseHex32, newTokenId: lowercaseHex32, oldPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64, newPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64, ownerRevocationApprovalId: stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$ | null, ownerRevokedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis | null, revocationProofKind: 'cloudflare_token_self_verify_denied' | null, providerRevocationReceipt: lowercaseHex64 | null }` and contains no R2 probe field. Its four nullable revocation properties are all null through `awaiting_owner_revocation` and all non-null in `old_revoked | committed`; mixed combinations are invalid.

`R2ParentInitializationJournal` adds exactly `{ kind: 'provider', providerKind: 'r2_s3_parent', operation: 'initialize', credentialName: 'storefront-r2-publisher-parent' | 'storefront-r2-gc-parent', state: 'prepared' | 'activated' | 'new_verified' | 'committed', newTokenId: lowercaseHex32, newPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64, r2ProbeObjectKey: stringMatching^__baci_control__/credential-probe/[0-9a-f-]{36}\.txt$, r2ProbeCleanupState: 'pending' | 'deleted', r2NewAcceptedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64 | null }`. The probe-key UUID must equal `rotationId`; the proof is null with `pending`, and `new_verified | committed` require `deleted` plus a non-null proof. It contains no old identity/policy/proof or owner-revocation field. `R2ParentRotationJournal` adds exactly `{ kind: 'provider', providerKind: 'r2_s3_parent', operation: 'rotate', credentialName: 'storefront-r2-publisher-parent' | 'storefront-r2-gc-parent', state: 'prepared' | 'activated' | 'new_verified' | 'awaiting_owner_revocation' | 'old_revoked' | 'committed', oldTokenId: lowercaseHex32, newTokenId: lowercaseHex32, oldPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64, newPolicySha256: lowercaseHex64, r2ProbeObjectKey: stringMatching^__baci_control__/credential-probe/[0-9a-f-]{36}\.txt$, r2ProbeCleanupState: 'pending' | 'deleted', r2OldAcceptedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64, r2NewAcceptedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64 | null, r2OldRevokedProofSha256: lowercaseHex64 | null, ownerRevocationApprovalId: stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$ | null, ownerRevokedAt: rfc3339UtcMillis | null, revocationProofKind: 'r2_s3_credential_invalid' | null, providerRevocationReceipt: lowercaseHex64 | null }`. Its probe key has the same rotation binding; new accepted proof and cleanup become non-null/`deleted` by `new_verified`; the old-revoked proof plus all four owner-revocation properties remain null through `awaiting_owner_revocation` and are all non-null in `old_revoked | committed`. Every provider initialization advances `prepared -> activated -> new_verified -> committed`; every provider rotation advances `prepared -> activated -> new_verified -> awaiting_owner_revocation -> old_revoked -> committed`. No schema admits an initialization revocation state, a provider-kind-incompatible proof, or a partial state tuple.

`HmacCredentialRotationJournal` retains `{ kind: 'hmac', operation: 'initialize' | 'rotate', databaseOperationAction: 'initialize' | 'rotate', databaseOperationApprovalId, databaseOperationCompletedAt, databaseOperationReceiptSha256, previousExpiresAt, currentProofVerifiedAt, previousProofVerifiedAt, previousRemovalApprovalId, databaseRemovalReceiptSha256, previousRemovalEvidenceSha256, previousRemovedAt }` and forbids every provider field. Its `operation` equals `databaseOperationAction`. Initialization has the mandatory shared null old-ciphertext hash, null expiry/previous-proof/removal fields, and advances `prepared -> activated -> new_verified -> committed` after the bound current proof. Rotation has a non-null old-ciphertext hash plus non-null expiry and advances `prepared -> activated -> new_verified -> overlap_wait -> previous_expired -> committed`. The four database-operation fields are required from `prepared` onward and equal the supplied receipt plus proof-returned `currentOperation`; the four removal fields remain null before the owner removal checkpoint and are all required afterward. All schemas use Zod `strict()`, define canonical property order, and have exhaustive property-presence, nullability, and state-combination tests.

After operator fingerprint confirmation, the importer saves the active ciphertext as `previous/<name>` when one exists, atomically installs `next/<name>` at the one exact active path, fsyncs it and `/etc/credstore.encrypted`, reloads PID 1, and runs only the mapped unit's credentialless/dry-run probe. Failure before `new_verified` restores `previous/<name>` atomically when present or removes the failed initial active ciphertext when absent. For `kind: 'provider'`, success records `new_verified` and performs the new-credential identity/policy/read-back/capability-denial proof. If `operation = 'initialize'`, it requires the mandatory shared `oldCiphertextSha256: null`, requires every provider-specific old/revocation property plus the previous ciphertext to be absent, records `committed`, and performs idempotent journal cleanup without printing or requesting a revocation checkpoint. If `operation = 'rotate'`, it requires the saved previous ciphertext and complete old identity/policy/accepted-proof evidence, records `awaiting_owner_revocation`, prints only the rotation ID, old token ID, reviewed policy hash, and exact owner instruction, then exits without possessing or requesting any token-management credential. The owner revokes that exact old token through a separate authenticated Cloudflare dashboard/operator surface. A later mutually exclusive `--resume <rotationId>` invocation is legal only for provider rotation in `awaiting_owner_revocation`; it reads one canonical non-secret TTY receipt `{ "version": 1, "rotationId": uuid, "credentialName": fixedName, "oldTokenId": lowercaseHex32, "revokedAt": rfc3339UtcMillis, "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$ }`, requires exact journal equality, and never accepts a token value. It decrypts the saved old ciphertext only through an anonymous pipe from exact-path `/usr/bin/systemd-creds decrypt --name=<fixedMappedCredentialName> <previousCiphertextPath> -`, then requires the old narrow Cloudflare token's self-verify request to return the qualified credential-invalid result or the old R2 S3 credential to return the qualified authentication-invalid result on the fixed harmless canary read while the new credential succeeds. Permission denial, timeout, ambiguous 4xx/5xx, policy drift, or failure to distinguish invalid credentials leaves `awaiting_owner_revocation`. `providerRevocationReceipt` is exactly the lowercase SHA-256 of `canonicalOwnerReceiptBytes + "\n" + canonicalOldInvalidNewValidProofBytes`; only that matching receipt plus proof records `old_revoked`, after which the importer deletes/fsyncs the previous ciphertext and records `committed`. The importer never calls a token create/update/delete endpoint and recovery never restores a credential once `old_revoked` is durable.

For `kind: 'hmac'`, the owner runs the installed database-secret manager `--rotate`; its direct `postgres`-only routine installs the new `current`, retains the old value as `previous`, and returns the canonical database-generated operation receipt with its 15-minute `previousExpiresAt`. The owner supplies the same new value plus that exact receipt to the no-echo importer. Before activation, the importer recalculates the scalar-core receipt hash and requires a new-current proof whose complete `currentOperation` equals the receipt; a secret mismatch, forged receipt, stale approval, or superseding operation fails while the database previous slot keeps the old publisher valid. The importer persists the bound rotation approval ID/receipt hash in its HMAC journal, then invokes `/usr/bin/systemd-creds decrypt --name=<fixedMappedCredentialName> <ciphertextPath> -` twice in strictly sequential closed child graphs—first for the active ciphertext and then for the saved previous ciphertext—feeding each plaintext only through an anonymous pipe into the same installed importer's fixed `--verify-hmac-slot` child mode. That child uses the same closed non-secret Supabase configuration as the coordinator, constructs a fresh fixed-action/fixed-subject/empty-payload proof, calls only `verify_storefront_publisher_secret_rotation_proof`, requires `matchedSlot` to be `current` then `previous`, requires `previousPresent: true`, null `previousRemoval`, the same non-null expiry, and the same bound `currentOperation` from both valid responses, records the database-returned timestamps, and zeroes its mutable buffer before the child exits; no process holds both plaintext HMAC values. Only then may the journal enter `overlap_wait`. At or after database `previousExpiresAt`, the importer first repeats the active proof and requires the same current-operation binding, then stops with a bounded instruction containing the stored rotation approval ID and exact expiry; the owner runs the database-secret manager `--remove` with a distinct approval ID and that expected expiry. The importer never receives database-owner authority. After that owner action, `--resume <rotationId>` reads exactly `{ "version": 1, "rotationId": uuid, "credentialName": "storefront-publisher-hmac", "databaseRemovalReceipt": { "version": 1, "action": "remove", "approvalId": stringMatching^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$, "expectedPreviousExpiresAt": rfc3339UtcMillis, "removedAt": rfc3339UtcMillis, "receiptSha256": lowercaseHex64 } }`; the nested value is the exact manager receipt, while the outer envelope binds it to the sole unfinished journal. The importer recalculates its scalar-core hash, requires the expected expiry to equal the journal, repeats the current proof, and requires `matchedSlot: 'current'`, `previousPresent: false`, `previousExpiresAt: null`, the unchanged bound `currentOperation`, and `previousRemoval` exactly equal to the nested receipt. It persists the nested `receiptSha256` unchanged as `databaseRemovalReceiptSha256`, then submits the saved-previous proof and one schema-valid fixed-action/fixed-subject/empty-payload control proof signed with a fresh random unknown key and requires their canonical denial status/body bytes to be exactly equal. The former-previous denial proves only that the old value is no longer accepted; the current-valid response plus matching removal receipt is the authority that the previous slot is absent. It sets `previousRemovalEvidenceSha256` to the lowercase SHA-256 of `canonicalDatabaseRemovalReceiptBytes + "\n" + canonicalCurrentValidPreviousAbsentProofBytes + "\n" + canonicalFormerPreviousDenialBytes + "\n" + canonicalRandomUnknownDenialBytes`, records the database-derived `previousRemovedAt`, and only then enters `previous_expired`. The two hashes are never compared or substituted for one another. It never uses VPS wall time and cannot remove the previous ciphertext before all database-derived observations and denial-equivalence checks succeed.

Every importer start scans unfinished journals before accepting a new secret. Common `prepared`/`activated` recovery removes or resumes the candidate and either restores a still-authoritative previous value or advances after a successful mapped probe. Provider initialization at `new_verified` re-proves only the new credential and commits directly; provider rotation at `new_verified` advances to `awaiting_owner_revocation`, that state performs no mutation until a matching owner receipt and old-invalid/new-valid proof arrive, and `old_revoked` verifies the new active ciphertext and removes the unusable previous ciphertext. An initialization journal can never enter or resume a revocation state. HMAC `new_verified | overlap_wait` reloads database `previousExpiresAt`, sequentially re-proves the state permitted at that database time, and waits without deleting; `previous_expired` verifies current-only behavior and removes the previous ciphertext. `committed` performs idempotent cleanup for any union member. Ambiguous provider/database status, clock source other than database time for HMAC expiry, variant-field mismatch, hash/name/path mismatch, two active journals for one name, an unexpected file in the active credstore, or loss of the required owner-revocation/removal receipt fails closed for owner recovery. Receipts contain only the variant's ciphertext SHA-256, exact active credential name/path, bounded provider IDs/policy hashes/owner approval or HMAC expiry/proof timestamps, journal transitions, and probe result—never plaintext hashes beyond the operator-only truncated confirmation, secret values, or decrypted bodies. Tests interrupt after every encrypt/write/fsync/rename/reload/probe/owner-checkpoint/read-back/database-expiry/journal transition, restart the importer in every state of all five union members, inspect the credstore search path plus `/proc`-equivalent argv/environment/filesystem snapshots, and prove exact-name `LoadCredentialEncrypted` resolution, deterministic recovery, direct first-import commit without old-token fields, no token-management API call, no provider-rotation transition without owner receipt plus independent denial, strict sequential HMAC plaintext lifetimes, no premature HMAC cleanup, no rollback after proven provider revocation, and no plaintext artifact.

The credential importer is a ninth standalone esbuild entrypoint. The database-secret manager is a tenth standalone esbuild entrypoint with `postgres` bundled and no external module resolution. `apps/web/src/schemas/storefront-release-credential-rotation.ts` has exactly one primary runtime export, `credentialRotationJournalSchema`; its four provider-member schemas, HMAC schema, inferred member types, canonical-order tables, and state refinements remain module-internal. The importer and tests import only `credentialRotationJournalSchema` from that file and narrow through its discriminants; the artifact contains compiled importer/manager bundles, not source `.mjs` runtime scripts. Both metafiles and bundle hashes are governed exactly like the service entrypoints. Static and clean-runtime tests prove the manager alone contains `postgres`/database-owner connection code, the importer alone contains credential rotation/decrypt child code, and neither can import or execute the other.

- [ ] **Step 2B: Implement immutable writes and verification**

Object layout:

```text
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/documents/index.html
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/documents/404.html
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/documents/products/{slug}/index.html
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/documents/ads.txt
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/documents/{defaultIndexNowKey}.txt
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/sitemap.xml
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/assets/theme.css
releases/{merchantId}/{releaseId}/assets/cart.js
hosts/{normalizedHostname}.json
```

Immutable content objects use `If-None-Match: *`. Immediately before each Put, the publisher revalidates the claim token, generation, and publication epoch through the fixed proof-gated RPC; a lifecycle transition may therefore leave harmless orphan objects but cannot activate a stale release. The publisher calculates SHA-256 before each content-object upload, reads it back through the authenticated S3 endpoint, verifies its complete body/length/content type once, and records the returned exact ETag plus SHA-256 in either the normal route table or `manifest.notFound`. Only after all content-object metadata is final does it revalidate again, serialize and hash `manifest.json`, upload that manifest with `If-None-Match: *`, and read it back to verify its exact body/hash/length/content type/ETag. The manifest never records its own hash or ETag; the pointer records `manifestSha256`, while the release ledger records the separately returned manifest ETag/length. Pointer creation also uses `If-None-Match: *`; pointer replacement must use `If-Match: {previousEtag}` and fail on any mismatch. Revalidate once more immediately before pointer CAS. A pointer contains exactly the shared discriminated `StorefrontReleasePointer` fields. An older release, lifecycle generation, or publication epoch must never overwrite a newer pointer even if its worker finishes last.

- [ ] **Step 3A: Implement activation and reconciliation only after proof**

Read every uploaded content object's body/metadata through the authenticated publisher endpoint and compare the route/not-found SHA-256/length/content type/ETag, then independently read and verify `manifest.json` against the locally serialized bytes and the hash destined for `pointer.manifestSha256`. This full-body integrity work happens at publication, not on the public request hot path. Immediately before CAS, reload the current generation and the count of non-deleted release prefixes through the proof-gated RPC; refuse activation when it would exceed checked-in `maximumReleasesPendingDeletion`, leaving the current pointer serving while eligible cleanup restores headroom. After CAS, record the pointer ETag/version in PostgreSQL. If the database call fails after CAS, leave the new pointer serving and let the reconciler repair the ledger; never replace it with the previous pointer automatically. If CAS itself fails, read the current pointer and succeed only when it already names the same release/generation.

`baci-storefront-release-publisher.timer` is installed disabled with a one-minute cadence and, only after Task 12 Step 4A records successful bucket/domain, HMAC, parent-policy, canary, credential-import, and unit read-back receipts, is enabled to start the non-root `baci-storefront-release-publisher.service`; no user crontab entry, user service, mutable checkout script, root application supervisor, or deploy-time sudo path may start the publisher. The service executes the closure-local Node plus publisher-coordinator bundle as `baci-storefront-publisher-coordinator`, receives only Supabase anon configuration and `LoadCredentialEncrypted=storefront-publisher-hmac`, and has no provider credential or provider-client import. Two root-installed socket/template pairs are named exactly `baci-storefront-release-publisher-r2-signer.socket`/`baci-storefront-release-publisher-r2-signer@.service` and `baci-storefront-release-publisher-r2-worker.socket`/`baci-storefront-release-publisher-r2-worker@.service`. Each socket has one literal `ListenStream` below a tmpfiles-created `root:baci-storefront-publisher-coordinator` mode-`0710` directory, socket owner `baci-storefront-publisher-coordinator`, mode `0600`, `Accept=yes`, `FileDescriptorName=connection`, no `Service=`, `MaxConnections=1`, `Backlog=1`, `TriggerLimitIntervalSec=0`, `PollLimitIntervalSec=60s`, and `PollLimitBurst=4`. Each same-basename template receives exactly fd 3 with `LISTEN_FDS=1` and `LISTEN_FDNAMES=connection`, sets `StandardInput=null`, `StandardOutput=journal`, `StandardError=journal`, and `CollectMode=inactive-or-failed`, and constructs exactly `new net.Socket({ fd: 3, readable: true, writable: true, allowHalfOpen: true })`. Application code can connect only to those literal sockets; it cannot create/replace a socket node, call D-Bus, `systemctl`, or `busctl`, select a unit/socket path, or parse a request as root.

`baci-storefront-edge-pointer-rollback.timer` is likewise installed disabled and may be enabled only with the same Task 12 provisioning receipts. It runs every five seconds with `AccuracySec=1s`, `RandomizedDelaySec=0`, `Persistent=true`, and overlap prevention through the shared database admission plus absolute R2 mutation lock. Its fixed non-root service executes only the closure-local rollback-processor bundle, receives the same anon/HMAC inputs as the publisher coordinator, and can activate only the existing publisher signer/worker sockets. The rollback service has `RuntimeMaxSec=45s`, cannot select ordinary release/lifecycle work, and treats an active lifecycle admission or job as a retryable higher-priority block. Installation/read-back tests require both timers enabled before the production apex route, prove no GitHub workflow or Vercel runtime holds an R2 credential, and inject scheduler delay, lock contention, process crash, stale epoch, pointer precondition failure, exact already-committed recovery, and lifecycle preemption while measuring owner-request-to-confirmed-origin p99 at or below 30 seconds. If that bound is not proved, pointer CAS cannot be advertised as the 60-second first rollback path; exact-route removal remains the first safety action.

Priority must preempt a lock holder, not merely reorder the next admission. Every publisher/GC coordinator and provider worker runs a concurrent database epoch/admission watcher at a fixed two-second cadence while it owns or waits for the shared lock. Every provider request has a ten-second total deadline wired to an `AbortController`. When rollback/lifecycle advances the epoch or revokes the admission, the watcher aborts in-flight I/O, prevents every new provider call/checkpoint, returns a fenced result, and releases the shared lock within 15 seconds of the database transition; it never treats an aborted mutation as retry permission and leaves reconciliation to exact read-only evidence. The watcher uses the same bounded HMAC status RPC and carries no extra provider authority. Real-systemd tests hold the lock in every ordinary publisher/GC phase, trigger rollback and lifecycle separately, inject a nonresponsive provider, and require database transition-to-lock-release at most 15 seconds, no post-revocation provider call, no second POST/Put, and the correct higher-priority job next. These bounds are included in the measured 30-second rollback p99; without them the production route gate fails.

The signer template runs directly as `baci-storefront-publisher-r2-signer`, receives only `LoadCredentialEncrypted=storefront-r2-publisher-parent`, and has no HMAC or S3 client. After verifying the EOF-terminated authorization request and recording issuance, it binds `/run/baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session/session.sock`, sends `signer_ready` and response EOF on fd 3, and waits for the sole credential-source connection. The worker template runs directly as `baci-storefront-publisher-r2-worker`, receives `LoadCredential=baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session:/run/baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session/session.sock` plus `LoadCredentialEncrypted=storefront-publisher-hmac`, and has no parent. It hashes/parses the exact temporary credential bytes, verifies the matching delivery record before the first provider call, and imports the publisher/reconciler/R2-writer graph. Both templates use the closure-local Node, distinct non-login users and credential directories, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `PrivateTmp=true`, `PrivateMounts=true`, `ProtectProc=invisible`, `ProcSubset=pid`, `NoNewPrivileges=true`, empty capability bounding/ambient/inheritable sets, minimal `RestrictAddressFamilies`, closed environments, and exact read/write allowlists. The root service manager is the intended privileged activation/credential-transfer component.

The one-shot credential-source socket is deliberately a root trust boundary, not a claimed sub-root authentication boundary. Its parent directory is signer-owned mode `0700`, its socket is mode `0600`, and no other service user can traverse or connect; host root remains in the trusted computing base because root can already read the encrypted parent source, inspect service memory, or replace units. Node `24.11.1` exposes neither Unix `SO_PEERCRED` nor the abstract `getpeername()` value through a supported public API, so implementation code must not use private `_handle` methods, add an ungoverned native addon, or claim it has authenticated PID 1, a unit name, a credential ID, or systemd's documented abstract peer name. The real-systemd qualification gate therefore does not require an unimplemented peer-name observer. Instead it reads back the installed root-owned unit with `systemctl cat` plus `systemctl show`, requires the exact worker template `ExecStart`, exact literal `LoadCredential=baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session:/run/baci-storefront-publisher-r2-session/session.sock`, expected `User`, credential ID, fragment/drop-in paths and hashes, starts that worker only through its fixed socket, proves the signer accepts exactly one credential-source connection while alive, and requires the worker's credential-file SHA-256 and parsed session SHA-256 to equal the database-recorded signer values byte-for-byte before the first provider call. The qualified syscall trace remains limited to observable connect/accept/read/write/shutdown/close ordering and must not infer an unavailable peer address. Filesystem ownership, exact unit read-back, byte-exact transfer, and the explicit root TCB are the runtime security contract. If excluding another root process becomes a requirement, implementation stops for a separately reviewed attested native credential broker rather than silently weakening this contract.

Process tests inspect `/proc` status, mountinfo, descriptors, groups, capabilities, `NoNewPrivs`, environment, credential directories, and static bundle graphs for all three publisher users. They prove the coordinator has HMAC but no provider secret/client, the signer has the parent but no HMAC/S3 client/worker directory, and the worker has HMAC plus one temporary session but receives `EACCES`/absence for the parent and signer directory. No unit runs as root, regains privilege, observes a second provider credential, inherits an unexpected descriptor, or resolves host Node/`PATH`; explicitly reject `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, a parent in coordinator/worker, a temporary session in coordinator/signer, GC-family variables, and any wrong credential filename.

Each coordinator first obtains a database-derived `lifecycle_pointer | edge_pointer_rollback | release_publish` admission, then waits for the shared absolute `/run/lock/baci-storefront-r2-mutation.lock`, revalidates admission after locking, and only then claims the exact work class allowed by that fixed executable. It holds the OS lock and admission while it requests signer activation, validates readiness EOF, requests worker activation, waits for the bounded worker result, and confirms the final fenced database state; the worker independently revalidates claim/generation/epoch before every provider operation and final RPC. Task 8 GC follows the same admission-before-lock/revalidation protocol. Separate per-script locks are forbidden; an admission timeout while waiting for `flock` performs no claim/provider call. The ordinary coordinator has `RuntimeMaxSec=240s`; rollback coordinator has `RuntimeMaxSec=45s`; signer has `RuntimeMaxSec=45s`; worker has `RuntimeMaxSec=180s`; all have `TimeoutStopSec=15s`. The ordinary coordinator refuses to activate the worker after second 45, the worker refuses a new provider call in the final 15 seconds of its exact-180-second session, and the ordinary coordinator reserves its final 15 seconds for database checkpoint/release. The publisher session therefore expires no later than second 225 and at least 15 seconds before the four-minute admission; these bounds keep the sequential signer-plus-worker path inside the coordinator envelope without granting either coordinator process-control authority. A `lifecycle_pointer` admission carries one closed database-derived deletion phase: `lifecycle_pointer_to_gone` may perform only the terminal-pointer action and must release its claim/admission after erasure admission; `lifecycle_finalize` is a later fresh admission/claim available only after the terminal-GC receipt and may perform only the finalizer sequence; `edge_pointer_rollback` can direct the rollback worker only to the immutable owner rollback job; and `release_publish` can direct the ordinary worker only to the claimed release build. The database never grants release publication while erasure-admitted `gone` GC is pending. Because every publisher credential expires before its four-minute lease, the erasure-admitted `gone` admission proof may use the exact nine-minute bound of one pre-existing lease plus one five-minute GC cadence. The one-minute publication interval leaves enough budget for the 120-second activation p95 target, while the rollback service uses its independently demonstrated fast path and five-second cadence. Installation tests verify timer/socket/template ownership, immutable `ExecStart`, fd-3 half-close sequencing, one-shot manager credential transfer, issuance/delivery records, distinct users/credentials/import graphs, exact independent runtime/stop/session deadlines, explicit root TCB and absence of a false PID-1 authentication claim, zero root-running application services, admission priority/revalidation, shared-lock identity, no publisher/rollback crontab entry, dry-run default, receipt reproduction, and rollback/read-back.

`backfill-storefront-hostname-registry.ts` is renamed/implemented as an Ogabassey-only dry-run-by-default seed. It resolves `ogabassey.com` through trusted live database ownership, prints the redacted merchant match and reason codes, and requires an owner-confirmed UUID before `--apply`. It rejects CLI-supplied alternate hosts/merchants and never enumerates or writes other merchants. Re-running is idempotent. Production routing is blocked until the one registry row and validated pointer match the approved merchant/generation.

**Interfaces:**

- Produces `selectDeletableReleasePrefixes(input): readonly DeletableReleasePrefix[]`.
- Defines the database-derived `ActiveReleaseServingHost` closed union: ordinal `0` is `releases.ogabassey.com`; ordinal `1` is `releases-preview.ogabassey.com`; valid active sets are only `[0]` after sealed preview detachment or `[0,1]` while preview is attached. An erasure-admitted GC claim persists the ordered ordinals, `active_serving_hosts_sha256` over the canonical ordinal/hostname array, and one append-only per-ordinal state `pending_classification | active_owned | ownership_recorded | purge_recorded | detached_absent | r2_rechecked | public_absence_verified`. The claim is created only from the lifecycle's profile-locked immutable topology receipt; neither CLI, socket, R2, provider response, fresh inventory, nor caller body selects/reorders/extends the set.
- `claim_storefront_release_gc_batch` first locks the one Ogabassey profile, requires a topology-bound deletion-intent event chain or an `erasure_recovery` event, and atomically copies its sealed `{ intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, releaseDomainTopologyState, activeServingHostsSha256, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256 }` into the claim. Ordinary claims reject an executing/reconciling/terminal-drift topology operation; an accepted deletion in terminal drift instead uses the recovery transaction's conservative `[0,1]` seal. Both `[0]` and `[0,1]` reference the intent's non-expiring `lifecycle_deletion` fence so preview cannot be reattached while singleton erasure is active; it remains held across every prefix page, replacement claim, socket phase, retry, crash-recovery read, VPS outage, terminal-GC seal, and finalization. Every phase/checkpoint/finalizer accepts and rechecks the complete copied tuple. A missing/mismatched intent, event head, fence, generation, or host-set hash blocks provider action and enters same-intent recovery; it cannot release topology authority, discard the deletion request, or permit teardown/attachment. Recovery may replace a fence only atomically with another fence for the same immutable intent. Successful erasure finalization is the sole transition that clears it.
- Produces `verifyCloudflareReleaseCacheHostReadPolicy(...) => VerifiedReleaseCacheHostReadCapability` for one expiring, IP-restricted token with only the current `DNS Read` permission on the exact `ogabassey.com` zone plus the narrowest current `Workers R2 Storage Read` resource/permission that Gate B proves can read the production bucket's two permitted custom-domain endpoints. It rejects Cache Purge, DNS write, R2 write, or another bucket/account/zone and can call only the fixed DNS-record read and production-bucket custom-domain reads derived from a database-issued ordinal.
- Produces `readCloudflareReleaseCacheHostClassification(...) => Promise<CloudflareReleaseCacheHostClassificationProof>` from live provider responses, implemented in the existing ownership-reader module so the cache-read socket remains the sole read-only provider boundary. Its request contains only the claim-bound ordinal and single-use proof; after database validation it derives the exact domain. The result is a closed union. `active_owned` requires exactly one qualified proxied DNS record plus the exact enabled/active production bucket/custom-domain ownership tuple and emits the two-minute ownership proof. `detached_absent` requires the qualified R2 custom-domain endpoint to return the exact authoritative absence shape for that derived domain, plus DNS absence or a live DNS record independently proven not to route to the deleted bucket, and preview Worker-domain absence/non-serving when the ordinal requires it; it emits a bounded non-serving proof and never a purge authorization. Both parse each response's qualified Cloudflare HTTP `Date` independently and bind the deployment, read policy, intent/event head, host-set hash, ordinal, derived-host hash, and observations. A missing, invalid, future-skewed, expired, duplicated, paginated, mixed, foreign-serving, or ambiguous tuple and every 401/403/429/5xx/timeout returns only `retryable_failure` and records no classification. The reader cannot import or invoke purge code.
- Produces `verifyCloudflareReleaseCacheHostPurgePolicy(...) => VerifiedReleaseCacheHostPurgeCapability` for a different expiring, IP-restricted production token with only Cache Purge on the exact `ogabassey.com` zone. The owner-exported token policy, live token ID/status, current permission-group ID, expiration, zone resource, and reviewed policy SHA-256 must all match before branding the capability. Provider authority is unavoidably zone-wide; the wrapper derives one active hostname from a database-issued ordinal and emits only that singleton body's canonical bytes, with no generic purge client exported. No application function, process, unit, environment, credential directory, or import graph may receive both read and purge capabilities.
- Produces a provider-credentialless `cleanup-storefront-releases.ts` coordinator plus `requestStorefrontReleaseGcPhase(request, phase): Promise<StorefrontReleaseGcPhaseResult>`. `phase` is a closed TypeScript enum mapped inside the client to exactly four literal filesystem sockets; no caller supplies a path or unit name. Every canonical maximum-8-KiB request variant contains `{ version, runId, claimToken, publicationEpoch, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256, ...phaseProofs, expiresAt }`; `hostOrdinal` is database-issued and never a hostname. Cache-read/cache-purge use one phase action proof bound to that complete ordered tuple. The R2 worker uses the same tuple in `verifying_public_absence`; the R2 signer additionally carries `signerRunId` plus distinct issuance/delivery proofs, each bound to the same claim/intent/event/generation/fence/ordinal/set tuple. Before any provider credential is read or provider call occurs, the phase RPC rechecks exact equality with the live database claim, authority, event head, profile fence, and current ordinal. The selected socket supplies phase identity. The coordinator remains unprivileged/provider-credentialless, generates the phase-specific single-use proof set from the database-returned tuple, half-closes after one request, and accepts only the strict bounded result; the service manager activates the matching non-root phase directly with no generic launcher.
- Cache-read/cache-purge socket results are canonical closed objects `{ version, runId, result: 'ownership_recorded' | 'detached_absent_recorded' | 'purge_recorded' | 'retryable_failure', intentId, intentEventSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, derivedHostnameSha256, phaseReceiptSha256, reasonCode }`; the R2/public-absence result is the same shape with `result: 'public_absence_verified' | 'retryable_failure'`. The coordinator, database journal, provider-operation receipt, and terminal-GC/finalization receipts require byte-identical claim/intent/event/fence/ordinal/set-hash/generation/result binding. A result with a raw hostname, omitted/changed tuple field, mismatched derived-host hash, future phase, or duplicate receipt is terminal drift rather than a retry; terminal drift preserves the lifecycle fence and durable intent. Golden-vector tests mutate each tuple field independently in every request/result/action proof and require rejection before credential access, provider calls, or database checkpoints, including an old envelope after same-intent fence-token rotation.

- Claims at most one database-approved prefix per drain invocation only when no publisher/lifecycle lease is live, captures the publication epoch, and deletes at most 100 R2 objects per page using an exact-prefix temporary GC session. It persists the opaque next cursor after each successful page and may process multiple pages only while the five-minute process deadline retains a 30-second shutdown margin; interruption resumes from the fenced cursor without marking completion.
- Retains the `edge` pointer target, the database-active release for reversible `origin | suspended | unpublished` modes, the two newest retired releases, and every release newer than seven days. After both a proven `gone` pointer and an `erasure_admitted` deletion intent, all release prefixes for that immutable merchant ID become immediately eligible and must be removed within 24 hours; the `hosts/{hostname}.json` gone tombstone is never garbage-collected by this job.
