- [ ] **Step 3: Implement double-fenced cleanup**

`cleanup-storefront-releases.ts` is the coordinator, not a provider client. It runs as the dedicated unprivileged `baci-storefront-gc-coordinator` user, first obtains the database-derived `erasure_admitted_gc | retention_gc` admission, then waits for `/run/lock/baci-storefront-r2-mutation.lock`, revalidates admission after locking, and holds both through the final RPC. Installation creates root-owned `/run/lock/baci-storefront-r2/` through a tmpfiles rule with group `baci-storefront-r2-mutators` and mode `0770`; only the GC coordinator and publisher coordinator are members, the shared lock is opened mode `0660` without following symlinks, and no signer/worker phase service joins that group. Its systemd unit has only the Supabase anon configuration and the narrow GC HMAC credential loaded through `LoadCredentialEncrypted`; it has no R2 or Cloudflare credential and the TypeScript import graph contains no provider SDK/client. It claims one candidate through `claim_storefront_release_gc_batch` only when the returned class matches the database candidate, canonicalizes the bounded admission/run/claim/epoch/action-proof/hash/expiry request in memory, and calls `requestStorefrontReleaseGcPhase` with that request and the database-approved closed phase. Expired/mismatched admission releases the OS lock and performs no provider action. It creates no per-phase handoff file: the database is the only durable cross-phase handoff, while the socket request is an expiring, single-use authorization envelope that every phase independently verifies against current database state.

For every erasure-admitted claim, the coordinator fetches the database-sealed deletion-intent fact/event head, `ActiveReleaseServingHost` array, hash, topology generation, and same-intent lifecycle-deletion fence before the first cache phase and treats the array as a work queue. It can request cache-read for the current database-owned ordinal and cache-purge only after that ordinal is `active_owned`; it includes the sealed intent/event hash, host-set hash, generation, and fence binding in every canonical envelope and never carries a hostname. The database advances the per-host phase only on a matching canonical result and cannot seal the terminal-GC receipt until every ordinal reaches `public_absence_verified` through either `active_owned` purge acknowledgement or `detached_absent` non-serving proof. The GC finish transaction seals that receipt and leaves the intent at `erasure_admitted`; the next publisher cadence obtains a fresh `lifecycle_pointer` admission with `deletionPhase: 'lifecycle_finalize'` plus a fresh lifecycle claim; that finalizer transaction independently revalidates it, appends `finalized`, refreshes the projection, deletes auth/merchant state, scrubs authority, and releases only the same-intent fence. Crash recovery rereads the same immutable fact/event/hash/generation/fence/ordinal state rather than rescanning, recomputing, renewing, or expiring topology authority.

Four root-installed filesystem socket units provide the only activation boundary: `baci-storefront-release-gc-r2-signer.socket`, `baci-storefront-release-gc-r2-worker.socket`, `baci-storefront-release-gc-cache-read.socket`, and `baci-storefront-release-gc-cache-purge.socket`. Each has one literal `ListenStream` and `FileDescriptorName=connection` under a tmpfiles-created `root:baci-storefront-gc-coordinator` mode-`0710` directory, socket ownership `baci-storefront-gc-coordinator`, and mode `0600`; the coordinator may traverse and connect but cannot create, unlink, rename, or replace a socket node. Each uses `Accept=yes` and relies only on systemd's mandatory same-basename template pairing (`name.socket` to `name@.service`); `Service=` is absent because systemd permits it only with `Accept=no`. The connection instance `%i` is transport metadata only and is never interpreted as a run ID or authority. Each matching service receives the accepted stream only through systemd's native socket-activation descriptor at file descriptor 3 with exactly `LISTEN_PID=<self>`, `LISTEN_FDS=1`, and `LISTEN_FDNAMES=connection`; it sets `StandardInput=null`, `StandardOutput=journal`, `StandardError=journal`, and `CollectMode=inactive-or-failed`. The entrypoint rejects a missing/extra/wrong-owner/wrong-type descriptor and constructs exactly `new net.Socket({ fd: 3, readable: true, writable: true, allowHalfOpen: true })` as the sole full-duplex stream. `allowHalfOpen: true` is mandatory because the coordinator deliberately sends request EOF before the phase finishes database/provider work; Node's default `false` would automatically close the response half and make the protocol race-dependent. The entrypoint never opens a caller-selected path and sends protocol bytes only on fd 3 so logs cannot contaminate the response. Each socket sets `MaxConnections=1`, `Backlog=1`, `TriggerLimitIntervalSec=0`, `PollLimitIntervalSec=60s`, and `PollLimitBurst=4`; Task 8's live systemd `255.4` preflight is a hard gate because `PollLimit*` requires systemd 255 or newer. The non-fatal poll limit temporarily slows excess polling instead of placing the socket in a failed state. A held-open connection, missing EOF, oversized/partial request, repeated connection, or activation storm therefore consumes at most one bounded phase slot and is terminated by the service deadline without growing an unbounded queue or requiring privileged recovery. Tests force a fifth poll, prove temporary throttling, then prove the socket remains `active (listening)` and accepts a valid request after the interval without `reset-failed` or restart.

Every activated phase runs directly as its distinct non-root provider user and reads exactly one EOF-terminated, maximum-8-KiB canonical JSON request from the accepted socket under an entrypoint deadline shorter than its unit runtime. The entrypoint requires byte equality with its fixed-order canonical serializer and then applies a strict Zod schema, rejecting duplicate/unknown/reordered properties, whitespace variants, non-UUIDv4 IDs, trailing frames, expired envelopes, and the wrong claim/proof/epoch. The socket identity supplies the phase; every entrypoint also requires the database's current phase and exact action proof to match before reading a provider credential or making a provider call. It sends at most one maximum-512-byte canonical result on the same connection under the same deadline. Signer, cache-read, and cache-purge services use `RuntimeMaxSec=60s`; the R2 worker uses `RuntimeMaxSec=240s`; all use `TimeoutStopSec=30s`, `MemoryMax=256M`, `TasksMax=64`, and `LimitNOFILE=128`. Installation read-back tests require `ProtectSystem=strict`, `PrivateTmp=true`, `PrivateMounts=true`, `ProtectProc=invisible`, `ProcSubset=pid`, `NoNewPrivileges=true`, an empty capability bounding set, phase-specific minimal `RestrictAddressFamilies` (`AF_UNIX` plus `AF_INET`/`AF_INET6` only where the phase calls a provider), explicit `ReadWritePaths` allowlists, and a closed environment. Parent/Cloudflare secrets use `LoadCredentialEncrypted`; the temporary R2 session alone uses systemd's one-shot `AF_UNIX` `LoadCredential` source. Each provider unit receives exactly one provider credential under its private `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY`; provider secrets never appear in argv, socket requests/results, inherited environment, coordinator memory, or logs. The four units have disjoint credential names/directories and entrypoints, and each rejects every other provider credential filename/variable. The coordinator rereads the database after every bounded local result and either requests the next fixed socket or exits retryably; a socket result, EOF, process exit, or connection failure never constitutes durable completion.

For `deleting | verify_from_start | verifying_public_absence`, the coordinator first connects to the fixed R2-signer activation socket, sends the proof-bound request, and calls `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` on its client socket so request EOF is explicit. In `verifying_public_absence` the signed request is additionally bound by the database to the current host ordinal and host-set hash; the signer derives no host data and the R2 worker later proves the fresh empty listing for that same ordinal's purge phase. The signer's `allowHalfOpen: true` stream treats that EOF only as end-of-request and keeps its writable half open while it validates, signs, and commits database issuance. Only this short-lived signer unit receives the IP-restricted R2 GC parent through `LoadCredentialEncrypted`; it runs as `baci-storefront-gc-r2-signer`, cannot import the R2 list/delete client, and has no access to the worker credential directory. `sign-storefront-release-gc-r2-session.ts` derives the exact stored prefix/action scope from the verified database claim, locally signs an at-most-ten-minute session, serializes a maximum-16-KiB canonical UTF-8 JSON object with fixed ordered keys for the temporary access key, secret, session token, account endpoint, bucket, exact action/path scope, expiry, and fresh 256-bit transfer nonce, and computes `sessionSha256 = SHA256(exactRawCanonicalJsonBytes)` plus the separate SHA-256 of the canonical nonce bytes. After the supplied issuance proof makes `record_storefront_r2_mutation_session` commit the exact `signerRunId`, it binds the one shared absolute, mode-`0600` one-shot stream socket `/run/baci-storefront-gc-r2-session/session.sock` under a root-created, signer-owned mode-`0700` runtime directory. It writes exactly one maximum-512-byte canonical `{ version: 1, runId, result: 'signer_ready', reasonCode: 'ready' }` response on the sole fd-3 stream, waits for the write callback, calls `socket.end()`, waits for the final flush/close event, and then destroys only the wrapper; the qualified syscall trace must contain `shutdown(fd3, SHUT_WR)`. No stdin/stdout duplicate of the accepted connection exists. The coordinator must receive and validate the complete readiness response followed by kernel EOF before connecting to the worker socket; a partial response, missing EOF, extra frame, mismatched run ID, or deadline expiry closes the client and records no local completion. Closing fd 3 does not terminate the signer, which remains alive solely to serve the separate credential-transfer socket. The existing shared absolute GC lock guarantees only one signer/session socket exists. On the sole connection admitted by the signer-owned mode-`0700` directory and mode-`0600` socket, the signer writes the raw canonical credential JSON bytes only and closes: EOF is the framing, with no length prefix or wrapper bytes. The explicit root TCB and no-false-PID-1-authentication contract from Task 7 applies identically; runtime JavaScript performs no unsupported `SO_PEERCRED`, private `_handle`, or abstract-peer-name inspection. It then calls `record_storefront_r2_mutation_session_delivery` with that signer run ID, the live claim token, matching session and transfer-nonce hashes, and the distinct supplied delivery proof; the database derives family `gc`. The signer unlinks the socket, zeroes mutable buffers on a best-effort basis, and exits; process termination, not JavaScript zeroization, is the authoritative memory-lifetime boundary. VPS qualification uses the installed real `.socket`/`@.service` units, reads back the exact GC worker `ExecStart`, `User`, literal `LoadCredential=baci-storefront-gc-r2-session:/run/baci-storefront-gc-r2-session/session.sock`, credential ID, fragment/drop-in paths, and hashes, inserts a deterministic delay after request EOF, traces only the observable later response-side shutdown and transfer I/O, proves the coordinator receives the complete delayed response and EOF while the signer PID remains alive, then proves `LoadCredential` preserves the exact session bytes. It neither records nor claims an unavailable abstract peer name. The worker hashes the credential file byte-for-byte before parsing, so the stored and observed session hashes have one unambiguous preimage. The session never enters a regular file, stdout, argv, environment, request, result, or log. Unit tests and the real-systemd integration test enforce the exact request `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` → delayed validation/signing/database issuance → readiness write/flush → response `shutdown(fd3, SHUT_WR)` → coordinator EOF → worker activation → manager credential fetch → delivery-RPC order and prove both the prior wait-for-EOF deadlock and Node's default auto-close race cannot recur. A crash before issuance records nothing; a crash after issuance but before delivery leaves a replaceable undelivered record; a crash after socket delivery but before the delivery RPC causes the worker to reject the unconfirmed credential without any R2 call, after which a new signer run replaces the undelivered record.

After validating the EOF-terminated `result: 'signer_ready'` response, the coordinator connects to the fixed R2-worker activation socket with the same run-bound authorization envelope. Starting the worker causes the root service manager to resolve `LoadCredential=baci-storefront-gc-r2-session:/run/baci-storefront-gc-r2-session/session.sock`, acquire the one-shot raw payload, and place it only in the worker unit's private credential directory. The worker runs as the distinct `baci-storefront-gc-r2-worker` user and cannot traverse the signer runtime/credential directory or read the encrypted parent source. `run-storefront-release-gc-r2-phase.ts` rejects the parent credential name/path/environment, reads at most 16 KiB, hashes the exact credential bytes before strict canonical parsing, and validates the session hash/family/scope/expiry plus matching delivered-at database record before its first provider call; a bounded race waiting for the delivery RPC may retry database reads only and otherwise exits retryably. The plaintext worker credential disappears when the unit deactivates. Static import tests prove signer code cannot reach list/delete primitives and worker code cannot reach parent-signing/session-issuance primitives; process tests run the two units under their real distinct users/mount namespaces and prove the worker gets `EACCES`/absence for the signer credential directory and parent source while the signer never receives access to the worker credential directory. This split follows [systemd's service-credential model](https://systemd.io/CREDENTIALS/): credentials are regular files available to all code running as that service user, while `LoadCredential` may acquire a credential from another service through an `AF_UNIX` socket, so wrapper/child separation inside one unit is not accepted as a boundary.

Before every list page, delete batch, checkpoint, and phase transition, the R2 worker revalidates the live hostname pointer and the complete `{ claimToken, publicationEpoch, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256 }` tuple against the current claim, authority, event head, profile fence, and host-phase row, plus absence of a publisher lease and the delivered temporary-session fence. Any intent/event/generation/fence/ordinal/set drift—including same-intent fence-token rotation—stops the process before another provider call or checkpoint and preserves the durable claim for recovery. It also abandons the claim if the live pointer names the candidate or its ETag differs from the claim's observed pointer ETag. In `deleting`, list at most 100 keys below only the exact claimed immutable prefix from the returned nullable cursor. For a non-empty result, delete those exact returned keys, hash the ordered listed-key sequence, and checkpoint the input cursor, provider's nullable next cursor, digest, page count, and cumulative count through `checkpoint_storefront_release_gc_prefix`; never accept a raw prefix/cursor from CLI input and never synthesize a cursor from a key. A terminal non-empty page checkpoints `nextContinuationCursor: null` and enters `verify_from_start`. An empty deleting scan calls `checkpoint_storefront_release_gc_empty_scan` and enters `verify_from_start` with a database fence. In `verify_from_start`, a new no-cursor listing strictly after that fence either returns to deletion for exact keys or, when independently empty, completes ordinary retention and moves erasure-admitted `gone` to `purging_cache`. Tests rotate the same-intent fence and mutate every other lifecycle-tuple field between each list/delete/checkpoint boundary and require zero subsequent provider calls/checkpoints. A crash before checkpoint repeats idempotently; a crash after checkpoint resumes from database state without skipping keys.

For `purging_cache` while its current database-owned ordinal is `pending_classification`, the coordinator connects only to the fixed cache-read activation socket. Its encrypted credential must match `.github/cloudflare/ogabassey-release-cache-host-read-policy.json` and contains only the Gate-B-qualified exact-zone DNS Read plus exact-production-bucket R2 custom-domain read authority and, where Gate B requires it, the exact read-only Worker-domain status endpoint. `read-storefront-release-cache-host-ownership.ts` strictly parses the canonical `{ claimToken, publicationEpoch, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256, actionProof }` envelope first. The action proof binds those scalars in that order, and a database preflight must exact-match them to the current claim/authority/event/profile/fence/ordinal before the process reads its Cloudflare credential or opens a provider connection. It then derives the sole permitted domain, performs only the fixed reads, and calls `record_storefront_release_gc_cache_host_ownership` for `active_owned` or `record_storefront_release_gc_detached_absent` for the authoritative non-serving union. It cannot accept a raw hostname, import the purge client, or accept a purge token. If observations expire while the closed read set is in flight, it records nothing and retries the entire set. Timeout, 401/403/429/5xx, incomplete response, missing/future/ambiguous time, inventory drift, or a tuple that is neither fully owned nor authoritatively non-serving leaves the ordinal retryable and sends no purge. Parser/preflight tests mutate and omit each lifecycle-tuple field, including an old fence token for the same intent, and require zero credential reads and provider calls.

For `purging_cache` with an unexpired stored `active_owned` proof for the current ordinal, the coordinator connects only to the fixed cache-purge activation socket. Its distinct encrypted credential must match `.github/cloudflare/ogabassey-release-cache-host-purge-policy.json` and contains Cache Purge only. `purge-storefront-release-cache-host.ts` strictly parses the same canonical `{ claimToken, publicationEpoch, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256, actionProof }` envelope. Its distinct purge action proof binds that ordered tuple, and a database preflight exact-matches it plus the stored `active_owned` proof before the process reads the purge credential or opens a provider connection. It then requires the database clock inside the ownership window, derives the sole hostname, and sends exactly `{ "hosts": [derivedHostname] }` to the fixed qualified zone. It persists the bounded acknowledgement through `record_storefront_release_gc_cache_purge`. The database and process reject a `detached_absent` ordinal, raw/caller hostname, alternate purge shape, wrong/replayed ordinal, any omitted/changed lifecycle-tuple field, proof/policy/deployment/zone drift, expired token/proof, or `success !== true` before provider mutation. A 401/403/429/5xx/timeout before a recorded acknowledgement remains retryable; an API-success/database-checkpoint crash safely repeats the idempotent exact-host purge. Parser/preflight tests independently mutate every lifecycle-tuple scalar and require zero credential reads/provider calls. The process cannot import the ownership reader or R2 client. Use the [Cache Purge API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/cache/methods/purge/), [purge-by-hostname behavior](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/purge-cache/purge-by-hostname/), and [R2 custom-domain read endpoint](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/r2/subresources/buckets/subresources/domains/subresources/custom/methods/get/) as implementation references.

When an `active_owned` purge or `detached_absent` classification enters `verifying_public_absence`, the coordinator obtains a fresh action-proof set over the same complete `{ claimToken, publicationEpoch, intentId, intentEventSha256, releaseDomainTopologyGeneration, lifecycleDeletionFenceTokenSha256, hostOrdinal, activeServingHostsSha256 }` tuple. Every cache-read, R2-signer, R2-worker, and public-probe phase exact-matches that tuple to live database state before credential access or provider I/O. The active path repeats the signer/R2-worker empty listing after the purge acknowledgement. The detached path performs a fresh cache-read-socket authoritative non-serving recheck after its classification and relies on the already verified empty release prefix; it never acquires purge authority. Both derive the ordinal's hostname solely from the sealed claim and run cache-busting-disabled `GET` and `HEAD` probes for the former manifest plus a deterministic document/asset sample from two independent locations. Every probe must return 404/410 with the old body/hash absent; `CF-Cache-Status` may be `MISS`, `EXPIRED`, or a newly cached negative response, but never an old-object `HIT`. For active-owned, the purge acknowledgement plus post-purge R2-empty proof is authoritative and probes are supporting evidence. For detached-absent, the second authoritative provider non-serving receipt plus public absence is authoritative and there is deliberately no purge receipt. A failed read/list/probe keeps only that ordinal retryable. The database advances only after the outcome-specific state is recorded; terminal-GC sealing requires unchanged pointer/epoch/session/lease/intent/fence bindings and `public_absence_verified` for every ordinal. Tests mutate every tuple field at every public-absence phase, including a rotated same-intent fence token, and require zero credential reads/provider calls; they also exercise both branches across all four provider-unit boundaries, prove the purge socket is unreachable for detached ordinals, and cover crash/requalification, stale sampled bodies, ordinal resume, and database-only resume. Cloudflare documents that deleted R2 objects can remain available through a custom-domain cache until TTL expiry or purge, so an R2-empty proof alone is insufficient: [R2 consistency and cache behavior](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/reference/consistency/).

The coordinator defaults to `--dry-run`; production deletion requires `--apply` plus `STOREFRONT_RELEASE_GC_ENABLED=true`. The proof separately exercises the `[0]` singleton only after sealed preview detachment and the `[0,1]` pair, with no averaged host latency. If that inequality fails, activation and erasure-completion reporting remain blocked. Task 7's root-only installer materializes the exact reviewed immutable runtime closure, creates the four distinct non-login phase users plus coordinator user, installs shared-lock/tmpfiles rules and the timer/service/socket units, runs `systemd-sysusers`, `systemd-tmpfiles --create`, `systemd-analyze verify`, and `systemctl daemon-reload`, leaves the timer and four sockets disabled, and emits the same owner-approved installation receipt. Task 12 Step 4A enables them only after the production bucket/domain, HMAC, R2-parent, cache-read, cache-purge, canary, capacity, and unit read-back receipts all pass. Read-back requires root-owned immutable `ExecStart` paths, socket-directory owner/group/mode, socket owner/mode/`Accept`, `FileDescriptorName=connection`, absence of `Service=`, same-basename template existence, `StandardInput=null`, `StandardOutput=journal`, `StandardError=journal`, `MaxConnections=1`, `Backlog=1`, disabled fatal trigger limiting, exact non-fatal poll limits, `CollectMode=inactive-or-failed`, and every service's user, runtime, memory, task, file-descriptor, sandbox, credential, and network boundary. It refuses a mutable/user-owned executable path, missing or unreproduced installation receipt, plaintext credential/session files, missing encrypted injection for a parent/Cloudflare secret, an R2 temporary session source other than the exact one-shot signer socket, parent and temporary R2 credentials in one unit/user/credential directory, a provider credential or ambient capability on the coordinator, any root phase service, any root request parser, any D-Bus/`systemctl`/`busctl` launcher in implementation code, any caller-selected socket path, socket-connected stdin/stdout, or any cron entry targeting this subsystem. Logs contain counts, salted prefix hashes, provider operation IDs, unit/phase names, and bounded reason codes only—never object bodies, merchant IDs, hostnames, secrets, full keys, URLs, raw cursors, claim tokens, authorization envelopes, transfer nonces, socket paths, or credential paths.

The GC timer is static and self-healing rather than dynamically rewritten: `OnCalendar=*-*-* *:00/5:00`, `Persistent=true`, `AccuracySec=1s`, and `RandomizedDelaySec=0`. Every tick performs the same bounded coordinator invocation. The database admission RPC prioritizes erasure-admitted `gone` work on every tick and allows a matching GC claim only after the coordinator obtains/revalidates that admission; it returns ordinary retention eligibility only when the persisted daily due time has arrived. Therefore pending erasure is admitted within five minutes after any pre-existing publisher lease expires without granting application code timer-edit or systemd-start authority. A failed invocation leaves the timer active for the next calendar tick, while `Persistent=true` catches a missed tick after downtime. Tests validate the calendar expression with `systemd-analyze calendar`, stop/fail each child and the coordinator, verify the timer remains loaded/active, advance across reboot/catch-up, prove `gone` precedence under continuous publisher attempts, and prove ordinary retention is not claimed more than once per database day.

- Produces `serializeCspHashSource(hash: CspScriptHash): string`, accepting only the manifest's canonical bare `sha256-<base64>` form and emitting exactly `'sha256-<base64>'`; prequoted values, whitespace, delimiters, and other algorithms fail closed.

Because Cloudflare versions capture bindings and compatibility settings, any other settings difference is artifact drift and `STOP`. Each invocation executes exactly one structured console event carrying the privacy-bounded delivery/error classification: ordinary outcomes use `console.log`, while caught failures use `console.error` for correct severity, never both. No second console call, uncaught-error path, raw request identity, URL, cookie, hostname, or release/customer ID is permitted. Static checks and runtime tests assert one custom event on success, rejection, origin failure, caught exception, and terminal paths. Deploy one bucket, `baci-ogabassey-storefront-releases`, with two read-only custom domains: `releases-preview.ogabassey.com` for preview cache isolation and `releases.ogabassey.com` for production. Both domains expose the same immutable objects and canonical `hosts/ogabassey.com.json`; disable the bucket's `r2.dev` URL. Before either custom domain attaches, bootstrap the fixed root object `robots.txt` outside every release/host prefix with exact UTF-8 bytes `User-agent: *\nDisallow: /\n`, SHA-256 `331ea9090db0c9f6f597bd9840fd5b171830f6e0b3ba1cb24dfa91f0c95aedc1`, `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, s-maxage=300`. It is never linked, overwritten, or garbage-collected; the canonical apex Worker still serves its separate manifest-selected public robots contract. Cache Rules bypass `/hosts/*` and cache only immutable `/releases/*`; the release rule sets the approved long edge TTL while uploaded object metadata caps browser `max-age` at 21,600 seconds. Read back and probe both independent TTLs, including absence of a conflicting Browser Cache TTL rule. Apply credentialless read-only CORS, exclude both release-origin hostnames from Worker routing, and generate a Response Header Transform Rule scoped to exactly those two raw R2 custom domains that always overwrites `X-Robots-Tag` with `noindex, nofollow, nosnippet`. Preview Worker responses apply the same exact header in code. Generated-config tests reject any wider hostname expression, missing overwrite semantics, alternate value, or production-apex match. The preflight sends `Origin: https://edge-preview.ogabassey.com` to CSS, JavaScript, and WOFF2 objects through the canonical `releases.ogabassey.com` asset origin, while separately proving pointer/manifest/object reads through `releases-preview.ogabassey.com`. Generated-config tests and deployed-settings read-back must prove the exact five version-scoped observation bindings, both compatibility flags, explicit observability/event-count contract, and qualified dedicated-host cache-erasure contract are present before the preview Worker Custom Domain or production Worker Route is enabled.

The public R2 CORS artifact is a first-class sealed deployment input, not an informal setting: while preview exists it must serialize exactly `origins = ['https://ogabassey.com','https://edge-preview.ogabassey.com']`, `methods = ['GET','HEAD']`, and `allowCredentials = false`; it allows neither wildcard/reflection nor write methods/request headers. The generated-config test and Cloudflare read-back compare the whole closed policy. At preview teardown, change/read back the singleton canonical-origin policy, verify preview-origin rejection across each asset class, then delete/read back the preview custom domains; the deletion is incomplete otherwise.

After fallback parity passes, upload `edge-v1`, bind its exact Scripts Get Version Detail modules/settings hash to the governed bundle accepted on isolated preview, and create/read back the explicit `origin-only-v0@100%` plus `edge-v1@0%` staging deployment qualified by Gate B. Neither version mutates cookies until the final response metadata is known. A malformed value is ignored for Transform affinity and replaced only on that same eligible navigation. - [ ] **Step 3: Implement safe release delivery**

For every request, first reject unless the normalized routed hostname equals the environment allowlist (`edge-preview.ogabassey.com` in preview, `ogabassey.com` in production), then fetch `${RELEASE_ORIGIN_BASE_URL}/hosts/${POINTER_HOSTNAME}.json` with `cache: 'no-store'`. Accept only an age-free pointer whose `CF-Cache-Status` belongs to the exact Gate-B `acceptedCfCacheStatuses`, whose live Trace rule ID, ruleset version, expression hash, and `cache: 'no-store'` request match the sealed qualification, whose hostname equals `POINTER_HOSTNAME`, whose merchant slug is `ogabassey`, and whose merchant ID equals `OGABASSEY_MERCHANT_ID`. A missing or out-of-set cache status, any cache-path status, Trace/fingerprint drift, invalid, cross-tenant, timeout, or 5xx pointer response fails at the edge and never calls origin. In production, `mode: origin` uses same-host pass-through and emits `origin-fallback`; terminal modes stay at the edge. Preview is static-only on a Worker Custom Domain: it accepts only an `edge` pointer for static candidates, returns a bounded `307` to the same path/query on canonical `https://ogabassey.com` for shared-policy `GET`/`HEAD` origin routes, and returns edge `405` for every mutation method. It never invokes Vercel or claims to prove same-host origin behavior. Preview release HTML remains canonical to `https://ogabassey.com`; production `origin-only-v0` later proves unchanged same-host dynamic behavior before any edge percentage.

Once `mode: edge` is observed, corruption, manifest/object absence, metadata failure, unknown routes, rejected methods, queries, and cookies must never fall back to origin: unknown `GET`/`HEAD` routes select only the validated `manifest.notFound` object and return status `404`; unapproved non-`GET`/`HEAD` combinations return edge `405`; query policy redirects or searches at the edge; and corrupt/missing release data returns an edge-generated `503` with `Retry-After` plus a bounded error code. Hash the bounded manifest and compare it with the pointer, then validate the selected normal-route or not-found metadata's merchant/release prefix, status, ETag, `Content-Length`, `Content-Type`, and path/content-type/`responsePolicy` compatibility against that manifest. A normal route must be `200`, the dedicated not-found object must be `404`, and the reserved storage path is never directly routable. Strip upstream `Cache-Control`, `CDN-Cache-Control`, `Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control`, `Expires`, `Vary`, `Set-Cookie`, `X-Robots-Tag`, and every `X-Baci-*` value from release-origin responses; construct canonical cache/vary headers only through `buildStorefrontReleaseResponseHeaders(selectedMetadata.responsePolicy)`. Do not buffer or hash the full HTML/asset body and do not parse HTML on each public request; the sole writer already performed full-body verification before activation, keys are immutable, and the manifest is pointer-hashed. Stream the validated object response for `GET`; return the same validated status/headers with a null body for `HEAD`. Never construct an object URL directly from an unvalidated raw path. The R2 custom domain is public by design, so every stored byte must already satisfy the public-data contract.

All response paths pass through one `respondWithDelivery({ outcome, originAttempted }, response, env)` helper. It strips untrusted delivery markers, adds the authoritative marker, and writes exactly one Analytics Engine data point with only `eligible`, `originAttempted`, `hostClass`, `pathClass`, `delivery`, `statusClass`, Worker version, and UTC observation bucket. It never records raw hostnames, paths, URLs, query strings, IPs, cookies, merchant/release/customer IDs, request headers, or response bodies. Tests prove success, redirect, 404, terminal, origin, caught-error, and failed-origin-fetch paths each emit exactly once; a failed fetch retains `originAttempted: true`, and an unhandled exception is converted to bounded `edge-error` 503 so it cannot bypass telemetry or origin controls.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement same-host dynamic/origin-mode forwarding without cache poisoning**

Origin fallback clones the original request with the same-host URL. It must preserve method/body/query/cookies/`Origin` and all normal browser negotiation headers, strip every client-supplied `X-Baci-*` request header, and never synthesize `Host`, `x-merchant-slug`, `x-custom-domain`, or a Vercel URL. Set `originAttempted = true` immediately before calling `fetch`, not after a response arrives. After `fetch`, strip any upstream `X-Baci-*` response value and set the one authoritative delivery marker in the Worker. The origin response otherwise keeps its existing cache/security policy; any `Set-Cookie` response is always `private, no-store`. This same-host contract keeps the unchanged `proxy.ts` responsible for tenant rewrites, auth refresh, CSRF, and dynamic security headers.

- [ ] **Step 5: Apply the static security and cache contract**

Canonical HTML/XML/JSON responses use `document-no-store` and therefore return `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; `/ads.txt` uses `ads-txt-public-host-vary` and returns exactly `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600, s-maxage=86400` plus `Vary: Host`; the IndexNow ownership file uses `indexnow-public-immutable` and returns exactly `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400, immutable` with no `Vary`. These are closed shared policies, not manifest-supplied header strings. Do not call the Worker Cache API or create a custom cache key. Build the CSP only from the closed `ASSET_ORIGIN_BASE_URL`, the route's pointer-hashed `cspScriptHashes`, and the manifest's Zod-validated `StaticNetworkPolicy`: `default-src 'self'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'; img-src 'self' https: data: {imageOrigins}; style-src 'self' {release-origin}; script-src 'self' {release-origin} {scriptOrigins} {serialized-route-csp-hashes}; font-src 'self' {release-origin}; connect-src 'self' {connectOrigins}; frame-src {frameOrigins-or-none}; worker-src 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests`. The manifest stores bare canonical hashes, but `serializeCspHashSource` is the only path into the header and emits each as a single-quoted CSP hash-source. Empty integration arrays add no third-party source, and unknown/runtime-supplied origins fail validation rather than widening CSP. Renderer and publisher tests verify that every inline `application/ld+json` block matches the manifest list, the exact response header contains quoted hashes and no bare/prequoted/injected value, no other inline script/style exists, preserved integrations work under enforced CSP after consent, disabled integrations make no third-party request, and hostile release input cannot add a source. The Worker does not repeat HTML parsing on every request. For HTML routes whose manifest names the immutable speculation-rules asset, emit the exactly quoted `Speculation-Rules` response header and require the referenced object to be manifest-verified `application/speculationrules+json`; otherwise omit the header. Also apply HSTS, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy`, and the current parity-approved `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`. Preview responses always overwrite `X-Robots-Tag` with exactly `noindex, nofollow, nosnippet`; production-apex responses must omit `X-Robots-Tag` unless the route's separately reviewed canonical robots policy requires it. Immutable objects retain `release-object-immutable` only on the separate release origin; a canonical document route cannot select that policy.

- [ ] **Step 6: Configure exact Ogabassey routing and origin rollback**

Create the Worker Custom Domain `edge-preview.ogabassey.com` for static-only preview and, after all gates, the Worker Route `ogabassey.com/*` for production. A preview Custom Domain makes the Worker the origin and therefore requires no placeholder/proxied application DNS record; it is never treated as evidence for production Route fallback. `www.ogabassey.com` and `ogabassey.usebaci.com` use exact-host Redirect Rules only for shared-policy static-candidate `GET`/`HEAD`, not Worker routes; policy-allowed dynamic/machine/mutation combinations retain current origin handling and generated WAF rules block every other mutation combination. Do not create a wildcard, other merchant, Workers for Platforms, or Cloudflare for SaaS route. Before production, read and record current DNS/origin/ruleset state. Attach the apex route only while `origin-only-v0` is at 100%; prove this is behaviorally equivalent to the previous origin, then introduce `edge-v1` through a percentage deployment. The route requires `request_limit_fail_open: false`. Emergency rollback first deploys `origin-only-v0` at 100%; if Worker/quota health is unsafe, it removes only `ogabassey.com/*`, reads back absence, and probes the unchanged apex origin. WAF rules remain active during route rollback because they protect the restored origin; their separately tested emergency disable requires explicit owner action.

**Interfaces:**

- `POST /api/merchant/publish` returns `202` with `{ publicationJobId, generationDecimal, status: 'building' }` for static-tier stores; the route, web/mobile clients, and fixtures use the shared branded schema and never coerce it through a JavaScript number.
- `GET /api/merchant/publish/status` returns `building | assets_verified | pointer_committed | active | stale_origin | unpublishing | unpublished | deletion_accepted | waiting_topology | erasure_recovery | deleting | gone | finalizing | finalized | failed` plus bounded `{ requestedGenerationDecimal, activeGenerationDecimal, activeAgeSeconds, freshnessDeadline }` for the authenticated Ogabassey owner. Both generation fields are the shared branded decimal string or null and are never coerced through a JavaScript number. The response exposes no claims, secrets, R2 keys, pointer ETags, hostnames, user IDs, or provider errors. Internal `gone_committed | erasure_admitted` both project to the public `gone | deleting` sequence; clients never infer finalization from `gone`.
- Unpublish returns `202 { status: 'unpublishing' }` until an `unpublished` pointer is committed; it must not claim success merely because the PostgreSQL flag changed.
- Account deletion is durable and closed-phase on the existing route: the authenticated `POST /api/merchant/auth/account-deletion` first locks one unique authority row and atomically records or reuses its immutable deletion-intent fact, database `acceptedAt`, and initial append-only event, then returns `202 { deletionRequestId, status: 'deletion_accepted' | 'waiting_topology' | 'erasure_recovery' | 'deleting' }`. An executing/reconciling/terminal-drift topology operation may change only the derived status, never whether the authority/fact/event chain exists. `GET` returns `deletion_accepted | waiting_topology | erasure_recovery | deleting | gone | finalizing | failed` while the session exists. After topology binding/recovery, the lifecycle publisher proves `gone`, appends `gone_committed` then `erasure_admitted`, and priority GC seals the terminal-GC receipt. Only then does the HMAC-gated lifecycle finalizer delete auth/merchant rows, append `finalized`, scrub authority, and clear the same-intent fence. No second client POST, owner action, or still-valid session is required; no auth/merchant row is removed before every erasure proof, and the 24-hour clock remains the original `acceptedAt`.
- Existing stores keep their prior active release while a replacement builds.
- R2 pointer state controls storefront serving; the database status is an observable/reconciled projection.
- Produces `evaluateOgabasseyEdgeEligibility(input): { status: 'eligible'; rendererVersion: 'ogabassey-static-v1' } | { status: 'blocked_new_release'; reason: 'capacity_or_parity_gate' } | { status: 'origin_required'; reason: 'merchant_mismatch' | 'hostname_mismatch' | 'domain_inactive' }`. Eligibility requires the trusted slug `ogabassey`, the owner-approved live merchant UUID, active canonical domain `ogabassey.com`, and the Task 5 parity/capacity contract. Every other merchant returns `origin_required` without creating a pointer.
- Builder drafts remain authenticated origin-only state and never enter a release object, public pointer, or R2. Draft save performs a merchant-scoped optimistic update of only `draft_config` and its draft revision; authenticated draft preview remains origin-only and neither action queues publication. The existing authenticated `PUT /api/builder` remains the enforcement entry point: `builderPublishSchema` requires the expected draft revision and a bounded UUID idempotency key, the route selects the verified merchant through its existing request context, and `publishOgabasseyBuilderDraft` validates and sanitizes the complete draft into the exact shared `PublishedBuilderProjection`. It resolves every commerce reference against the same merchant's current public catalog and rejects unsupported/private/arbitrary/missing/cross-tenant components before mutation. The route never treats the client helper as authority and preserves the existing origin publication path for every merchant outside the sealed Ogabassey eligibility result.

  Task 10 may merge before Task 12 applies Task 6, so every new web/mobile surface is dormant by contract until a bounded capability probe confirms the exact Task 6 RPC and delivery profile. Missing-table, missing-function, schema-cache, or uninitialized-profile results map only to `pilot_not_activated` and preserve the byte-for-byte existing origin publication path; they never partially publish, enqueue, retry in the background, or expose the provider error. Tests run against a database with Task 6 absent and prove Ogabassey plus every other merchant retain the current origin behavior. Task 12 Step 3B applies and verifies Task 6; activation occurs only after that receipt and requires no pre-migration route call to succeed.

  `builder-client.tsx` remains wiring only despite its existing size: idempotency-key creation/reuse, retry state, request construction, and response parsing live in the already listed `publish-builder-draft.ts` helper and its tests. The client passes state and renders outcomes without adding inline publication logic.

  The still-unapplied Task 6 release-ledger migration defines authenticated `publish_ogabassey_builder_draft(p_slug text, p_expected_draft_revision timestamptz, p_idempotency_key uuid, p_request_sha256 text, p_public_projection jsonb)` plus its immutable Ogabassey-only idempotency/publication receipt storage. The function is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted only to `authenticated`, accepts no merchant selector, derives `auth.uid()` and the single owned merchant, and locks the matching `page_configs` and delivery-profile rows in one documented order. It rechecks the expected revision, strict projection shape/hash, and every referenced product/category/condition-offer under that lock. One transaction stores an immutable published revision and validated public projection while keeping raw published configuration database-only, advances `latestCommittedPublicGeneration`, records its database commit time/deadline, and enqueues exactly that generation. It returns `{ publishedRevisionId, publicationJobId, generationDecimal, status: 'building', committedAt }`. Same-key/same-hash replay returns those exact stored values; same-key/different-hash, stale revision, merchant/slug mismatch, missing/private/cross-tenant reference, or a concurrent catalog change performs no mutation. A later draft save cannot alter the published snapshot or release. The old verified pointer stays active until the replacement is fully verified and committed by CAS. This reuses `page_configs` plus a pilot-specific immutable receipt; it adds no generic revision platform, plugin registry, scheduled publishing, or cross-merchant builder service.

Add builder route/schema/RPC regressions for a missing or malformed idempotency key, draft-save causing no public generation, atomic published-config/immutable-receipt/generation enqueue, same-key/same-hash replay returning byte-identical identifiers, same-key/different-hash rejection, stale expected revision, concurrent catalog/reference mutation, cross-merchant reference rejection, RPC rollback after each proposed write, publish during an active build, source mutation during rendering, newest-generation coalescing, repeated build failure crossing the freshness deadline, stale-to-origin fallback, and later fresh-release recovery. Prove the client reuses one idempotency key across transport retry and navigation recovery, while the authenticated route and RPC—not the client helper—enforce replay. Add web/mobile client contract tests for the legacy synchronous response, the Ogabassey `202` release response, branded decimal generation fields, and proof that a generic free merchant never enters this flow.

- [ ] **Step 2: Extract publication orchestration from the oversized route**

Keep authentication first and use the existing authenticated Supabase client. Replace the direct static-tier publish flip with one database transaction that commits the reviewed published builder configuration, records the publication request/commit time/freshness deadline, and enqueues its generation. Do not call the R2 operation atomic with this transaction. Initial publication becomes publicly visible when the valid R2 pointer reaches `mode: edge`; replacement publication keeps serving the previous pointer while building. PostgreSQL transitions to `active` after pointer proof or through reconciliation.

Every oversized Task 10 file remains wiring only. The 419-line merchant publish route delegates static publication to `request-storefront-publication.ts`; the 601-line onboarding action and 592-line mobile-onboarding route delegate renderer/delivery selection to `select-initial-static-renderer.ts`; neither gains a second eligibility or enqueue branch. The 760-line setup checklist and 814-line dashboard client render `storefront-publication-status.tsx`, whose polling/state transitions live in `use-storefront-publication-status.ts`. The 593-line mobile profile delegates all publication polling/idempotency state to the existing under-300-line `useStorePublish` hook. The 1,007-line builder client remains subject to the earlier `publish-builder-draft.ts` wiring contract. Colocated helper/hook/component tests prove each large caller performs one small delegation, preserves existing non-Ogabassey behavior, and adds no duplicate polling, retry, status mapping, or database logic.

Freshness fallback is separate from owner rollback authority. A fixed database-internal `evaluate_storefront_publication_freshness()` path, never executable as an authenticated-owner RPC and accepting no merchant, hostname, release, pointer, reason, approval, or generation selector, locks the Ogabassey profile, derives the active pointer/release and newest overdue committed `generationDecimal`, advances the publication epoch, freezes edge promotion, and creates or byte-identically reuses one `initiator: 'freshness_fallback'` origin-pointer job keyed by that overdue generation. It has no owner identity or approval. The existing VPS rollback claimant accepts the closed initiator union without widening the owner RPC: owner jobs require their authenticated receipt and four closed reason codes; fallback jobs require the database freshness receipt and null owner fields. The deletion classifier outranks, blocks, or supersedes fallback under the same lock. Tests cover equality at the deadline, concurrent evaluators, VPS restart/replay, owner rollback racing fallback, deletion before and after claim/provider CAS, stale-generation activation attempts, and a fresh later generation restoring edge. Edge service resumes only after a later generation is fully rendered, verified, committed by pointer CAS, reconciled active, and passes the normal owner/guardian gates; a recovered job cannot reactivate an older generation.

Replace the static-tier direct unpublish update with `request_storefront_unpublish`; poll until the publisher proves `mode: unpublished`. Replace immediate `delete_current_user` for an edge-enrolled owner with `request_storefront_account_deletion`. The initial deletion route authenticates through the existing `authenticateApiRequest` helper in `apps/web/src/lib/api-auth.ts`, so both cookie web sessions and mobile Bearer sessions are genuinely authenticated before the durable intent is accepted. It returns `202` even when execution must wait for topology reconciliation; a provider/topology busy state can never turn an authenticated deletion into an unrecorded retry obligation. `GET` reads only the authenticated caller's deletion state while that session exists; it is observability, not the completion trigger. The publisher owns completion through `finalize_storefront_account_deletion` only after `gone_committed`, `erasure_admitted`, and the matching terminal-GC receipt. Non-enrolled/origin-only accounts retain the current deletion path. Repeated initial requests converge through the unique active-intent authority and preserve the original `acceptedAt`; a retry after finalization returns only the bounded matching tombstone without selecting another user or creating another fact. If topology reconciliation, lifecycle publication, GC, or finalization fails, keep the intent durably queued/incident-visible and never delete auth/merchant state, clear the last pointer/fence, or restart the 24-hour clock.

An Ogabassey mutation that breaks the parity/capacity contract enqueues and waits for a fenced transition to `mode: origin`, keeping the previous valid pointer until origin mode is proven.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write fail-closed boundary tests**

Test the 25th/26th active product, 20th/21st category, 10th/11th published post and normalized content page, exact 250 MiB media boundary, failed upload compensation, delete compensation, create versus overwrite byte deltas, an authenticated direct Storage upload without a reservation, expired/path-mismatched/undersized/oversized/wrong-MIME/wrong-user reservations, upload-success-without-finalize reconciliation, expired reservation with no object, web upload, mobile logo upload, mobile product-image upload, retry after an interrupted mobile upload, malformed/expired plan, custom-domain attempts, provider-boundary denial before free AI calls, non-default builder publication, concurrent activation/publication/upload reservations, and downgrade from paid above the free limit.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add one server-side entitlement helper**

Return stable codes `FREE_PRODUCT_LIMIT`, `FREE_CATEGORY_LIMIT`, `FREE_BLOG_LIMIT`, `FREE_PAGE_LIMIT`, `FREE_MEDIA_LIMIT`, `PAID_AI_REQUIRED`, `PAID_DOMAIN_REQUIRED`, and `STATIC_THEME_REQUIRED`. UI checks are advisory only. Product/category/blog/page limits are enforced transactionally in database functions/triggers so mobile, bulk, and concurrent callers cannot bypass them. A “public content page” means one enabled normalized output among the fixed free-plan routes; arbitrary Puck blocks are not counted or accepted by the static tier. Existing incompatible configurations remain stored and origin-delivered. For every touched route already over 300 lines, move the new quota decision/response mapping into the named `free-static-storefront/*-limit-enforcement.ts` helper and leave only auth-first validation plus a small delegation in the route. Do not add another inline quota block to an oversized file.

Web and mobile public-media callers reserve the exact merchant-owned object path, declared bytes, content type, and operation (`create | replace`) through `reserve_merchant_media_bytes` before upload. Quota checks include finalized usage plus every live reservation. `POST /api/media/reservations` returns only `{ reservationId, path, expiresAt }`; after upload, `PATCH` finalizes by having PostgreSQL read the authoritative `storage.objects` size/content type rather than trusting client-reported actual bytes. Replacement reserves the conservative positive delta while retaining the prior and proposed sizes needed for reconciliation. The client releases the reservation on every known failure/delete path.

Expiry never blindly releases bytes. At the start of every reserve/finalize/delete operation, a bounded same-merchant reconciliation locks up to 25 expired reservations: if the exact object exists, it finalizes usage from authoritative `storage.objects.metadata` (including replacement delta); only when no object exists may it release the reservation. This lazy reconciliation keeps the hot scope merchant-local and prevents repeated abandoned uploads from escaping the 250 MiB ceiling without adding a Vercel cron. A successfully uploaded object remains covered by its reservation until charged, even after the client-visible expiry.

The append-only migration replaces the current `media` bucket INSERT/UPDATE policies for merchant-public prefixes with policies that require a live matching reservation bound to `auth.uid()`, merchant membership, path, operation, declared size, declared content type, and the server-observed `storage.objects.metadata` size/content type. The observed byte size must be nonnegative and no greater than reserved bytes; malformed/missing size metadata fails closed. Existing non-storefront prefixes retain their exact current behavior and are not silently folded into the storefront quota. This prevents free authenticated clients from bypassing the route with a direct Storage SDK upload or reserving one byte for a large object. `LogoPicker` and `createProductEditImageActions` must use the reservation API before their existing direct Supabase upload and finalize afterward. Successfully finalized usage remains conservative until an authenticated delete first proves the object is absent and then releases it.

- [ ] **Step 3: Preserve existing paid merchants and safe downgrades**

A downgrade does not delete products, posts, pages, categories, or media. It prevents new publications/uploads above the limit until the merchant reduces content or upgrades. Free merchants remain origin-delivered in this plan; there is no invented per-merchant Worker request allowance or `suspended` pointer for them. Existing origin rate limits remain authoritative until a later multi-merchant edge plan defines account-wide capacity and rollback.

- [ ] **Step 4: Update product copy**

Describe the free plan honestly: one Baci subdomain using the default static theme, current origin delivery, 25 active products, 20 categories, 10 posts, 10 content pages, 250 MiB media, manual/deterministic branding, and no included AI/image generation. Do not say or imply that free merchants receive Worker/R2/static-edge delivery in this plan. Describe the traffic value as an abuse-review threshold, not a guaranteed hard bandwidth allowance, until live Cloudflare billing proves the sustainable number.

- [ ] **Step 6: Merge and independently prove the free-tier production migration**

After green required checks, fresh exact-head review, and expected-head merge, require the protected main `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` run for that merge SHA to apply `20260728100000_free_static_storefront_limits.sql` and then execute `.github/scripts/verify-free-static-storefront-production-database.mjs` inside the existing migration credential boundary. The verifier selects explicit catalog columns and proves the exact migration version/checksum, quota tables/functions/triggers, reservation RLS, Storage policies, grants/revokes, and absence of unauthorized overloads; it emits only a redacted receipt bound to source SHA, workflow run ID, database identity hash, migration hash, and observation time. A failed apply/read-back blocks Task 11 deployment/remediation but does not block the independent Ogabassey edge pilot. Do not reuse Task 12's release-ledger verifier or claim that a successful migration-history row proves live catalog/grant state.
