- Modify: `.github/scripts/apply-pending-migrations.test.sh` to prove the exact mode permits Task 6 already-applied or solely pending and rejects every additional pending migration.
- Modify: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` to add closed required `migration_only`, protected-rollout-tag, and complete `StorefrontRolloutIdentity` dispatch inputs; verify the tag/identity/artifact before migration credentials; run the bounded post-migration Ogabassey release-ledger read-back; make `migration_only: true` structurally skip every build/deploy job; preserve Task 11's independent normal-main verifier; and, if required for normal deployments, exclude the Worker app from the existing prebuilt Vercel artifact.

**Interfaces:**

- Compares the immutable Ogabassey release with the current `https://ogabassey.com` origin across every Task 5 parity invariant and dynamic-route handoff.
- Produces normal pointer rollback plus emergency removal of only the `ogabassey.com/*` Worker route, with read-back and multi-location probes. Emergency removal is independently available from a separately protected break-glass tag and, only if GitHub dispatch and the guardian are both unavailable, an owner-only deletion runbook using the same reviewed deletion guard.
- Produces protected preview/production workflows scoped to the Baci account and `ogabassey.com` zone; the short-lived bootstrap alone may touch the exact `ogabassey.usebaci.com` DNS/redirect resources in the `usebaci.com` zone.
- Produces exact-host, `GET`/`HEAD`-only alias redirects, shared-policy invalid-method/path WAF blocks, allowed-origin rate limits, and an ingress proof covering every Ogabassey hostname without enrolling another merchant or changing an approved dynamic transport.
- Produces one generated/read-back cohort Transform Rule that sets `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Key` only from exactly one `baci-edge-cohort` cookie matching `^[a-f0-9]{32}$`; it is absent outside the canary and is never used as application authority. The database guard/profile receipt carries `affinityRuleState: 'absent' | 'active' | 'cleanup_pending'` plus the last exact rule fingerprint/read-back. Applying the rule transitions `absent -> active` only after provider read-back. Successful 100% completion removes and proves absence before setting `absent`. Any abort, rollback, ambiguous deployment, or route-only guardian removal first restores safe origin service or removes the apex route, then records `cleanup_pending` until the normal protected deploy workflow deletes that exact rule and proves full ruleset equality apart from its removal. A cleanup failure keeps the route absent, alerts, and blocks re-enrollment, preview acceptance, staging, or route recreation; the break-glass route token remains unable to change Transform Rules.
- Both `origin-only-v0` and every release-serving candidate declare the generated Wrangler `version_metadata` binding named `CF_VERSION_METADATA`; no hand-written `Env` interface is permitted. Gate B verifies the live binding schema exposes the exact current version ID on this account and compatibility date before production upload. Every uploaded version carries the five generated observation text bindings. Origin-only and current-stable versions must retain the exact fail-closed disabled sentinels; only the isolated preview and its byte-identical candidate version may carry the active `StorefrontEdgeObservationAdmission`. Scripts Get Version Detail binds the exact active or disabled tuple to each version's module/settings receipt, and the active tuple must equal the owner-accepted preview. All versions contain the identical credentialless terminal route code only through the shared `edge-observation` classifier result: `GET /__baci/edge-observation/<challenge>?n=<nonce>`, where `<challenge>` is exactly lowercase hex64 and `<nonce>` exactly lowercase hex32. Before returning success, the handler hashes the challenge, compares it with the version-scoped expected digest using `node:crypto.timingSafeEqual`, and requires the rollout identity, capability-artifact hash, and half-open runtime window to match the sealed admission. The route performs no origin/R2/service fetch, cache API call, logging of the challenge/cookie/nonce, mutation, or background work and returns empty `204` with exactly these application-controlled headers: `Cache-Control: private, no-store, max-age=0`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `X-Baci-Edge-Version: <CF_VERSION_METADATA.id>`, and `X-Baci-Observation-Challenge: <challenge>`. It emits no application `Set-Cookie`, `Age`, `ETag`, body, redirect, or `X-Robots-Tag`; malformed path/query, a missing or extra suffix segment, duplicate/unknown query, wrong challenge, missing/drifting admission binding, time before `notBefore`, time at/after `notAfter`, and a non-apex production host return the bounded edge `404`, while `HEAD` and every mutation return the shared edge `405`. None may redirect, consult the manifest/pointer, or fetch origin. The two observation headers exist only on this route and are stripped from every normal storefront response. Shared-policy, generated-config, production-`workerd`, and integration tests cover apex, separately configured isolated preview, every alias/raw-release/other host, valid/invalid/future/expired admission, boundary equality, valid and invalid challenge/nonce, locally rejected observer fragments, GET/HEAD/every mutation, cache isolation, and exhaustive classifier switching; they prove the response is generated anew per challenge/version, neither a cached nor origin response can emit the marker, the UUID equals that uploaded version's provider ID, and a missing/malformed binding fails closed before route attachment.
- Produces a maximum-4-KiB `StorefrontEdgeObservationCapabilityArtifact = { schemaVersion: '1'; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; challenge: string; challengeSha256: string; notBefore: string; notAfter: string; holdVerificationReceiptSha256: string; canonicalSha256: string }` before final isolated-preview probing. A credentialless preparation job first generates the 256-bit lowercase-hex route challenge plus a distinct fresh hold-verification challenge with Web Crypto, then calls capability-authorized `issue_storefront_edge_observation_capability_window(holdId, beginReceiptSha256, rolloutIdentitySha256, challengeSha256, holdVerificationChallengeSha256)`. That RPC atomically validates the exact live hold/tuple, obtains database time, stores one immutable single-use grant, and returns `{ grantId, notBefore, notAfter, holdVerification: HoldVerificationReceipt }`, where `notBefore` is the nested receipt's exact `databaseNow`, `notAfter` is its exact `databaseExpiresAt`, and the positive half-open lifetime is no longer than two hours. The job builds the artifact from that grant, then calls `register_storefront_edge_observation_capability(grantId, holdId, beginReceiptSha256, capabilityArtifact)`; SQL strictly parses the maximum-4-KiB object, recomputes its challenge and canonical hashes, requires every field to equal the unused grant, consumes the grant, stores only redacted evidence, and returns `StorefrontEdgeObservationCapabilityRegistrationReceipt = { schemaVersion: '1'; grantId: string; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; capabilityArtifactSha256: string; observationChallengeSha256: string; observationNotBefore: string; observationNotAfter: string; capabilityHoldVerification: HoldVerificationReceipt; databaseRegisteredAt: string; canonicalSha256: string }`. Both RPCs are `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''`, executable only by `anon` and `authenticated`, revoked from `PUBLIC` and `service_role`, accept no merchant/hostname/release/generation selector, and derive the tuple from the same random hold capability pair. Registration is idempotent only for the byte-identical already-consumed grant/artifact pair; conflicting reuse, a second grant for the hold/rollout, or registration after hold terminality/expiry fails closed.
- The artifact's canonical core uses ASCII separator `baci-storefront-edge-observation-capability-v1\n`, then length-frames exactly `schemaVersion`, `rolloutIdentitySha256`, `challenge`, `challengeSha256`, `notBefore`, `notAfter`, and `holdVerificationReceiptSha256`; `canonicalSha256` is excluded. The registration receipt uses `baci-storefront-edge-observation-capability-registration-v1\n`, then length-frames `schemaVersion`, `grantId`, `rolloutIdentitySha256`, `capabilityArtifactSha256`, `observationChallengeSha256`, `observationNotBefore`, `observationNotAfter`, the complete capability hold-receipt core preimage as one byte string, its `verificationReceiptSha256`, and `databaseRegisteredAt`; its hash is excluded. The raw `challenge` is supplied transiently only to the registration RPC, never stored by the database, and never appears in logs, owner acceptance, or the final proof. The governed version-upload jobs receive only the validated artifact fields, set the five active admission bindings only on the isolated preview/candidate uploads, preserve disabled sentinels on origin/current-stable uploads, and bind the artifact hash plus settings into each exact version-detail receipt. The owner-facing acceptance and final proof embed the complete redacted registration receipt, so browser, server, and SQL can recompute the nested database receipt and registration hash and verify the artifact/window binding without receiving the raw artifact. The artifact grants only the bounded observation `204`, carries no provider/database credential, cannot select a version or bypass the override guard, and becomes unusable at `notAfter`. Shared SQL, TypeScript, and governed Node ESM fixtures contain literal Base64 preimages and expected hashes for artifact and registration success, one-byte drift, challenge/hash mismatch, timestamp reformatting, hold-receipt substitution, grant replay/conflict, raw-challenge leakage, sentinel confusion, and every half-open boundary.
- The canary observer downloads that exact previously sealed capability artifact, verifies its same-run lineage and every binding/read-back hash, and then generates from one through 2,000 distinct cryptographically random valid affinity-cookie values, sends sequential credentialless requests to the bound challenge path, and stops only after observing the candidate UUID. It never generates or accepts a replacement challenge. Each request uses a unique query nonce as additional cache separation, `cache-control: no-cache`, and no version-override header. It rejects any response with `Age`, `CF-Cache-Status` outside the Gate-B-qualified non-cache set when present, a mismatched challenge, unknown/duplicate marker header, body, `Set-Cookie`, wrong status, version outside the exact deployment tuple, reused cookie hash, or runtime time outside the sealed half-open window. The attempt ceiling is a failure boundary, not permission to infer candidate health: if no candidate appears, confirmation is forbidden, route safety runs first, and the attempted deployment enters read-only reconciliation/terminal ambiguity under the existing ledger rules. These synthetic requests and hostile probes are included in Worker/log capacity accounting but identified by their artifact hash and excluded from the 99.9% origin-avoidance numerator and denominator and every customer-performance/error gate.
- Produces one generated/read-back Response Header Transform Rule scoped to exactly `releases-preview.ogabassey.com` and `releases.ogabassey.com`, overwriting `X-Robots-Tag` with exactly `noindex, nofollow, nosnippet`; the preview Worker emits the same header, while canonical production-apex responses strip it.
- Produces `StorefrontPreviewAcceptance = { releaseId: string; generationDecimal: string; pointerEtag: string; manifestSha256: string; workerVersionId: string; previewRouteFingerprint: string; cutoverHoldId: string; ownerApprovalId: string; acceptedAt: string; expiresAt: string; beginReceiptSha256: string; acceptanceHoldVerification: HoldVerificationReceipt; observationCapabilityRegistration: StorefrontEdgeObservationCapabilityRegistrationReceipt; canonicalSha256: string }`. `generationDecimal` matches `^[1-9][0-9]{0,18}$` and is never represented as JavaScript `bigint` in JSON. The entire object is constructed, stored, and returned by `record_storefront_preview_acceptance`; clients may validate it but never construct or supply it. Its complete nested hold-verification receipt is live on the same tuple, `acceptedAt` is exactly the one transaction's database-generated `databaseNow`, its challenge is distinct from the capability-time challenge and must later be distinct from the three production-proof challenges, and its core/hash are canonical. The complete capability registration must recompute, carry a distinct live nested hold challenge, match the same hold/tuple/rollout, and prove the raw artifact's hash, observation challenge hash, and exact database-derived window without disclosing the raw challenge. The acceptance requires the preview version-detail receipt to contain those exact observation admission bindings, requires `observationNotBefore <= acceptedAt < observationNotAfter`, requires `observationNotAfter === expiresAt`, and derives all three observation values only from `observationCapabilityRegistration`. The database-backed cutover hold freezes that exact tuple and observation admission only for the final bounded cutover window; it is opened after production rule qualification, then either completed immediately after the exact 1% production proof, cancelled, invalidated by lifecycle work, or allowed to expire with cutover aborted.
- Produces `InitialProductionCutoverProof = { rolloutIdentitySha256: string; cutoverHoldId: string; releaseId: string; generationDecimal: string; pointerEtag: string; manifestSha256: string; ownerApprovalId: string; beginReceiptSha256: string; previewAcceptance: StorefrontPreviewAcceptance; holdVerifications: { preAttach: HoldVerificationReceipt; preMixedDeployment: HoldVerificationReceipt; postProbe: HoldVerificationReceipt }; originOnlyWorkerVersionId: string; edgeWorkerVersionId: string; stagedDeploymentId: string; stagedDeploymentCreatedAt: string; stagedDeploymentReceiptSha256: string; candidateArtifactReadbackSha256: string; deploymentId: string; deploymentCreatedAt: string; candidateObservation: CandidateObservationReceipt; routeFingerprint: string; responseHeaderRulesetSha256: string; cohortRuleSha256: string; overrideGuardRuleSha256: string; probeSetSha256: string; observedAt: string; canonicalSha256: string }`. The staged fields must identify the confirmed 100/0 `stage_candidate` head that immediately precedes the confirmed 99/1 proof deployment; the candidate artifact receipt binds that exact staged candidate's version-detail modules/settings to the governed preview-accepted bundle, and the complete candidate-observation receipt must equal the immutable ledger row for the confirmed 99/1 deployment, name the exact edge version, bind the exact override-guard receipt, require its observation challenge hash, capability-artifact hash, and raw-evidence interval to equal the nested preview acceptance's database-registered observation admission, and satisfy `probeSetSha256 === candidateObservation.rawEvidenceArtifactSha256`. The override-guard receipt proves all public override attempts remain blocked. Both provider creation timestamps come from their exact provider read-backs, never the runner clock, are ordered, and are no later than `observedAt`; the observation receipt's bounded interval lies within that same ordered window. The complete preview acceptance—including its complete acceptance hold receipt and redacted capability-registration receipt—the three production hold-receipt objects, and the database-registered candidate-observation receipt are the complete redacted canonical proof inputs and are included, not merely referenced by hashes, in the proof object and canonical serialization. Every nested receipt must match its independently stored database evidence. The protected workflow writes this receipt bundle only after the exact 1% observation-route probes pass and after every distinct challenge-bound database receipt proves the same live hold/tuple. It has no Supabase user session and cannot begin, complete, cancel, or request pointer rollback.
- Produces `StorefrontEdgeGateArtifact = { schemaVersion: 1; repositoryId: string; workflowRunId: string; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; generationDecimal: string; deploymentId: string; deploymentCreatedAt: string; percentage: 1 | 5 | 25 | 50 | 100; windowStartedAt: string; windowEndedAt: string; completeUtcDays: string[]; measurementArtifactSha256: string; evidenceFingerprintSha256: string; originAvoidanceVerdict: 'PASS'; budgetVerdict: 'PASS'; canonicalSha256: string }` and `StorefrontEdgeDeploymentReceipt = { schemaVersion: 1; operationId: string; kind: 'bootstrap_origin' | 'stage_candidate' | 'temporary_proof' | 'initial_confirmation' | 'promote' | 'rotate' | 'rollback'; generationDecimal: string; priorDeploymentId: string | null; deploymentId: string; providerCreatedAt: string; targetPercentage: 0 | 1 | 5 | 25 | 50 | 100; versions: Array<{ versionId: string; percentageDecimal: string }>; workersMessage: string; requestSha256: string; providerResponseSha256: string; gateReceiptSha256: string | null; candidateObservationSha256: string | null; rolloutIdentitySha256: string; databaseReceiptSha256: string; canonicalSha256: string }`. Target zero is valid only for `stage_candidate`, always represents stable/origin at `100` plus candidate at `0`, and never creates a promotion gate receipt. `temporary_proof | rotate` require the exact stored observation hash; every other kind requires null. Every operation other than stage requires a nonzero target and the exact expected tuple. Shared canonical serializers use domain separators `baci-storefront-edge-gate-v1\n` and `baci-storefront-edge-deployment-receipt-v1\n`, fixed field order, UTC-millisecond timestamps, canonical decimal percentages, provider request order for version arrays, and the existing length framing. SQL, TypeScript, and Node consume one fixture containing zero staging, every promoted percentage, bootstrap/stage/temporary/initial/rotation/rollback cases, required/forbidden observation hashes, retry boundaries, and one-byte drift failures. The exact RPC, claimed-job, UI, workflow artifact, receipt, and R2 JSON boundaries use `generationDecimal`; SQL may expose only a canonical text alias and tests cover values above `2^53`, the maximum PostgreSQL bigint, overflow, signs, leading zeros, and length-then-lexicographic ordering.
- `packages/shared/src/storefront/edge-cutover-proof.ts` is the browser/server authority for all proof hashes and exports strict schemas plus serializers/hashers for the capability registration, preview acceptance, and initial proof. Preview acceptance uses ASCII domain separator `baci-storefront-preview-acceptance-v1\n` followed by length-framed fields in exact order `releaseId`, `generationDecimal`, `pointerEtag`, `manifestSha256`, `workerVersionId`, `previewRouteFingerprint`, `cutoverHoldId`, `ownerApprovalId`, `acceptedAt`, `expiresAt`, `beginReceiptSha256`, the complete acceptance hold-receipt core preimage as one byte string, its `verificationReceiptSha256`, the complete capability-registration core preimage as one byte string, and its `canonicalSha256`; the acceptance hash is excluded. Initial proof uses `baci-storefront-initial-production-cutover-proof-v1\n` followed by exact order `rolloutIdentitySha256`, `cutoverHoldId`, `releaseId`, `generationDecimal`, `pointerEtag`, `manifestSha256`, `ownerApprovalId`, `beginReceiptSha256`, the complete preview-acceptance core preimage as one byte string, its `canonicalSha256`, then for each `preAttach`, `preMixedDeployment`, and `postProbe` in that order the complete receipt-core preimage bytes as one length-framed byte string followed by its `verificationReceiptSha256`, then `originOnlyWorkerVersionId`, `edgeWorkerVersionId`, `stagedDeploymentId`, `stagedDeploymentCreatedAt`, `stagedDeploymentReceiptSha256`, `candidateArtifactReadbackSha256`, `deploymentId`, `deploymentCreatedAt`, the complete `CandidateObservationReceipt` core preimage as one length-framed byte string followed by its `canonicalSha256`, then `routeFingerprint`, `responseHeaderRulesetSha256`, `cohortRuleSha256`, `overrideGuardRuleSha256`, `probeSetSha256`, and `observedAt`; the outer hash is excluded. Scalar framing, ASCII restrictions, UUID/hash casing, and UTC-millisecond timestamps are identical to the receipt contract. Nested preimages recursively bind every receipt field, not merely their hashes. No generic JSON canonicalization participates.
- `storefront-edge-cutover-proof-vectors.json` contains literal Base64 preimages and expected lowercase SHA-256 values for capability registration, preview acceptance, and initial proof success/boundary cases. The shared TypeScript implementation, browser/server tests, independent Node ESM implementation inside `create-ogabassey-cutover-proof.mjs`, and the migration's SQL issuance/registration/proof serializers and hash functions consume those same vectors. SQL is an independent final authority for the capability registration and nested hold core, preview acceptance and nested acceptance-hold core, three production hold-receipt cores, complete candidate-observation core, and outer initial-proof core; its tests fail on any omission, preimage, field-order, framing, timestamp, nested-hash, or outer-hash drift. `create-ogabassey-cutover-proof.mjs` must produce bytes/hash identical to both the shared TypeScript and SQL authorities before the workflow may register a proof. `packages/shared/src/storefront/index.ts` exports the receipt/proof schemas and serializers for existing `@baci/shared/storefront` consumers; no application imports an unpublished package-private path, and the plain-Node `.mjs` scripts use their governed ESM implementation plus golden vectors rather than attempting to import TypeScript directly.
- Produces an owner-only authenticated control surface at `/dashboard/settings/storefront-delivery` and `POST /api/storefront/ogabassey-edge-cutover`. The Server Component requires the existing merchant session before rendering; the client control accepts only the discriminated `begin | accept_preview | complete | cancel | pointer_rollback` schema and requires an action-specific exact typed confirmation plus explicit review checkbox. Every mutation sends strict `x-baci-cutover-action: begin | accept_preview | complete | cancel | pointer_rollback`. Immediately before each mutation, the client performs same-origin `GET /api/csrf` with `credentials: 'same-origin'` and `cache: 'no-store'`, requires the bounded token response plus matching cookie, and sends `buildCsrfHeaders(...)` with same-origin credentials. The mutation route's first database-affecting operation is to create the ordinary server Supabase client and call `auth.getUser()`; unauthenticated requests return before action/body parsing. After authentication and before parsing or RPC, it calls `checkCsrfProtection(request)` and returns its bounded 403 response on a missing token header, missing token cookie, mismatch, or failure. Only then does it Zod-validate the action/content-type headers. Begin/cancel/pointer rollback use strict maximum-8-KiB JSON bodies and the shared discriminated schemas. `accept_preview` requires `Content-Type: application/json`, exact typed confirmation `ACCEPT OGABASSEY EDGE PREVIEW`, and the closed object/string-only body `{ holdId, beginReceiptSha256, ownerApprovalId, workerVersionId, previewRouteFingerprint, acceptanceChallengeSha256, capabilityRegistrationSha256, confirmation }`; no database time, receipt object, tuple projection, observation-window value, or acceptance hash is accepted. The server reads at most 16,384 original bytes through the same authoritative bounded stream counter used by complete, prevalidates canonical `Content-Length` when present, requires an exact count match, performs one fatal UTF-8 decode, rejects BOM, and parses with `bounded-unique-json.ts` into a null-prototype object before strict Zod validation, rejecting duplicate decoded keys, dangerous keys, trailing input, arrays, numbers, booleans, nulls, and unknown members. It calls only `record_storefront_preview_acceptance` through the authenticated user-scoped client, validates the database-returned complete acceptance plus both nested receipt hashes against the shared schema/serializers, returns it with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, and blocks staging until the stored hash read-back matches. Pointer rollback requires exact expected pointer ETag, release ID, approved reason, approval ID, and typed `ROLL BACK OGABASSEY TO ORIGIN`; the route passes only those bounded fields to `request_storefront_edge_pointer_rollback` and returns its immutable acceptance receipt. Complete uses `Content-Type: application/vnd.baci.storefront-cutover-proof+json` and sends the exact original proof-file bytes—not a client-reserialized object—with no tuple supplied outside the proof. The existing proxy Origin check remains defense-in-depth and `proxy.ts` is not modified. The database derives and verifies the Ogabassey owner. No service-role client, raw JWT display/export, browser storage token extraction, CLI token, refresh token, or database credential exists.
- `readBoundedJsonBody(request, { maxBytes })` is the single streaming byte-counter/fatal-decoder authority used by both `accept_preview` with `maxBytes: 16_384` and complete with `maxBytes: 65_536`; it returns the exact original bytes plus decoded text only after canonical present-`Content-Length` prevalidation, running-limit enforcement, declared/observed equality, fatal UTF-8 decoding, and BOM rejection. `read-bounded-cutover-proof.ts` composes that utility with the proof-specific parser/schema and never implements a second reader. Complete accepts proof bytes only when the original `File.size` and server-observed streamed byte count are both from 1 through 65,536. The browser uses `file.arrayBuffer()` and the server uses a bounded streaming reader; a present `Content-Length` is prevalidated while an absent one proceeds only through the same running byte ceiling, so chunked or dishonest bodies remain bounded. Both decode exactly once with `new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true })` and reject a UTF-8 BOM. Neither calls `File.text()` or native `JSON.parse`. `bounded-unique-json.ts` is a dependency-free browser-safe recursive-descent parser for exactly JSON objects whose values are object, string, or null: maximum depth 8, maximum 192 total members, maximum decoded key 80 Unicode scalar values, maximum decoded string 2,048 UTF-8 bytes, no arrays/numbers/booleans, exact JSON string escape/surrogate validation, duplicate rejection on decoded key values, dangerous-key rejection for `__proto__`, `prototype`, and `constructor`, trailing-byte rejection, and null-prototype output. The 192-member ceiling is derived from the closed maximum shape containing the complete preview acceptance, its two nested database receipt chains, three production hold receipts, and candidate observation, with no extension members permitted. The strict Zod proof schema then enforces that exact closed shape and tighter field bounds. Its tests cover the exact maximum member count, 193-member rejection, every token/escape boundary, duplicate raw-versus-escaped keys, nesting/member/string limits, invalid/lone surrogates, invalid UTF-8, BOM, trailing input, dangerous keys, and prototype pollution.
- `Content-Length` absence is permitted because browsers/proxies may stream; when present it must be one canonical decimal from 1 through 65,536 and is rejected before reading if malformed or over-limit. The streaming counter is always authoritative and must equal a present declared length exactly, so a short, long, chunked, or dishonest body cannot bypass the same ceiling.
- Before submitting complete, the browser recomputes the raw-file SHA-256; capability-registration hash and nested capability hold-receipt hash; preview-acceptance hash and nested acceptance hold-receipt hash; outer initial-proof hash; all three production hold-receipt core hashes; and the complete candidate-observation core/hash. It derives the artifact hash, challenge hash, and half-open window only from the validated capability-registration receipt, requires them to equal the preview version admission and candidate observation, and validates the observation's exact attempt count and interval, shared observation path/query grammar, deployment and Version Metadata IDs, locked-guard fingerprint/selected proof mode, raw-evidence artifact binding, rollout identity, and `probeSetSha256 === rawEvidenceArtifactSha256`. It then validates all five distinct hold challenges, live statuses, database times/expiries, and exact release tuple, displays the capability registration, preview acceptance, three production hold receipts, and candidate-observation summaries, and requires explicit review plus typed confirmation. The authenticated route independently repeats the same raw-byte hash, parse, strict schema, every nested preimage/hash and semantic check, challenge/tuple/time/expiry, and registration check using the exact received bytes; it calls `get_storefront_edge_cutover_proof_registration`, requires the immutable workflow registration to match the raw file hash, canonical proof hash, embedded preview-acceptance hash, embedded capability-registration hash, rollout identity, all five hold-receipt hashes, candidate-observation hash, hold, tuple, deployment/version/admission/capability/guard/raw-evidence bindings, and owner approval, requires the joined cutover-secret operation to be the matching canonical `revoked` receipt, and only then passes the verified canonical proof hash to `complete_storefront_edge_cutover`. The route returns only the bounded redacted database receipt with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. A local-only success, arbitrary SHA-256, reserialized proof, omitted nested acceptance/registration/receipt, recomputed-but-semantically-invalid evidence, unregistered proof, active/mismatched secret operation, or client/server/parser/SQL disagreement performs zero completion RPC. Cancel requires the bounded reason and matching approval.
- Uses the exact evidence source approved in Task 1 and reports `NOT_PROVEN` when that source becomes sampled/incomplete.
- Produces a no-provider-secret scheduled dispatcher plus protected mutation workflows that execute from an immutable protected rollout tag whose peeled commit equals the recorded merge SHA after artifact equivalence with the exact reviewed and required-checks identities. It also produces a separately protected break-glass tag at that same merge commit.

**Mandatory execution order:** Steps 1-3 create and locally validate code, workflows, policy templates, and generated Cloudflare artifacts only. Step 3A must then commit them, pass green required checks plus fresh exact-head review, merge, record the reviewed-head/checks/merge identities and artifact hash, prove governed-path byte equivalence, verify the two independent repository tag protections, and create/read back the rollout and break-glass annotated tags at the merge SHA. No Ogabassey R2 bucket/custom domain, preview Worker Custom Domain, DNS, R2 public setting, Cloudflare rule, Worker script/deployment/route, secret, enabled VPS unit, guardian schedule, or production resource may be created or changed before those gates pass. Task 12 Step 3B is the only pre-preview operational gate: it proves the Task 6 release-ledger migration is present and correct in production, then builds, attests, installs, and independently reproduces the exact final `mergeSha` VPS closure with every timer/socket disabled and no provider credential. Task 11 remains independent. Only after Task 12 Step 3B passes may Steps 4-6 run through mutation workflows loaded from the protected rollout tag and given the recorded identity tuple. Emergency removal uses the separately protected break-glass tag; the direct owner-only deletion exception remains dormant unless both tagged dispatch and guardian removal are unavailable. Operational evidence is committed afterward in the evidence-only Step 7 PR and may not modify executable files or checked-in policy templates.

Define `StorefrontRolloutIdentity = { reviewedHeadSha: string; requiredChecksSha: string; mergeSha: string; mergeMethod: 'merge' | 'squash' | 'rebase'; artifactManifestSha256: string; workerBundleSha256: string; supabasePublicConfigSha256: string }`. Every commit SHA is exactly 40 lowercase hexadecimal characters and each content/config hash is exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters. `supabasePublicConfigSha256` hashes the fixed scalar serialization `v1 + "\n" + normalizedProductionSupabaseUrl + "\n" + publishableOrAnonKey` after URL/project-ref validation; the raw public values remain repository Actions variables rather than source files or secrets. `reviewedHeadSha` is the PR `headRefOid` that received the fresh substantive Codex/GitHub review; require that review's `commit_id` to equal it. `requiredChecksSha` is the exact PR-head, test-merge, or merge-group commit GitHub identified as satisfying the required checks; seal the check-run IDs/names/conclusions and require every governed artifact and bundle hash at that commit to equal the reviewed-head values. `mergeSha` is the commit returned by the exact-head-guarded merge operation and reachable from the protected default branch. `deploy-storefront-edge.yml`, `confirm-ogabassey-edge-cutover.yml`, `rotate-ogabassey-storefront-edge.yml`, `measure-storefront-origin.yml`, and `guard-ogabassey-edge-budget.yml` are `workflow_dispatch` rollout-tag workflows. The Cloudflare job sends that exact request once, with fixed `workers/triggered_by = "deployment"` and the database-derived message, reads back the resulting current deployment, and a separate HMAC-only job confirms it with both candidate-observation arguments null because the immutable owner-reviewed temporary proof already established candidate execution and this replacement deployment starts the independently measured 24-hour gate. A replay reads the operation first: `confirmed` returns its receipt; `prepared` may perform the attempt transition and one POST; `provider_attempted` performs provider reads only and confirms exactly one match; zero records `ambiguous` and sends no POST; more than one or a conflicting current deployment removes the route. There is no branch in which zero after `provider_attempted` creates again.

`deploy-storefront-edge.yml` has required `mode: cutover | promote` plus the common rollout tag/seven-field identity. `cutover` additionally requires the same-repository preview-acceptance artifact run ID/hash, cutover hold ID, begin-receipt hash, owner approval ID, and exact confirmation `CONFIRM_OGABASSEY_INITIAL_EDGE_CUTOVER`; it rejects every gate-artifact input. It performs no production version-override request. For temporary proof, the deployment-token job only creates/read-backs the first origin-99/candidate-1 deployment; a credentialless observer downloads and verifies the sole sealed observation-capability artifact, exercises its shared-classifier challenge route within the half-open window with at most 2,000 unique affinity cookies and sends the hostile guard probes; the isolated observation-read job enforces the selected Gate-B branch—complete WAF action plus exact zero matching invocations for `complete-worker-log`, or complete unsampled terminal-WAF action count equality with no Worker-log absence claim for `unsampled-terminal-waf`—and the HMAC-only job validates and combines both bounded artifacts, constructs the canonical `CandidateObservationReceipt` including that mode/source fingerprint, and confirms only when its Version Metadata ID equals the exact candidate. Every response-loss boundary recovers by reading the database operation plus exact provider deployment/ruleset state; no cycle retries a POST after `provider_attempted`, and zero/multiple/conflicting matches become ambiguous with route removal. Workflow graph tests interrupt before and after every prepare, attempted, provider mutation, read-back, observation/event artifact upload/download, receipt construction, and confirmation boundary for all three deployment cycles and both control-rule families, and reject sampled-log absence in the Analytics branch or mode/source substitution.

`deploy-storefront-edge.yml` promotion mode additionally requires `gate_artifact_run_id` as canonical positive decimal, `gate_file_sha256` as lowercase hex64, `current_deployment_id` in the provider ID grammar, and exact confirmation `CONFIRM_OGABASSEY_NEXT_CANARY_STAGE`; it rejects every cutover-only input. It has no percentage, version, annotation, predecessor, generation, or operation-ID input. Its credentialless job downloads exactly one `ogabassey-edge-gate-v1.json` artifact from the same repository's successful `measure-storefront-origin.yml` run, requires that run's protected tag/identity and deployment ID to equal the current database head, requires exactly one regular file no larger than 65,536 bytes, recomputes the raw/canonical hashes, proves a complete at-least-24-hour window and both `PASS` verdicts, and rejects expired Actions evidence until the owner restores the byte-identical file through a reviewed evidence commit whose blob/hash/run provenance matches the immutable run metadata. The next HMAC-only job prepares the operation; PostgreSQL derives the sole next member of `1 -> 5 -> 25 -> 50 -> 100`, predecessor, weights, versions, annotation, and request hash. The workflow marks that operation `provider_attempted` before exposing its exact request artifact to the Cloudflare-token job, which posts once. Confirmation records the exact provider response and new head. Recovery obeys the same closed state machine as initial confirmation and never creates after an attempted/ambiguous operation. Percentages 1/5/25/50 contain the registered origin and edge versions at complementary weights; 100 contains only the registered edge version. The resulting database and file receipts bind gate, predecessor/successor IDs, provider creation times, weights, version IDs, annotation bytes, operation/request/response hashes, and rollout identity.

`rotate-ogabassey-storefront-edge.yml` is the only steady-state Worker code/configuration upgrade path after the initial generation reaches 100%. Its closed inputs are the new rollout tag and seven-field identity, current stable deployment ID, recreated preview-acceptance artifact run ID/hash, candidate version ID/bundle hash, and exact confirmation `CONFIRM_OGABASSEY_WORKER_ROTATION`; it accepts no traffic percentage or provider request body. The new Worker change first passes a normal reviewed PR, required checks, merge, complete governed-closure equivalence, and a new immutable protected tag. The workflow recreates both preview domains if absent, generates a fresh maximum-two-hour observation capability/admission, binds it into the new candidate version settings, serves that candidate only on isolated preview while proving parity, obtains fresh authenticated owner preview acceptance of the same admission, and requires the database current head to remain the old stable version at 100%. PostgreSQL then prepares the new generation and its `stage_candidate` operation; after the at-most-once POST is confirmed/read back as old-stable `100` plus new-candidate `0`, the workflow binds the exact candidate version-detail module/settings/admission hash to the accepted preview bundle and sole capability artifact and re-proves that the production override guard remains an unconditional block. Only then may PostgreSQL prepare `rotate`, deriving old-stable `99` plus new-candidate `1`; the provider job must produce the same bounded observation artifact through the candidate's identical observation route, and the separate HMAC-only job must construct, validate, and atomically register the same canonical receipt before confirmation. Subsequent `deploy-storefront-edge.yml` promotion runs use the same registered 24-hour gates and operation journal for `1 -> 5 -> 25 -> 50 -> 100`. At rotation 100%, confirmation atomically makes the candidate stable, retires the predecessor generation, and retains the previous stable version and complete ledger for at least seven days; rollback during that window uses a separately journaled database-derived rotation operation, never an unregistered provider deployment. The guardian accepts only the active database generation or its one pending operation. An ambiguous stage or rotation is terminal for that script identity/generation, removes the route, and requires a newly named script plus full re-enrollment rather than another POST. After the rollback window and seven-day evidence seal, an owner-approved cleanup may delete the retired version only after the database and provider prove it is neither current, pending, rollback-selected, nor referenced. Compatibility-date, compatibility-flag, dependency, observability, or Worker-code changes outside this rotation protocol mandate exact-route removal before deployment.

The deploy workflow is unavailable unless Task 1's `CloudflareWorkerArtifactReadbackQualification` passed on the same account and exact Scripts Versions/Deployments endpoint-schema contract and its fingerprint is present in the rollout record. It uploads only the already-hashed reviewed production bundle through `POST /accounts/{account_id}/workers/scripts/{script_name}/versions`; it may not invoke an unconstrained provider-side source build or substitute the Workers Beta API family. After each upload and before uploading another, fetch `GET /accounts/{account_id}/workers/scripts/{script_name}/versions/{version_id}`, canonically reconstruct `resources.script` modules plus version-scoped settings, and require their module graph/content hash, script ETag, compatibility flags (including `nodejs_compat`), and observability settings to equal `workerBundleSha256` and the governed manifest. The script-scoped latest raw-download/settings endpoints are corroboration only. Bind the version-detail proof immediately to the upload response's version ID; after both versions exist, list/read both version details and the Scripts Deployment tuple and require them to match the two individually sealed proofs. Any family/endpoint/schema fingerprint drift, ambiguous latest-only response, mixed-family ID, inability to distinguish both version resources, unrecognized transformation, or content hash that cannot be tied to uploaded bytes is `STOP`; a successful CLI exit is not provenance evidence. Seal API-family fingerprint, script/version/deployment IDs, version resources/settings, ETags, and matching bundle hashes before any route mutation.

`emergency-disable-ogabassey-edge.yml` is a separate manual-only `workflow_dispatch` workflow invoked with the break-glass tag as `ref`, the complete recorded identity tuple, and exact confirmation `DELETE_OGABASSEY_APEX_ROUTE`. Its credentialless first job runs `verify-storefront-rollout-ref.mjs` in `breakglass` mode and deliberately does not require the rollout tag, rollout ruleset, scheduler variables, or normal deployment environment. After owner approval, the `ogabassey-edge-breakglass` job receives only the dedicated break-glass token, verifies the complete Worker-route inventory equals the sealed inventory except for permitted runtime counters, deletes only the exact `ogabassey.com/*` route if its expected script and prior-origin fingerprint match, reads back its absence, proves the recorded origin from at least two locations, and verifies no other route/script/deployment/DNS/ruleset/R2 resource changed. It is idempotent when the exact route is already absent and fails closed on every other target or inventory difference.

`dispatch-ogabassey-edge-guardian.yml` is the only scheduled workflow. After both tag read-backs, Step 3A stores only the non-secret rollout tag plus the seven immutable `StorefrontRolloutIdentity` fields in explicitly named repository Actions variables, reads them back into the owner-approved rollout record, and dispatches one `verify_only` run before enabling the schedule. No per-cutover hold, proof, deployment, timestamp, or route value is stored in or passed through Actions variables or dispatch inputs. The dispatcher has no Cloudflare, R2, Supabase secret, Vercel, break-glass, or deployment-environment secret, has only `actions: write` plus required read permissions on its per-job `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and passes that exact identity tuple with `mode: enforce` to the tagged `guard-ogabassey-edge-budget.yml`. The tagged guardian reads the already hash-bound public Supabase URL/key variables, generates a fresh challenge, and obtains all mutable cutover state exclusively from `get_ogabassey_storefront_edge_guard_state` before its provider-secret job is eligible. A missing/malformed variable, identity/tag/public-config mismatch, invalid/stale/replayed guard receipt, variable read-back drift, or failed `verify_only` run keeps scheduling disabled or removes an active apex route and is `STOP`. Any later immutable identity-variable change invalidates the sealed rollout fingerprint and the seven-day evidence window until owner reapproval and a fresh read-back. GitHub documents `workflow_dispatch` as an exception that creates a downstream run when emitted with `GITHUB_TOKEN`; test the real repository permission during preflight and stop scheduling if dispatch is denied. The dispatcher cannot call provider APIs or perform rollback itself. Tests prove scheduled execution never imports mutation code, never receives provider credentials, never accepts mutable cutover state from GitHub, and cannot substitute a branch, default-branch head, PR ref, unprotected tag, a different merge method, or any identity tuple/public configuration that the tagged mutation workflow rejects. Use the current [workflow-dispatch ref contract](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflows#create-a-workflow-dispatch-event), [event SHA/ref semantics](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows#workflow_dispatch), and [`GITHUB_TOKEN` downstream-run exception](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/github_token#when-github_token-triggers-workflow-runs) as implementation references.

`build-ogabassey-alias-redirect-rules.ts` generates the checked-in exact rules from the shared route-and-method policy: each expression matches the exact alias hostname, `GET`/`HEAD`, and only paths for which `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest` returns `static-candidate`; it emits explicit exclusions for every platform/auth/runtime/dynamic/machine rule. `build-ogabassey-origin-waf-rules.ts` consumes the same canonical policy JSON and emits: (1) one exact-host-set block expression for non-`GET`/`HEAD` combinations that do not match an allowed origin rule; and (2) the approved rate-limit expressions for allowed expensive namespaces such as `/api` and `/baci-relay`. The generated artifacts embed the same `eligibilityPolicySha256`; generation fails if Cloudflare expression size/rule quotas cannot represent the policy, and production stops rather than dropping a clause.

`build-ogabassey-version-override-waf-rule.ts` emits one checked-in WAF custom-rule template for the exact apex and every production ingress host capable of reaching the production script. The rule unconditionally blocks any presence of `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Overrides`, including malformed, canonical single-member, multi-member, duplicate, differently cased, truncated-map, or oversized forms. It contains no script/version/nonce placeholder and has no temporary allow mode. Generated-rule tests exercise `http.request.headers`, duplicate arrays, and `http.request.headers.truncated`; Cloudflare Trace plus independent Internet probes prove every override attempt terminates before Worker invocation. There is no production request-header Transform Rule for either override or probe headers and no production nonce to store, transfer, redact, rotate, or expire.

Before production staging, one journaled `lock_override_guard` operation installs and reads back that unconditional guard. The exact `100/0` candidate identity is proved without public override traffic: same-run Scripts Get Version Detail binds the staged candidate ID's modules/settings to the governed bundle hash, and that hash must equal the already accepted isolated preview bundle. The first request allowed to execute the candidate through production ingress is the journaled 1% canary; the bounded observation route must return its live Version Metadata ID and the canonical receipt must be inserted atomically before `temporary_proof` or `rotate` can confirm. The guard remains unchanged throughout staging, canary, promotion, rollback, and rotation. It is removed and read back only after single-version 100% completion or protected abort cleanup. Cleanup failure keeps the route absent, records `cleanup_pending`, and blocks re-enrollment.

The 1% confirmation probe is bounded and fail-closed rather than probabilistically assumed. After the exact 99/1 deployment read-back and global-visibility wait qualified by Task 1, the credentialless observer sends at most 2,000 synthetic eligible `GET /__baci/edge-observation/<challenge>?n=<nonce>` requests, each with a distinct valid 128-bit lowercase-hex affinity cookie and exact lowercase-hex32 nonce, stopping immediately when `X-Baci-Edge-Version` equals the candidate. These requests are separately bound in the combined evidence artifact, excluded from production origin-rate and business evidence, and the candidate response is accepted only after the candidate version's normal parity probes separately preserve the accepted release/pointer/manifest tuple. If no candidate response is observed, any response lacks exact Version Metadata/challenge/cache isolation, the locked override guard fails its selected mode-specific terminal proof, the read-only export lag is incomplete, or the bounded hold/recovery deadline would be crossed, confirmation fails and route safety precedes read-only reconciliation. The Analytics branch never upgrades sampled diagnostic-log absence into evidence; it must use the qualified unsampled terminal-WAF source. No synthetic cookie, request count, or observed distribution is treated as traffic-share evidence; the later 24-hour gate uses only its qualified unsampled production sources.

`build-ogabassey-cohort-transform-rule.ts` emits the single checked-in request-header Transform Rule for `ogabassey.com`: it extracts exactly one `baci-edge-cohort` cookie, requires lowercase hexadecimal length 32, rejects duplicate/malformed/oversized values, and sets `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Key` only from that validated value. Generated-rule tests prove no raw `Cookie` header forwarding, no fallback value, no alias/other-host match, and no use of the cohort in redirect/WAF/origin authorization. Its expression and required ruleset phase must exactly match the capability proven and cost-approved by Task 1 Operational Gate B; drift returns `STOP` before merge rather than weakening the predicate.

`build-ogabassey-release-origin-response-header-rule.ts` emits the single checked-in response-header Transform Rule whose closed expression is exactly the two raw R2 custom-domain hosts and whose only action overwrites `X-Robots-Tag` with `noindex, nofollow, nosnippet`. Generated-rule and live read-back tests reject a wildcard, alias, apex, third host, append action, alternate value, disabled rule, missing hostname, a later matching rule that can overwrite the header, or any ordered ruleset drift. The sealed fingerprint includes the rule ID, complete expression/action, phase, enabled state, ordinal, every later rule's ID/expression/header actions, ruleset version, and canonical ordered-ruleset SHA-256. The rule is installed before either public custom domain is accepted and remains on `releases.ogabassey.com` after preview teardown; the fixed raw-origin root `robots.txt` is its independent crawler defense.

The dashboard/page tests prove the control is invisible to an unauthenticated or non-Ogabassey merchant, the mutation route authenticates before CSRF/body parsing/database mutation, and after authentication it invokes `checkCsrfProtection` before parsing or RPC. Integration fixtures obtain the cookie through the existing `/api/csrf` route and prove missing header, missing cookie, mismatched token, malformed/hostile Origin, and failed CSRF helper all return 403 with zero mutation RPC; the valid matching token plus same-origin request proceeds. Begin/accept_preview/complete/cancel call only their exact RPC signatures through the user-scoped client. `accept_preview` tests prove byte counts 0 and 16,385, malformed/noncanonical/dishonest `Content-Length`, count mismatch, invalid UTF-8, BOM, malformed/trailing JSON, excessive nesting, duplicate raw-versus-escaped keys, dangerous keys, arrays, numbers, booleans, nulls, unknown fields, supplied database times/receipt objects/tuple projections/acceptance hashes, and missing exact confirmation all fail before the acceptance RPC; 1 and 16,384 bytes reach decoding but must still satisfy the closed schema. SQL concurrency/golden tests prove the acceptance RPC locks the hold, uses one `transaction_timestamp()`, constructs the nested live hold receipt and complete preview acceptance itself, persists and returns their exact shared-vector bytes/hashes, returns byte-identical evidence on same-input replay, and rejects a second challenge, caller-authored receipt/time, stale capability registration, deadline equality, lifecycle invalidation, tuple/version/route/approval drift, and every terminal-state race. Final proof-import tests separately prove byte lengths 0 and 65,537 are rejected before read/decode/parse; 1 and 65,536 reach decoding but must still satisfy the schema; invalid UTF-8, BOM, malformed JSON, excessive nesting, 193 total members, duplicate/unknown keys, arrays, oversized strings, a missing/substituted preview acceptance, capability registration, nested hold receipt, production hold receipt, or candidate observation, an extra nested receipt, or any numeric/boolean observation field fails locally with zero request. Tests prove canonical decimal generations and every database-constructed time receipt plus shared SQL/TypeScript golden capability-registration/preview/hold/observation hash round-trip; malformed acceptance or capability-registration preimage/time, leaked raw challenge, grant replay/substitution, observation admission/capability/counts/path/deployment/version/guard/evidence hashes, an expired/mismatched hold, another RPC name, another merchant/host, staff/non-owner sessions, service-role-shaped input, or an unreviewed/unrecorded preview acceptance or proof fails before mutation. Returned receipts are private/no-store and contain no cookie, JWT, credential, or raw database error; complete cannot run without the exact protected-workflow proof bundle, recursively recomputed database-constructed preview acceptance, capability registration, all five hold-receipt hashes, registered candidate-observation receipt/hash, five distinct hold challenges, and explicit owner review. `verify-ogabassey-edge-cutover-hold.test.mjs` proves each of the three production-proof boundaries generates a fresh 256-bit challenge, calls only the read-only verifier with the exact hold ID/begin receipt/challenge, requires live status, validates database time/remaining duration and every tuple/hash field, preserves the complete redacted receipt in the final bundle, and fails closed on denial, terminal state, lifecycle invalidation, expiry equality, tuple drift, challenge mismatch/reuse, or replay. Separate capability/acceptance tests prove their two distinct database-constructed challenges and receipts cannot be substituted for those three. `docs/ops/ogabassey-storefront-cutover-hold.md` gives the literal browser path, required redacted input fields, both byte ceilings, retry-on-database-busy behavior, two-hour deadline, lifecycle-preemption result, full recursive proof-bundle download/import/recomputation steps, and mandatory cancel/rollback procedure without containing or asking the owner to extract a token.

Route integration tests repeat every proof-import adversarial case against the server, prove the exact client file bytes are the request body, and assert zero registration lookup/completion RPC on byte/content-type/action/parser/schema/hash/receipt/tuple/time failures. Success requires a preseeded immutable workflow registration plus its matching database `revoked` receipt and proves the route derives every RPC argument from the validated proof rather than headers or duplicate JSON fields. Separate workflow/database tests prove `create-ogabassey-cutover-proof.mjs` emits the shared golden bytes; the registration workflow exposes `OGABASSEY_CUTOVER_PROOF_HMAC` only as a step-scoped environment variable on the one literal `node .github/scripts/register-ogabassey-cutover-proof.mjs` step; that step passes no secret argument/input, contains no `${{ secrets.* }}` interpolation in `run:`, deletes `process.env.OGABASSEY_CUTOVER_PROOF_HMAC` immediately after strict Base64 decoding, never prints child environment/error objects, and uploads no artifact or cache containing process state. Every other step/job has the variable absent. Tests also prove its HMAC proof cannot authorize another RPC/action/hold/rollout, one registration disables further use, database revocation blocks every new registration without invalidating the immutable row, and direct owner requests with an arbitrary recomputable proof hash remain unable to complete. Guard-state SQL/TypeScript/Node golden vectors cover every union member, nullable terminal hash, challenge replay/mismatch, timestamp/deployment/route drift, unregistered live hold, completed exact proof, and every failed terminal state. Confirmation tests parse the workflow graph, prove only the verifier job has Actions artifact read, only the provider job has the deploy token, reject every input/artifact/guard/deployment mismatch, and inject lost responses plus zero/one/multiple exact annotation matches. The runbook requires owner confirmation that the GitHub production-environment secret and database secret were initialized from the same no-echo 32-byte-or-stronger value, then requires GitHub-secret deletion through the environment settings UI plus database revocation read-back immediately after registration or abort. Its pre/post checklists cover environment, repository, and every organization secret scope available to the repository and stop on incomplete visibility. GitHub absence is recorded as owner-reviewed operational evidence, not asserted by the application or used as a server authorization fact; the database `revoked` receipt is the enforceable completion boundary.

It requires explicit owner confirmation, a clean checkout of `mergeSha` whose artifact manifest matches the sealed hash, the separately vaulted break-glass token, exact zone ID/route pattern/expected Worker/prior-origin inputs copied from the sealed record, and before/after full route-inventory hashes. It may issue only the idempotent delete for `ogabassey.com/*`; any absent/mismatched target or inventory drift aborts before mutation. It then verifies route absence, unchanged sibling inventory, restored origin from three locations, token ID, timestamps, and incident evidence. This exception cannot deploy code, recreate a route, or change scripts, DNS, rulesets, Redirect/WAF/Transform Rules, R2, or another hostname.

If the owner-approved Paid capacity gate is not selected, `read-cloudflare-account-worker-usage.mjs` must first pass a seven-day shadow qualification without a production route. It returns `{ utcDay, observedAt, accountRequestCount, sourceComplete, sourceLagSeconds }`, rejects a non-account-wide/sampled/estimated counter, rejects a UTC-day mismatch, and cross-checks the observed delta against controlled requests to a second account Worker as well as the preview Worker. Compute `maximumDetectionAndRemovalMinutes = ceil((maxSourceLagSeconds + maxGithubScheduleDelaySeconds + maxWorkflowStartupSeconds + maxRouteDeleteAndReadbackSeconds) / 60)` and `worstCaseWindowRequests = measuredMaxAccountRequestsPerMinute * maximumDetectionAndRemovalMinutes`. The Free exception passes only when `hardAllowance - removalThresholdCount > worstCaseWindowRequests * 2`; otherwise production requires Paid.

For `live_workflow`, the guardian requires the live hold, rollout identity, route fingerprint, provider current deployment, affinity-rule state, override-guard state, their sole pending control-rule operations, and the sole pending `bootstrap_origin | stage_candidate | temporary_proof | rollback` deployment operation to match the database receipt. Before route attachment, a prepared/attempted bootstrap may coexist only with an absent apex route; after confirmed bootstrap, the exact origin-only head may coexist with the route. A prepared/attempted stage permits only that prior head or the exact stable/origin-100 candidate-0 successor and requires the override guard confirmed active; the candidate is ineligible for 1% until the successor, guard, complete preview-acceptance hash, nested capability-registration hash, and exact version-detail artifact receipt all agree. During temporary proof, the attempted-state recovery rules permit only the confirmed staged head or one exact 99/1 successor, with both guard and affinity state active; confirmation additionally requires the atomically stored candidate-observation receipt to bind that exact successor/version/guard/evidence artifact. The state expires at the earlier of hold expiry, operation recovery deadline, or the plan's two-hour cutover ceiling. Any missing operation, second pending operation of one kind, unexpected route presence/absence, provider mismatch, rule-ledger/observation drift, or deadline equality mandates exact-route removal. Either `cleanup_pending` state requires route absence and blocks preview, enrollment, staging, promotion, and recreation. This closes the interval between route attachment and proof registration without trusting workflow timing.

Enable `guard-ogabassey-edge-budget.yml` every five minutes before the first apex route only after that proof. Every verify/enforce run reads the profile's confirmed topology generation/state/hash and any topology operation before provider access: `preview_attached` requires both raw-release domains/robots/noindex probes and closed two-origin CORS; `preview_detached` requires preview Worker and R2-domain absence plus singleton canonical CORS; `prepared | provider_attempted | ambiguous` permits no inferred topology, no GC-claim assistance, and no topology mutation. An unexpected resource or CORS tuple freezes promotion and invokes only the separate exact-route safety removal; guardian never confirms, resumes, retries, or releases a topology operation. Every verify/enforce run also reads and hashes the complete ordered response-header ruleset, requires the sealed noindex rule to remain effective with no later overwrite, and sends independent `GET`/`HEAD` probes to every expected-present raw release domain requiring the fixed root `robots.txt` bytes/hash/metadata plus exact `X-Robots-Tag`; after preview teardown it instead requires the preview domain absent and probes only the unchanged production domain. It generates a fresh database challenge and verifies the canonical `StorefrontEdgeGuardState` before its provider job. `inactive` permits no apex route; `live_unregistered` with an active route, every failed terminal state, denial/malformed/replayed receipt, or live Cloudflare deployment/route evidence inconsistent with a registered state mandates exact-route removal. For `live_registered`, the receipt itself supplies the exact temporary deployment ID, provider creation time, database expiry, and route fingerprint; while that deployment remains current, reaching the earlier of expiry or creation plus two hours mandates exact-route removal. For `completed_registered` before the first ledger confirmation, the exact registered temporary deployment is explicitly valid only until the earlier of database hold expiry or provider creation plus two hours; an empty confirmed chain is a closed, tested transition state. After confirmation, the guardian compares the provider's current deployment only with canonical `currentDeployment` from the fresh database guard receipt. A `prepared` operation leaves the prior confirmed head authoritative. A `provider_attempted` operation permits the prior head or one exact expected successor only during the bounded recovery interval; the guardian never confirms it itself. `ambiguous` with the prior head still current freezes deployment and alerts for script retirement/new enrollment, while an unexpected current deployment, duplicate exact matches, or any field drift mandates route removal. An ambiguous operation is terminal: it can become confirmed only by a late exact provider match; otherwise the route stays absent and the same script identity/generation can never receive another deployment POST. Confirmed stage heads contain stable/origin `100` and candidate `0`; confirmed 1/5/25/50 heads contain the database generation's stable/candidate pair at exact weights; a confirmed 100 head contains only its candidate/stable edge version. The guardian never requires a predecessor to remain in provider history and never infers authority from annotations alone. Drift freezes promotion immediately; if the apex route is active, the guardian removes only that route and verifies restored origin, then alerts for manual repair. It has no authority to rewrite the Transform Rule, R2 object, CORS, Worker Custom Domain, or release-domain topology. It records/recognizes `cleanup_pending`, requires the route absent, and blocks re-enable until the protected cleanup workflow proves the cohort rule absent. At the lower of 35% or the forecasted safe-removal threshold, the guardian freezes promotion and alerts. At the lower of 45% or `hardAllowance - (worstCaseWindowRequests * 2)`, and immediately on telemetry incompleteness/lag-bound breach/unsafe spike, it uses the pre-authorized route-only guardian token to delete only the exact apex route, verifies the prior-origin fingerprint, seals the full redacted calculation/input artifact, and notifies the owner. It never deploys Worker code or mutates another route. The workflow is idempotent, has no re-enable action, rejects token-ID/policy mismatch, and is tested against every guard-state union member, receipt challenge/replay/hash/field drift, topology generation/state/hash/operation drift, response-rule deletion/reordering/later overwrite, raw-robots deletion/body/metadata drift, header-case-insensitive exact-value checks, another Worker consuming the account quota, UTC reset races, delayed/stale counters, delayed GitHub starts, the staged/temporary deployments immediately before/at/after each deadline, prepared/attempted/confirmed/ambiguous operation states, zero/one/multiple provider matches after an attempted POST, terminal ambiguity and forbidden same-script retry, provider-history truncation, initial and rotated generations, exact database-head drift, the zero-weight stage, the single-version 100% terminal deployment, all affinity-rule states, cleanup failure with route absence, terminal `expired_registered | invalidated_registered | cancelled_registered` states, attempts to mutate another route/zone/Worker, and route-deletion/readback failure. The separately tagged `emergency-disable-ogabassey-edge.yml` and final owner-only runbook expose the same delete-only guard without depending on the rollout tag or scheduler variables. Any failed shadow day or unbounded delay removes the Free option rather than weakening the margin.

The following is authoritative over the response-header-only and cohort-only wording above: every guardian run reads the complete ordered response-header, WAF custom-rule, and late request-header Transform rulesets and compares their exact IDs, versions, ordered hashes, raw expressions, and unrelated inventories with the v3 database receipt. Any active route with a staged, canary, or rotating production script requires the override guard to be `active` with the exact unconditional-block expression; there is no allowed temporary mode or pending open operation. Rule deletion/reorder/expression drift, an override-targeting Transform Rule, unrelated inventory drift, or any Internet override probe lacking the selected Gate-B mode-specific terminal proof mandates exact-route removal. Affinity must match the lifecycle projection independently. Cleanup remains incomplete until both affinity and override guard are confirmed absent. After any route-removal attempt, the provider job emits a canonical redacted receipt of at most 16 KiB to `report-ogabassey-edge-route-safety.yml`; the distinct HMAC-only reporter records it, and every guardian tick retries reporting until the ledger confirms the exact receipt. Tests cover unconditional blocking of missing-value, malformed, canonical, multi-member, duplicate, alternate-case, oversized, and truncated-map override requests; complete invocation-log absence in `complete-worker-log`; exact unsampled terminal-WAF count equality without a log-absence claim in `unsampled-terminal-waf`; sampled/adaptive-source rejection; each ruleset's deletion/reorder/unrelated drift; both cleanup states; reporter replay/failure; and route-deletion/read-back failure.

For topology specifically, this paragraph supersedes the guardian's abbreviated `prepared | provider_attempted | ambiguous` wording. The guardian projects `prepared | executing | reconciling | confirmed | confirmed_no_effect | cancelled_preempted | terminal_drift`, plus the current step state and deletion intent status `accepted_waiting_topology | topology_bound | gone_committed | erasure_recovery | erasure_admitted | terminal_drift | finalized`. It never reconciles or confirms a topology step itself. The guardian verifies the immutable fact, unique authority mapping, valid hash-chain head, original `acceptedAt`, initial-event nullability contract, and for every later state the non-null generation/set hash plus exact `topologyFenceIntentId`. It alerts before the remaining 24-hour budget breaches the recovery reserve and treats a missing/replaced intent, authority fork, SLA reset, expired or wrong-intent lifecycle fence, illegal event skip, or teardown reservation while an intent exists as route-removal drift. A deletion-associated terminal drift must expose recovery, a same-intent fence, conservative pair, per-host outcome, and remaining budget. `erasure_admitted` must expose the `gone` receipt and GC progress; `finalized` must expose the matching terminal-GC receipt and a scrubbed/null active authority without losing immutable fact/event evidence.

Export the Task 1 selected exact sources after each closed UTC day, hash/seal artifacts for at least 14 days, and keep synthetic probes separate. Join the Worker census with exact method-grouped Redirect Rule, WAF-block, rate-limit, and HTTP request counts for every Task 1 hostname and report raw counts per host/method/path class/rule ID before computing eligibility. Assign only `ogabassey.com` to `canonicalEligibleRequestCount`/`canonicalEligibleOriginAttemptCount`; assign every other inventoried alias only to `aliasEligibleRequestCount`/`aliasEligibleOriginRequestCount`; and reject overlap, omission, or unknown hosts. Require seven contiguous complete days, one constant hostname-inventory hash, eligibility-policy hash, alias ruleset version, WAF ruleset version, ordered response-header ruleset SHA-256, raw-origin `robots.txt` ETag/body/metadata hash, Worker deployment ID, `origin-only-v0` ID, and `edge-v1` ID; exact decision/invocation equality; `(canonicalEligibleOriginAttempts + aliasEligibleOriginAttempts) / (canonicalEligibleRequests + aliasEligibleRequests) <= 0.001`; zero eligible alias `GET`/`HEAD` origin requests; zero rejected-method origin requests; zero cross-host/tenant origin attempts; and `PASS`. Expected policy-allowed alias API/mutation origin requests remain in the raw dynamic report but outside both eligible numerator and denominator; invalid mutation blocks are reported separately and may not be hidden by eligibility filtering. Failed/timed-out eligible origin fetches remain in the canonical numerator. If configuration changes mid-window or any alias, Worker source, WAF source, or independent denominator samples/drops records, seal `NOT_PROVEN`, discard the partial window, and restart after the new fingerprint is active; do not substitute adaptive or weighted estimates.
