## Rollout PR Boundaries

Implement as these reviewable PRs; do not combine them into one large migration:

1. Task 1 tooling/policy PR only; after exact-head merge, run Gates A/B from its recorded SHA and place redacted baseline/qualification evidence in a separate non-executable PR.
2. Task 2A: opaque authorization, lazy gateway, and guarded transports; do not deploy independently.
3. Task 2B: foreground/signup/provider migrations and over-300-line extractions; do not deploy independently.
4. Task 2C: raw/background migrations and the global provider boundary.
5. Task 3A: shared deterministic-branding primitive; may proceed independently of Task 2.
6. Task 3B: zero-cost free/pre-signup onboarding controls; stacks on Tasks 2C and 3A.
7. Shared release, pointer, projection, complete platform/machine route-and-method policy, proxy drift tests, and canonical CSP-hash contracts.
8. Bounded Ogabassey route-and-interaction parity renderer, origin-owned machine-contract handoff, content-hashed client/integration runtimes, external speculation rules, and exact cart handoff.
9. Ogabassey-only release/lifecycle ledger, owner/security-reviewed lifecycle-bound deletion finalizer, dependency triggers, and PostgreSQL tests.
10. Ogabassey-allowlisted HMAC-capability VPS publisher, publication-epoch fencing, database-prioritized R2 mutation admission ahead of the shared VPS lock, exact-path temporary publisher sessions, lifecycle tombstones, durable deletion finalization, and activation reconciler.
11. Release-retention garbage collector with a separate exact-path temporary session, exact-prefix Cloudflare cache purge, public-absence proof, and dry-run mode.
12. Credentialless exact-host Cloudflare Worker, route-and-method terminal behavior, quoted CSP serialization, `origin-only-v0`/`edge-v1` versioned canary, cohort affinity, and credentialless-read R2 CORS for both preview and the persistent production release domain.
13. Reconciled Ogabassey publish/unpublish/account-deletion status flow with web and mobile clients.
14. Free-tier limits and cross-client media reservations, followed by their own protected-main migration apply/catalog-grant read-back; failure blocks only that independent cost-saving rollout.
15. Task 12 executable rollout-tooling/policy PR only: exact `ogabassey.com` pilot routing, method-gated exact-host alias redirects, generated invalid-mutation WAF/rate-limit controls, exact cohort-cookie Transform Rule, raw-origin robots/noindex controls with scheduled ordered-ruleset drift detection, authenticated owner dashboard/API cutover hold, bounded server-verified and workflow-HMAC-registered proof bundle, database-authoritative guardian/deployment-ledger receipts, durable pre-topology deletion-intent ledger, lifecycle-owned topology fence, step-reconcilable release-domain topology operation, and separate write/read credential boundary, at-most-once post-completion deployment confirmation, exact 24-hour promotion-gate registration, steady-state reviewed Worker rotation, immutable-tag-bound release-ledger migration proof, full transitive build/deploy closure plus reviewed bundle/read-back proof, separately protected rollout and break-glass tags, credentialless scheduled dispatcher, delete-only owner runbook, and Paid-capacity/default or proven account-wide Free guardian. After exact-head merge and closure/bundle equivalence proof, owner-approved operations run from the rollout tag; emergency removal remains independently available from the break-glass tag, and fingerprint-bound census/cost results land in a separate non-executable evidence PR.

Every PR must have a fresh exact-head review, green required checks, and a rollback description. Production resource creation, the exact apex route, R2 public-serving configuration, migration application, secret changes, garbage-collection activation, and crontab activation are separate owner-approved deployment actions. Wildcard routing, Cloudflare for SaaS, and enrollment of another merchant are explicitly outside phase one and require a later plan.

## Architecture Decisions

### Why this is an ecommerce builder, not a static CMS

Baci's merchant control plane and commerce model remain authoritative. The edge release is a derived, immutable browsing projection for one merchant; it does not introduce a plugin API, generic block registry, scheduled-publishing subsystem, transactional edge state, or a second catalog. WordPress is relevant only as an operational pattern for separating authoring from delivery.

### Why AI authorization sits at the provider boundary

Route-local buttons and entitlement checks are easy to miss as flows and fallbacks evolve. An opaque authorization required by the only provider-construction gateway makes anonymous/free spending fail closed even when a caller forgets a UI check; the repository boundary test prevents a second unguarded provider path.

### Why R2, not KV, owns activation

R2 provides global strong read-after-write consistency. KV is eventually consistent and may temporarily serve older values. Release objects and hostname pointers therefore live in R2. Pointer paths bypass CDN caching so the serving pointer remains useful as activation and rollback authority; KV may be considered later only as a non-authoritative cache.

### Why releases are immutable

Overwriting canonical objects creates CDN-consistency and partial-publication hazards. Versioned releases allow complete validation before one fenced pointer switch, preserve instant rollback, and make old-object cleanup asynchronous.













### Why the business case is measured before building

The apex may already be a Cloudflare cache hit while aliases or dynamic paths still reach Vercel. A storefront rewrite is justified only by attributable, reducible origin cost—not by request volume alone. Task 1 therefore inventories every Ogabassey ingress hostname, reconciles Cloudflare and Vercel evidence by path class, and stops before renderer/publisher work when the measured savings or owner-approved payback do not support the operational surface being added.

### Why canonical HTML is not cached in phase one

Caching canonical HTML would require globally correct purge keys and would weaken the pointer's rollback guarantee. Phase one pays one lightweight Worker invocation and pointer read per eligible document while immutable manifest/assets use release-ID cache keys. Canonical caching may be proposed later only after live Worker/R2 cost proves it necessary and a multi-POP purge test exists.

### Why immutable objects use Smart Tiered Cache but pointers do not

Immutable release keys cannot become stale because they are never overwritten, so long-lived edge caching and Smart Tiered Cache reduce R2 reads without weakening activation. Hostname pointers are mutable serving authority; caching them would relax R2's strong-consistency guarantee and delay activation, rollback, unpublish, suspension, or deletion. The two prefixes therefore have explicit opposite Cache Rules, and production disables the alternate `r2.dev` access path.

`BYPASS` is not accepted as proof: it means the request was initially cache-eligible and only rejected at response time. The pointer contract requires `DYNAMIC`, which Cloudflare documents for request-time cache ineligibility, plus no `Age` header. Every other or missing status fails closed.

### Why the Worker has no R2 binding

An R2 Worker binding exposes write/delete methods as well as reads. Because release data is already public, the serving Worker fetches it through a public R2 custom domain instead. This makes the public request handler credentialless and keeps all storage mutation authority in the publisher environment.

### Why Cloudflare credentials are split

Evidence qualification write/read, bucket/DNS/cache/WAF bootstrap, routine Worker deployment, release-domain topology teardown, topology reconciliation, production analytics measurement, emergency route removal, and release-object publication have different blast radii. The isolated evidence probe uses one short-lived write token plus a separate read-only event token and revokes both before its journal closes. A different short-lived bootstrap token handles one-time production control-plane changes; the routine token can update only the Worker script/route; the topology-write token is exposed only to one ledgered protected-tag provider step and is policy-scoped to the exact preview Worker Custom Domain, the single R2 bucket's CORS configuration, and the preview R2 custom domain—never an apex route, DNS, cache purge, Worker script, or another bucket. The separate topology-read token can read only those exact three resource/configuration endpoints and cannot mutate them; only `reconcile-ogabassey-release-domain-topology.yml` receives it, and that workflow has no topology-write token. Production measurement is analytics-read-only; the guardian can perform only the API permission Cloudflare requires for bounded exact-route deletion; and the VPS writer is bucket-scoped. Provider jobs have no topology HMAC, while prepare/attempt/reconcile/confirm jobs have no provider token. No long-lived CI credential receives the union of those powers. Because a policy document cannot prove which secret is installed, every workflow binds the reviewed policy to the live token ID returned by Cloudflare's verification endpoint before it acts.

### Why the exact Ogabassey route fails closed and has a route-disable escape hatch

The Worker is the Ogabassey origin-cost and release-integrity boundary. Fail-open would silently restore expensive Vercel traffic during a Worker failure, so the exact route reads back `request_limit_fail_open: false`. Because fail-closed quota exhaustion would make the storefront unavailable, production defaults to a measured Paid envelope. A Free exception exists only after an exact account-wide counter and every observation/scheduling/removal delay pass the seven-day qualification: promotion freezes at the lower of 35% or the forecasted safe threshold, and the guardian removes only `ogabassey.com/*` at the lower of 45% or the hard allowance minus twice the proven worst-window demand. It then proves the recorded origin is restored. It cannot deploy code or re-enable the route; an incomplete counter or unbounded delay makes Free ineligible.

### Why the canary has two real Worker versions

A percentage on a single edge implementation is not a rollback control. The first routed version is an origin-only Worker that preserves and measures the pre-cutover behavior; the second is the release-serving Worker. Cloudflare's version deployment and affinity key then make 1/5/25/50/100 promotion executable, attributable by Version Metadata, and reversible to a known origin-only version without waiting for a code build.

### Why aliases canonicalize only safe methods

Canonical redirects are an SEO and anonymous-navigation optimization, not a dynamic transport. Baci's existing proxy deliberately keeps alias APIs and legitimate non-`GET`/`HEAD` traffic same-origin because redirecting those requests can alter methods, lose bodies/cookies, or fail CORS; public machine contracts likewise need their current host-aware origin behavior. Exact Cloudflare redirects therefore match only shared-policy static-candidate `GET`/`HEAD`. The same policy authorizes known dynamic method/path pairs and generates a pre-origin WAF block for every other mutation combination on apex and aliases. Policy-allowed dynamic traffic remains reported outside the eligible static origin-avoidance denominator, while rejected traffic and any rejected-method origin attempt are reported explicitly.

### Why complete platform and machine-readable contracts remain origin-owned in phase one

Ogabassey exposes platform authentication redirects plus agent-commerce, OAuth/agent-auth, feed, markdown, app-link, `llms-full`, and well-known contracts whose capability/auth/repair projections exceed the bounded browsing release. Treating them as unlisted release documents would turn existing endpoints into edge 404s. A shared route-and-method vocabulary enumerates every concrete contract, and a behavior-level proxy test prevents drift without modifying protected `proxy.ts`. Unknown well-known names keep the current terminal 404 semantics at the edge rather than paying for an origin lookup.

### Why account deletion has a durable finalizer

The `gone` pointer must exist before merchant/auth deletion, but acceptance and completion cannot depend on the initiating browser or mobile session surviving topology, publication, or GC waits. The authenticated request locks one unique authority row and records its immutable deletion-intent fact, initial hash-chained event, and database `acceptedAt` before inspecting topology; an in-flight teardown changes only the derived `waiting_topology` status. After topology binding or conservative recovery, the publisher proves the matching pointer and appends `gone_committed` then `erasure_admitted`; GC independently seals the terminal-GC receipt without deleting account state or finalizer inputs. The existing HMAC capability then invokes the one Ogabassey-only lifecycle finalizer. It accepts no user/merchant/intent selector, derives and verifies the authority/event/fence/terminal receipt under lock, commits account deletion plus `finalized` plus same-intent fence release atomically, preserves the original 24-hour clock, and leaves the hostname tombstone and bounded immutable evidence. The VPS still receives no service-role key.

### Why third-party integrations use a compiled network policy

Revenue and attribution parity requires public browser identifiers and selected Google/ad-platform origins, but neither a release row nor merchant content may inject CSP text or URLs. The release projection carries strict public IDs/booleans only; a code-owned closed map compiles the exact network policy into the pointer-hashed manifest. Integration loaders are immutable external scripts, consent-gated, and preview-tested under enforced CSP. Speculation rules use an immutable external rules object and response header, avoiding an inline-script exception.

### Why live decision evidence is required

Synthetic probes prove selected routes behave correctly but do not measure real shopper traffic, attacks, unknown paths, or quota failures. The Worker emits one privacy-bounded decision event for every handled request and records origin attempts independently from final delivery. The seven-day gate passes only on an unsampled census whose decision count exactly matches Worker invocations. Adaptive-sampled Analytics Engine data may guide operations but cannot prove the contractual rate. Probes and Vercel evidence are independent cross-checks, not substitutes for the live denominator.

### Why the Worker starts on the current compatibility date

Cloudflare recommends a current compatibility date for new Workers so runtime fixes and documented API behavior match production. The first configuration pins `2026-07-31` for reproducibility; later updates are tested changes rather than an unbounded automatic mutation.

### Why publication is reconciled, not called atomic

PostgreSQL and R2 cannot share one transaction. The serving pointer is authoritative; database states record progress and a fenced reconciler repairs crashes before or after pointer CAS. The previous valid pointer remains available during a failed replacement build.

### Why unpublish and deletion use pointer tombstones

Deleting a pointer would make the Worker treat the host as unenrolled and send future traffic back to Vercel; leaving the prior edge pointer would keep stale content public. `unpublished` and `gone` are explicit fenced states that remain cheap at the edge, while account deletion waits for `gone` before removing control-plane identity.

### Why full-body integrity checks happen at publication

The authenticated publisher is the sole writer, uses immutable keys, and verifies complete object bytes before activation. The public Worker therefore hashes only the bounded manifest and validates route metadata/ETag before streaming; re-hashing and parsing every HTML/asset body would consume avoidable Worker CPU without creating an independent trust boundary.

### Why the dedicated Ogabassey theme comes before a generic free theme

Ogabassey already has a dedicated storefront and is the merchant whose Vercel bill is being reduced. Replacing it with the generic 25-product free theme would be a functional regression. The pilot therefore freezes reviewed route and interaction contracts—including seasonal theming, product configuration, cart/search/chrome behavior, and explicit revenue/conversion decisions—plus live-derived capacity bounds. Generic free-store edge delivery is a separate future design.

### Why only the apex is routed while aliases redirect at the edge

`ogabassey.com` is the canonical production storefront already represented by the current deployment and Cloudflare zone. An exact serving route gives a reversible merchant-scoped pilot. The known `www` and Baci-subdomain aliases currently form part of Ogabassey's ingress and should not pay Vercel merely to canonicalize safe browsing requests, so exact-host `GET`/`HEAD` Redirect Rules preserve their path/query at the edge. Mutation/API alias traffic preserves existing same-origin semantics. No wildcard, other merchant, Cloudflare for SaaS, alias pointer, or alias Worker route is introduced.

### Why the VPS publishes but does not serve storefront requests

Publication is bounded background work proportional to merchant changes. Public reads scale with shoppers and belong at the edge. The VPS can fail or restart without taking an already-published storefront offline.

### Why checkout remains dynamic

Static releases are merchandising snapshots, not transaction authority. Existing checkout code must continue validating product state, price, discounts, inventory, shipping, tax, and payment server-side.

## Self-Review Checklist

- [ ] The WordPress analogy is limited to authoring, immutable publication, and edge delivery mechanics; Baci's catalog and commerce model remain authoritative.
- [ ] `PublishedBuilderProjection` is a closed, versioned, Ogabassey-only commerce-aware block union; it adds no generic CMS/plugin/block platform.
- [ ] Commerce blocks store bounded identifiers and presentation options only, resolve against the same merchant's public release projection, and cannot embed price, stock, arbitrary fetches, HTML, JSX, or private/draft data.
- [ ] Draft save and authenticated draft preview have zero R2/queue effect; publish requires expected revision plus idempotency and atomically commits one validated immutable published projection/generation.
- [ ] Catalog, PDP, condition-offer, variant, cart handoff, checkout, inventory freshness, reviews, customer/order, and merchant publish journeys satisfy the ecommerce acceptance matrix.
- [ ] Dynamic checkout recomputes current product/offer/variant, price, promotion, inventory, shipping, tax, final payment amount, merchant, and customer state before accepting payment or creating an order.
- [ ] The 99.9% origin-avoidance contract includes only eligible static browse and redirect traffic; dynamic commerce interactions are sealed and reported separately.
- [ ] Freshness fallback is database-internal, selector-free, idempotent by overdue generation, has no fabricated owner identity/approval, and loses to deletion authority.
- [ ] Every public generation boundary uses canonical `generationDecimal` text and rejects values outside PostgreSQL positive bigint range without JavaScript-number conversion.

- [ ] Every Ogabassey request class has an R2 release path, an explicit route-and-method origin rule, or an edge terminal response; no blanket method fallback exists and every other merchant remains outside the Worker.
- [ ] Every Ogabassey write path that can change public output transactionally enqueues a release generation.
- [ ] The dependency matrix covers `merchant_feature_settings`, `product_categories`, published builder state, and every other field consumed by the normalized release projection.
- [ ] No release contains draft/private/customer/credential data.
- [ ] Release and pointer schemas are shared and runtime-validated.
- [ ] The manifest has one separately validated `notFound` object with status `404`; every normal route is `200`; unknown routes return that object as `404`; and `manifest.json` is excluded from its own object table and verified through the pointer hash plus independent upload metadata.


- [ ] Free AI and image generation are zero by default and fail closed server-side.
- [ ] Every provider SDK primitive is reachable only through the opaque spend-authorization gateway, including background/admin/image/vision paths.
- [ ] Tests cover source/destination tenant changes, deletions, renames, stale leases, and partial uploads.
- [ ] The public Worker has no privileged backend credentials and no R2 binding.
- [ ] The Module Worker uses `wrangler.jsonc`, generated binding types, `compatibility_date: "2026-07-31"`, exactly `global_fetch_private_origin` plus `nodejs_compat`, no `global_fetch_strictly_public`, no public `workers.dev` route, production-`workerd` and governed-polyfill tests, explicit priced Workers Logs sampling fixed by the selected evidence mode, exactly one custom console event per invocation, and traces disabled/read back.
- [ ] The exact `ogabassey.com/*` route reads back `request_limit_fail_open: false`; production defaults to 4x p99 Paid headroom, and any Free exception has a seven-day-qualified account-wide counter plus a proven 2x worst-window removal margin; Cloudflare 1027 is never reached intentionally.
- [ ] Production origin fallback uses the original same-host request and preserves method, body, query, cookies, and `Origin`.
- [ ] Preview is a static-only Worker Custom Domain with no application DNS origin or Vercel fetch: it reads canonical `hosts/ogabassey.com.json`, serves the exact immutable candidate, redirects shared-policy dynamic `GET`/`HEAD` to canonical `https://ogabassey.com`, rejects mutations at the edge, and is never accepted as evidence for production same-host fallback. Only after production rule evidence passes, the authenticated Ogabassey owner dashboard uses explicit double-submit CSRF enforcement to open a hold after atomic stale-lease recovery and database proof that no live admission, live claim, live undelivered session, or unexpired pointer-capable publisher credential exists. The authenticated `accept_preview` body carries no database time or receipt object; PostgreSQL atomically constructs and stores the complete owner acceptance, which is embedded completely in the final proof and recursively seals its database-constructed acceptance hold receipt plus the database-issued capability-registration receipt and nested capability hold receipt; three additional distinct-challenge capability-bound production receipts are preserved beside it. Browser, server, Node, and SQL recompute every nested preimage/hash and keep the protected rollout tied to the same live hold, exact database-derived observation window, registered private-artifact hash, and governed Supabase public configuration without exposing the raw challenge. The hold blocks all release publication until the matching owner-reviewed 1% proof completes while lifecycle work invalidates it and wins.
- [ ] Canonical HTML and pointer responses cannot be served from Cloudflare cache in phase one.
- [ ] Every pointer probe is sent with `cache: 'no-store'`, matches the sealed Cloudflare Trace rule ID/ruleset/expression, reports no `Age`, and reports only the nonempty Gate-B-qualified `DYNAMIC` status; a missing status, out-of-set status, `HIT`, `MISS`, `EXPIRED`, `REVALIDATED`, `STALE`, `UPDATING`, or Trace drift fails closed.
- [ ] Release-domain topology is one database-authoritative Ogabassey profile tuple with monotonic generation, closed `preview_attached | preview_detached` state, active-host-set hash, and at most one `lifecycle_deletion | teardown` fence. The authenticated deletion RPC locks one unique intent-authority row, persists/replays only its `activeIntentId`, original `acceptedAt`, and hash-chained append-only event stream before topology inspection; a transactionally derived projection is observable but never receipt authority. It returns `accepted_waiting_topology` rather than losing a request during an in-flight operation. It seals `[0,1]` before detach or confirmed `[0]` after detach; a pair uses a lifecycle-owned fence with no expiry/renewal and retains it through every claim replacement, host proof, outage, recovery, and finalization. Every lifecycle fence stores the matching non-null `topologyFenceIntentId`; transfer is atomic and same-intent, and only successful erasure finalization clears the last deletion fence. The initial event alone has null topology fields; every successor is topology-bound. Only the HMAC-ledgered protected-tag operation can detach preview. Every attempted step is at most once and ordered preview Worker absence, canonical-only CORS/read-back/negative probes, then preview R2-domain absence; a lost response reconciles exact after to confirmed/next-step or exact stable before after the visibility bound to `confirmed_no_effect`/no retry. With a waiting deletion, step-1 no-effect binds attached `[0,1]`, while step-2/3 no-effect is partial and must enter `terminal_drift -> erasure_recovery` with conservative `[0,1]`; it can never become `topology_bound`. A never-attempted operation preempted by deletion is `cancelled_preempted`. Anything else becomes terminal drift; with an accepted deletion it automatically seals conservative `[0,1]` and drives each host through `active_owned | detached_absent` erasure recovery without an owner wait or foreign-host purge. Guardian/break-glass route safety may remove only `ogabassey.com/*` and never changes topology.
- [ ] The single R2 bucket's `r2.dev` URL is disabled; during preview both exact R2 custom domains attach to that same bucket, and after production reaches 100% the preview release domain plus preview Worker Custom Domain are removed/read back absent while `releases.ogabassey.com` remains unchanged. The fixed raw-origin root `robots.txt` has exact disallow-all bytes/hash/metadata and is never garbage-collected. The two raw R2 custom domains and preview Worker return exactly `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow, nosnippet`; the canonical production apex strips it, and the ordered production raw-origin Transform Rule remains after preview teardown. The five-minute guardian hashes the ordered ruleset and probes both protections; drift freezes promotion and removes an active apex route. `/hosts/*` bypasses cache; `/releases/*` has a read-back long edge TTL but a maximum six-hour browser TTL; no conflicting Browser Cache TTL rule exists; approved Smart Tiered Cache state is read back.
- [ ] Production contains the exact Task 6 release-ledger migration version and live catalog/grant/RLS/RPC contracts, including the immutable Ogabassey builder-publication receipt and authenticated idempotent publication RPC, proved from the protected rollout tag before credentials initialize; the exact final `mergeSha` runtime closure has matching SLSA/reproducible-install receipts and every timer/socket is read back disabled before any provider credential is initialized. Task 11 has its own independent protected-main migration/read-back receipt and is not an Ogabassey pilot gate.
- [ ] The Worker imports the shared `classifyStorefrontEdgeRequest`, exhaustively handles its four results, and defines no second route or method vocabulary. The first-class `edge-observation` rule is exact-host/GET-only, validates one hex64 challenge plus the sole hex32 `n` query, bypasses static canonicalization, and returns bounded edge `404`/`405` for every invalid host/path/query/method without redirect, pointer/R2 access, or origin fetch. Origin/current-stable versions carry only the exact five-member disabled sentinel; isolated-preview/candidate settings bind one database-issued and database-registered maximum-two-hour capability artifact. The raw challenge stays outside the owner bundle, while its complete redacted registration receipt proves the artifact hash, challenge hash, nested live hold receipt, and database-derived window; candidate observation receipts bind that exact registration before SQL or owner completion can accept them.
- [ ] Only the approved Ogabassey merchant UUID/slug/apex can create or serve a pointer; exact alias Redirect Rules cover only shared-policy static-candidate `GET`/`HEAD`, policy-allowed dynamic/machine/mutation requests preserve same-origin behavior, generated WAF rules block every other mutation combination on all Task 1 hosts, and every other merchant/host is untouched.
- [ ] Public CSS, JavaScript, WOFF2, and speculation-rules assets pass credentialless R2 CORS/SRI probes from both sealed origins while preview exists; the byte-read policy is exactly `GET, HEAD`, exact canonical-plus-preview origins, and no credentials, wildcard/reflection, request-header, or write-method permission. The topology operation first removes/read-backs the preview Worker Custom Domain, then reads back canonical-only policy and fails preview-origin probes, then detaches the preview R2 custom domain; no pair-host deletion may begin or be disrupted during that sequence.
- [ ] The Worker hashes only the bounded manifest, validates object metadata, and streams release bodies without full-body hashing or HTML parsing.
- [ ] Static HTML has no inline executable code/style, every inline JSON-LD block matches a canonical manifest CSP hash serialized as one single-quoted hash-source, speculation rules are an immutable manifest-verified external object, and no `/_next/`, PostHog relay, or Vercel analytics dependency exists.
- [ ] Retention cleanup protects the active pointer target and two rollback releases, fails closed on pointer/epoch/session/lease/admission drift, and uses a provider-credentialless non-root coordinator plus four fixed root-installed systemd socket-activation endpoints for disjoint non-root R2-signer/R2-worker/cache-read/cache-purge units. There is no root request parser, D-Bus client, generic unit launcher, caller-selected socket path, or `Service=` override on an `Accept=yes` socket; phase identity comes from the fixed same-basename socket/template pair, while each maximum-8-KiB expiring request is independently verified against database phase/action-proof state. Every service receives exactly one full-duplex native activation stream on fd 3 with `FileDescriptorName=connection`, socket stdio is disabled, logs go only to journal, and every socket has exact connection/backlog/non-fatal poll caps with fatal trigger limiting disabled. Every template is collected after inactive-or-failed, and every phase has exact runtime/memory/task/file-descriptor limits. Signer readiness is a canonical response followed by a traced `shutdown(fd3, SHUT_WR)` and kernel EOF before worker activation; a real-systemd test proves that EOF arrives while the signer PID remains alive for the separate credential fetch. The R2 parent exists only in the signer unit; the qualified systemd manager transfers only raw EOF-delimited canonical JSON bytes for a hash-bound, database-recorded, at-most-ten-minute session from the signer's single-use socket into a different worker credential directory/user, and no session is stored in a regular file. Cleanup checkpoints each at-most-100-object R2 page, independently proves R2 empty, then drives each sealed `ActiveReleaseServingHost` ordinal through independent ownership read, derived singleton purge body, post-purge R2 recheck, and public-absence proof before finalization; two-location probes remain failure-detecting samples rather than authority. Scheduling includes database-priority admission, both R2 units, proof expiry/requalification, repeated per-ordinal purges, crash recovery, the bounded nine-minute worst-case admission delay, and 2x headroom for the 24-hour SLA.
- [ ] Finalized-gone GC snapshots the exact active raw-serving-host set before any deletion, persists one independent ownership/purge/absence state machine per member, and never substitutes a two-location sample for an authoritative host-specific acknowledgement. Its capacity proof, signed journal, token-policy receipt, SQL constraints, and negative tests reject an absent/extra/reordered host, a preview-host omission while attached, a multi-host purge body, or a completion after only the production host is absent. This requirement supersedes every abbreviated single-host purge example above: `{ "hosts": ["releases.ogabassey.com"] }` is valid only after the sealed preview-detachment read-back establishes the canonical singleton host set.

- [ ] Every fd-3 activation entrypoint uses `new net.Socket({ fd: 3, readable: true, writable: true, allowHalfOpen: true })`; request EOF cannot auto-close the response half. Unit and real-systemd tests delay work after coordinator `shutdown(SHUT_WR)`, then prove complete response flush, signer `shutdown(fd3, SHUT_WR)`, coordinator EOF, signer survival, and manager credential fetch in order.
- [ ] `unpublished`/`gone` pointer transitions atomically advance the publication epoch and invalidate stale claims; unpublish/deletion never report completion early, the immutable owner deletion fact plus valid event-chain head exists before any topology wait can be returned, and the lifecycle appends `gone_committed` then `erasure_admitted`, GC seals a terminal receipt without deleting authority, and the lifecycle-bound finalizer subsequently completes without a client session through every crash/retry window. Gone releases plus cached representations are removed within 24 hours measured from the original database `acceptedAt`, which is immutable across topology reconciliation, automated erasure recovery, `gone`, GC claims, retries, and finalization. The terminal-GC and finalized receipts contain the fact/acceptance/event-chain hash plus the exact active or conservative raw-serving-host set: finalized cleanup either binds confirmed preview detachment before sealing the singleton or independently proves `active_owned` purge acknowledgement and public absence, or `detached_absent` authoritative detachment/non-serving and public absence, for each sealed production/preview host. GC completion without a terminal receipt, GC deletion of finalizer inputs, missing/extra/stale/caller-selected hosts, a missing/forked intent event, a projection mismatch, an attempted purge of unknown/foreign ownership, an uncleared host, a non-finalization fence clear, or a restarted SLA clock fails finalization.
- [ ] The VPS publisher rejects `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`; lifecycle, dedicated edge-pointer rollback, release publication, and GC share one absolute lock behind a database admission gate ordered `lifecycle_pointer > edge_pointer_rollback > erasure_admitted_gc > release_publish > retention_gc`; continuous publication cannot starve deletion, and GC/release cannot delay an owner-requested origin pointer behind more than the bounded live higher-priority lease. Every release/rollback RPC validates a fresh single-use HMAC proof and matching live admission; separate IP-restricted R2 parents locally sign exact-action/exact-path sessions, with publisher sessions fixed at 180 seconds and expiring at least 15 seconds before their four-minute admission while GC sessions remain at most ten minutes. Local temporary credentials use the frozen Cloudflare HS256 JWT header/claims/action/path/time derivation, SHA-256 temporary secret, and padded `jwt/` session token defined in Task 7; no generic JWT signer or caller-selected grant exists. Each parent-only signer receives distinct coordinator-minted issuance/delivery proofs bound to one signer run and claim; issuance stores that run before the root service manager transfers a session, and matching delivery is recorded before the worker's first provider call, and GC waits past every publisher expiry; this preserves the proved four-minute-plus-five-minute worst-case admission bound. Ordinary deployment only stages the exact protected merge commit. The runtime build pins Node `24.11.1`, pnpm `11.7.0`, esbuild `0.28.1`, `aws4fetch` `1.0.20`, `fast-xml-parser` `5.10.1`, and manager-only `postgres` `3.4.9`, closes the complete module/tool/config/dependency-store graph including the fixed rollback processor, builds twice from separate network-disabled stores/checkouts, and emits a certificate-bound SLSA-v1 attestation verified against the exact repository, signer workflow, source SHA/ref, subject digest, and GitHub-hosted runner. One owner-run interactive-sudo installer verifies that commit and reviewed manifest, copies the runtime to an immutable root-owned content-addressed `/opt/baci/storefront-release-runtime/<protectedMergeSha>/` closure, installs system timers/units plus the separate root-only importer/database manager, and emits a reproducible secret-free receipt; no credential-bearing unit executes from the `bassey`-owned checkout, and rollback selects only a previously receipted immutable closure. A separate root-only no-echo TTY importer creates/rotates each allowlisted `LoadCredentialEncrypted` entry atomically, never accepts secrets in argv, environment, a shell pipeline, or a regular file, uses only anonymous pipes to `systemd-creds`, validates provider policy/token identity, pauses for owner-mediated provider revocation, independently proves old-invalid/new-valid before cleanup, sequentially proves current/previous HMAC slots through the fixed proof-only RPC, and leaves no plaintext artifact. The database manager is never a runtime service and uses only ephemeral direct `postgres` owner credentials through prepared parameters; it adds no service-role edge.

- [ ] The sealed pre-implementation business case covers every Ogabassey ingress host, prices at least one invocation plus one custom decision log event for every sampled Worker invocation, accounts for every other Worker consuming the shared allowance, applies the correct Free-daily or Paid-monthly reset/overage contract, and proves 4x worst-case daily account events remain strictly below forced sampling before proving positive reducible savings.
- [ ] The business case includes any zone-plan upgrade required by `http.request.cookies`; unsupported, unapproved, or uneconomic affinity stops before renderer/publisher work.
- [ ] `origin-only-v0` is proven at 100% before route attachment; the unconditional override guard is confirmed active; `edge-v1` is explicitly deployed/read back at 0% beside origin at 100%; its exact version-detail module/settings hash equals the governed bundle accepted on isolated preview; and every production version-override request remains WAF-blocked under the selected exact guard-proof branch. `complete-worker-log` requires complete matching invocation-log absence; `unsampled-terminal-waf` requires exact unsampled terminal-block count equality and asserts no sampled-log absence. The first candidate execution is the journaled 1% canary proved through the shared first-class no-cache observation route, isolated mode-specific read-only evidence, and one SQL/TypeScript/Node-canonical receipt carrying the selected mode/source fingerprint and atomically bound into the deployment ledger and owner proof before rollout to 5/25/50/100 with cohort affinity and version partitioning.
- [ ] Cohort cookies are issued/replaced/expired only on successful browser HTML navigations and force private/no-store; `/ads.txt`, IndexNow, robots, machine/API/non-HTML/HEAD/redirect/error responses never emit cohort `Set-Cookie`. The exact validated-cookie Transform Rule and unconditional override guard are removed and read back absent at single-version 100% and after every abort/rollback; failed cleanup of either records `cleanup_pending`, keeps the apex route absent, alerts, and blocks re-enrollment without expanding break-glass authority.
- [ ] The production origin-rate parser uses the explicit disjoint canonical-plus-alias numerator/denominator equations; overlap, omission, unknown hosts, alias eligible origins, or a zero denominator cannot pass.
- [ ] Every ordinary provider mutation runs from a protected rollout tag whose name contains both the full merge SHA and the 16-hex public-config suffix, while its annotated identity carries the full config hash; same-merge config rotation creates a new immutable tag pair and an existing mismatched tag is never forced, moved, or deleted. The merge commit's complete transitive build/deploy closure, canonical module graph, and dry-run production bundle are byte-identical to the recorded reviewed-head and required-checks identities; every external Action is pinned to a full commit SHA; deployed Worker modules/settings are read back and hash-equal before routing. Emergency removal is independently available from the protected break-glass tag, with only the owner-approved exact-route delete exception when both tagged dispatch and guardian removal are unavailable. The scheduled dispatcher has no provider credentials, and operational evidence is isolated in a later non-executable PR.
- [ ] Cutover receipts, capability-window/registration receipts, candidate-observation receipts, preview acceptance, guard state, initial-production proof, deployment gates, and deployment operations use byte-exact domain-separated length-framed cross-runtime preimages with canonical UTC-millisecond timestamp strings and shared golden vectors. The protected workflow registers the exact proof hash, complete embedded preview acceptance and capability registration, complete candidate-observation receipt/hash, and temporary deployment ID/provider time through a distinct single-use rollout-bound HMAC, then that HMAC is deleted/revoked after all GitHub scopes are checked; owner completion sends the original maximum-65,536-byte redacted proof bytes, and the authenticated route independently performs bounded unique-key parsing, recursive schema/hash/receipt/tuple/time checks, immutable registration/revocation read-back, and only then the completion RPC. The private raw capability artifact is never required by the owner because its database registration receipt provides the recomputable artifact/window/hold binding; a hash or timestamp copied from workflow state alone cannot satisfy the proof. A separate rotatable edge-deployment HMAC can register only gates and database-derived operation transitions, and confirmation recomputes rather than trusts the bounded observation JSON/hash before atomically storing it. Every Cloudflare POST follows an atomic `provider_attempted` receipt and executes at most once; zero after an attempted/ambiguous operation never creates again. The scheduled guardian obtains mutable cutover/deployment authority only from the challenge-bound Ogabassey guard RPC and compares Cloudflare's current deployment with the database head rather than reconstructing old provider history. An arbitrary owner-supplied observation/proof hash, percentage, version tuple, annotation, completion receipt, or provider-history entry cannot complete or promote a hold.
- [ ] Affinity and override-rule mutations use the closed control-rule operation ledger and exact prepare/attempt/confirm/ambiguous RPCs; the guardian verifies all three ordered rulesets against v3 receipts, removes the route on deadline equality or drift, and sends each bounded redacted route-safety receipt to the distinct HMAC-only reporter until the database confirms it.
- [ ] Pointer rollback closure supports exactly `completed | retryable_failure | terminal_drift | superseded` with byte-identical replay evidence; terminal outcomes require route-safety proof and cleanup pending. Every derived R2 session expires at `notAfter` unless its parent is revoked earlier, and parent revocation is documented and tested as a family-wide emergency kill switch rather than selective session revocation.
- [ ] Initial and rotated Worker generations have an append-only database ledger, exact current head, immutable 24-hour gate receipts, prepared/attempted/confirmed/terminal-ambiguous recovery tests, an explicit stable-100/candidate-0 stage plus exact artifact/preview identity proof before every first 1% canary, and a protected `1 -> 5 -> 25 -> 50 -> 100` rollout. Ambiguity never permits a second POST or same-script replacement; an unresolved case removes the route and requires a fresh script identity/enrollment. A later Worker code/configuration change uses a new reviewed merge/tag, recreated isolated preview acceptance, database-derived old-stable/new-candidate zero-weight staging and rotation, and retained rollback generation; the original `edge-v1` is not a permanent guardian lock.
- [ ] Worker tooling pins Wrangler `4.115.0`, generates types through the workspace-local binary, and has the exact named runtime test files.
- [ ] The reviewed plan hash is recorded on the execution branch before a worktree starts implementation.
- [ ] Cost completion is based on a current seven-day production comparison.
